000030  ## Scope and result
000032  This document reproduces the submitted table styles, figure compositions, and
000033  manuscript-scale canvases from the earlier manuscript-generating descriptive
000034  analysis, but fills them with the updated verified data. It also repairs errors that
000035  could not responsibly be copied forward. The main analytical measurement is
000036  near-eye melEDI; chest measurement is shown only as a complementary description
000037  and the two placements are never pooled.
000039  The replicated layouts include the wide participant-and-site table, the wide
000040  site-by-metric table with miniature distributions, the five-part study
000041  overview, the 3 × 3 site profiles, the 4 × 4 metric grid, the four-panel
000042  time-series explanation, the latitude–photoperiod diagnostic, and the
000043  site-by-state light-context table.
000120  All 13 required shared manifests matched their pinned SHA-256 fingerprints
000121  before any analytical input was read. The current display registry is
000122  `config/site_display_registry.csv`; it supplies the labels, visible order, and
000123  colours in every table and figure. The visible site sequence is
000124  `r paste(site_display$display_name, collapse = ", ")`.
000126  ## Updated sample
000128  The normalized participant roster contains **191 participants**. After the
000129  pre-specified ≥80% full-day completeness rule and exact-all-zero screen, the
000130  main near-eye dataset contains **141 participants, 816 participant-days, and
000131  1,175,160 one-minute real observations**. The complementary chest dataset
000132  contains **154 participants, 902 participant-days, and 1,298,880 one-minute
000133  real observations**. The paired subset contains **112 participants and 643
000134  participant-days**.
000136  Two of 818 otherwise eligible near-eye participant-days and three of 905
000137  otherwise eligible chest participant-days were exact-all-zero melEDI days and
000138  were excluded. A participant was not removed merely because one of their days
000139  was excluded.
000162  ## Table 1: participant and site characteristics
000164  @tbl-participant-site keeps the earlier wide site-column organization. Cells
000165  now identify whether a quantity belongs to near-eye, chest, paired, roster, or
000166  diary data. Participant information covers the complete normalized roster.
000167  Collection dates and civil photoperiod use the union of every recorded
000168  non-all-zero participant-day across both placements; screened-day counts
000169  retain the main-analysis completeness rule.
000179  ### Manuscript Table 1 variant
000181  The rendered submitted manuscript used the reduced `tbl1_red` variant rather
000182  than the expanded table above. The following table reproduces that exact row
000183  selection with the updated data and corrections. As in the submitted variant,
000184  it omits Morningness–Eveningness Questionnaire score, gender, collection
000185  dates, weekday/weekend counts, workday/free-day diaries, social jetlag, and
000186  sleep duration; all remaining rows retain their original order and styling.
000197  Important corrections embodied in @tbl-participant-site are:
000199  - MPI is displayed as Tübingen and TUM as Munich, repairing their interchanged
000200    institution labels in the earlier table.
000201  - Participant and participant-day rows show the whole available roster plus
000202    near-eye, chest, and paired subsets. Participant time retains the screened
000203    near-eye basis and is limited to its two largest time units.
000204  - Declared non-wear is reported for near-eye only; the earlier chest display
000205    reused the near-eye input and has therefore been removed.
000206  - Screened days show only the final result after the completeness and all-zero
000207    screens. Civil photoperiod comes from verified site-and-solar context across
000208    the whole available roster.
000209  - Clock-time chronotype summaries are circular. During rebuilding, a
000210    `quantile()` argument typo in the first implementation was detected,
000211    documented, and corrected before this final table was generated.
000213  ## Table 2: metric distributions
000215  @tbl-near-eye-metrics reproduces the old metric categories, order, ten site
000216  columns, scaling column, and enlarged miniature distribution column. Every
000217  numerical cell states `N`, the number of participants, and `d`, the number of
000218  participant-days with a finite value. The site columns have equal widths and
000219  each median, interval, mean ± SD, and size line is kept intact. A compact line
000220  under each metric name states what the metric captures and why that construct
000221  is physiologically or health-relevant; these notes are context, not health-
000222  effect estimates from this descriptive dataset.
000224  MDER is the arithmetic mean of viable one-minute melEDI / photopic illuminance
000225  ratios, with both channels finite and strictly positive. Each day
000226  is evaluated on a complete 1,440-minute local wall-clock grid and retained
000227  with at least 720 viable ratios (the inclusive 50% rule). Fall-back duplicate
000228  local minutes are averaged within channel before the ratio is formed;
000229  spring-forward absent minutes remain missing.
000231  For the L10 mean, source windows verified as entirely zero are retained as
000232  exact 0 lx. Machine-precision residuals from the averaging calculation are
000233  not interpreted as positive exposure.
000243  The numerical values in @tbl-near-eye-metrics come from the verified metric
000244  artifacts; the table does not recalculate any metric. Clock-time means and
000245  middle-50% intervals are circular, rather than treating 23:59 and 00:01 as far
000246  apart. The miniature plots are redundant visual summaries: the adjacent cells
000247  remain the authoritative values and provide the exact denominators.
000249  ## Figure 1: study overview
000251  The study overview retains the submitted five-part composition: protocol,
000252  site map, collection timing, photoperiod coverage, and a repeated 48-hour
000253  near-eye profile. The second 24-hour cycle is a display duplicate, not
000254  additional data.
000255  Collection spans are shown as rectangles and split only when a site has at
000256  least six consecutive dates without any available data.
000267  Panel B restores the submitted country and coordinate information while
000268  retaining the registered site names and colours. The black and site-coloured
000269  profiles use `LightLogR::aggregate_Datetime()` in
000270  15-minute floor bins after removing participant grouping; the numeric handler
000271  is the median with `na.rm = TRUE`. Nested grey ribbons show the central 50%
000272  and 90% of eligible one-minute values in each overall bin, with direct arrow
000273  labels. They are value intervals, not confidence intervals. The red band from
000274  the lower plot edge to −0.1 shows average diary sleep and the blue shading
000275  shows average civil night; declared non-wear is not displayed. The melEDI
000276  axis uses `LightLogR::symlog_trans(base = 10, thr = 1, scale = 1)` and is
000277  capped above 10,000 lx to reserve less space for unsupported extremes.
000279  ## Figure 2: site-specific profiles
000281  The main near-eye profiles retain the earlier 3 × 3 double-plot layout and
000282  registered site order. Each site uses the same 15-minute
000283  `aggregate_Datetime()` median with nested central 50%, 75%, and 95% value
000284  intervals. The interval legend shows the corresponding ribbon opacities. The
000285  red mean-sleep strip extends from the lower plot edge to −0.1. The taller,
000286  450-dpi exports preserve the accepted visual grammar while improving detail.
000297  The complementary chest profile uses the same visual grammar in a complete
000298  four-by-two layout. Tübingen is
000299  omitted because the updated chest main dataset contains no eligible MPI site
000300  panel; the remaining sites retain their registered order and colours.
000311  Chest values describe the environment at the chest sensor. They are not
000312  treated as equivalent to near-eye or ocular exposure.
000314  ## Figure 3: reader-facing metric distributions
000316  @fig-near-eye-metric-distributions reproduces the old 4 × 4 ordering and
000317  ridge-plus-box construction. Site names remain visible in the first column of
000318  panels, so colour is not the only way to identify a distribution.
000329  ## Figure 4: from time series to a daily metric
000331  The submitted explanatory figure used seven fixed German-site examples over
000332  five aligned study days. @fig-time-series-to-metrics retains the same IDs and
000333  the same submitted study days 2–6, then orders participants from top to bottom by their
000334  median TAT250 across those days. The source CSV preserves each participant’s
000335  actual local dates, while the visible x-axis retains the submitted Wednesday
000336  to Sunday alignment.
000347  Panel A uses the stored floor-aligned 30-minute near-eye arithmetic means.
000348  Panels B–D recalculate daily TAT250 from
000349  exactly those displayed daytime samples with
000350  `LightLogR::duration_above_threshold()`. This figure illustrates the fixed
000351  examples and does not estimate a population weekday effect.
000353  ## Figure 5: latitude and observed photoperiod
000355  The latitude diagnostic is retained as supplementary context based on the 816
000356  main near-eye participant-days. Its black impossible-region ribbons consume
000357  the verified theoretical bounds from the current H1 reporting artifact, so
000358  the panel follows the submitted and current H1 visual grammar without a new
000359  calculation in the descriptive pipeline. The density curves and observations
000360  are drawn first and the black feasibility curtains are drawn over them.
000361  Points use deterministic two-dimensional jitter to avoid artificial bands
000362  without changing any underlying photoperiod or latitude.
000373  ## Light exposure in recommendation contexts
000375  @tbl-recommendation-context preserves the earlier site-row and state-column
000376  organization. The thresholds are wake ≥250 lx melEDI, before sleep ≤10 lx
000377  melEDI, and sleep ≤1 lx melEDI [@brown2022]. The results are deliberately
000378  described as *fractions of valid measured minutes in a recommended range*, not
000379  as adherence, compliance, or evidence of a biological response.
000391  Each percentage is followed by one grey `relevant / all` count. Wake,
000392  before-sleep, and sleep cells divide minutes within the
000393  recommended context by all valid one-minute observations in that diary state;
000394  the combined column pools all classified valid minutes. The four state-share
000395  columns divide minutes in the displayed state by all eligible real minutes.
000396  Diary-defined sleep combines the
000397  near-eye sensor while awake with a bedside sensor during sleep; the sleep row
000398  therefore describes the sleep environment rather than direct ocular exposure.
000400  ## Exact comparison with the earlier render
000402  The earlier HTML was read only as a record of displayed cells; no old `.RData`
000403  workspace was loaded. Machine-readable comparisons cover every displayed
000404  publication-table cell:
000432  The comparison summary above reports all current cells and classifies each
000433  against the submitted render. Metric timing rows also changed to circular
000434  summaries, while the contextual table reflects the updated eligible-day and
000435  state denominators.
000437  Every numerical cell, plus the explanation assigned to its difference, is in:
000439  - `audit/descriptives/previous_table1_comparison.csv`
000440  - `audit/descriptives/previous_table2_comparison.csv`
000441  - `audit/descriptives/previous_recommendation_comparison.csv`
000443  Every old table, figure, panel, and batch export is covered by
000444  `audit/descriptives/output_difference_explanations.csv`. Redundant reduced
000445  tables, letter-only metric exports, and individual site PDFs were not copied
000446  as separate deliverables because they add no distinct construct; their values
000447  and panels remain in the full replicated tables, composite figures, and exact
000448  source CSVs.
000450  ### Stand-alone visual exports
000452  The four publication tables are exported as PNG files with their registered
000453  `gtsave()` viewport widths. Each of the six durable figures uses its registered,
000454  content-specific canvas and export scale to create a 300-dpi PNG, a

000459  use 3.5--5 pt. The following record gives the

000485  ## Reproducibility and output contract

000500  these values does not close the figure-specific detail QA. The 10.5-by-10-inch
000501  base

===== notebooks/preparation/01_import_state_alignment.qmd =====
000589  ## Purpose and position in the analysis chain
000591  Preparation 01 establishes the measurement record that all later preparation
000592  steps use. The production workflow reads the fixed light-logger, sleep-diary,
000593  and wear-log releases from nine study sites. It checks their identities,
000594  converts the logger observations to complete one-minute values, preserves both
000595  actual and local clock time, and assigns a measurement context to each minute.
000596  Its outputs enter Preparation 02, where hourly and daily coverage is assessed.
000598  The exact inputs are the 35 fixed local source files recorded in
000599  [`pinned_downloads.csv`](../../artifacts/12_manifests/pinned_downloads.csv).
000600  Their repository revisions, DOIs, URLs, byte sizes, and SHA-256 digital
000601  fingerprints are propagated to
000602  [`source_audit.csv`](../../artifacts/02_aligned/source_audit.csv). A SHA-256
000603  fingerprint is used here to establish file identity; it does not describe the
000604  scientific contents of the file.
000606  ::: {.callout-note appearance="simple" title="What is calculated during this render?"}
000607  This report reads already stored CSV manifests and audits. During rendering it
000608  checks the existence, byte size, and SHA-256 fingerprint of every listed
000609  Preparation 01 artifact and fixed source file; checks the columns needed for
000610  the displays; and calculates only compact descriptive totals from stored audit
000611  rows. It does not retrieve a source file, import logger data, aggregate a
000612  minute, align a diary or wear period, replace a signal value, rebuild an
000613  artifact, or run the production verifier.
000614  :::
000630  ## 1. Establish the exact source files
000632  The production import uses one recorded release per site. Table
000633  @tbl-preparation01-source-identity shows the human-readable site names in the
000634  submitted-manuscript order. The source audit contains four inputs per site
000635  (sleep diary, wear log, near-eye light, and chest light), except Tübingen,
000636  where no chest logger file is part of the study input.
000681  ## 2. Preserve actual time and local clock time
000683  Two time coordinates are retained because they answer different questions.
000684  `datetime_utc` identifies the actual instant and is used to order readings and
000685  measure elapsed time. `datetime_wall` represents the local clock reading, so
000686  08:00 has the same time-of-day meaning at every site. At an autumn clock
000687  change, one local minute can correspond to two actual instants. The stored
000688  audit contains 360 such participant-level wall-clock minutes—120 for Madrid
000689  near-eye data, 120 for Madrid chest data, and 120 for Tübingen near-eye data.
000690  They remain distinct in UTC and are not discarded or averaged at this stage.
000692  The original logger interval is inferred separately for each participant and
000693  placement. A one-minute value is retained only when all expected original
000694  readings in that minute are present and finite. Table
000695  @tbl-preparation01-aggregation-regimes shows the resulting regimes; the
000696  production audits recorded no incomplete original-reading schedule.
000728  ## 3. Prepare sleep and wear periods
000730  The sleep diary defines the interval from sleep preparation to wake. Its start
000731  is included and its end is excluded. These measurements characterize the
000732  bedside sleep environment and remain part of the intended hybrid 24-hour
000733  record. The wear log supplies additional context. An `off` label while awake
000734  is treated as true non-wear; `sleep` and `site_leave` labels are retained for
000735  provenance but do not independently remove a light value.
000750  Valid intervals are stored for later projection onto complete participant-day
000751  grids. Twenty-two sleep-derived records could not define a supported wake
000752  interval and are listed with participant, source rows, bounds, and reason in
000753  [`state_interval_audit.csv`](../../artifacts/02_aligned/state_interval_audit.csv).
000754  They were not silently repaired. No wear-log interval required review.
000799  ## 4. Align measurement context without losing rows
000801  Sleep and wear periods are matched to each one-minute light record using
000802  participant and actual time. All 17 site-placement joins retained their input
000803  row count. A missing wear-log label is not interpreted as non-wear. Table
000804  @tbl-preparation01-state-outcomes therefore reports missing wear information
000805  as provenance, not as an automatic exclusion.
000826  ## 5. Apply the melEDI operating limit
000828  The valid melanopic equivalent daylight illuminance operating range is
000829  strictly below 100,000 lx melEDI [@condor2023actlumus]. Values at or above
000830  that boundary remain in the stored raw channel and receive an explicit flag,
000831  but the analytical melEDI channel is unavailable for that minute. The rule
000832  does not remove illuminance solely because melEDI reached its limit.
000869  ## 6. Stored sample after import and alignment
000871  The result is a complete set of one-minute grid rows for every imported
000872  participant stream. A grid row can contain an unavailable signal because of
000873  missing original data, non-wear, or the melEDI operating limit; therefore grid
000874  rows and valid signal minutes are reported separately.
000922  Near-eye and chest measurements remain separate. Chest measurements are a
000923  complementary placement and are not pooled with the near-eye data.
000925  ## 7. Checks that had to pass
000927  The stored audits expose the checks performed during production. Rendering
000928  does not rerun their producing verifier; it confirms the identities of the
000929  stored evidence and summarizes the recorded outcomes.
000963  ## Production scripts and why the operations are separate
000965  The producing code is listed in execution order in Table
000966  @tbl-preparation01-script-map. Separating source identity, temporal
000967  aggregation, interval preparation, state matching, and verification leaves a
000968  stored checkpoint at each scientifically consequential boundary.
000983  ## Exact artifacts passed to Preparation 02
000985  Preparation 02 receives the two combined one-minute files and the checked
000986  state intervals. The intervals are needed because Preparation 02 inserts
000987  explicit missing-minute rows before calculating coverage; known sleep, wake,
000988  and non-wear periods must also be assigned to those inserted rows.
001010  ## Render environment

===== notebooks/preparation/02_coverage_sample_flow.qmd =====
000468  ## Purpose and position in the analysis chain
000470  Preparation 02 evaluates whether the aligned one-minute records from
000471  Preparation 01 contain enough valid melEDI to represent an hour or a complete
000472  participant-day. It reads the stored near-eye and chest files separately,
000473  constructs a complete local-clock grid for every participant-date present in
000474  those files, and records hourly and daily coverage decisions. The accepted
000475  outputs enter Preparation 03 for reference-profile learning and Preparation
000476  04 for metric derivation.
000478  The primary dataset uses the full 1,440-minute hybrid measurement day,
000479  including bedside sleep-environment measurements. A day passes when at least
000480  80% of those minutes have valid melEDI. The 50% hourly rule is applied only
000481  when an hourly summary is needed; a failed hour does not erase its observed
000482  minutes from the daily decision or from minute-based metrics.
000484  The separately predefined **gap-timing-unaware dataset** still passed the
000485  general 50%-per-hour and 80%-per-day coverage rules. Its name means that the
000486  timing of remaining missing observations was not used for an additional
000487  metric-specific adjustment. For contrast, the primary dataset could be
000488  interpreted as a “time-sensitive primary metric dataset.” This report presents
000489  the general coverage step for the primary dataset; the separate dataset is
000490  constructed and identified later in the preparation chain.
000492  ::: {.callout-note appearance="simple" title="What is calculated during this render?"}
000493  This report reads the stored coverage manifest, settings, hourly decisions,
000494  daily decisions, reason-coded missing-data periods, and sample-flow table.
000495  During rendering it checks file identities and required columns and calculates
000496  only compact descriptive summaries of those stored rows. It does not construct
000497  a participant-day grid, reassess an hour or day, change a coverage flag,
000498  rebuild a coverage file, derive a metric, or run the production verifier.
000499  :::
000516  ## 1. The decisions applied
000518  Coverage uses melEDI as the common availability signal. Illuminance retains
000519  its own missingness, so an unavailable melEDI value does not erase an observed
000520  illuminance value. Table @tbl-preparation02-rules separates the hourly and
000521  daily decisions and shows when each affects later use.
000536  The daily denominator differs from the preregistered sleep-excluded
000537  denominator. Sleep-excluded rules remain prespecified sensitivities; they are
000538  not substituted for the primary decision on this page. Preparation 04 later
000539  checks whether the timing and amount of remaining support are adequate for
000540  each specific metric.
000542  ## 2. Overall input and retained coverage
000544  No observation ordered in actual time is deleted. Coverage decisions are
000545  stored as flags and eligible signal columns. The aligned values, raw values,
000546  time variables, and sleep/wear provenance remain available in the placement
000547  files.
000563  The primary day rule retained 816 of 1,136 near-eye participant-days and 902
000564  of 1,249 chest participant-days. The corresponding eligible files contain
000565  1,131,060 valid near-eye melEDI minutes and 1,253,505 valid chest melEDI
000566  minutes. Near-eye and chest measurements remain separate throughout.
000568  ## 3. Coverage by site
000570  Table @tbl-preparation02-site-days shows both the numerator and denominator at
000571  each site. “Days retained by direct daily rule” are days that reached 80%
000572  coverage across the day but would have failed if sub-50% hours had first been
000573  discarded. The direct daily rule retained seven such near-eye days and eight
000574  such chest days.
000608  Daily valid melEDI fractions were not concentrated only near the 80% boundary.
000609  Their stored distribution ranged from near-zero coverage to complete coverage,
000610  with medians of 94.7% for near-eye days and 95.8% for chest days.
000626  ## 4. Why measurements were unavailable
000628  The stored missing-data records distinguish a missing one-minute record, an
000629  observed record whose signal was unusable, an otherwise passing day with only
000630  zero melEDI values, and a day below 80% valid melEDI. These categories can
000631  overlap: missing readings are the underlying event, while hourly or daily
000632  failure is a consequence for a later summary.
000668  Five otherwise passing days were excluded by the flat-zero screen: two
000669  near-eye and two chest days at Izmir, plus one chest day at Delft. Individual
000670  zero readings remain valid measurements, and the inclusive sensitivity flag
000671  remains stored for those days.
000673  ## 5. Sample flow through each branch
000675  The full sample-flow file reports each stage both overall and by site. Table
000676  @tbl-preparation02-sample-flow shows the overall branches without treating
000677  actual-time rows, local-clock rows, and valid signal minutes as interchangeable
000678  denominators.
000708  The all-zero-inclusive and hour-screened rows are stored comparisons, not the
000709  primary dataset. They do not replace the accepted direct daily rule.
000711  ## 6. Checks that passed
000742  ## Production scripts and execution order
000757  ## Exact artifacts passed forward
000779  ## Render environment

===== notebooks/preparation/03_reference_profiles.qmd =====
000803  ## Purpose and position in the preparation chain
000805  Preparation 03 learned fixed time-of-day reference profiles from the
000806  participant-days retained in [Preparation 02](02_coverage_sample_flow.qmd).
000807  These profiles describe when melEDI and illuminance were typically observed
000808  across the day while giving each participant equal influence. Preparation 04
000809  uses them to judge whether a participant-day was observed at the times that
000810  matter for a particular metric. The profiles do not replace an observed
000811  measurement and, except for the explicitly permitted additive dose
000812  correction, do not change a metric value.
000814  The exact scientific inputs were:
000816  - [near-eye Rule A one-minute records](../../artifacts/03_coverage/light_glasses_coverage.rds);
000817  - [chest Rule A one-minute records](../../artifacts/03_coverage/light_chest_coverage.rds);
000818  - [the stored Rule A settings](../../artifacts/03_coverage/coverage_settings.csv);
000819    and
000820  - [the current Preparation 03 artifact manifest](../../artifacts/12_manifests/reference_profile_artifacts.csv).
000822  The outputs are the fixed intensity profiles, the strict-threshold timing
000823  distribution, metric-specific relevance maps, their support summaries, and
000824  the provenance records under
000825  `artifacts/04_reference_profiles/`. They are passed to
000826  [Preparation 04](04_metric_derivation.qmd).
000828  ::: {.callout-note appearance="simple" title="What is calculated during this render?"}
000829  This page reads already stored Preparation 03 artifacts. During rendering, it
000830  checks their paths, byte sizes, SHA-256 fingerprints, required columns, stored
000831  row counts, and recorded support summaries. It also filters the stored pooled
000832  profiles for the displayed figure and calculates compact descriptive totals
000833  from the stored support tables. Rendering does **not** relearn a profile,
000834  rebuild a relevance map, reconstruct scientific values from one-minute data,
000835  write an analytical artifact, or run any Preparation 03 verifier.
000836  :::
000853  ## What the production step did
000855  The production workflow separated time alignment, profile learning, and
000856  artifact verification so that each scientific operation had a narrow,
000857  checkable responsibility. @tbl-learning-decisions states the rules in the
000858  order in which they affected the data.
000878  The complete full day is the intended hybrid 24-hour measurement cycle,
000879  including bedside measurements during diary-defined sleep. Wake, sleep, and
000880  the three hours before sleep are separate profile domains; they do not redefine
000881  which days passed the general coverage rule.
000900  ## What the pooled profiles look like
000902  @fig-pooled-reference-profiles shows the pooled full-day profiles that provide
000903  the clearest overview of the learned time-of-day pattern. Each point is one
000904  stored 30-minute reference value. The figure is descriptive: its lines connect
000905  stored values and do not interpolate missing support.
000972  [Download the source data for @fig-pooled-reference-profiles](../../artifacts/04_reference_profiles/reference_profiles.csv).
000974  ## Exact inputs and clock handling
000976  The near-eye and chest input files retained their actual-time rows and were
000977  identified by SHA-256 fingerprints before profile learning. @tbl-profile-inputs
000978  shows the participant and minute support for each signal. The slight
000979  signal-specific differences in usable minutes reflect the stored missing
000980  values; one signal was not used to erase an observed value in the other.
001003  Autumn daylight-saving changes can assign the same local clock label to two
001004  different actual observations. Preparation 03 averaged those repeated labels
001005  only in the separate local-clock copy used for learning. It kept both
001006  observations in the fixed actual-time input. For diary-period profiles, the
001007  period was selected in actual time before repeated local labels were averaged,
001008  so a repeated clock label could not move an observation across a sleep or wake
001009  boundary.
001029  ## Support for the intensity profiles
001031  Each profile retains all 48 half-hour periods. A period without the required
001032  number of participants remains in the stored table with no usable reference
001033  value; it is never interpolated. Consequently, a profile can be estimable
001034  overall while containing explicitly unsupported periods. @tbl-profile-support
001035  summarizes all 288 stored profile groups.
001056  Full-day and pooled wake profiles had support in every stored half-hour
001057  period. Support was sparser within sleep and the three hours before sleep,
001058  especially in individual-site profiles. Five site-specific sleep profiles
001059  were not estimable; the stored diagnostics preserve that limitation rather
001060  than supplying a replacement.
001062  ## Probability of exceeding 250 lx by time of day
001064  The timing of strict melEDI values above 250 lx cannot be inferred from whether
001065  the population median profile itself exceeds 250 lx. Individual
001066  participant-days may cross the threshold even when the population median
001067  remains lower. Preparation 03 therefore learned a separate probability for
001068  each half-hour period:
001070  1. within every participant-day, it calculated the fraction of valid minutes
001071     with melEDI strictly greater than 250 lx;
001072  2. it gave days equal weight within each participant; and
001073  3. it then gave participants equal weight within the pooled, site-specific,
001074     or leave-one-site-out distribution.
001076  @tbl-threshold-timing-support shows that all 36 stored distributions were
001077  estimable and had positive probability in at least some periods. Preparation
001078  04 expands each half-hour probability across its 30 constituent minutes only
001079  to measure temporal support. It does not weight or change an observed timing.
001100  ## How Preparation 04 may use each map
001102  The intensity and threshold distributions were converted into
001103  metric-specific relevance maps. These maps state which times of day matter
001104  for checking the observation support of a metric. They do not all have
001105  permission to change a value. @tbl-map-use makes that distinction explicit.
001127  The M10 and L10 maps check whether enough relevant times were observed for the
001128  brightest and darkest 10-hour windows. The timing map checks support for the
001129  first, last, and mean timing above 250 lx. None of these active support-only
001130  maps scales a level, duration, or timing, and no L5 profile or map was
001131  produced.
001133  Under [METRIC-010](../../audit/decisions/mder_mean_of_viable_ratios.md), MDER
001134  is the arithmetic mean of viable one-minute melEDI-to-illuminance ratios. No
001135  reference profile weights, scales, or gates that calculation. The stored
001136  paired-channel maps describe the superseded ratio-of-integrals method and are
001137  retained only as historical provenance; they are not active Preparation 04
001138  inputs for MDER.
001140  ## What is verified for the current artifacts
001142  The current manifest and every file it lists passed the bounded identity
001143  checks in @tbl-current-validation. Stored dimensions and support fields are
001144  also internally consistent with the current settings. These checks establish
001145  which artifacts this report read; they do not independently reconstruct the
001146  scientific values.
001168  ::: {.callout-note appearance="simple" title="Version-specific independent verification"}
001169  The complete independent reconstruction that passed **394 of 394 checks**
001170  applied to the **preceding** manifest with SHA-256
001171  `c8e02302521360d3a5cb18f49e0a97aed4a1f0ea64343ead68bde274cddce10d`.
001172  It reconstructed 288 median-profile groups and 36 strict-threshold
001173  distributions and matched that manifest's 11 file fingerprints. It must not
001174  be treated as verification of the current manifest
001175  `5e08099602682a4b951b0304764f341932ef2f03ccd4c62f32793ea82084e062`.
001177  [PREP-002](../../audit/decisions/preparation03_version_specific_verification.md)
001178  therefore records independent reconstruction of the current manifest as open
001179  audit item **FIND-043**. This provenance gap is not evidence that a current
001180  profile, metric, or downstream result is incorrect. This report does not run
001181  the verifier.
001182  :::
001184  ## Producing scripts in execution order
001186  @tbl-script-execution-map identifies the code that originally produced and
001187  checked these artifacts. The two-column layout keeps the scientific operation
001188  and its reason together instead of forcing explanatory prose into a narrow
001189  table column.
001209  ## Artifacts passed to metric derivation
001211  Preparation 04 reads the stored RDS forms of the fixed profiles and relevance
001212  maps. The CSV forms provide the paired reader- and machine-readable source
001213  data. @tbl-artifact-handoff lists the exact durable paths.
001250  ## Handoff to Preparation 04
001252  Preparation 03 passes forward fixed, versioned temporal reference information:
001253  13,824 intensity-profile rows, 1,728 strict-threshold distribution rows, and
001254  36,288 metric-relevance rows, together with their support diagnostics,
001255  settings, input fingerprints, and artifact manifest. Preparation 04 may read
001256  and apply those stored objects under their recorded permissions. It may not
001257  relearn, interpolate, or silently substitute a profile during rendering.

===== notebooks/preparation/04_metric_derivation.qmd =====
001549  ## Purpose and position in the preparation chain
001551  Preparation 04 converts the eligible one-minute light records from
001552  Preparation 02 into the daily, diary-period, 30-minute, hourly, and
001553  participant-level metrics used by later analyses. It also records whether
001554  each individual metric had enough relevant data to be interpreted. A failed
001555  support check makes only that metric unavailable; it does not discard the
001556  participant-day from unrelated analyses. For offset geometric means, it also
001557  records when floating-point roundoff is normalized to a source-verified exact
001558  zero.
001560  The exact inputs are the Preparation 02 Rule A coverage files, the
001561  Preparation 01 sleep and wear intervals, and the fixed time-of-day profiles
001562  and relevance maps learned in Preparation 03. The main outputs are eight R
001563  data files containing participant-day, participant, 30-minute, and hourly
001564  values for the near-eye and chest placements. Preparation 06 combines those
001565  files with site and participant context; this report does not inspect any
001566  hypothesis result.
001581  ::: {.callout-note title="What is calculated during this render?"}
001582  This report reads already stored CSV outputs. During rendering it checks file
001583  presence, byte size, SHA-256 identity, required columns, and fixed row-count
001584  relationships. It also formats lightweight summaries already stored by the
001585  primary METRIC-010 audit and the gap-timing-unaware repair audit. It does
001586  the same for the sealed METRIC-011 numerical-zero evidence. It does **not**
001587  run a preparation builder, repair or change a metric, independently
001588  reconstruct MDER, L10, or another support classification, fit a model, or
001589  overwrite an accepted analytical artifact.
001590  :::
001592  ::: {.callout-important title="Interpret the full-day metrics as a hybrid measurement"}
001593  During wake and the three hours before sleep, the record represents the
001594  logger's worn placement: near-eye or chest. During diary-defined sleep, it
001595  represents the bedside sleep environment. A metric spanning the full day is
001596  therefore an environmental-light summary of this planned hybrid record, not a
001597  continuous 24-hour measure of ocular exposure.
001598  :::
001600  ## Exact inputs
001620  Preparation 02 had already retained only participant-days with valid melEDI
001621  for at least 80% of the fixed 1,440-minute hybrid day. The 50% requirement
001622  within an hour governs whether an hourly value can be calculated; it does not
001623  remove otherwise valid one-minute measurements from daily or minute-based
001624  metrics. All-finite, all-zero melEDI days had also been removed from the
001625  primary coverage files while remaining available as a predefined sensitivity.
001627  ## Production sequence and stored hand-offs
001629  The producing scripts are shown in execution order. They remain separate so
001630  that support decisions, state reconstruction, metric calculation, and file
001631  storage each have an inspectable input and output boundary.
001651  ## Scientific rules applied during production
001664  ### Source-verified zeros after geometric means
001666  Geometric means can include true zero measurements because production first
001667  adds 0.1 lx, averages on the log10 scale, transforms back, and subtracts the
001668  same 0.1 lx. In exact arithmetic, an all-zero source window returns zero.
001669  Floating-point arithmetic can instead leave a minute positive remainder: the
001670  eight affected L10 means were
001671  `4.163336342344337e-17` lx, far below the source-verified numerical tolerance
001672  of `2.220446049250313e-14` lx.
001674  Under [METRIC-011](../../audit/decisions/l10_numerical_zero_normalization.md),
001675  a positive remainder within that tolerance is stored as exact zero only when
001676  every finite source minute is itself exactly zero. Missing minutes remain
001677  missing; they are counted separately and never imputed as zeros. A materially
001678  negative result stops production, and a small positive result with any
001679  positive source minute is retained.
001705  Exactly eight primary L10 mean cells changed: three near-eye and five chest.
001706  Seven selected 600-minute windows contained 600 observed zero minutes. The
001707  remaining chest window contained 547 observed zero minutes and 53 missing
001708  minutes; all 547 observed values were zero, and the 53 missing minutes stayed
001709  missing. L10 remains available for all 816 near-eye and 902 chest
001710  participant-days, so neither the sample nor its support rule changed.
001711  Every non-L10 value and all samples are unchanged. No hypothesis model is run
001712  or rebuilt by this report.
001714  The sealed audit contains 12 numerical-zero normalizations in total: four
001715  previously documented negative roundoff residuals and the eight positive
001716  residuals above. A zero-capable analysis retains these participant-days as
001717  exact zeros; a later two-part model retains them in its zero/occurrence part
001718  and excludes them only from a strictly positive magnitude part by definition.
001720  The central distinction is between participant-day eligibility and
001721  metric-specific availability. A day that passed the general coverage rule
001722  remains in the prepared data even when, for example, its pre-sleep period is
001723  unknown, its M10 window has insufficient support, or its paired illuminance
001724  data cannot support MDER.
001726  ## Cohort and time-grid outputs
001757  Near-eye metrics contain 141 participants from nine sites and 816
001758  participant-days. Chest metrics contain 154 participants from eight sites and
001759  902 participant-days. These are the current stored Preparation 04 samples,
001760  not downstream model-specific complete-case samples.
001762  ### Diary-period support
001764  Wake, the three hours before sleep, and sleep each use a metric-specific 80%
001765  support rule. The 70% and 90% columns show the fixed sensitivity cutoffs; they
001766  do not change general participant-day eligibility.
001797  At the primary cutoff, the near-eye output retains 737 wake, 655 pre-sleep,
001798  and 778 sleep-environment values. The chest output retains 818, 743, and 861,
001799  respectively. An unavailable value carries one of three distinct explanations:
001800  the diary could not bound the day's state domains, the target period did not
001801  occur, or the target period contained too little valid melEDI.
001803  ### MDER from viable one-minute channel pairs
001805  The melanopic daylight efficacy ratio (MDER) describes melEDI relative to
001806  photopic illuminance. For each participant-day, production first represents
001807  the complete 1,440-position local wall-clock grid. If the autumn clock change
001808  creates two observations for one local minute, melEDI and illuminance are each
001809  averaged within that clock minute before their ratio is formed. A local minute
001810  that does not exist during the spring clock change remains missing.
001812  A minute contributes only when both channels are finite and strictly greater
001813  than zero. This restriction defines a viable proportional comparison: a zero
001814  illuminance denominator is undefined, and a zero in either channel does not
001815  represent a meaningful positive channel ratio for this estimand. MDER is the
001816  arithmetic mean of the viable one-minute melEDI/illuminance ratios. At least
001817  720 of the 1,440 positions must be viable; the boundary is inclusive, so
001818  exactly 720 passes. If fewer are viable, only MDER is unavailable. The
001819  participant-day and all other metrics remain eligible.
001821  No time profile weights, gates, or scales enter current MDER, and it is not a
001822  ratio of daily channel integrals. Records describing a profile-supported
001823  ratio-of-integrals method and 70%, 80%, or 90% MDER gates are historical
001824  METRIC-003 provenance, not the active method.
001826  The **gap-timing-unaware dataset** still passed the general 50%-per-hour and
001827  80%-per-day coverage rules. Its name means that the timing of remaining
001828  missing observations was not used for an additional metric-specific
001829  adjustment. For this first explanation only, the primary dataset could be
001830  interpreted as a **time-sensitive primary metric dataset**; hereafter it is
001831  called simply the **primary dataset**. Both datasets now use the same
001832  momentary-ratio MDER definition.
001872  In the primary dataset, MDER is available for 702 of 816 near-eye
001873  participant-days from 137 participants (mean 0.7239; median 0.7239) and 732 of
001874  902 chest participant-days from 152 participants (mean 0.7565; median
001875  0.7494). In the repaired gap-timing-unaware dataset, it is available for 687
001876  of 811 near-eye participant-days from 137 participants (mean 0.7243; median
001877  0.7239) and 723 of 897 chest participant-days from 152 participants (mean
001878  0.7567; median 0.7495). The earlier 725 near-eye and 729 chest availability
001879  wording described the incomplete first repin and is superseded by these
001880  independently verified counts.
001882  The gap repair changed only MDER. Its stored comparison found all 25,620
001883  non-MDER participant-day cells exactly unchanged. A failed MDER support rule
001884  therefore still removes only MDER, not the day or any other metric. This
001885  report presents the stored repair evidence; it does not repeat the repair.
001938  [Download the source data for @fig-mder-availability](../../artifacts/08_diagnostics/mder_METRIC-010/verification_summary.csv).
001940  ### Thirty-minute and hourly values
001971  The regular grids preserve unsupported periods as explicit missing values.
001972  This matters because deleting rows would compress time and could create false
001973  adjacency, change a window's apparent duration, or hide where coverage was
001974  lost.
001976  ## Which metric values are available?
001999  A known absence and an unknown value remain different. For example, an
002000  adequately observed day with no exposure above a threshold has a duration of
002001  zero, but first, last, and mean timing do not exist. For the longest period
002002  above 250 lx, the observed duration remains a lower bound when a missing
002003  interval could conceal a longer period; the separate exact-only sensitivity
002004  is available only when the lower and possible upper bounds agree.
002006  ## Gaps and daylight-saving changes
002061  Actual-time metrics retain both occurrences of a repeated autumn hour and
002062  respect the true elapsed gap during the spring transition. Clock-aligned
002063  metrics use a fixed 24-hour local-clock grid; repeated autumn values are
002064  averaged only on that grid, and structurally absent spring minutes remain
002065  absent. Missing light, unknown diary state, and a daylight-saving transition
002066  are therefore not treated as the same event.
002068  ## Exact artifacts passed forward

002090  construct the primary dataset in Preparation 06. The repaired
002091  gap-timing-unaware participant-day and MDER-support files use the same
002092  momentary-ratio MDER estimand and are passed forward separately. Historical
002093  internal filenames remain for provenance but are not visible dataset labels.
002094  The two audit bundles document the exact primary verification and the repaired
002095  gap-timing-unaware sample flow, support reasons, and non-MDER invariance. The
002096  METRIC-011 bundle separately records the source minutes, raw back-transforms,
002097  eight changed L10 cells, manifest transition, and exact non-L10 invariance.
002099  Preparation 04 creates the primary dataset's metric layer. Preparation 05
002100  acquires questionnaire and diary inputs, and Preparation 06 brings the metric
002101  and non-light inputs together. The gap-timing-unaware dataset remains a
002102  predefined prepared-data sensitivity and is not substituted into the primary
002103  files listed above.

===== notebooks/preparation/05_model_input_acquisition.qmd =====
000687  ## Purpose and position in the preparation chain
000689  Preparation 05 obtains the fixed questionnaire and diary files needed for
000690  participant characteristics, chronotype, habitual light-exposure behaviour,
000691  visual light sensitivity, exercise, self-reported lighting context, and
000692  sleep. Each site's data release is identified by its repository, complete
000693  commit identifier, and DOI. The result is a local, checksummed copy of every
000694  expected file, plus stored descriptions of each object's rows and columns.
000696  This step does not clean variables, calculate scores, harmonize factor levels,
000697  interpret timestamps, join participants to light metrics, or filter a model
000698  sample. Preparation 06 performs those operations from the fixed files recorded
000699  here. The light-exposure metrics from Preparation 04 form a separate input
000700  branch into Preparation 06.
000713  ::: {.callout-note title="What is calculated during this render?"}
000714  Rendering reads the stored registries, acquisition manifest, object audit, and
000715  column audit. It checks their schemas, hashes the 63 already local files,
000716  compares the nine sleep diaries with the Preparation 01 inventory, and
000717  calculates compact row and file totals. It does **not** connect to the network,
000718  open raw questionnaire values, run the acquisition builder or verifier,
000719  normalize a variable, construct a model input, or overwrite any stored
000720  artifact.
000721  :::
000723  ## Exact inputs that fix the acquisition
000742  The availability registry contains all 63 combinations of nine sites and
000743  seven modalities. Its explicit grid matters because a missing download cannot
000744  be mistaken for an intentionally unavailable questionnaire. The registry can
000745  carry a reason for an expected absence, although no current entry is absent.
000747  ## Fixed source releases by site
000767  All download addresses embed the recorded commit. None contains `/main/` or
000768  another moving branch label. Consequently, revisiting the same address refers
000769  to the same repository state rather than whatever happens to be current at a
000770  later date.
000772  ### Corrected TUM exercise source
000774  One file deliberately uses a modality-specific commit instead of the default
000775  TUM release. The upstream exercise diary corrects the participant identity at
000776  the source; Preparation 05 does not rewrite that identifier locally.
000796  ## What the acquired objects contain
000798  The acquisition audit records structure rather than displaying participant
000799  responses. Row counts below describe the unchanged objects across all nine
000800  sites. Preparation 06 later decides how columns are interpreted and which
000801  ones may enter an analysis.
000829  The 63 objects contain 963 recorded columns in total. That count is a schema
000830  audit, not a claim that all columns are suitable for analysis. In particular,
000831  raw free-text fields remain in the untouched source objects but are not shown
000832  here; Preparation 06 explicitly excludes them from analytical outputs.
000834  ## How files were obtained in the recorded run
000849  The recorded run reused 62 files and downloaded one. The reused files comprise
000850  nine sleep diaries from Preparation 01 and 53 files from the verified
000851  model-input cache. Reuse is not based only on a filename: the release identity,
000852  local path, byte size, and SHA-256 must all agree. A SHA-256 is a digital
000853  fingerprint; changing even one byte changes the fingerprint with overwhelming
000854  probability.
000856  ## Production sequence
000876  Separating the expected-file specification from cache or download decisions
000877  prevents local availability from defining the scientific input. Separating
000878  object/column inspection from later normalization likewise ensures that a
000879  changed upstream schema is visible before values are interpreted or joined.
000881  ## Exact stored artifacts passed to Preparation 06
000902  The local RData objects remain separated by site and modality. Preparation 05
000903  does not combine sites, rename values, recalculate questionnaire scores,
000904  select categories, or discard incomplete intervals. The manifest and audits
000905  allow Preparation 06 to perform those operations against known, immutable
000906  inputs.
000908  ## Checks tied to the current stored files
000933  All 63 local files match the acquisition manifest with SHA-256
000934  `ac79a83c2d60dde1ac563f6e2cb09c58d4d7d3b06549d9b9262e4acd5a50a58b`.
000935  The nine sleep diaries also match the Preparation 01 inventory independently
000936  on repository, commit, DOI, address, local path, fingerprint, and bytes.
000938  ### Stored independent verification
000958  The exact-current verification record is documented in the
000959  [Preparation 05 input-provenance finding](../../audit/findings/preparation05_input_provenance_gaps.md)
000960  and the
000961  [Preparation 06 normalization gate](../../audit/reconciliation/preparation06/normalization_gate.md).
000962  The verifier is not rerun during this reader-facing render.
000963  It establishes that the fixed source objects reached normalization without an
000964  unrecorded release or schema change. It does not establish a hypothesis
000965  result, model sample, effect estimate, or scientific claim.

===== notebooks/preparation/06_model_ready_datasets.qmd =====
001548  ## Purpose and position in the analysis chain
001550  Preparation 06 turns the checked light-exposure metrics from Preparation 04
001551  and the fixed participant, questionnaire, and diary files acquired in
001552  Preparation 05 into inputs that hypothesis analyses can use. It first makes
001553  participant files structurally consistent, then adds participant and site
001554  context to the metric files, preserves the distinction between local clock
001555  time and actual elapsed time, and finally stores a separate input when a
001556  hypothesis method has been approved. H01 is the first such build. This step
001557  does not fit a statistical model.
001559  The predefined **gap-timing-unaware dataset** still passed the general
001560  50%-per-hour and 80%-per-day coverage rules. Its name means that the timing of
001561  remaining missing observations was not used for an additional metric-specific
001562  adjustment. For contrast, the primary dataset could be interpreted as a
001563  “time-sensitive primary metric dataset.” From this point onward, this report
001564  calls it simply the **primary dataset**.
001566  The exact inputs used by the Preparation 06 producing scripts are recorded in
001567  the eight manifests checked on this page. They cover standardized participant
001568  inputs, the separate prepared-data comparison, site and daylight context,
001569  elapsed-time provenance, shared model inputs, both H01 preparations, and the
001570  stored descriptive comparison. The current metric manifest, independent
001571  primary MDER audit, repaired gap-timing-unaware MDER evidence, and downstream
001572  repin evidence are checked separately. The current METRIC-011 evidence bundle
001573  then identifies the exact L10 changes and the refreshed preparation identities
001574  entering the shared inputs. The corresponding paths are reported in
001575  @tbl-current-mder-provenance, @tbl-l10-preparation-provenance,
001576  @tbl-manifest-identity, and
001577  @tbl-script-execution-map.
001579  ::: {.callout-note appearance="simple" title="What is calculated during this render?"}
001580  This report reads already stored RDS and CSV outputs. During rendering it
001581  checks that every manifest-listed file has the recorded size and SHA-256
001582  digital fingerprint, verifies the current metric, the MDER evidence bundles,
001583  and the METRIC-011 evidence bundle, checks the columns needed for the displays, calculates only lightweight
001584  descriptive counts from stored rows, and redraws one
001585  reader-facing plot from its stored source-data CSV. It does not source or run
001586  any producing script, retrieve data, standardize inputs, derive metrics,
001587  construct or overwrite a model frame, fit a model, estimate autocorrelation,
001588  or write an analytical artifact.
001589  :::
001629  ## 1. Standardize participant, questionnaire, and diary structures
001631  The first producing script,
001632  `scripts/pipeline/build_model_input_normalization.R`, reads only the fixed
001633  local files acquired in Preparation 05. Its function module,
001634  `scripts/pipeline/model_input_normalization.R`, places site identifiers,
001635  participant identifiers, labels, column types, and time representations into
001636  a consistent structure. Elapsed time is represented in Coordinated Universal
001637  Time (UTC); time-of-day fields retain the site's local clock time. This
001638  structural work is kept separate from later joins so that recorded values,
001639  missing values, and excluded free text can be checked before metrics and
001640  participant information meet.
001642  The procedure did not recalculate questionnaire totals or factor scores. The
001643  963 field-level value-preservation records all passed, and all 63 audited
001644  free-text fields were excluded from analytical RDS files without exporting
001645  their content.
001667  Records without a complete interval remain in their prepared input file, but
001668  they cannot contribute to a calculation that requires both interval
001669  endpoints. The stored audit identifies 27 such light-exposure-diary records
001670  (one at RISE and 26 at THUAS) and one MPI sleep-diary record without a wake
001671  time.
001693  ## 2. Keep the prepared-data comparison separate
001695  The next script, `scripts/pipeline/build_manuscript_prepared_data.R`, and its
001696  function module, `scripts/pipeline/manuscript_prepared_data.R`, assemble the
001697  gap-timing-unaware dataset independently of the primary dataset. That
001698  separation prevents a preparation difference from being erased when both are
001699  later passed through the same hypothesis implementation.
001701  The stored data cover participant-level, participant-day, 30-minute, and
001702  one-hour values. Exact metric-support hours cannot be recovered from these
001703  retained prepared artifacts; later tables therefore show those hours as
001704  unavailable rather than zero or a reconstructed estimate.
001727  One objectively verified participant-identifier error is documented rather
001728  than silently changed: record DEV-056 assigns seven TUM exercise-diary rows
001729  to `TUM_S001` instead of the recorded `TUM_S101`. The correction did not
001730  change a scientific light value.
001746  ## 3. Add site and daylight context
001748  `scripts/pipeline/build_site_solar_context.R` reads the approved site
001749  coordinates and time zones and the local dates required by both prepared
001750  datasets. Its separate function module,
001751  `scripts/pipeline/site_solar_context.R`, stores civil dawn, civil dusk,
001752  photoperiod, and descriptive solar-noon information for 618 site-date
001753  combinations across nine sites, including four site-dates with a
001754  daylight-saving transition.
001756  The repaired gap-timing-unaware input repinned this manifest because it
001757  supplies supplemental site dates. The stored downstream check found all 618
001758  rows and all 47 context fields exactly unchanged; only their provenance
001759  identities changed.
001761  Every merge uses site and local date as a many-to-one key: many measurement
001762  rows may use one site-date context record. All six joins retained their input
001763  row counts, and none lacked a matching site-date record.
001798  ## 4. Preserve time of day and actual elapsed time
001800  Local clock time and elapsed time answer different questions. A 30-minute row
001801  labelled 08:00 represents 08:00 at every study site, which is useful for
001802  time-of-day comparisons. UTC records how much real time separated two rows,
001803  which is necessary for continuity and autocorrelation. The script
001804  `scripts/pipeline/build_temporal_sequence_provenance.R` and function module
001805  `scripts/pipeline/temporal_sequence_provenance.R` preserve both coordinates
001806  without changing melEDI values.
001808  When clocks move back, a local-clock interval may represent two real
001809  intervals; its stored local-clock outcome is their average. When clocks move
001810  forward, a local-clock interval can have no corresponding real interval.
001811  Only an unambiguous, usable real interval can continue an elapsed-time
001812  sequence. A real gap, unusable outcome, repeated autumn interval, or
001813  structural spring gap starts or interrupts a sequence.
001833  ## 5. Combine checked data without choosing a hypothesis sample
001835  `scripts/pipeline/build_base_model_data.R` reads the checked metric files,
001836  standardized participant inputs, and site context. Its function module,
001837  `scripts/pipeline/base_model_data.R`, adds one matching participant record and
001838  one matching site/date record to each measurement row. It does not apply a
001839  complete-case filter or a hypothesis-specific exclusion. Instead, it records
001840  which questionnaire and diary inputs are available, allowing each hypothesis
001841  to state its own requirements later.
001843  The shared primary near-eye input contains
001844  `r dplyr::n_distinct(near_eye_grid$Id)` participants,
001845  `r dplyr::n_distinct(paste(near_eye_grid$Id, near_eye_grid$local_date))`
001846  participant-days, and `r format(nrow(near_eye_grid), big.mark = ",")`
001847  30-minute grid rows. The complementary chest input contains
001848  `r dplyr::n_distinct(chest_grid$Id)` participants,
001849  `r dplyr::n_distinct(paste(chest_grid$Id, chest_grid$local_date))`
001850  participant-days, and `r format(nrow(chest_grid), big.mark = ",")` rows. Chest
001851  measurements remain complementary; they are not pooled with the primary
001852  near-eye measurements.
001897  All participants with near-eye or chest metrics have matching participant
001898  characteristics, chronotype, LEBA, VLSQ-8, and sleep-diary inputs. Exercise
001899  diary data are absent for three participants at each placement, and
001900  light-exposure diary data are absent for one participant at each placement.
001901  These are availability flags, not exclusions at this stage.
001931  ### Current MDER in the shared inputs
001933  The shared-model-input manifest pins the current metric manifest and the
001934  input-bundle identity shown below. In these files, daily MDER is the arithmetic
001935  mean of viable one-minute melEDI/illuminance ratios on a complete
001936  1,440-position local wall-clock grid. Both channels must be finite and
001937  strictly greater than zero; a zero-denominator ratio is undefined, and a zero
001938  in either channel does not define the positive proportional comparison used by
001939  this estimand. When the autumn clock change produces duplicate local minutes,
001940  the two channels are averaged separately within that clock minute before the
001941  ratio is formed. An absent spring-forward minute remains missing.
001943  At least 720 viable ratios are required, and exactly 720 passes the inclusive
001944  50% rule. Failing that rule makes MDER alone unavailable: the participant-day,
001945  its other metrics, and its attached participant and site context remain in the
001946  shared inputs. Current MDER uses no time-profile weight, gate, or scale and is
001947  not a ratio of daily integrals. The older ratio-of-integrals and profile-gate
001948  records remain available only as historical METRIC-003 provenance.
001986  In the primary dataset, MDER is available for 702 of 816 near-eye
001987  participant-days from 137 participants (mean 0.7239; median 0.7239) and 732 of
001988  902 chest participant-days from 152 participants (mean 0.7565; median
001989  0.7494). In the repaired gap-timing-unaware dataset, the same momentary-ratio
001990  estimand is available for 687 of 811 near-eye participant-days from 137
001991  participants (mean 0.7243; median 0.7239) and 723 of 897 chest participant-days
001992  from 152 participants (mean 0.7567; median 0.7495). The earlier 725 near-eye
001993  and 729 chest availability wording described the incomplete first repin and is
001994  superseded by these independently verified counts.
001996  The repair changed only MDER: all 25,620 non-MDER participant-day cells were
001997  exactly unchanged. The downstream rebuild also retained all 618 × 47
001998  site/daylight context values exactly. Thus the repair changed MDER and the
001999  identities needed to trace it, but no non-MDER or site-context scientific
002000  value.
002029  ### Source-verified L10 zeros in the shared inputs
002031  L10 is the mean melEDI during the darkest 10-hour window of a participant-day.
002032  Its geometric mean can represent true zeros because production adds 0.1 lx,
002033  averages `log10(melEDI + 0.1)`, transforms back, and subtracts 0.1 lx. In exact
002034  arithmetic, an all-zero source window therefore returns zero. Floating-point
002035  arithmetic left eight primary L10 means at
002036  `4.163336342344337e-17` lx instead. This remainder is far below the
002037  source-verified tolerance of `2.220446049250313e-14` lx.
002039  Under [METRIC-011](../../audit/decisions/l10_numerical_zero_normalization.md),
002040  a positive remainder within that tolerance is stored as exact zero only when
002041  every finite source minute is exactly zero. Missing minutes remain missing;
002042  they are counted separately and are never imputed as zeros. A small positive
002043  remainder is retained if any finite source minute is positive, and a materially
002044  negative result stops production.
002078  Exactly eight primary L10 mean cells changed: three near-eye and five chest.
002079  Seven 600-minute windows contained 600 observed zero minutes. The remaining
002080  chest window contained 547 observed zero minutes and 53 missing minutes; all
002081  547 observed values were zero, while the 53 missing minutes stayed missing.
002082  All 816 near-eye and 902 chest participant-days remain in the shared inputs.
002084  Every non-L10 scientific value, every sample definition, all 30-minute and
002085  hourly values, and all gap-timing-unaware scientific values are unchanged.
002086  The shared primary inputs inherit the eight exact-zero L10 values; H01's
002087  primary prepared rows inherit those values without a sample or contract
002088  change. The gap-timing-unaware H01 files were repinned for provenance but did
002089  not change scientifically. No hypothesis model was fitted or refitted.
002091  For an analysis that permits zero outcomes, these participant-days remain as
002092  zeros. In a two-part analysis, they remain in the zero/occurrence component
002093  and are excluded only from the strictly positive magnitude component by that
002094  component's definition.
002125  ## 6. Store the first hypothesis-specific inputs
002127  Once the H01 method was approved,
002128  `scripts/pipeline/build_h01_model_data.R` used the shared inputs and the H01
002129  metric contract to store outcomes, site and daylight predictors, participant
002130  identifiers, metric-specific inclusion records, and sample flow. Its function
002131  module is `scripts/pipeline/h01_model_data.R`. The corresponding adapter,
002132  `scripts/pipeline/build_h01_manuscript_prepared_data.R`, applies the same H01
002133  implementation contract to the gap-timing-unaware dataset through
002134  `scripts/pipeline/h01_manuscript_prepared_adapter.R`. Neither operation fits a
002135  model.
002137  H01 covers 17 metric contracts. The following tables report the all-available
002138  near-eye prepared frames. “Rows in prepared frame” is the potential input at
002139  this boundary, not a claim about the final fitted-model sample. Any later
002140  transformation or model-specific missing-value removal is rechecked and
002141  stored by the H01 analysis. Valid measurement hours describe the support from
002142  which a metric was derived; they are not additional model observations.
002172  For the brightest and darkest 10-hour means and their midpoints, the primary
002173  metric files do not export the exact contributing minutes; the corresponding
002174  hour cells remain unavailable. The gap-timing-unaware artifacts do not retain
002175  exact metric-support hours for any H01 metric, so the full column is shown as
002176  unavailable.
002207  The H01 files also prepare paired participant-day comparisons between
002208  near-eye and chest measurements where possible. Interdaily stability and
002209  intradaily variability each produce one participant-level value from a series
002210  of days. Their paired versions would require recomputation from the same
002211  minute-level day set at both placements. They are therefore explicitly marked
002212  unavailable for both placements in both datasets—eight scenario records in
002213  total—instead of being approximated from unequal day sets.
002215  ## 7. Describe composition before hypothesis fitting
002217  `scripts/pipeline/build_preanalysis_comparison.R` reads both stored
002218  preparations through `scripts/pipeline/preanalysis_comparison.R`. It records
002219  counts, distributions, key reconciliation, and paired descriptive
002220  comparisons. These outputs help readers see preparation differences before a
002221  hypothesis is tested; they are not hypothesis tests themselves.
002223  @fig-site-composition shows the near-eye participant composition used by this
002224  stored comparison. It is drawn during render from the paired source-data CSV,
002225  using the submitted-manuscript site names, order, and colours. The two panels
002226  use the same horizontal scale.
002273  [Download the source data for @fig-site-composition](../../artifacts/08_diagnostics/preanalysis_comparison/categorical_levels.csv).
002275  The figure uses a 7.5-inch by 6.8-inch canvas and 9–10 pt axis, tick, facet,
002276  and count labels before HTML scaling. Final-display typography, clipping,
002277  label wrapping, panel balance, bar distinguishability, and colour use are
002278  checked after the HTML render under REPORT-011.
002280  ## 8. Verify provenance and hand off stored artifacts
002282  The page checked every file listed in the eight preparation manifests, the two
002283  MDER repair/rebuild evidence manifests, and the METRIC-011 evidence manifest
002284  before displaying any result. A PASS means that the file existed and both its
002285  byte size and SHA-256 digital fingerprint matched the manifest; it does not
002286  mean that the render reran the producing calculation.
002321  The producing scripts are listed below in execution order. Their separation
002322  is scientific provenance: each step has a narrower responsibility and a
002323  stored output that can be checked before the next step begins. Rendering this
002324  report reads those outputs only.
002382  The following files and directories are the durable handoff from Preparation
002383  06. They—not values recalculated by this report—enter the hypothesis-specific
002384  analysis-preparation companions and model-fitting scripts.

===== notebooks/preparation/07_example_days.qmd =====
000492  ## Purpose and position in the preparation chain
000494  This final preparation page is a **display-only inspection** of the prepared
000495  one-minute data. It shows one participant-day from each study site before any
000496  hypothesis model is fitted. The profiles combine melanopic equivalent daylight
000497  illuminance (melEDI; lx), local daylight, diary-defined periods, and declared
000498  non-wear so that readers can see what the accepted minute-level records look
000499  like.
000501  The exact scientific inputs are the prepared near-eye minute data from
000502  Preparation 02, the site- and date-specific daylight context assembled in
000503  Preparation 06, and the site metadata used to interpret local time. A fixed
000504  selection and its display files were produced separately by
000505  `scripts/pipeline/build_prepared_day_showcase.R`. **None of the Preparation 07
000506  selection, source-data, or figure files enters an H01–H11 model.** The
000507  hypothesis analyses receive their stored inputs from Preparation 06. There is
000508  no hypothesis-analysis handoff from this page.
000510  ::: {.callout-note title="What is calculated during this render?"}
000511  This report reads the stored selection, settings, one-minute figure source
000512  data, display manifest, and site display registry. It calculates lightweight
000513  counts for the tables, compares current file sizes and SHA-256 fingerprints
000514  with the manifest, hashes—but does not open—the prepared coverage and daylight
000515  RDS inputs, and redraws the profiles from the stored source-data CSV for a
000516  more readable page layout. It does not rerun the fixed-seed selection, reload
000517  the prepared coverage data, rebuild any accepted file, recalculate a metric,
000518  fit a model, or call the independent production verifier.
000519  :::
000534  ## Exact inputs
000536  The display manifest has SHA-256
000537  `r current_manifest_sha256`. It records the three exact preparation inputs
000538  used when the example days were selected and plotted. The render confirmed
000539  that the current files have those same fingerprints without loading the two
000540  RDS data objects.
000571  ## How the example days were produced
000573  The display has its own narrow production workflow so that an illustrative
000574  choice cannot silently alter the prepared data. The producer reads accepted
000575  inputs and writes only display artifacts. The independent verifier is kept in
000576  a separate module and does not source the producer, allowing it to reconstruct
000577  the selection and compare the stored outputs by a different route.
000622  ### Eligibility and selection rules
000624  A participant-day could enter the selection pool only if it passed the general
000625  coverage rules from Preparation 02: at least 50% valid observations within
000626  each summarized hour and at least 80% valid observations across the full
000627  24-hour day. It also needed all 1,440 local-clock minutes and one complete
000628  diary sleep interval with a visible sleep start and wake transition. One day
000629  per site was then chosen with fixed seed 20260730; the selection was not
000630  repeated to find especially tidy or favourable profiles.
000677  The 80% daily rule covers the complete 24-hour measurement cycle. It combines
000678  near-eye measurements while participants were awake with bedside measurements
000679  during the diary-defined sleep interval. Retaining both contexts preserves the
000680  full day; it does not imply that the bedside sleep measurement was made at the
000681  eye.
000683  ## Selected participant-days
000685  The two compact tables separate timing from data availability so that neither
000686  has to be compressed into narrow cells. Submitted site names, order, and colour
000687  markers come from `config/site_display_registry.csv`.
000797  The valid share ranges from
000798  `r scales::percent(min(selected_days$valid_melEDI_percent) / 100, accuracy = 0.1)`
000799  to
000800  `r scales::percent(max(selected_days$valid_melEDI_percent) / 100, accuracy = 0.1)`.
000801  All nine selected days therefore remain above the 80% daily threshold. A zero
000802  melEDI value is a measurement and remains visible. Declared non-wear and other
000803  unavailable minutes remain missing, so the exposure line is not connected
000804  across them. The 50% hourly rule governs hourly summaries; it does not erase
000805  an observed one-minute value from this inspection.
000807  ## One-minute profiles
000809  The exact 12,960 rows shown below are available as the paired
000810  [figure source-data CSV](../../artifacts/11_source_data/prepared_day_showcase.csv).
000811  The accepted production figure is also stored as
000812  [PNG](../../artifacts/10_figures/prepared_day_showcase.png) and
000813  [vector SVG](../../artifacts/10_figures/prepared_day_showcase.svg). For this
000814  HTML page, the same stored source data are split into three three-panel figures
000815  so that axes, times, participant labels, and annotations remain readable at
000816  the final display width.
000854  ### How to read the profiles
000856  - Civil night is the shaded part of the day when the Sun is more than 6°
000857    below the horizon; the unshaded interval is civil day.
000858  - The strip below zero shows the diary-defined waking daytime, the three hours
000859    before sleep, and sleep. Red overwrites the strip where the wear log records
000860    “off” outside diary-defined sleep; wear-log entries do not define sleep.
000861  - The dashed step is a contextual reference rather than a fitted value: 250 lx
000862    during waking daytime, 10 lx during the three hours before sleep, and 1 lx
000863    during sleep.
000864  - All panels use the same transformed y-axis so that zero, dim exposure, and
000865    high daytime exposure can appear together. The transformation changes only
000866    display spacing; the source CSV retains the stored melEDI values in lx.
000868  ## Checks on the current stored bundle
000870  The production verifier is designed to reopen the prepared inputs, reproduce
000871  the fixed-seed selection, and compare the selected minutes, state masks,
000872  daylight context, and figure files. That result-producing verifier was not run
000873  for this documentation render. The table below is intentionally narrower: it
000874  reports only file-identity, schema, and lightweight structural checks performed
000875  on the current bundle while this page rendered.
000900  ## Stored display artifacts and analytical handoff
