000906  `r unique(manifest$ggplot2_version)`. Their current byte sizes and SHA-256
000907  fingerprints match the manifest.
000962  These artifacts document and visualize already prepared data. They do not
000963  change an exposure value, state assignment, inclusion decision, sample, model
000964  frame, or hypothesis result, and they are not inputs to the hypothesis
000965  analyses.

===== notebooks/hypotheses/H01.qmd =====
001065  ## Hypothesis and analytical question
001067  The preregistered H1 hypothesis was:
001069  > “Personal light-exposure metrics differ across sites after accounting for latitude and photoperiod.”
001071  The scientific question is whether the 17 personal-light-exposure metrics show
001072  site, photoperiod, or latitude associations across the international study
001073  sites. Near-eye measurements are primary because they most closely represent
001074  light reaching the eyes during wear. Chest measurements provide complementary
001075  evidence and are not pooled with near-eye measurements.
001077  The analysis treats site and latitude as separate explanations. Each site has
001078  one latitude, so a fixed-site model and a linear-latitude model cannot be
001079  interpreted as independent covariates in one ordinary fixed-effects model.
001080  Instead, both models use the same observations and photoperiod adjustment, and
001081  their adequacy is compared on that common frame.
001083  ::: {.callout-note title="Answer in brief"}
001084  After correction in four separate complete 17-test Benjamini--Hochberg
001085  families, the primary near-eye analysis supported
001086  `r primary_support$supported_metrics[primary_support$question_order == 1]`
001087  metrics for overall site,
001088  `r primary_support$supported_metrics[primary_support$question_order == 2]` for
001089  photoperiod, and
001090  `r primary_support$supported_metrics[primary_support$question_order == 3]` for
001091  latitude. Consequential examples per additional hour of photoperiod were a
001092  `r format_effect_ci(primary_mean_medi$photoperiod_estimate_practical, primary_mean_medi$photoperiod_conf_low_practical, primary_mean_medi$photoperiod_conf_high_practical, primary_mean_medi$photoperiod_effect_type, primary_mean_medi$display_unit)`
001093  ratio in mean melEDI, and
001094  `r format_effect_ci(primary_pre_sleep$photoperiod_estimate_practical, primary_pre_sleep$photoperiod_conf_low_practical, primary_pre_sleep$photoperiod_conf_high_practical, primary_pre_sleep$photoperiod_effect_type, primary_pre_sleep$display_unit)`
001095  less calendar-day cumulative time below 10 lx melEDI before sleep; last light
001096  above 250 lx melEDI occurred
001097  `r format_effect_ci(primary_last_light$photoperiod_estimate_practical, primary_last_light$photoperiod_conf_low_practical, primary_last_light$photoperiod_conf_high_practical, primary_last_light$photoperiod_effect_type, primary_last_light$display_unit)`
001098  later. Fixed effects represented
001099  `r formatC(100 * fixed_r2_range[[1]], format = "f", digits = 1)`--`r formatC(100 * fixed_r2_range[[2]], format = "f", digits = 1)`%
001100  of outcome variation across metrics, while complete models represented
001101  `r formatC(100 * model_r2_range[[1]], format = "f", digits = 1)`--`r formatC(100 * model_r2_range[[2]], format = "f", digits = 1)`%.
001102  Complementary chest evidence retained the broad geographic pattern; on the
001103  exact common samples, `r paired_summary$same_side` of
001104  `r paired_summary$estimands` matched photoperiod or latitude estimates lay on
001105  the same side of the null, although `r paired_summary$support_switches` support
001106  decisions differed, and the gap-timing-unaware near-eye sensitivity changed
001107  `r gap_primary_summary$support_switches` of
001108  `r gap_primary_summary$evaluated_cells` metric-question decisions.
001109  :::
001111  [How the H01 analysis was prepared](../../audit/hypotheses/H01/H01_analysis_preparation.html)
001113  ### Deviations from the preregistered specification
001140  ## What was analysed
001142  The response package contains 17 metrics spanning temporal dynamics, level,
001143  duration, exposure history, spectrum, and timing. Participant-day outcomes use
001144  a participant random intercept. Participant-level dynamics outcomes use
001145  ordinary Gaussian models because each participant contributes one analytical
001146  observation. Each response uses its declared Gaussian transformation or
001147  Tweedie log-link family; the same implementation is used for both data
001148  scenarios.
001150  The sensitivity analysis also uses a **gap-timing-unaware dataset**. It still
001151  passed the general 50%-per-hour and 80%-per-day coverage rules;
001152  “gap-timing-unaware” means that the timing of the remaining missing
001153  observations is not used for metric-specific adjustment. For this contrast
001154  only, the primary dataset can be understood as a **time-sensitive primary
001155  metric dataset**. Below, it is called simply the primary dataset, while the
001156  sensitivity scenario remains the gap-timing-unaware dataset.
001175  ## Statistical models
001177  Site, photoperiod, latitude, and site-versus-linear-latitude adequacy are four
001178  separate model-level questions. Their raw p-values are adjusted as four
001179  separate complete 17-test Benjamini–Hochberg families. A site-specific
001180  follow-up is made only when the corresponding overall site test has a
001181  BH-adjusted p < 0.050.
001182  That follow-up compares each site with the equally weighted overall site mean,
001183  reports a difference or ratio with a 95% confidence interval, and applies a
001184  within-metric multiplicity adjustment.
001186  Gaussian fixed-effect comparisons use maximum likelihood; final Gaussian
001187  participant-day fits use restricted maximum likelihood. Tweedie models use a
001188  log link and maximum likelihood throughout. The following evaluated R cells
001189  show the exact Wilkinson formula objects supplied to the fitted and comparison
001190  models.
001192  ### Participant-level formula objects
001211  ### Participant-day formula objects
001230  ### Preregistered-scope sensitivity formula objects
001290  ## Results overview
001292  In the primary near-eye analysis, the four complete correction families
001293  supported `r primary_support$supported_metrics[primary_support$question_order == 1]`
001294  metrics for overall site,
001295  `r primary_support$supported_metrics[primary_support$question_order == 2]` for
001296  photoperiod,
001297  `r primary_support$supported_metrics[primary_support$question_order == 3]` for
001298  latitude, and
001299  `r primary_support$supported_metrics[primary_support$question_order == 4]` for
001300  site-versus-linear-latitude adequacy. The complementary chest analysis
001301  supported `r chest_support$supported_metrics[chest_support$question_order == 1]`,
001302  `r chest_support$supported_metrics[chest_support$question_order == 2]`,
001303  `r chest_support$supported_metrics[chest_support$question_order == 3]`, and
001304  `r chest_support$supported_metrics[chest_support$question_order == 4]` metrics,
001305  respectively. This overview uses each placement's all-available sample, so it
001306  describes where each analysis retained support but does not by itself isolate
001307  a placement difference.

001313  ### Matched near-eye and chest comparison
001315  The placement display below uses identical participants and participant-days
001316  for each near-eye/chest pair. Across the 30 comparable photoperiod and latitude
001317  estimands, `r paired_summary$same_side` point estimates lay on the same side of
001318  the null and `r paired_summary$support_switches` BH-adjusted support decisions
001319  differed between placements. The matched samples contained

001322  participant-days and observations, and `r paired_summary$sites` sites,
001323  depending on metric. Proximity to the identity line describes agreement of
001324  point estimates; it is not an equivalence test and does not establish a causal
001325  sensor-placement effect.

001380  ### Variation represented
001382  Marginal R² represents the complete fixed-effect set; conditional R² represents
001383  fixed and participant random effects together. The participant-associated
001384  share is conditional minus marginal R² for participant-day models. Site and
001385  photoperiod part-R² values are non-overlapping model-reduction summaries within
001386  their own calculations, but they are not added because their information can
001387  overlap. Latitude part R² belongs to the separate latitude model.

001544  ## Model diagnostics
001546  All 34 primary and complementary point fits converged with positive-definite
001547  Hessians. A model is **acceptable** when no reviewed diagnostic is flagged,
001548  **acceptable with limitations** when optimization is valid but a residual,
001549  prediction-bound, construct, or clock-scale signal requires disclosure, and
001550  **not acceptable** when a required fit or hard diagnostic fails. None of the
001551  34 models is classified as not acceptable. A review signal is therefore not a
001552  convergence failure and does not by itself justify changing a response family.
001554  The diagnostic matrix evaluates:
001556  - convergence and Hessian validity, which indicate whether the numerical
001557    optimum was obtained and locally identified;
001558  - random-effect singularity, which checks whether an estimated variance
001559    component collapsed to its boundary;
001560  - residual distribution, which detects residual-shape, dispersion, zero-mass,
001561    or outlier discrepancies;
001562  - prediction bounds, which check verified physical lower and upper limits;
001563  - the calendar-day cumulative construct audit for pre-sleep time; and
001564  - the strict-after-16:00 linearization check for clock outcomes.
001566  ![Model diagnostic assessment for each primary near-eye and complementary chest metric. P denotes pass, R denotes review, and a dash denotes a non-applicable check.](../../artifacts/10_figures/H01/stage3/H01_stage3_diagnostic_assessment.png){#fig-h01-diagnostic-assessment fig-alt="Two-panel diagnostic matrix for near-eye and chest models. Rows are the 17 metrics and columns are convergence and Hessian, random-effect singularity, residual distribution, prediction bounds, construct audit, and clock-time cut. Blue P cells pass, yellow R cells require review, and grey dashes mark non-applicable checks. Convergence and Hessian checks pass throughout; review signals are concentrated in residual distribution and a small number of prediction-bound checks." width="100%"}

001648  ## Sensitivity analyses
001650  At the individual metric-by-family support level, the battery is
001651  **qualitatively sensitive**, although the broad geographic pattern remains.
001652  Relative to the primary near-eye analysis, support switches occur in both the
001653  gap-timing-unaware dataset and paired/common-sample analyses and are most
001654  frequent for the complementary chest placement. Photoperiod support is
001655  comparatively stable; site and latitude support vary more with placement and
001656  sample definition.
001679  ### Same-model scenario and paired/common-sample results
001700  ### Paired near-eye and chest estimands
001702  The matched visual and its interpretation are shown in
001703  @fig-h01-paired-placement in the results overview. The table below retains the
001704  component 95% confidence intervals and exact common sample for every plotted
001705  estimand. The two participant-level dynamics metrics are not estimable in the
001706  paired sample and are omitted.
001753  ### Darkest-10-hour midpoint clock cut
001755  The primary midpoint conversion subtracts 24 hours only for clock values
001756  strictly after 16:00. The registered same-row sensitivity uses a noon cut. Both
001757  use the same Gaussian linear model implementation and identical fitted rows.
001832  ### Exactly identified continuous periods
001834  The continuous-period sensitivity excludes censored periods and retains only
001835  exactly identified periods. It uses the same response-family implementation
001836  as the corresponding main model.
001868  ### Preregistered photoperiod scope
001870  The main implementation adjusts all 17 metrics for photoperiod. The
001871  preregistered-scope sensitivity restricts photoperiod adjustment to the
001872  duration metrics declared for that purpose.
001907  ### Marginalization, participant influence, and latitude leverage
001909  Equal-site marginalization gives every site equal weight; observed-sample
001910  marginalization weights sites by their fitted analytical sample. Participant
001911  deletion assesses whether one participant dominates a coefficient, while
001912  leave-one-site-out latitude refits assess the structural leverage of individual
001913  sites on the latitude estimate.
001975  ## Interpretation
001977  The primary results support the hypothesis that personal light exposure
001978  differs geographically, but they do not reduce that pattern to one universal
001979  site ordering or one latitude effect. After the four separate 17-test
001980  Benjamini--Hochberg corrections, overall site was supported for
001981  `r primary_support$supported_metrics[primary_support$question_order == 1]` of
001982  17 metrics, photoperiod for
001983  `r primary_support$supported_metrics[primary_support$question_order == 2]`,
001984  latitude for
001985  `r primary_support$supported_metrics[primary_support$question_order == 3]`,
001986  and site-versus-linear-latitude adequacy for
001987  `r primary_support$supported_metrics[primary_support$question_order == 4]`.
001988  The two dynamics metrics retained none of these associations. In contrast,
001989  the three level metrics retained site, photoperiod, and latitude support for
001990  `r with(filter(primary_category_support, manuscript_category == "level-based"), paste(site, photoperiod, latitude, sep = ", "))`
001991  metrics, respectively; the five duration metrics retained
001992  `r with(filter(primary_category_support, manuscript_category == "duration-based"), paste(site, photoperiod, latitude, sep = ", "))`;
001993  and the five timing metrics retained
001994  `r with(filter(primary_category_support, manuscript_category == "timing-based"), paste(site, photoperiod, latitude, sep = ", "))`.
001995  Thus geography appeared most consistently in light level and timing, whereas
001996  duration responses were more consistently related to photoperiod.
001998  Among supported ratio-scale photoperiod effects, an additional hour of day
001999  length corresponded to approximately
002000  `r formatC(100 * (photoperiod_supported_ratio_range$minimum - 1), format = "f", digits = 0)`--`r formatC(100 * (photoperiod_supported_ratio_range$maximum - 1), format = "f", digits = 0)`%
002001  higher responses, depending on metric. Examples include mean melEDI at
002002  `r format_effect_ci(primary_mean_medi$photoperiod_estimate_practical, primary_mean_medi$photoperiod_conf_low_practical, primary_mean_medi$photoperiod_conf_high_practical, primary_mean_medi$photoperiod_effect_type, primary_mean_medi$display_unit)`,
002003  calendar-day cumulative pre-sleep time below 10 lx melEDI at
002004  `r format_effect_ci(primary_pre_sleep$photoperiod_estimate_practical, primary_pre_sleep$photoperiod_conf_low_practical, primary_pre_sleep$photoperiod_conf_high_practical, primary_pre_sleep$photoperiod_effect_type, primary_pre_sleep$display_unit)`,
002005  and last light above 250 lx melEDI at
002006  `r format_effect_ci(primary_last_light$photoperiod_estimate_practical, primary_last_light$photoperiod_conf_low_practical, primary_last_light$photoperiod_conf_high_practical, primary_last_light$photoperiod_effect_type, primary_last_light$display_unit)`.
002007  Hierarchical site contrasts then identify which sites differ from the
002008  equally weighted site mean, but only for metrics with a supported overall site
002009  test; their varying directions rule out a single north-to-south site ranking.
002011  The R² summaries distinguish complete-model variation, participant-associated
002012  variation, and term-level part R². They should not be interpreted as an
002013  additive decomposition: site and photoperiod can contain overlapping
002014  information, and latitude belongs to a separate same-frame model. Across the
002015  17 primary metrics, fixed effects represented
002016  `r formatC(100 * fixed_r2_range[[1]], format = "f", digits = 1)`--`r formatC(100 * fixed_r2_range[[2]], format = "f", digits = 1)`%
002017  and complete models represented
002018  `r formatC(100 * model_r2_range[[1]], format = "f", digits = 1)`--`r formatC(100 * model_r2_range[[2]], format = "f", digits = 1)`%.
002019  For participant-day outcomes, the participant-associated share ranged from
002020  `r formatC(100 * participant_share_range[[1]], format = "f", digits = 1)` to
002021  `r formatC(100 * participant_share_range[[2]], format = "f", digits = 1)`%.
002022  When averaged only over metrics whose corresponding model-level test was
002023  supported, term part R² was
002024  `r formatC(100 * supported_part_r2$estimate[supported_part_r2$measure == "site_part_r2"], format = "f", digits = 1)`%
002025  for site (`r supported_part_r2$supported_n[supported_part_r2$measure == "site_part_r2"]`
002026  metrics),
002027  `r formatC(100 * supported_part_r2$estimate[supported_part_r2$measure == "photoperiod_part_r2"], format = "f", digits = 1)`%
002028  for photoperiod (`r supported_part_r2$supported_n[supported_part_r2$measure == "photoperiod_part_r2"]`
002029  metrics), and
002030  `r formatC(100 * supported_part_r2$estimate[supported_part_r2$measure == "latitude_part_r2"], format = "f", digits = 1)`%
002031  for latitude (`r supported_part_r2$supported_n[supported_part_r2$measure == "latitude_part_r2"]`
002032  metrics).
002034  The complementary chest analysis reproduced the broad geographic signal, but
002035  the placement-specific all-available heatmap is not a test of placement. In
002036  the exact common samples, `r paired_summary$same_side` of
002037  `r paired_summary$estimands` comparable photoperiod and latitude point
002038  estimates were on the same side of the null and
002039  `r paired_summary$support_switches` BH-adjusted support decisions differed.
002040  This indicates material metric-level placement sensitivity without implying
002041  equivalence, a formal placement effect, or that one placement can substitute
002042  for the other. The gap-timing-unaware near-eye sensitivity changed
002043  `r gap_primary_summary$support_switches` of
002044  `r gap_primary_summary$evaluated_cells` support decisions, so individual
002045  metric claims remain conditional on the timing-aware metric preparation even
002046  though the broad geographic conclusion persists.
002048  ## Limitations
002050  - The design is observational; associations with site, photoperiod, and
002051    latitude are not causal effects.
002052  - Site and latitude are structurally linked because every site has one
002053    latitude. Separate same-frame models address estimability but cannot remove
002054    all geographic confounding.
002055  - Many models are acceptable with limitations because residual-shape or
002056    prediction-bound checks require review. The representative plots and stored
002057    diagnostic values make the main warning types inspectable and should temper
002058    tail-sensitive interpretation.
002059  - Near-eye and chest sensors measure related but different exposure
002060    environments. Chest measurements are complementary and must not be relabelled
002061    as ocular exposure.
002062  - Rows from the gap-timing-unaware dataset do not retain measurement-support
002063    hours, so those hours remain unavailable.
002064  - The calendar-day cumulative pre-sleep outcome can include more than one
002065    diary-defined pre-sleep interval. Values strictly above six hours are audited
002066    but are not capped.
002068  ## Figure and table source data
002070  Each plotted figure has a paired CSV, and the publication tables are read from
002071  the corresponding H01 result CSVs without refitting or resampling.

===== audit/hypotheses/H01/H01_analysis_preparation.qmd =====
000214  ::: {.callout-note title="About this analysis record"}
000215  This page explains how verified personal light-exposure metrics were turned
000216  into the H01 results. It describes the analytical samples, transformations,
000217  response families, model implementation, diagnostics, sensitivity branches,
000218  and the connection between code and stored outputs. The companion
000219  [H01 results report](../../../notebooks/hypotheses/H01.html) presents the
000220  scientific findings.
000222  Rendering verifies bounded file identities and calculates descriptive
000223  summaries from the H01-specific model rows and sample-flow records. It does
000224  **not** call `lm()`, `lmer()`, `glmmTMB()`, `emmeans()`, `predict()`, or
000225  `simulate()`, refit any model, regenerate predictions, or rerun a bootstrap.
000226  Model estimates, 95% confidence intervals, diagnostics, influence results,
000227  and bootstrap summaries are read from the stored outputs named below.
000228  :::
000230  ::: {.callout-note title="Bounded darkest-10-hour mean amendment"}
000231  Numerical values indistinguishable from zero were normalized to exact zero in
000232  eight primary darkest-10-hour mean records before the four affected primary
000233  L10 branches were refitted. Each affected branch has at least 1,000 retained
000234  successful joint bootstrap refits. The focused verifier found no change in
000235  support, diagnostic, sensitivity, or claim disposition. The other 16 metric
000236  fits, the gap-timing-unaware L10 fits, and the separate MDER amendment remain
000237  outside this bounded update.
000238  :::
000240  ## Purpose
000242  H01 asks whether personal light exposure differs among study sites. The
000243  analysis treats near-eye measurements as primary and chest measurements as
000244  complementary evidence. It evaluates 17 prespecified light-exposure metrics,
000245  while distinguishing an overall site effect from photoperiod and absolute
000246  latitude.
000248  This record explains **which data and code produced those results, why each
000249  operation was needed, what it contributed, and what evidence shows that it
000250  behaved as intended**. The sections follow the data-to-result sequence.
000252  ## Analysis map
000274  The arrows show dependencies. They do not imply that this page repeats the
000275  fitting, prediction, or resampling operations.
000277  ## What is calculated when this page opens
000301  ## 1. Data entering the analysis
000303  ### Verified inputs
000305  Two prepared datasets enter the analysis. The primary dataset could be
000306  interpreted as a **time-sensitive primary metric dataset** because the timing
000307  of remaining missing observations is used where it matters for the metric.
000308  The predefined sensitivity is the **gap-timing-unaware dataset**. It still
000309  passed the general 50%-per-hour and 80%-per-day coverage rules, but the timing
000310  of remaining missing observations was not used for an additional
000311  metric-specific adjustment. This name does not mean that gaps, missingness,
000312  or coverage were ignored.
000341  <details>
000342  <summary>Show the complete pinned manifest identities</summary>
000344  - Primary model-data manifest:
000345    `r input_contract$main$manifest_sha256`
000346  - Gap-timing-unaware model-data manifest:
000347    `r input_contract$manuscript_prepared_data$manifest_sha256`
000348  - Shared H01 implementation contract:
000349    `r input_contract$implementation_contract_sha256`
000350  - Shared implementation source:
000351    `r input_contract$shared_implementation_sha256`
000353  </details>
000355  ### What these files contain
000357  Each H01 data bundle contains the 17-metric contract, long model rows, sample
000358  flow, exclusions, predictor centres, scenario availability, a variable
000359  dictionary, and input provenance. The primary long table contains
000360  `r format_count(nrow(main_rows))` rows and 50 named fields. Its composite key
000361  is scenario, placement, sample definition, metric, site, participant, and—when
000362  the outcome is daily—local date. The construction code verifies uniqueness
000363  within the appropriate analytical unit before a model frame is written.
000365  The model rows retain the original metric value and units, the response
000366  construct, the metric-specific support denominator when available, site and
000367  participant identifiers, photoperiod, latitude, and the centred predictors
000368  supplied to the models. Missing support is never converted to zero exposure.
000370  ### Why prepared records did not enter every model frame
000372  The same metric-availability rules are applied before the site and latitude
000373  models are constructed. The table counts primary-dataset records excluded
000374  from each placement's all-available frame; it lists each record once by using
000375  the site-plus-photoperiod frame, because the corresponding latitude frame
000376  applies the same outcome-availability screen.
000438  ## 2. The 17-metric response package
000496  ### Construct-specific rules
000519  ## 3. Model-frame and sample construction
000521  ### Eight analysis scenarios
000523  The same implementation creates two placements × two prepared datasets × two
000524  sample definitions. The all-available near-eye primary dataset is the primary
000525  analysis. Chest is fitted separately. Placement-matched samples retain the
000526  same participants and participant-days at both placements for direct
000527  near-eye–chest comparison; they are not pooled models.
000594  ### Exact primary fitted samples
000596  Participant-level dynamics models have one fitted outcome per participant;
000597  their participant-day count records the days contributing to that summary.
000598  Participant-day models have one fitted outcome per retained participant-day.
000599  Support hours are reported only when the stored preparation carries a valid
000600  metric-specific denominator.

000673  #### Chest
