000740  [Source data for @fig-h01-prep-sample-support](H01_analysis_preparation_files/source-data/H01_preparation_fitted_sample_support.csv)
000742  ### Retention from prepared rows to fitted rows
000803  [Source data for @fig-h01-prep-model-frame-retention](H01_analysis_preparation_files/source-data/H01_preparation_model_frame_retention.csv)
000805  ### Site-specific support
000807  The complete per-metric, per-site sample record is stored in
000808  [`H01_exact_samples_by_site.csv`](../../../artifacts/09_tables/H01/H01_exact_samples_by_site.csv).
000809  The compact table below shows the range across the 17 outcomes without hiding
000810  that some outcome-specific model frames are smaller.
000863  ## 4. Model construction
000865  ### Exact formula set
000867  The implementation uses ordinary Gaussian linear models for participant-level
000868  outcomes and Gaussian linear mixed models or Tweedie generalized mixed models
000869  for participant-day outcomes. Participant-day models include a participant
000870  random intercept. Site and latitude models are fitted separately so that a
000871  nine-level site factor is never interpreted as a single latitude coefficient.
000895  The complete site model estimates site and photoperiod jointly. The reduced
000896  site model removes site; the reduced photoperiod model removes photoperiod.
000897  The latitude model substitutes centred absolute latitude, expressed per 10°,
000898  for the site factor while retaining the same photoperiod term. All comparison
000899  fits use maximum likelihood; final Gaussian mixed models are refitted with
000900  restricted maximum likelihood for coefficient and variance reporting.
000902  ### Central settings
000929  ## 5. Estimands, uncertainty, and multiplicity
000954  The four model-level questions form four separate complete 17-test
000955  Benjamini–Hochberg families. Adjustment is never performed over only the
000956  metrics that happened to have small raw p-values.
000982  ## 6. Bootstrap, diagnostics, and influence
000984  ### Production joint refits
001056  The refit draws are stored separately for every run and metric. They are not
001057  recreated for table or figure revisions. The bootstrap outputs and the
001058  production execution record are covered by
001059  `H01_response_family_bootstrap_production_artifacts.csv` and the larger model
001060  result manifest.
001062  ### Diagnostic and influence chain
001086  The results report shows the corresponding diagnostic assessments and
001087  representative plots. This page records where those assessments came from.
001088  For the two primary all-available placements, the stored diagnostic table
001089  contains `r nrow(diagnostics |> filter(.data$run_id %in% c(primary_near_id, primary_chest_id)))`
001090  metric-level records.
001092  ## 7. Sensitivity branches
001119  ## 8. Code map and execution order
001121  ### Reusable modules
001143  ### Executable entry points
001176  ### Commands
001178  The principal commands were run from the repository root in fresh R 4.6.1
001179  sessions using the activated project library. They are shown for
001180  reproducibility; this page does not execute the computationally intensive
001181  commands.
001201  ## 9. Output provenance and file identities
001203  The manifest chain records output paths, SHA-256 identities, byte sizes,
001204  producing code, and R version. The table verifies the manifests that lead from
001205  the H01 data bundles to the standalone report. The final H01 inventory is
001206  built after the reader-facing pages so that it can contain their final
001207  identities without creating a circular checksum dependency.
001247  ## 10. How to examine or reproduce H01
001249  A reader can inspect the chain in four passes:
001251  1. compare `h01_contract.R` with the two pinned model-data manifests to confirm
001252     the 17 responses, eight runs, transformations, and formulas;
001253  2. compare the sample and retention displays above with `model_rows.csv`,
001254     `sample_flow.csv`, and `H01_exact_samples_by_site.csv`;
001255  3. follow the producing script and SHA-256 identity in each manifest, using
001256     the stored model, bootstrap, diagnostic, table, and source-data files; and
001257  4. read the [H01 results report](../../../notebooks/hypotheses/H01.html)
001258     alongside its full-precision source tables rather than inferring numeric
001259     values from figure pixels.
001261  Changes to shared metric preparation must be made upstream and propagated to
001262  every affected hypothesis. A display-only change can instead be made from the
001263  stored reporting objects without refitting the models or repeating the
001264  bootstrap.
001266  ::: {.callout-tip title="Reader takeaway"}
001267  The H01 result comes from two verified model-data bundles, a single declared
001268  17-response implementation, exact response-specific model frames, four
001269  complete multiplicity families, stored joint-refit bootstrap draws, and
001270  separate diagnostic and sensitivity records. This page connects those pieces
001271  without changing the scientific result.
001272  :::
001274  ## Environment

===== notebooks/hypotheses/H02.qmd =====
000728  ::: {.callout-note title="How this result was produced"}
000729  The companion [H02 analysis preparation and provenance
000730  page](../../audit/hypotheses/H02/H02_analysis_preparation.html) follows the
000731  data-to-result chain, identifies every executable script and central
000732  parameter, and displays intermediate input and diagnostic checks. It does not
000733  rerun the fitted models or production bootstraps.
000734  :::
000736  ## Question
000738  The preregistered hypothesis was:
000740  > “Within-participant variance in hourly melanopic EDI, with participants
000741  > nested in sites, exceeds variance between sites.”
000743  The preregistered model was:
000751  The scientific question is whether daily melEDI patterns differ among sites,
000752  how much people within a site differ from one another, and whether those
000753  within-site differences are larger than the differences among sites.
000755  ::: {.callout-tip title="Answer in brief"}
000756  Near-eye daily patterns differed among sites
000757  ($\chi^2=$ `r fmt_number(site_test$test_statistic, 2)`,
000758  `r fmt_number(site_test$test_df, 0)` degrees of freedom,
000759  BH-adjusted $p$ = `r fmt_p_value(site_test$p_adjusted, site_test$p_adjusted < 0.05)`).
000760  Participant curves plus participant-day shifts were
000761  `r fmt_ci(near_combined_ratio$estimate, near_combined_ratio$lower_95, near_combined_ratio$upper_95, 2)`
000762  times as dispersed as site curves. In the conditional Shapley analysis, those
000763  two participant-level blocks received
000764  `r with(filter(near_dominance_comparison, comparison_id == "participant_plus_day_to_site_shapley_ratio"), fmt_ci(estimate, lower_95, upper_95, 2))`
000765  times as much in-sample model-fit credit as the site-pattern block. Chest
000766  measurements gave the same combined ordering.
000767  :::
000769  ## What was analysed
000771  Near-eye measurements are primary because they are closer to the light
000772  reaching the eye. Chest measurements are analysed separately as complementary
000773  evidence; the two placements are never pooled. During reported sleep, both
000774  placements characterize the bedside light environment rather than light at
000775  their nominal worn position.
000777  The model used local wall-clock time to describe the daily pattern. True UTC
000778  time determined observation ordering. A 30-minute bin entered the model when
000779  at least 15 of its 30 one-minute melEDI values were valid. Missing, unsupported,
000780  non-wear, and out-of-range values remained missing, not zero. Repeated
000781  fall-back clock bins retained their fold provenance; residual sequences were
000782  reset at each participant-day, after any elapsed-time discontinuity, and
000783  around a wall-clock outcome without a one-to-one true-time coordinate.
000785  ### Deviations from preregistration
000787  The tables below list the scientific and implementation differences that are
000788  material to H02. They are stated here because they change the exact outcome,
000789  model, or interpretation.
000829  ## Model
000831  The exact Wilkinson formula was:
000841  Here, `response` is
000842  $\log_{10}(\mathrm{melEDI}+0.1\ \mathrm{lx})$.
000843  The first term is the **global time effect**: the daily pattern shared across
000844  sites, which joins smoothly at midnight. The `sz` term estimates how each site
000845  departs from that global pattern. Because those departures sum to zero, the
000846  global time effect is centred equally across sites rather than being the curve
000847  of a reference site or an observation-weighted site average. The participant term gives
000848  each person a smooth departure from the site pattern. Participant identifiers
000849  are site-prefixed, so people remain nested within sites. The final term allows
000850  each participant-day to shift up or down without changing its shape.
000852  Only the global time effect is explicitly cyclic. The default time marginals
000853  inside the `sz` site term and `fs` participant term are not forced to meet
000854  at midnight. A fully cyclic formulation is therefore included as a
000855  model-form sensitivity.
000857  The model was fitted with `mgcv::bam()` under fREML and a boundary-aware
000858  AR(1) working correlation. Site curves and their pointwise conditional 95%
000859  intervals were obtained from the fitted linear-predictor matrix and full
000860  coefficient covariance. Smoothing parameters are treated as fixed in those
000861  pointwise intervals. `gratia` was used for residual appraisal and
000862  concurvity diagnostics.
000864  ### Quantities used to compare sites and people
000866  The first comparison describes the spread of the fitted curves. On a common
000867  48-bin clock grid,
000869  $$
000870  V_{\mathrm{site}} =
000871  \frac{1}{48}\sum_b
000872  \operatorname{Var}_{s}\{\hat f_s(t_b)\},
000873  $$
000875  and participant-curve variation is the average—giving every site equal
000876  weight—of the within-site variance among participant curves, averaged over the
000877  same clock grid. Participant-day variation likewise gives each site equal
000878  weight when averaging the variance of day-specific intercept contributions.
000879  Their ratios compare fitted curve
000880  dispersion on the modelling scale. They are not proportions of response
000881  variance and are not “variance explained.”
000883  The second comparison asks which model block contributes more to in-sample
000884  fit. The global time effect is retained in every model. Site pattern,
000885  participant pattern, and participant-day shift are then included in every
000886  possible combination. For each block, the conditional Shapley allocation is
000887  its average increase in row-weighted in-sample $R^2$ across all possible
000888  orders of entry. This allocation is descriptive: it is neither causal
000889  importance nor cross-validated predictive importance.
000891  Both sets of 95% confidence intervals use 2,000 hierarchical cluster
000892  resamples: sites, participants within sampled sites, and participant-days
000893  within sampled participants. The fitted curve contributions or fitted subset
000894  models are held fixed. The intervals therefore describe cluster-sampling
000895  uncertainty conditional on the fitted smoothing structure.
000897  ## Primary near-eye result
000899  The primary model fitted `r near_counts$participants` participants,
000900  `r near_counts$participant_days` participant-days,
000901  `r format(near_counts$observations_30_minute, big.mark = ",")` 30-minute
000902  observations, and `r near_counts$sites` sites.
000927  ### Fitted-curve dispersion
000945  Participant curves were
000946  `r fmt_ci(near_participant_ratio$estimate, near_participant_ratio$lower_95, near_participant_ratio$upper_95, 2)`
000947  times as dispersed as site curves. Adding participant-day shifts increased
000948  that ratio to
000949  `r fmt_ci(near_combined_ratio$estimate, near_combined_ratio$lower_95, near_combined_ratio$upper_95, 2)`.
000950  Thus, fitted variation within sites was larger than fitted variation among
000951  sites under the predeclared clock-grid definition.
000953  ### Relative contribution to fitted-model performance
000986  Participant-specific patterns received
000987  `r with(filter(near_dominance_comparison, comparison_id == "participant_to_site_shapley_ratio"), fmt_ci(estimate, lower_95, upper_95, 2))`
000988  times as much in-sample model-fit credit as site patterns. Participant
000989  patterns and participant-day shifts together received
000990  `r with(filter(near_dominance_comparison, comparison_id == "participant_plus_day_to_site_shapley_ratio"), fmt_ci(estimate, lower_95, upper_95, 2))`
000991  times as much credit as site patterns and accounted for
000992  `r with(filter(near_dominance_comparison, comparison_id == "participant_plus_day_share_of_heterogeneity"), fmt_percent_ci(estimate, lower_95, upper_95, 1))`
000993  of the fitted $R^2$ increment beyond the global time effect.
000995  This is the most direct answer to “how much more relevant were participants
000996  within sites than sites themselves”: in this fitted sample and model,
000997  participant-level blocks contributed substantially more to in-sample fit than
000998  the site-pattern block. It does not imply that changing a person's behaviour
000999  would cause that amount of change.
001001  ### Daily profiles
001003  Panel A shows the global time effect. Panel B overlays the fitted
001004  participant curves. Panel C shows the absolute fitted curve for each site;
001005  each facet states its fitted participant-day count. Panel D shows the site's
001006  fitted curve divided by the global time effect. A factor of 2 means twice the
001007  fitted $\mathrm{melEDI}+0.1\ \mathrm{lx}$ quantity; a factor of 0.5 means
001008  half. Grey vertical bands show the mean civil-night intervals.
001010  Ribbons in A, C, and D are pointwise conditional 95% intervals. Red segments
001011  in D identify individual 30-minute bins whose interval excludes one. These
001012  segments are local descriptions, not a jointly multiplicity-controlled test
001013  across the day or across sites.
001046  ### Model assessment
001077  The model converged at full rank, the basis-size checks did not indicate that
001078  the available time bases were too small, and the AR(1) correction reduced
001079  overall lag-1 residual correlation from
001080  `r fmt_number(filter(residual_acf, run_id == near_id, stage == "preliminary_no_AR1", lag_30_minute_bins == 1)$correlation, 3)`
001081  to
001082  `r fmt_number(filter(residual_acf, run_id == near_id, stage == "final_AR1_standardized", lag_30_minute_bins == 1)$correlation, 3)`.
001083  These are strengths for estimating average daily patterns.
001085  The residual diagnostics are not ideal. The QQ plot has an S-shaped departure
001086  from normality, and the line of observations at response $-1$ reflects the
001087  large number of exact zeros after the $\log_{10}(\mathrm{melEDI}+0.1)$
001088  transform. Residual magnitude also increases modestly with fitted exposure.
001089  Some participant-days retain positive lag-1 correlation. In addition, formal
001090  concurvity is high between the common and site time bases even though the
001091  sum-to-zero constraint is satisfied at full rank. The model is therefore
001092  adequate for a conditional description of mean curves and their complete
001093  contrasts, but it is not a perfect generative description of the full
001094  zero-heavy response distribution. Isolated smooth coefficients should not be
001095  interpreted as independent effects.
001097  ## Complementary chest result
001099  The chest model used the identical formula, transform, fitting algorithm,
001100  curve definitions, and interval procedures. It fitted
001101  `r chest_counts$participants` participants,
001102  `r chest_counts$participant_days` participant-days,
001103  `r format(chest_counts$observations_30_minute, big.mark = ",")` 30-minute
001104  observations, and `r chest_counts$sites` sites. Tübingen is absent because
001105  chest measurements were unavailable there.
001130  ### Fitted-curve dispersion
001148  Chest participant curves were
001149  `r fmt_ci(chest_participant_ratio$estimate, chest_participant_ratio$lower_95, chest_participant_ratio$upper_95, 2)`
001150  times as dispersed as chest site curves. That interval includes one. After
001151  participant-day shifts were included, the ratio was
001152  `r fmt_ci(chest_combined_ratio$estimate, chest_combined_ratio$lower_95, chest_combined_ratio$upper_95, 2)`,
001153  retaining the combined participant-level ordering.
001155  ### Relative contribution to fitted-model performance
001188  At the chest, participant patterns received
001189  `r with(filter(chest_dominance_comparison, comparison_id == "participant_to_site_shapley_ratio"), fmt_ci(estimate, lower_95, upper_95, 2))`
001190  times as much in-sample model-fit credit as site patterns. Participant
001191  patterns plus participant-day shifts received
001192  `r with(filter(chest_dominance_comparison, comparison_id == "participant_plus_day_to_site_shapley_ratio"), fmt_ci(estimate, lower_95, upper_95, 2))`
001193  times as much credit. The complementary placement therefore reproduces the
001194  relative-relevance ordering.
001196  ### Daily profiles
001229  ### Model assessment
001260  The chest model also converged at full rank. The AR(1) correction reduced
001261  overall lag-1 residual correlation from
001262  `r fmt_number(filter(residual_acf, run_id == chest_id, stage == "preliminary_no_AR1", lag_30_minute_bins == 1)$correlation, 3)`
001263  to
001264  `r fmt_number(filter(residual_acf, run_id == chest_id, stage == "final_AR1_standardized", lag_30_minute_bins == 1)$correlation, 3)`.
001265  Its basis-size checks were acceptable. The same lower-bound pattern,
001266  non-normal tails, fitted-dependent residual spread, and high common/site
001267  concurvity remain. The chest model is therefore corroborating evidence with
001268  the same interpretive limitations as the near-eye model.
001270  ## Direct comparison of near-eye and chest patterns
001272  The placement-matched analysis retained exactly the same 112 participants,
001273  643 participant-days, and 29,786 30-minute observations at eight sites for
001274  near-eye and chest fits. Their observation keys match one-to-one. The two
001275  placements were fitted separately with the same response definition,
001276  transformation, temporal grid, and model structure; they were not pooled.
001278  A scalar near-eye-versus-chest identity plot would discard the feature H02 is
001279  designed to estimate: how the fitted pattern changes across the day. The
001280  figure therefore overlays the two fitted site curves at every matched
001281  half-hour. It uses only stored placement-matched predictions and does not fit a new
001282  model. The y-axis is evenly spaced on the modelling scale, with tick labels
001283  back-transformed to melEDI for interpretation.
001363  The ribbons are component pointwise conditional 95% intervals for each
001364  separately fitted placement curve. They are not an interval for the
001365  near-eye-minus-chest difference because the covariance between the two fitted
001366  models was not estimated. Similar-looking curves indicate descriptive
001367  concordance on this matched sample, not statistical equivalence.
001369  ## Sensitivity to other analytical choices
001371  The table changes one substantive choice at a time where possible. The
001372  **gap-timing-unaware dataset** is the predefined data-preparation sensitivity.
001373  It still passed the general 50%-per-hour and 80%-per-day coverage rules, but
001374  the timing of the remaining missing observations was not used for an
001375  additional metric-specific adjustment. For contrast, the primary dataset
001376  could be interpreted as a time-sensitive primary metric dataset because its
001377  preparation uses the timing of remaining missing observations where that
001378  timing is relevant to the metric; it is called simply the **primary dataset**
001379  elsewhere in this report. The cyclic-basis analysis forces site and
001380  participant deviations to meet at midnight. The placement-matched analyses
001381  restrict near-eye and chest fits to the same participant-day-clock
001382  observations.
001406  The participant-plus-day/site ratio remained above one under every reported
001407  choice. The point estimate changed from 1.99 in the primary near-eye analysis
001408  to 1.92 in the gap-timing-unaware dataset and 1.89 under the fully cyclic
001409  model. On the exact placement-matched sample it was 1.54 near-eye and 1.75 chest.
001410  The participant-only ratio was less stable: its interval included one in the
001411  chest and placement-matched analyses. The strongest reproducible conclusion is
001412  therefore that participant-specific patterns **together with day-to-day
001413  shifts** vary more than site patterns; the participant-pattern-only contrast
001414  is less certain outside the full near-eye sample.
001416  ## Interpretation
001418  The daily profile is dominated by the global time effect, but the
001419  remaining heterogeneity is not mainly geographic. People within the same site
001420  showed more distinct fitted daily patterns than the average differences among
001421  site curves, especially when day-to-day level shifts were included.
001423  Two complementary summaries support that interpretation:
001425  - fitted participant-plus-day curves were about twice as dispersed as site
001426    curves in the primary near-eye analysis; and
001427  - participant pattern plus participant-day shift received about 9.5 times as
001428    much conditional in-sample $R^2$ credit as site pattern.
001430  Those statements have different denominators and should not be numerically
001431  combined. The first compares model-implied curve spread. The second allocates
001432  in-sample model fit. Neither is a causal effect, a population-wide fraction of
001433  variance, or a guarantee of out-of-sample prediction.
001435  The site-specific time windows are useful for describing when a fitted site
001436  profile departs from the global time effect, but each ribbon is a pointwise
001437  interval. A reader should not interpret every red segment as a separate
001438  familywise-controlled discovery. The single confirmatory multiplicity family
001439  is the near-eye omnibus site-pattern test (`H02-F1-site-pattern`); it contains
001440  one test, so BH adjustment leaves its p-value unchanged.
001442  Overall, the model provides a defensible description of mean daily patterns
001443  and supports the preregistered ordering when participant-day variation is
001444  included. Confidence is tempered by the zero-heavy residual distribution,
001445  remaining day-specific serial correlation, high common/site concurvity, only
001446  nine near-eye sites, and the conditional—not model-refitting—nature of the
001447  bootstrap intervals.

===== audit/hypotheses/H02/H02_analysis_preparation.qmd =====
000161  ::: {.callout-note title="About this analysis record"}
000162  This page explains how verified 30-minute light-exposure data were turned into
000163  the H02 results. It shows the input and sample checks, model construction,
000164  diagnostic workflow, and the connection between analysis code and stored
000165  outputs. The companion [H02 results report](../../../notebooks/hypotheses/H02.html)
000166  presents the scientific findings.
000168  To keep this page reproducible without refitting the models, rendering verifies
000169  file identities and calculates descriptive summaries from the H02 model frames.
000170  It does **not** call `mgcv::gam()` or `mgcv::bam()`, estimate an AR parameter,
000171  calculate model predictions, rerun a bootstrap, or recompute a Shapley
000172  allocation. Results from those computationally intensive analyses are read
000173  from the listed output files.
000174  :::
000176  ## Purpose
000178  The scientific question was whether daily melEDI patterns differed among
000179  sites and whether differences among people within sites were larger than the
000180  differences among sites. Near-eye measurements were primary; chest
000181  measurements were analysed separately as complementary evidence.
000183  This page explains **which data and code produced the reported H02 result, why
000184  each analytical step was needed, and what evidence shows that the processing
000185  and model behaved as intended**. The sections follow the order of the analysis,
000186  from source-file verification to the outputs used in the results report.
000188  ## Analysis map
000211  The arrows represent data or fitted-output dependencies. They do not imply
000212  that this Quarto document reruns the downstream stages.
000214  ## What is calculated when this page opens
000237  ## 1. Data entering the analysis
000239  ### Verifying the source files
000241  The analysis starts from named, versioned files rather than an informal copy
000242  of the data. The function
000243  `h02_validate_inputs()` in `scripts/hypotheses/H02/h02_contract.R` compares
000244  the current files with pinned SHA-256 identities and checks the relevant base
000245  and temporal-provenance manifest rows. Every fitting and figure-building
000246  script performs this check before proceeding.
000248  Two prepared datasets enter the analysis. The primary dataset could be
000249  interpreted as a time-sensitive primary metric dataset because its preparation
000250  uses the timing of remaining missing observations where that timing is
000251  relevant to the metric. The predefined sensitivity is the
000252  **gap-timing-unaware dataset**. It still passed the general 50%-per-hour and
000253  80%-per-day coverage rules, but the timing of the remaining missing
000254  observations was not used for an additional metric-specific adjustment. This
000255  name does not mean that gaps, missingness, or coverage were ignored.
000302  <details>
000303  <summary>Show the exact current SHA-256 identities</summary>
000315  </details>
000317  ### What the source data already contain
000319  The source files already contain 30-minute arithmetic-mean melEDI,
000320  coverage and admissibility information, local dates and wall-clock bins, and
000321  participant/site identifiers. H02 does not recalculate these measurements.
000322  It joins them to verified true-time provenance so that local wall time can
000323  describe the daily profile while true UTC controls observation ordering.
000325  The primary dataset admitted a 30-minute bin when at least 15 one-minute
000326  melEDI values were valid. Unsupported bins remained missing; they were not
000327  converted to darkness. The support audit below is an output of
000328  `build_h02_model_data.R`, not a calculation introduced for this document.
000375  ## 2. H02-specific model frames
000377  ### Code and purpose
000379  `scripts/hypotheses/H02/build_h02_model_data.R` sources two function modules:
000381  - `h02_contract.R` fixes the input identities, model specification, scenario
000382    registry, transform, and reproducible seeds;
000383  - `h02_data.R` validates keys, joins temporal provenance, creates the fitted
000384    support, defines residual-sequence starts, and constructs the all-available
000385    and placement-matched samples.
000387  The script writes eight H02-only frames: two preparation rules × two
000388  placements × two sample definitions. The all-available near-eye frame is the
000389  primary sample. The all-available chest frame is complementary. The
000390  placement-matched frames retain only participant-day clock bins available for
000391  both placements and are sensitivity analyses—not a pooled model.
000448  ### Primary fitted samples by site
000450  The next two tables are calculated directly from the H02-specific primary
000451  frames during rendering. They are descriptive checks of the inputs to the
000452  models, not model results. Site names, order, and colours come from the shared
000453  display registry.
000538  ### Why the response was transformed
000540  The fitted response is
000541  $\log_{10}(\mathrm{melEDI}+0.1\ \mathrm{lx})$. The offset retains exact-zero
000542  observations and maps them to −1. The transformation compresses a range that
000543  extends from darkness to tens of thousands of lux, but it does not make the
000544  zero mass disappear. Because this discrete spike is much taller than any bin
000545  among the positive measurements, combining both in one histogram hides the
000546  shape of the positive distribution. The figure therefore reports the number
000547  and percentage of exact zeros separately and uses its vertical scale for
000548  positive melEDI observations only.
000645  The near-eye model frame contained `r format_count(response_distribution_zeros$exact_zero_observations[response_distribution_zeros$Placement == "Near eye"])`
000646  exact-zero observations (`r formatC(response_distribution_zeros$exact_zero_percentage[response_distribution_zeros$Placement == "Near eye"], format = "f", digits = 1)`%),
000647  and the chest frame contained `r format_count(response_distribution_zeros$exact_zero_observations[response_distribution_zeros$Placement == "Chest"])`
000648  (`r formatC(response_distribution_zeros$exact_zero_percentage[response_distribution_zeros$Placement == "Chest"], format = "f", digits = 1)`%).
000649  These observations remain in both fitted models; they are separated here only
000650  to make the continuous part of the input distribution legible.
000652  ### Clock support and participant-day completeness
000654  The temporal model uses a 48-bin wall-clock grid. Not every participant-day
000655  has all 48 bins, because unsupported intervals remain absent. The heatmap
000656  shows, for each site and clock bin, the percentage of fitted participant-days
000657  that supplied an observation. It is calculated here from the two primary
000658  model frames.
000775  ## 3. Model construction
000777  ### Exact selected formula
000779  `scripts/hypotheses/H02/h02_modeling.R` contains the fitting functions;
000780  `h02_contract.R` supplies their fixed specification. The exact Wilkinson
000781  formula was:
000791  The first smooth is the **global time effect**: the cyclic daily pattern
000792  shared across sites. The `sz` term estimates site-specific departures that
000793  sum to zero. This centres the global time effect equally across sites without
000794  making any one site the reference. The participant factor smooth estimates a
000795  person-specific daily shape within site. The participant-day random intercept
000796  allows an entire fitted day to shift up or down.
000798  Only the global time effect is forced to join at midnight in the primary
000799  model. A sensitivity analysis also makes the site and participant deviations
000800  cyclic.
000802  ### Model settings
000834  ### What `run_h02_analysis.R` does
000836  The primary near-eye model is fitted first. Reduced versions without the site
000837  pattern, participant pattern, or participant-day intercept are fitted with
000838  the same estimated AR parameter to compare model structures. The full
000839  structure is retained, and the identical selected structure is then applied
000840  to the other seven H02 frames. Each scenario estimates its own boundary-aware
000841  AR parameter from its preliminary residuals.
000843  For every fitted scenario, the script writes the selected model, a checkpoint,
000844  site-curve predictions, fitted term contributions, residual summaries, and
000845  the stored 2,000-draw hierarchical variation bootstrap. The following fit
000846  table is read from `model_fit_summary.csv`; no fit is performed here.
000951  ## 4. Ordering and residual dependence
000953  ### Why local time and true time are both needed
000955  Local wall-clock time answers the scientific question about time of day. It
000956  cannot by itself establish elapsed ordering across daylight-saving folds,
000957  gaps, or missing bins. `h02_data.R` therefore carries the verified true-UTC
000958  source-bin links into each model frame, orders observations by true elapsed
000959  time, and defines a new residual sequence:
000961  - at the start of every participant-day;
000962  - after a missing or otherwise discontinuous 30-minute interval; and
000963  - on both sides of a wall-clock outcome that has no one-to-one elapsed-time
000964    coordinate.
000966  The table is read from the recorded boundary check. “Continuous” rows are
000967  ordinary observations inside an existing sequence and are included only to
000968  make the denominator visible.
000999  ### What the AR step contributed
001001  The preliminary residual lag-1 correlation supplied rho. The final fit used
001002  that rho with the verified sequence boundaries. The plot below is drawn from
001003  the stored `residual_acf.csv`; it does not calculate residuals from a model.
001056  This step substantially reduced average temporal dependence. It did not
001057  guarantee that every participant-day became independent; the later diagnostic
001058  stage therefore also summarized residual autocorrelation separately within
001059  participant-days.
001061  ## 5. From fitted models to reported quantities
001063  ### Site curves and pointwise intervals
001065  `h02_modeling.R` and `h02_figure4_pointwise.R` evaluate the stored model on a
001066  common 48-bin clock grid. The site curve is the global time effect plus that
001067  site's sum-to-zero departure. Participant and participant-day terms are set to
001068  their reference contributions when constructing site curves. Pointwise
001069  conditional 95% intervals come from the fitted linear-predictor matrix and
001070  coefficient covariance with smoothing parameters held fixed.
001072  `build_h02_figure4_replication.R` applies the same builder to near-eye and
001073  chest models. It reads stored fits and contributions; it does not refit the
001074  model. Its principal outputs are:
001076  - `artifacts/10_figures/H02/figure4_exact_layout_replication.png`;
001077  - `artifacts/10_figures/H02/figure4_exact_layout_replication_chest.png`;
001078  - matching PDF files; and
001079  - `artifacts/11_source_data/H02/figure4_exact_layout_source*.rds`.
001081  ### Fitted-curve variation
001083  For every scenario, `run_h02_analysis.R` extracts the fitted global, site,
001084  participant, and participant-day contributions. It then calculates precisely
001085  defined curve-dispersion summaries on the modelling scale. Sites are weighted
001086  equally when within-site participant and day summaries are averaged.
001088  The stored 95% intervals resample sites, participants within sites, and
001089  participant-days within participants 2,000 times. The fitted contributions
001090  remain fixed. Thus, the bootstrap describes cluster-sampling uncertainty
001091  conditional on the fitted smooth structure; it is not a model-refitting
001092  bootstrap. The stored resampling draws are under
001093  `artifacts/08_diagnostics/H02/*__variation_bootstrap.rds`.
001095  ### Conditional Shapley/general dominance
001097  `run_h02_dominance_analysis.R` is intentionally narrower: it runs only for
001098  the main all-available near-eye and complementary chest samples. The global
001099  time effect is mandatory in all subset models. The script fits every one of
001100  the eight possible combinations of site pattern, participant pattern, and
001101  participant-day shift, then allocates their increments in row-weighted
001102  in-sample $R^2$ across all orders of entry.
001104  The 2,000-draw hierarchical bootstrap resamples clusters while holding the
001105  eight sets of fitted predictions fixed. The calculation is descriptive
001106  in-sample relevance—not causal importance and not cross-validated predictive
001107  importance. Stored results and execution evidence are under:
001109  - `artifacts/09_tables/H02/dominance_*.csv`;
001110  - `artifacts/11_source_data/H02/*__dominance_predictions.rds`;
001111  - `artifacts/08_diagnostics/H02/*__dominance_bootstrap.rds`; and
001112  - `artifacts/12_manifests/H02/H02_dominance_manifest.csv`.
001114  ## 6. Diagnostics and influence
001116  `run_h02_temporal_diagnostics.R` reads the stored selected models and model
001117  frames. It verifies coefficient rank and convergence, basis dimensions,
001118  sum-to-zero constraints, midnight continuity, boundary-aware residual
001119  behaviour, formal concurvity, and participant/site influence. `gratia` is used
001120  for the formal concurvity wrapper; `mgcv` supplies the fitted GAM machinery and
001121  core diagnostics.
001123  `build_h02_reader_diagnostics.R` converts the diagnostic source objects into
001124  the compact near-eye and chest figures used in the H02 results report. Neither
001125  script changes the selected formula. Key externally produced outputs are:
001150  The complete diagnostic assessment—including residual plots and exact
001151  near-eye/chest values—is presented in the
001152  [H02 results report](../../../notebooks/hypotheses/H02.html). The present page
001153  focuses on how those diagnostic outputs were generated rather than repeating
001154  their full interpretation.
001156  ## 7. Sensitivity branches
001158  The following branches each change one interpretable part of the analysis.
001159  They use separate output identities and do not silently replace the primary
001160  model.
001184  The global-time-basis diagnostic was fit without a bootstrap and retained the
001185  cyclic global time effect because residual diagnostics did not materially
001186  improve and midnight continuity worsened. Its tables and manifest are under
001187  `artifacts/08_diagnostics/H02/global_time_basis_*`,
001188  `artifacts/09_tables/H02/global_time_basis_model_comparison.csv`, and
001189  `artifacts/12_manifests/H02/H02_global_time_basis_diagnostic_manifest.csv`.
001191  ## 8. Code map and execution order
001193  ### Reusable function modules
001216  ### Executable entry points
001251  ### Commands
001253  The principal commands were run from the repository root in fresh R 4.6.1
001254  sessions using the activated project library. Computationally intensive stages
001255  are shown for reproducibility; this page does not execute them.
001277  ## 9. Output provenance and file identities
001279  Each analysis script writes a small file inventory, or *manifest*, containing
001280  output paths, SHA-256 identities, byte sizes, the producing script, R version,
001281  and—for fitted models—the exact analytical sample. These records connect the
001282  code to the files displayed in the reports and make later changes detectable.
001283  The table below verifies the manifests used to navigate that chain.
001328  ## 10. How to examine or reproduce H02
001330  A reader can examine the analysis in four passes:
001332  1. inspect `h02_contract.R` and `input_hashes.csv` to confirm the frozen input
001333     files and model specification;
001334  2. compare the descriptive samples and support displays above with
001335     `sample_counts.csv` and the model-frame RDS files;
001336  3. follow the producing script named in each stage manifest, checking that its
001337     recorded inputs and outputs match the quantity being estimated; and
001338  4. read the H02 results report alongside the result tables, diagnostic outputs, and
001339     source-data objects rather than attempting to infer results from a figure
001340     image alone.
001342  Changes to source data or processing rules should be made before the
001343  hypothesis-specific model frames are built and then propagated to every
001344  affected analysis. Changing H02 alone would otherwise break comparability with
001345  the other hypotheses. Alternative H02 model choices should remain clearly
001346  labelled sensitivity analyses until their estimands and diagnostics are
001347  established.
001349  ::: {.callout-tip title="Reader takeaway"}
001350  The H02 result was not generated inside a single opaque notebook. It came from
001351  a verified set of source files, H02-only model frames, a reusable temporal-model
001352  implementation, separate inferential and diagnostic stages, stored
001353  cluster-bootstrap evidence, and checksummed outputs. This page shows how those
001354  pieces connect without changing the reported scientific result.
001355  :::
001357  ## Environment

===== notebooks/hypotheses/H03.qmd =====
000334  The companion [H03 analysis preparation and
000335  provenance](../../audit/hypotheses/H03/H03_analysis_preparation.html) traces
000336  the verified inputs, model frames, formulas, diagnostics, sensitivities, and
000337  stored outputs underlying this report.
000339  ## Question
000341  The preregistered hypothesis was:
000343  > **H3: Hourly self-reported light exposure categories predict hourly geometric mean melanopic EDI.**
000345  For each accepted participant-hour, participants reported one primary light
000346  source. This analysis asks whether the seven registered categories are
000347  associated with measured one-hour melEDI after accounting for study site.
000349  ::: {.callout-note title="Answer in brief"}
000350  The seven light-source categories were associated with near-eye melEDI,
000351  *F*(6, 139) = `r sprintf("%.2f", near_category_test$f_statistic)`,
000352  *p* **`r format_p(near_category_test$p_raw)`**. Because category-by-site
000353  heterogeneity was supported, all descriptive means and ratios below come from
000354  the interaction model; only the preregistered primary omnibus comes from the
000355  additive model. Estimates are site-standardized and have 95% confidence
000356  intervals:
000358  - `r answer_category_line(1L)`
000359  - `r answer_category_line(2L)`
000360  - `r answer_category_line(3L)`
000361  - `r answer_category_line(4L)`
000362  - `r answer_category_line(5L)`
000363  - `r answer_category_line(6L)`
000364  - `r answer_category_line(7L)`
000366  The three largest higher and three largest lower supported near-eye site
000367  deviations among those passing the predeclared BH label were:
000369  - `r answer_site_line(1L)`
000370  - `r answer_site_line(2L)`
000371  - `r answer_site_line(3L)`
000372  - `r answer_site_line(4L)`
000373  - `r answer_site_line(5L)`
000374  - `r answer_site_line(6L)`
000376  Complementary chest summaries had the same broad ordering for the six
000377  categories that could be standardized across all eight chest sites. External
000378  light during sleep could not be standardized across all chest sites because
000379  one site-category cell was absent. The gap-timing-unaware dataset did not
000380  change the primary omnibus conclusion or any primary category classification.
000381  :::
000383  ## What was analysed
000385  Near-eye measurements are primary because they more closely represent light
000386  near the eyes. Chest measurements are analysed separately as complementary
000387  evidence; the placements are not pooled and similarity is not interpreted as
000388  equivalence. Sleep-category measurements describe the bedside sleep
000389  environment rather than light measured at a worn near-eye or chest position.
000391  The response is the accepted zero-aware geometric mean melEDI within each
000392  hour, including genuine zero values. The primary model contains 17,935
000393  near-eye participant-hours from 140 participants, 801 participant-days, and
000394  nine sites. The complementary model contains 19,512 chest participant-hours
000395  from 151 participants, 880 participant-days, and eight sites.
000397  A predefined sensitivity uses the **gap-timing-unaware dataset**. This dataset
000398  still passed the general 50%-per-hour and 80%-per-day coverage rules. The term
000399  means that the timing of the remaining missing observations is not used for an
000400  additional metric-specific adjustment; it does not mean that gaps,
000401  missingness, or coverage were ignored. For this contrast only, the approved
000402  preparation could be interpreted as a time-sensitive primary metric dataset.
000403  Below, it is called simply the primary dataset.
000446  All seven categories meet the pooled support rule. Outdoor electric light is
000447  the sparsest category, with 203 near-eye and 219 chest hours. Sparse
000448  category-by-site cells remain in the pooled models but are labelled
000449  non-estimable for site-specific contrasts when they contain fewer than 20
000450  participant-hours, five participants, or five participants shared with the
000451  site's indoor-electric reference.
000453  ### Model specification and operational clarifications
000455  The primary analysis is a population-mean quasi-Tweedie GLM with a log link,
000456  fixed site effects, fixed light-source effects, and variance power 1.539919.
000457  Indoor electric light is the category reference. Participant-clustered HC1
000458  covariance and finite-cluster *t* and *F* reference distributions account for
000459  repeated participant-hours and participant-days. This changes uncertainty;
000460  it does not remove temporal patterns from residuals.
000462  The registered random-effects architecture was not retained because its
000463  participant-day component collapsed and its response-distribution diagnostics
000464  were inadequate. The population-mean formulation preserves all seven
000465  categories and gives an estimable category omnibus. Site heterogeneity is
000466  assessed separately rather than folded into the primary question: near eye
000467  uses the full `site * light_source` interaction, while chest uses an equivalent
000468  observed-cell parameterization because one site-category cell is absent.
000498  Cross-site estimates are **site-standardized**: every included site
000499  contributes equally on the fitted log-mean scale, followed by one
000500  back-transformation. The resulting absolute estimate is the geometric mean of
000501  the fitted site-specific expected values. It is not weighted by the number of
000502  observations at each site.
000504  ## Category summaries from the heterogeneity model
000506  The additive primary model and the interaction model do not impose the same
000507  mean structure. The additive model forces every site to share one category
000508  ratio and estimates that common ratio from all participant-hours; its
000509  site-standardized indoor-electric estimate is 88.9 lx. The interaction model
000510  first estimates each site-category cell and then gives every site equal weight
000511  on the fitted log-mean scale; its corresponding estimate is 85.1 lx. The
000512  difference is therefore a consequence of the supported category-by-site
000513  heterogeneity, not a different sample or outcome.
000515  The preregistered additive-model omnibus remains the primary inferential test.
000516  Because that common-effect model is descriptively restrictive, all category
000517  means, ratios, confidence intervals, and site summaries from this point use
000518  the accepted heterogeneity model.
000568  For near-eye melEDI, the heterogeneity-model site-standardized outdoor-
000569  daylight mean was 959.6 lx (95% CI 786.8–1,170.2), or 11.274 times the
000570  indoor-electric mean (95% CI 8.709–14.595). Outdoor electric was instead 9.4
000571  lx (95% CI 6.9–12.8), or 0.110 times the reference (95% CI 0.078–0.156).
000572  The latter differs markedly from the additive-model summary because the
000573  outdoor-electric contrast varies strongly by site and is sparse in several
000574  site-category cells.
000576  The complementary chest interaction model gave the same broad ordering for
000577  the six fully standardizable categories. These separate placement fits do not
000578  establish that the two sensor positions are interchangeable.
000592  [Result source data](../../artifacts/09_tables/H03/H03_reader_heterogeneity_category_estimands.csv)
000593  and [figure source data](../../artifacts/11_source_data/H03/H03_reader_heterogeneity_category_figure_data.csv)
000594  retain the unrounded estimates and untransformed melEDI values.
000596  ### Omnibus tests
000628  The site-heterogeneity tests were also supported near eye,
000629  *F*(48, 139) = `r sprintf("%.2f", near_heterogeneity_test$f_statistic)`,
000630  *p* **`r format_p(near_heterogeneity_test$p_raw)`**, and at chest,
000631  *F*(41, 150) = `r sprintf("%.2f", chest_heterogeneity_test$f_statistic)`,
000632  *p* **`r format_p(chest_heterogeneity_test$p_raw)`**. The pooled category
000633  ratios are therefore cross-site summaries rather than effects expected to be
000634  identical at every site.
000636  ### Site-specific context
000638  The interaction model can be read as
000640  $$
000641  \mu_{sc} = A \times R_c \times D_{sc},
000642  $$
000644  where (A) is the site-standardized indoor-electric mean, (R_c) is the
000645  category ratio to indoor electric light, and (D_{sc}) is a site's deviation
000646  from the site-standardized mean for that category. The following near-eye
000647  table keeps those two reference systems distinct. Its overall row and site
000648  rows all come from the heterogeneity model; the primary additive-model
000649  omnibus remains the preregistered inferential result reported above.
000850  [Site-context source data](../../artifacts/09_tables/H03/H03_site_context_estimands.csv)
000851  contain every site-category estimate, interval, support label, and adjusted
000852  site-deviation *p*-value.
000854  ### Descriptive fixed-effect R²
000856  The primary analysis has no random effects, so mixed-model marginal and
000857  conditional R² are not defined. Descriptive R² summaries are instead based on
000858  the accepted heterogeneity model, including its category-by-site interaction.
000924  Under the near-eye working quasi-deviance definition, overall R² was 0.522;
000925  category, site, and interaction allocations were 86.3%, 8.7%, and 5.0% of
000926  that fitted improvement. These are scale-dependent descriptions of the fitted
000927  mean structure, not causal shares of exposure variance.
000929  ## Model assessment
000931  Both population-mean fits converged without warnings, retained full design
000932  rank, and had finite positive-definite participant-cluster covariances. Their
000933  working distribution nevertheless does not reproduce important features of
000934  individual-hour data.
000973  Residual spread increases with the fitted mean, and residual correlation at a
000974  one-hour lag remains 0.478 near eye and 0.615 at chest. Participant clustering
000975  protects coefficient uncertainty against arbitrary dependence within a
000976  participant, but it does not whiten residuals or turn the working Tweedie
000977  family into a calibrated probability model.
000979  The discrepancy is clearest for zeros. Exact zeros comprise 27.8% of hours,
000980  whereas the working Tweedie relationship implies 85.2% near eye and 94.8% at
000981  chest. The model is therefore interpreted only as a log-mean model with
000982  participant-robust inference, not as an individual-hour prediction model or a
000983  model of zero probability.
000997  Deleting each available site and the five participants with the largest score
000998  contributions at each placement produced 27 refits. No category-omnibus
000999  decision changed, and 146 of 149 estimable non-reference category ratios retained the
001000  full-data ratio within the deletion-refit interval. The three exceptions were
001001  localized chest results: sleep darkness after excluding Kumasi, external
001002  light during sleep after excluding Munich, and sleep darkness after excluding
001003  one Kumasi participant.
001005  ## Sensitivity analyses
001088  The gap-timing-unaware dataset retained 17,616 near-eye and 19,220 chest
001089  hours; all seven category rows were stable at both placements. Excluding
001090  boundary hours or unsupported cells and changing the working variance power
001091  also left every omnibus decision unchanged. The sparse outdoor-electric
001092  contrast became non-estimable in three reduced samples and crossed the null
001093  direction under two alternatives, but no alternative established a non-null
001094  outdoor-electric contrast. The within/between-participant sensitivity changed
001095  the magnitude of the external-light-during-sleep contrast without reversing
001096  its direction.
001098  ### Common-sample placement comparison
001100  The common-sample models use 14,103 matched participant-hours from 111
001101  participants, 631 participant-days, and eight sites at both placements.
001102  Outdoor electric light has only 162 matched hours and fails its prespecified
001103  support rule. The remaining contrasts have the same broad direction at both
001104  placements. The identity line is a visual concordance reference, not an
001105  equivalence boundary.
001119  [Common-sample source data](../../artifacts/09_tables/H03/H03_paired_placement_comparison.csv)
001120  give both component intervals and exact category-specific support.
001122  ## Exploratory time-of-day context
001124  ::: {.callout-note title="Exploratory analysis"}
001125  This analysis is separate from the preregistered category inference. It uses
001126  one global cyclic time-of-day smooth plus sum-to-zero light-source and site
001127  deviations, participant curves, participant-day shifts, and a boundary-aware
001128  AR(1) correction. Light-source curves are ratios to the global daily smooth,
001129  not to indoor electric light, and no curve-wide test is made.
001130  :::
001132  The exploratory model uses the untransformed one-hour geometric melEDI outcome
001133  with a fixed-power Tweedie mean and log link. This targets the conditional
001134  arithmetic mean rather than a back-transformed mean of a log-transformed
001135  outcome, which is why its daytime curves are substantially higher than curves
001136  for a geometric-scale centre. Site, participant, and participant-day smooths
001137  remain in the fitted model but are set to zero in the displayed global and
001138  category curves.
001140  The temporal model is closer to the primary model's raw-outcome mean target,
001141  but the two displays are not numerically interchangeable. The primary scalar
001142  averages over all contributed times and standardizes sites on its fitted
001143  log-mean scale. The temporal curves condition on clock time and show only the
001144  global and light-source components. Their values therefore need not average to
001145  the primary category estimates.
001159  [Near-eye curve data](../../artifacts/11_source_data/H03/H03_reader_temporal_near_eye_curves.csv),
001160  [ratio data](../../artifacts/11_source_data/H03/H03_reader_temporal_near_eye_ratios.csv),
001161  and [clock-time support](../../artifacts/11_source_data/H03/H03_reader_temporal_near_eye_support.csv)
001162  contain the plotted values and untransformed melEDI.
001176  [Chest curve data](../../artifacts/11_source_data/H03/H03_reader_temporal_chest_curves.csv),
001177  [ratio data](../../artifacts/11_source_data/H03/H03_reader_temporal_chest_ratios.csv),
001178  and [clock-time support](../../artifacts/11_source_data/H03/H03_reader_temporal_chest_support.csv)
001179  contain the plotted values and untransformed melEDI.
001181  ### R² and variance allocation
001261  Global time of day received 60.4% of the near-eye and 58.3% of the chest point
001262  allocation. Light-source deviations received 24.2% and 26.4%, site deviations
001263  5.2% and 6.5%, participant curves 7.6% and 5.7%, and participant-day shifts
001264  2.5% and 3.1%. These are allocations of fitted linear-predictor variation,
001265  not independent or causal percentages of raw melEDI variance.
001267  ### Temporal model assessment
001325  Both final fits converged at full coefficient rank with positive-definite
001326  smoothing-parameter Hessians. The chest fit with zero AR correlation, used
001327  only to estimate the fixed AR value, did not converge; the final fixed-AR fit
001328  did. The global smooths used 9.36 and 9.47 effective degrees of freedom out of
001329  ten available, with deterministic k-indices of 0.890 and 0.884. The smooths
001330  are therefore close to their basis capacity, and no permutation *p*-value was
001331  computed.
001333  The AR adjustment reduces but does not eliminate short-lag dependence:
001334  lag-one residual correlations are 0.146 near eye and 0.144 at chest. Residual
001335  spread remains fitted-value dependent, and the working zero fractions of
001336  0.465 and 0.508 exceed the observed fractions of 0.278 and 0.277. These
001337  limitations confine the temporal analysis to descriptive conditional-mean
001338  context. The ribbons are pointwise 95% intervals; they are not simultaneous
001339  bands and do not support curve-wide significance claims.
001353  [Residual points](../../artifacts/11_source_data/H03/H03_reader_temporal_residual_points.csv),
001354  [residual bins](../../artifacts/11_source_data/H03/H03_reader_temporal_residual_bins.csv),
001355  [autocorrelations](../../artifacts/08_diagnostics/H03/H03_reader_temporal_residual_acf.csv),
001356  and [zero calibration](../../artifacts/11_source_data/H03/H03_reader_temporal_zero_calibration.csv)
001357  provide the exact diagnostic source values.
001359  ## Exploratory linear latitude replacement
001361  Latitude cannot be estimated alongside a saturated fixed-site term because
001362  each site has only one latitude. A separate exploratory model therefore
001363  replaces site with category-specific linear **absolute-latitude** slopes:
001370  The coefficient for each category is the multiplicative change in expected
001371  one-hour melEDI per 10° farther from the equator. Centering at the equal-site
001372  mean absolute latitude—39.2° near eye and 38.1° at chest—changes the
001373  intercepts but not the slopes. Uncertainty is clustered at site, not
001374  participant, because latitude varies only across nine near-eye and eight chest
001375  sites. Seven slope p-values are BH-adjusted within each placement.
001434  Near eye showed BH-labelled positive gradients for indoor electric light
001435  (ratio 1.197 per +10°, 95% CI 1.081–1.326), indoor daylight (1.253,
001436  1.131–1.388), outdoor daylight (1.354, 1.105–1.661), and sleep darkness
001437  (1.428, 1.076–1.895). Each retained a positive slope in all nine
001438  leave-one-site-out fits. At chest, only indoor electric light retained the BH
001439  label (1.142, 1.061–1.229) and remained positive in all eight omissions.
001441  Both latitude models converged without warnings and retained design rank
001442  14/14. Their site-cluster covariance ranks were necessarily limited to eight
001443  and seven because only nine and eight independent site clusters were
001444  available. Accordingly, the report gives coefficient-wise slope tests and no
001445  joint seven-slope Wald test.
001459  [Latitude result data](../../artifacts/09_tables/H03/H03_reader_latitude_category_slopes.csv),
001460  [site latitudes](../../artifacts/11_source_data/H03/H03_reader_latitude_site_support.csv),
001461  [leave-one-site-out fits](../../artifacts/08_diagnostics/H03/H03_reader_latitude_leave_one_site_out.csv),
001462  and [model diagnostics](../../artifacts/08_diagnostics/H03/H03_reader_latitude_model_diagnostics.csv)
001463  provide the exact exploratory outputs.
001465  These are ecological associations across a small number of sites. Absolute
001466  latitude may stand in for season, climate, built environment, behaviour, or
001467  other site differences; the analysis does not identify a causal latitude
001468  effect or show that latitude adequately replaces the supported categorical
001469  site heterogeneity.
001471  ## Interpretation
001473  Self-reported primary light source is strongly associated with measured
001474  one-hour personal light exposure. Daylight, especially outdoors, corresponds
001475  to substantially higher melEDI than indoor electric light, whereas display
001476  and sleep-environment categories correspond to lower values. The magnitude of
001477  these contrasts varies materially by site. Outdoor electric is especially
001478  sparse and heterogeneous, so its cross-site descriptive value depends
001479  strongly on whether a common category contrast is imposed.
001481  The result is observational. It does not show that changing a reported light
001482  source causes a particular exposure, predict a health outcome, or establish
001483  equivalence between near-eye and chest measurements. Participant-clustered
001484  inference, sensitivity analyses, and deletion checks support the overall
001485  category conclusion, while residual and zero-mass diagnostics limit claims
001486  about the working Tweedie distribution. The time-of-day models add useful
001487  descriptive context but remain separate from the preregistered inference. The
001488  linear absolute-latitude replacement identifies several exploratory ecological
001489  gradients, but the small number of sites and site-level confounding preclude a
001490  causal latitude interpretation.

===== audit/hypotheses/H03/H03_analysis_preparation.qmd =====
000204  ::: {.callout-note title="About this analysis record"}
000205  This page explains how verified one-hour melEDI records and normalized
000206  self-reported primary-light-source categories were turned into the H03
000207  results. The companion [H03 results
000208  report](../../../notebooks/hypotheses/H03.html) presents the scientific
000209  findings.
000211  Rendering this page recalculates only current file identities, schema and key
000212  checks, and lightweight descriptive summaries from the frozen H03 model
000213  frames. It reads model estimates, confidence intervals, omnibus tests,
000214  diagnostics, influence refits, sensitivity results, exploratory smooths, and
000215  latitude results from their recorded files. It does not fit or refit a model,
000216  calculate predictions, re-estimate residual autocorrelation, rerun a
000217  leave-one-site-out analysis, bootstrap, simulate data, or recalculate a
000218  variance allocation.
000219  :::
000221  ## Purpose
000223  H03 asks whether all seven prespecified self-reported primary-light-source
000224  categories are associated with measured one-hour personal light exposure.
000225  Near-eye melEDI is primary; chest melEDI is complementary. The central
000226  estimand is the site-standardized geometric expected melEDI for each category
000227  and its ratio to indoor electric light. “Site-standardized” means that each
000228  included site contributes the same weight on the fitted log-mean scale,
000229  followed by one back-transformation.
000231  This page identifies the data and code that produced the result, explains why
000232  each analytical operation was needed, and shows the recorded evidence used to
000233  check it. The analysis is observational and does not identify causal effects,
000234  health outcomes, or equivalence between measurement placements.
000236  ## Analysis map
000262  The arrows describe dependencies. They do not mean that this page repeats
000263  model estimation.
000265  ## What is calculated when this page opens
000288  ## 1. Verified inputs
000290  ### File identity
000292  Six inputs determine the current analysis frames and displays: two accepted
000293  one-hour outcome files, the normalized diary, the predefined alternative
000294  preparation, the category dictionary, and the submitted-manuscript site
000295  registry. Their complete SHA-256 identities were recorded before estimation.
000355  ### Schema, keys, missingness, and category overlap
000357  Outcome and diary records were joined one-to-one by site, participant,
000358  local date, and local clock hour. The primary model frames contain only
000359  admissible outcome hours with a non-missing primary-light-source category.
000360  Each retained record has one analysis category. The normalized diary also
000361  retains seven source-indicator flags: 7,109 near-eye hours and 8,012 chest
000362  hours had more than one true source flag, but the declared primary category
000363  was one of those true flags and remained the single mutually exclusive
000364  analysis label. Thus the overlap information was audited without duplicating
000365  an hour across categories.
000399  ### Outcome definition and exact zeros
000401  The outcome is the exact one-hour zero-aware geometric mean of melEDI in lux,
000402  calculated from at least 30 valid minutes. No logarithmic offset is added to
000403  this primary response. Exact zero is meaningful and remained in the
000404  quasi-Tweedie models: 4,977 of 17,935 near-eye hours (27.8%) and 5,409 of
000405  19,512 chest hours (27.7%).
000492  The exact binned counts are available as [figure source
000493  data](../../../artifacts/11_source_data/H03/H03_preparation_positive_response_distribution.csv).
000495  ## 2. Model frames and support
000497  ### Primary samples and repeated observations
000499  The primary near-eye frame contains 17,935 participant-hours from 140
000500  participants, 801 participant-days, and nine sites. The complementary chest
000501  frame contains 19,512 participant-hours from 151 participants, 880
000502  participant-days, and eight sites. Participant-hours repeat within
000503  participant-days and participants; therefore the nominal hour count is not
000504  treated as the number of independent units.
000569  The participant-day rows used for this descriptive table are available as
000570  [source data](../../../artifacts/11_source_data/H03/H03_preparation_participant_day_support.csv).
000572  ### Category coding and pooled estimability
000574  The seven registered labels are retained in their submitted order. Indoor
000575  electric light is the reference for every reported category ratio. A pooled
000576  category was predeclared estimable only if it had at least 200 hours, 20
000577  participants, and three sites. All seven categories pass this pooled rule in
000578  both placements; this does not imply that every site-specific cell is
000579  supported.
000682  The exact bar values are available as [figure source
000683  data](../../../artifacts/11_source_data/H03/H03_preparation_category_support.csv).
000685  ### Site-specific support
000687  A site-category contrast was reportable only when the category had at least
000688  20 hours, five participants, and five participants also observed in the
000689  indoor-electric reference cell at that site. Near eye has 53 supported and 10
000690  observed-but-sparse cells. Chest has 48 supported cells, seven
000691  observed-but-sparse cells, and eight cells with no observations; seven of the
000692  latter are from the near-eye-only site. Unsupported cells are not assigned
000693  site-specific estimates or p-values.
000817  The exact tile and marker values are available as [figure source
000818  data](../../../artifacts/11_source_data/H03/H03_preparation_site_category_support.csv).
000820  ### Local-time availability and sequence boundaries
000822  The exploratory temporal displays use the same participant-hours. A
000823  category-hour is called locally sparse when it is nonzero but has fewer than
000824  20 participant-hours, fewer than five participants, or fewer than three
000825  sites. No curve is displayed where there are no observations, and sparse
000826  times are marked without being treated as unsupported for the primary
000827  all-hours GLM.
000902  The exact tile and marker values are available as [figure source
000903  data](../../../artifacts/11_source_data/H03/H03_preparation_clock_category_support.csv).
000905  ## 3. Primary model construction and inference
000907  ### Additive population-mean model
000909  The prespecified primary question is tested with a population-mean GLM:
000915  `geo_medi_1h` is the one-hour zero-aware geometric melEDI in lux. The model
000916  uses a log link and a quasi-Tweedie variance function
000917  `Var(Y | X) = phi * mu^1.539919`. Site is a fixed factor with sum contrasts;
000918  light source is a fixed seven-level factor with indoor electric light as the
000919  treatment reference. The formula contains no random effect. Repeated
000920  participant-hours are addressed for inference with participant-clustered HC1
000921  sandwich covariance, finite denominator degrees of freedom equal to the
000922  number of participant clusters minus one, and two-sided 95% confidence
000923  intervals.
000925  Cluster-robust covariance protects coefficient uncertainty against unspecified
000926  within-participant dependence under its large-cluster assumptions; it does
000927  not remove serial structure from residuals or change the fitted conditional
000928  mean. Participant-day repetition and within-day temporal dependence are
000929  therefore also examined directly in diagnostics and sensitivity analyses.
000956  ### Omnibus test, category contrasts, and standardization
000958  The primary omnibus is a six-restriction Wald F test that all six
000959  non-reference light-source coefficients equal zero. The near-eye test is the
000960  preregistered primary inference; the chest test is complementary. The six
000961  non-reference ratios to indoor electric light form one Benjamini–Hochberg
000962  (BH) family within placement. Category means and ratios average sites equally
000963  on the fitted log-mean scale and are back-transformed once; this is a
000964  site-standardized geometric expectation, not the arithmetic mean of
000965  response-scale site predictions.
001011  ### Site heterogeneity
001013  Site heterogeneity is examined separately from the additive primary omnibus.
001014  The near-eye frame supports the literal interaction:
001020  The chest frame lacks all categories at the near-eye-only Munich site and is
001021  therefore represented by one coefficient for each observed site-category
001022  cell:
001028  The second expression is an equivalent saturated observed-cell
001029  parameterization, not a different scientific estimand. Its restriction matrix
001030  tests whether the observed cells can be represented additively by site plus
001031  light source. The models use the same log link, quasi-Tweedie variance,
001032  participant-clustered HC1 covariance, and geometric site standardization as
001033  the additive models.
001074  Both full architectures converged, were full rank, had finite robust
001075  covariance, retained all planned restriction ranks, and passed the
001076  predeclared condition-number and cluster-influence thresholds. The recorded
001077  heterogeneity tests were F(48, 139) = 80.0 near eye and F(41, 150) = 13.1 at
001078  chest, both p < 0.001. Supported site-specific estimates were emitted only
001079  after these checks passed.
001081  ### Multiplicity families
001110  ### Descriptive model performance
001112  Squared-error and working-deviance summaries were calculated from the full
001113  heterogeneity models, so the interaction contribution is retained. These
001114  quantities describe in-sample fit and are not inferential substitutes for the
001115  omnibus tests. The participant-hour version weights every retained hour
001116  equally; the participant-balanced version first gives each participant equal
001117  total weight. The working-deviance version uses the quasi-Tweedie
001118  mean-variance relationship. Because these loss functions differ, their
001119  R-squared values are not interchangeable.
001160  ## 4. Diagnostics and influence
001162  ### Convergence, rank, and robust covariance
001220  Both additive models converged without warnings, were full rank, and had
001221  finite positive-definite participant-clustered covariance. Large dispersion
001222  and residual spread show that the working variance is only an approximation.
001223  The near-eye and chest lag-1 Pearson residual correlations were 0.478 and
001224  0.615 within contiguous participant-day sequences. Clustered covariance
001225  allows within-participant residual dependence when estimating uncertainty,
001226  but does not make these correlations vanish.
001228  ### Zero mass and residual shape
001230  The fixed-power Tweedie working model substantially overpredicted exact zeros:
001231  the observed overall fractions were 0.278 near eye and 0.277 at chest,
001232  compared with working expectations of 0.852 and 0.948. This is a
001233  distributional limitation, not evidence that observed zeros should be
001234  removed. The analysis therefore treats the Tweedie family as a working
001235  mean-variance model, uses robust covariance, and avoids probability claims
001236  about the zero process.
001291  The [results report diagnostic
001292  figure](../../../notebooks/hypotheses/H03.html#diagnostics) shows individual
001293  residual points, binned residual summaries, autocorrelation, and observed
001294  versus working zero fractions. Its exact source data are stored in
001295  `artifacts/11_source_data/H03/`.
001297  ### Influence checks
001299  Every site was removed once within each placement. The five participants with
001300  the largest cluster-score contribution were also removed once within each
001301  placement. The site deletions examine dependence on a whole site; the
001302  participant deletions examine the most influential independent covariance
001303  clusters. These deterministic refits preserve the model family, formula,
001304  contrast definitions, and standardization rule.
001331  All 27 deletion fits are retained in
001332  [the refit registry](../../../artifacts/08_diagnostics/H03/H03_influence_refit_registry.csv);
001333  category-ratio changes are in
001334  [the category refits](../../../artifacts/09_tables/H03/H03_influence_category_refits.csv),
001335  and site-context changes are in
001336  [the site-context refits](../../../artifacts/09_tables/H03/H03_influence_site_context_refits.csv).
001337  The category omnibus decision did not change in any deletion.
001339  ## 5. Predefined sensitivities
001341  One sensitivity uses the **gap-timing-unaware dataset**. It still passes the
001342  general requirement of at least 50% valid minutes per hour and at least 80% valid hours per day,
001343  but the timing of the remaining missing observations is not used for an
001344  additional metric-specific adjustment. At this first explanation only, the
001345  primary dataset could be interpreted as a time-sensitive primary metric dataset.
001346  Hereafter it is called simply the primary dataset.
001348  Other predefined branches use the placement-matched common sample, exclude
001349  hours that span diary-state or measurement-context boundaries, exclude
001350  unsupported site-category cells, vary the quasi-Tweedie working power to 1.30
001351  and 1.80, and add participant-level category-proportion terms to separate
001352  within-participant from between-participant information. These branches do
001353  not replace the primary estimand.
001415  Every sensitivity omnibus was estimable and p < 0.001. The recorded
001416  [category-level sensitivity
001417  comparison](../../../artifacts/09_tables/H03/H03_sensitivity_comparison.csv)
001418  contains the ratios, 95% intervals, relative changes, direction checks, and
001419  whether the primary point estimate falls inside each sensitivity interval.
001421  ## 6. Exploratory temporal and latitude context
001423  ### Time-of-day GAMs
001425  The time-of-day analysis is exploratory and separate from the preregistered
001426  GLM inference. It models the raw one-hour geometric melEDI outcome with a
001427  fixed-power Tweedie mean, log link, global cyclic time smooth, sum-to-zero
001428  light-source and site deviation smooths, participant-specific smooths, and
001429  participant-day random intercepts:
001440  The sum-to-zero smooths add category- and site-specific deviations to the
001441  global cyclic time-of-day smooth; indoor electric light is not a smooth
001442  reference. `bam()` used fREML, discrete computation, cyclic knots at 0 and 24
001443  hours, and a second fit with an AR(1) working error parameter estimated from
001444  the preliminary standardized residual lag-1 correlation. Participant,
001445  participant-day, and site smooths remain in the fitted model but are excluded
001446  from the displayed population curves. The stored figure curves average sites
001447  equally and then omit unsupported time points from the display.
001529  The [near-eye](../../../artifacts/10_figures/H03/H03_reader_temporal_near_eye.png)
001530  and [chest](../../../artifacts/10_figures/H03/H03_reader_temporal_chest.png)
001531  figures show the fitted category curves, category-to-global ratios, and
001532  available observations by local hour. Model appraisal, basis dimension,
001533  concurvity, residual autocorrelation, zero calibration, coefficient
001534  covariance, penalty-scale variances, weighted R-squared, and variance
001535  allocation remain in their recorded diagnostic and table files.
001537  ### Absolute latitude
001539  The exploratory ecological replacement of site uses centered absolute
001540  latitude and its category interaction:
001546  It uses the same quasi-Tweedie working power and log link, but uncertainty is
001547  clustered by site because latitude varies only between sites. Seven
001548  category-specific slopes form one BH family within placement. Leave-one-site-
001549  out fits check direction and point-estimate range; they do not create a
001550  larger effective site sample.
001588  The small number of independent sites limits ecological precision. Latitude
001589  may stand in for season, climate, built environment, behaviour, or other site
001590  differences; no causal latitude interpretation is made.
001592  ## 7. Code and output provenance
001594  ### Executable code in analytical order
001628  Only the last row creates the lightweight descriptive inputs for this page.
001629  All inferential and exploratory model outputs were produced before rendering
001630  and are read by identity.
001632  ### Stored output chain
001660  The preparation-report manifest records the authoring source, byte-identical
001661  website source copy, rendered HTML, H03 scripts and tests, stored scientific
001662  outputs, figure source data, and environment identities. It deliberately
001663  excludes itself from its checksum inventory.

001671  and `gt`; the temporal environment additionally records `mgcv` and `gratia`.
