===== notebooks/hypotheses/H04.qmd =====
000368  The companion [H04 analysis preparation and
000369  provenance](../../audit/hypotheses/H04/H04_analysis_preparation.html) traces
000370  the verified inputs, multi-select transformation, model frames, estimands,
000371  diagnostics, sensitivities, temporal construction, and recorded output chain
000372  behind this report.
000374  ## Question
000376  The preregistered hypothesis was:
000378  > **H4: Hourly self-reported activity categories predict hourly geometric mean melanopic EDI.**
000380  This analysis asks whether five interpretable reported activity categories are
000381  associated with measured one-hour melEDI after accounting for study site. The
000382  categories are not mutually exclusive behavioural states: when an hour has
000383  several retained labels, its contribution is shared equally among them.
000385  ::: {.callout-note title="Answer in brief"}
000386  Reported activity category was associated with near-eye melEDI,
000387  *F*(`r near_primary_test$restrictions`, `r near_primary_test$denominator_df`) =
000388  `r sprintf("%.2f", near_primary_test$f_statistic)`, raw *p*
000389  **`r format_p(near_primary_test$p_raw)`**. Equal-site standardized estimates
000390  and participant-cluster-robust 95% confidence intervals were:
000392  - `r answer_category_line("home")`
000393  - `r answer_category_line("working_indoor")`
000394  - `r answer_category_line("outdoors")`
000395  - `r answer_category_line("road_vehicle")`
000396  - `r answer_category_line("sleeping")`
000398  Because site-by-activity heterogeneity was supported, these descriptive means
000399  and ratios come from the site-heterogeneity model; only the primary
000400  five-category omnibus comes from the additive model.
000402  Chest estimates showed the same broad ordering. All four named-versus-home
000403  contrasts retained their direction and classification in every prespecified
000404  sensitivity. Other/unspecified activity remains visible as a heterogeneous
000405  display category but supports no scientific claim. These are observational
000406  associations, not causal effects of changing activity.
000407  :::
000409  ## What was analysed
000411  Near-eye measurements are primary because they more closely represent light
000412  near the eyes. Chest measurements are complementary evidence. The placements
000413  are fitted separately, and similarity is not interpreted as equivalence.
000415  The response is the zero-aware geometric mean melEDI within each
000416  hour, including genuine zero values. Before the activity data were reshaped,
000417  active travel, outdoor work, and outdoor free time were combined into
000418  **Outdoors**. Duplicate collapsed labels were removed within each
000419  participant-hour. If Other was co-selected with a named category, only the
000420  named category or categories were retained; Other was kept when it was the
000421  sole selection. No diary free text was inspected or recoded.
000423  An hour with $k$ retained distinct labels contributes $k$ rows, each with
000424  weight $1/k$. The total analytical weight of every fitted participant-hour is
000425  therefore one. Hours with no retained category are not interpreted as “no
000426  activity” and are excluded from the category model.
000459  The primary model contains 126 participants, 724 participant-days, 16,526
000460  unique participant-hours, 17,266 long rows, and 16,526 effective weighted
000461  hours. The complementary model contains 150 participants, 875
000462  participant-days, 20,128 unique participant-hours, 21,071 long rows, and
000463  20,128 effective weighted hours.
000529  Weighted hours can be smaller than category rows because a multi-select
000530  hour's weight is divided among its retained labels. Long-row weighting is part
000531  of the estimand: replacing $1/k$ by unit weight would instead analyse reported
000532  selections and let multi-select hours contribute more.
000534  ## Model and estimand
000536  The population-mean model uses a quasi-Tweedie variance function with power
000537  1.539919, a log link, fixed site and activity terms, and the exact $1/k$ prior
000538  weights. Participant-clustered HC1 covariance encompasses concurrent category
000539  memberships and all longitudinal observations from the same participant.
000540  Finite-cluster *t* and *F* reference distributions use the number of
000541  participant clusters minus one as denominator degrees of freedom.
000543  The primary equality test compares Sleeping, At home, vehicle or
000544  public-transport travel, indoor or home working, and Outdoors. The fitted
000545  Other coefficient remains unrestricted and does not enter that four-row joint
000546  restriction. A full six-category omnibus is reported secondarily.
000579  Category means are standardized to the sites observed for that placement.
000580  Every site contributes equally on the fitted log-mean scale, followed by one
000581  back-transformation. Thus an estimate is the geometric mean of the fitted
000582  site-specific means, not an observation-weighted arithmetic mean. At home is
000583  the reader-facing comparison reference.
000585  The estimand is average melEDI associated with each reported collapsed
000586  activity category, with an hour's contribution shared across concurrently
000587  reported categories. It is not a transition effect, an individual-specific
000588  effect, or a causal contrast between mutually exclusive behaviours.
000590  ## Category summaries from the heterogeneity model
000592  The additive primary model forces every site to share one activity ratio,
000593  whereas the site-heterogeneity model estimates site-specific category means
000594  before giving every observed site equal weight on the fitted log-mean scale.
000595  The preregistered additive-model omnibus remains the primary
000596  inferential test, but the descriptive means, ratios, differences, confidence
000597  intervals, and site summaries below use the less restrictive heterogeneity
000598  model. Other/unspecified activity is the sole exception: it is excluded from
000599  the heterogeneity model and remains visible only as a quiet additive-model
000600  display estimate.
000670  Near eye, the heterogeneity-model equal-site standardized mean was 76.4 lx at
000671  home, 198.9 lx during office or home working, 714.2 lx outdoors, 331.2 lx
000672  during vehicle or public-transport travel, and 4.3 lx while sleeping.
000673  Relative to At home, the four named ratios in that order were 2.604, 9.354,
000674  4.338, and 0.056. Chest means in the same category order were 78.0, 202.9,
000675  998.9, 512.9, and 4.7 lx; the corresponding ratios were 2.600, 12.800, 6.573,
000676  and 0.060.
000741  [Estimate data](../../artifacts/09_tables/H04/H04_reader_category_estimands.csv)
000742  and [figure source data](../../artifacts/11_source_data/H04/H04_reader_heterogeneity_category_figure.csv)
000743  retain the unrounded values.
000745  ### Omnibus tests
000787  The complementary chest primary test also rejected equality of the five named
000788  categories, *F*(4, 149) = `r sprintf("%.2f", chest_primary_test$f_statistic)`,
000789  raw *p* `r format_p(chest_primary_test$p_raw)`. This separate result supports
000790  the broad placement consistency; it is not an independent replication or an
000791  equivalence test.
000793  ## Site-specific context
000795  The additive population-mean omnibus remains the primary inferential analysis.
000796  The supported heterogeneity model supplies the descriptive category summaries
000797  above and tests whether the five named-category associations vary across
000798  sites. It excludes Other and uses only observed, supported cells; an
000799  unsupported site-category contrast is labelled non-estimable rather than
000800  forced into the model.
000857  Site heterogeneity was supported near eye, *F*(32, 125) = 8.03, raw *p*
000858  <0.001, and at chest, *F*(28, 149) = 11.62, raw *p* <0.001. This indicates
000859  that activity-associated means vary among sites; it is not a basis for
000860  post-hoc site ranking. The complete site-deviation families contain 44
000861  near-eye and 40 chest comparisons, of which 17 and 9 carry the BH-adjusted
000862  label.
001037  [Site-estimate data](../../artifacts/09_tables/H04/H04_site_activity_estimands.csv)
001038  and [figure source data](../../artifacts/11_source_data/H04/H04_site_activity_figure.csv)
001039  contain every supported and non-estimable cell.
001041  ### Descriptive fixed-effect R²
001043  The primary analysis has no random effects, so mixed-model marginal and
001044  conditional R² are not defined. The following point summaries instead use the
001045  site-by-activity heterogeneity architecture and preserve the $1/k$
001046  hour weights.
001112  Under the near-eye working quasi-deviance definition, overall R² was
001113  `r sprintf("%.3f", heterogeneity_r_squared$overall_r_squared[heterogeneity_r_squared$placement == "Near-eye" & heterogeneity_r_squared$loss_basis == "model_deviance"])`;
001114  activity, site, and interaction allocations were
001115  `r sprintf("%.1f%%", heterogeneity_r_squared$category_shapley_share_percent[heterogeneity_r_squared$placement == "Near-eye" & heterogeneity_r_squared$loss_basis == "model_deviance"])`,
001116  `r sprintf("%.1f%%", heterogeneity_r_squared$site_shapley_share_percent[heterogeneity_r_squared$placement == "Near-eye" & heterogeneity_r_squared$loss_basis == "model_deviance"])`,
001117  and
001118  `r sprintf("%.1f%%", heterogeneity_r_squared$interaction_share_percent[heterogeneity_r_squared$placement == "Near-eye" & heterogeneity_r_squared$loss_basis == "model_deviance"])`
001119  of that fitted improvement. They are scale-dependent descriptions of the
001120  fitted mean structure, not causal shares of exposure variance.
001122  ## Model assessment
001182  Both fits converged at full design rank, all registered robust restrictions
001183  were estimable, and no participant crossed the predeclared influence limits.
001184  The mean models are nevertheless **acceptable with limitation**. Exact zeros
001185  comprise 28.9% of near-eye and 29.4% of chest hours, whereas the corresponding
001186  working Tweedie zero fractions are 90.3% and 93.5%. The quasi mean model
001187  retains observed zeros but is not a separate model of zero probability.
001188  Residual spread changes with fitted magnitude, and lag-one residual
001189  correlations remain 0.445 and 0.498. Participant clustering is the basis for
001190  inference across the full longitudinal record; it does not make those model
001191  discrepancies disappear.
001204  [Residual points](../../artifacts/11_source_data/H04/H04_reader_primary_residual_points.csv),
001205  [residual bins](../../artifacts/11_source_data/H04/H04_reader_primary_residual_bins.csv),
001206  [autocorrelations](../../artifacts/08_diagnostics/H04/H04_reader_primary_residual_acf.csv),
001207  and [zero calibration](../../artifacts/11_source_data/H04/H04_reader_primary_zero_calibration.csv)
001208  provide the exact plotted values.
001210  ## Sensitivity analyses
001212  The predefined sensitivity set evaluates exactly-one-category hours;
001213  retention of co-selected Other as a fractionally weighted category; exclusion
001214  of Other-only hours; unit-weight long rows; gap-timing-unaware data; the exact
001215  placement-matched common sample; alternative working powers 1.30 and 1.80;
001216  and the complementary chest placement.
001218  The **gap-timing-unaware dataset** still passed the general per-hour and
001219  per-day coverage requirements. The term means that the timing of remaining
001220  missing observations is not used for an additional metric-specific
001221  adjustment; it does not mean that gaps or coverage were ignored.
001300  All four named contrasts were stable in every sensitivity at both placements.
001301  The deletion audit also removed each site and each of the five highest-score
001302  participants in turn; no primary decision changed. These checks support the
001303  direction and broad magnitude ordering, but they do not remove the model
001304  limitations or upgrade the observational claim.
001306  ### Common-sample placement comparison
001308  The exact common frame contains 110 participants, 625 participant-days,
001309  14,308 unique participant-hours, 15,001 long rows, and 14,308 effective
001310  weighted hours at each placement.
001323  [Common-sample estimates](../../artifacts/09_tables/H04/H04_paired_placement_estimands.csv)
001324  and [figure source data](../../artifacts/11_source_data/H04/H04_paired_placement_figure.csv)
001325  retain the exact placement-specific values. The display uses identical
001326  participant-hour keys but separately fitted models; it supplies no unplanned
001327  placement-difference *p*-value.
001329  ## Exploratory time-of-day context
001331  ::: {.callout-note title="Exploratory analysis"}
001332  This analysis is separate from the preregistered category inference. It uses
001333  one cyclic global time-of-day smooth plus thin-plate sum-to-zero activity and
001334  site deviations, participant curves, participant-day shifts, and a
001335  boundary-aware AR(1) correction. Activity curves are also shown as ratios to
001336  the global daily smooth, not to At home, and no curve-wide test is made.
001337  :::
001339  The exploratory model retains the same activity-long rows and $1/k$ weights.
001340  It contains a cyclic global time smooth, thin-plate sum-to-zero activity and
001341  site smooths, participant-specific time smooths, and participant-day random
001342  intercepts. Only the global smooth is cyclic. Activity and site deviations
001343  may therefore separate at midnight; the open endpoints make this visible and
001344  no line wraps from 24:00 to 00:00.
001346  Activity-specific autoregressive runs restart at participant-day, activity,
001347  repeated-hour, non-consecutive-hour, and gap boundaries. Consequently,
001348  concurrent long rows from the same hour never become lag neighbours.
001407  The activity smooth improved AIC by 3,215 near eye and 4,984 at chest, while
001408  deviance explained increased from 81.8% to 84.8% and from 79.5% to 83.2%.
001409  This supports retaining the profiles as descriptive context, not as a second
001410  primary activity test.
001412  The activity curves use a qualitative palette distinct from the light-source
001413  colours used elsewhere in this report series, so colour does not imply a
001414  mapping between different category systems.
001438  [Near-eye curves](../../artifacts/11_source_data/H04/H04_reader_temporal_near_eye_curves.csv),
001439  [near-eye activity/global ratios](../../artifacts/11_source_data/H04/H04_reader_temporal_near_eye_ratios.csv),
001440  [near-eye global curve](../../artifacts/11_source_data/H04/H04_reader_temporal_near_eye_global.csv),
001441  and [near-eye support](../../artifacts/11_source_data/H04/H04_reader_temporal_near_eye_support.csv)
001442  contain the primary plotted values. The corresponding
001443  [chest curves](../../artifacts/11_source_data/H04/H04_reader_temporal_chest_curves.csv),
001444  [ratios](../../artifacts/11_source_data/H04/H04_reader_temporal_chest_ratios.csv),
001445  [global curve](../../artifacts/11_source_data/H04/H04_reader_temporal_chest_global.csv),
001446  and [support](../../artifacts/11_source_data/H04/H04_reader_temporal_chest_support.csv)
001447  provide the complementary placement values.
001449  ### Temporal linear-predictor variance allocation
001484  Global time of day received 52.5% of the near-eye and 50.2% of the chest
001485  point allocation. Activity deviations received 30.7% and 32.6%, site
001486  deviations 5.6% and 6.5%, participant curves 8.4% and 7.1%, and
001487  participant-day shifts 2.8% and 3.5%. These are allocations of fitted
001488  linear-predictor variation, not independent or causal percentages of raw
001489  melEDI variance.
001491  ### Temporal model assessment
001538  Both temporal fits converged at full coefficient rank with positive-definite
001539  smoothing-parameter Hessians and no final warnings. Their minimum finite
001540  k-indices were 0.872 and 0.863. Residual lag-one correlations remained 0.176
001541  and 0.167, and 41 near-eye plus 40 chest clock/category cells were locally
001542  sparse. The non-cyclic deviations can show large endpoint ratios.
001543  The working zero fractions of 0.452 and 0.502 exceed the observed fractions
001544  of 0.289 and 0.294. The profiles are therefore assessed **acceptable with
001545  limitation**.
001547  For each time-by-activity profile, sites are averaged equally on the fitted
001548  log-mean scale after participant and participant-day contributions are set to
001549  zero. Uncertainty uses the fitted coefficient covariance from
001550  `stats::vcov(fit, unconditional = TRUE)`, with the fitted `Vp` matrix only as
001551  an error fallback, followed by one back-transformation. Ribbons are
001552  model-based pointwise 95% intervals. They are not simultaneous bands; no
001553  time-specific test, significant period, or curve-wide inference is reported.
001554  No resampling enters the displayed estimates or intervals.
001567  [Residual points](../../artifacts/11_source_data/H04/H04_reader_temporal_residual_points.csv),
001568  [residual bins](../../artifacts/11_source_data/H04/H04_reader_temporal_residual_bins.csv),
001569  [activity-run autocorrelations](../../artifacts/08_diagnostics/H04/H04_reader_temporal_residual_acf.csv),
001570  and [zero calibration](../../artifacts/11_source_data/H04/H04_reader_temporal_zero_calibration.csv)
001571  provide the exact diagnostic source values.
001573  ## Interpretation
001575  Reported activity is strongly associated with one-hour personal light
001576  exposure. Sleeping corresponds to markedly lower melEDI than At home, whereas
001577  vehicle or public-transport travel, indoor or home working, and especially
001578  Outdoors correspond to higher values. The ordering is similar at chest, and
001579  it persists in the exact placement-matched sample and across the predefined
001580  sensitivity set. Activity-associated means also vary materially by site.
001582  The result remains observational. Fractional membership is a transparent way
001583  to preserve one total contribution per participant-hour; it does not make
001584  simultaneously reported labels mutually exclusive or remove ambiguity in
001585  self-report timing. Other/unspecified activity is heterogeneous and supports
001586  no substantive interpretation. Equal-site standardization describes the
001587  observed site set and does not automatically generalize to other populations.
001588  Participant-clustered inference accommodates within-hour membership and the
001589  longitudinal record under the stated mean model, but it does not cure residual
001590  serial dependence, fitted-value-dependent spread, sparse site cells, or the
001591  model's limited representation of exact zeros. The time-of-day profiles add
001592  descriptive context only, and the separate sensor fits do not establish
001593  placement equivalence.

===== audit/hypotheses/H04/H04_analysis_preparation.qmd =====
000238  ::: {.callout-note title="About this analysis record"}
000239  This page explains how verified one-hour melEDI records and multi-select
000240  self-reported activity flags were turned into the H04 results. The companion
000241  [H04 results report](../../../notebooks/hypotheses/H04.html) presents the
000242  scientific findings.
000244  Rendering this page recalculates only current file identities and lightweight
000245  descriptive summaries from frozen, checksummed H04 model frames and source
000246  tables. It does not fit or refit a model, calculate a model prediction,
000247  re-estimate residual dependence, rerun a deletion analysis, bootstrap,
000248  simulate data, or recalculate a variance allocation. Model estimates,
000249  confidence intervals, tests, diagnostics, sensitivities, and exploratory
000250  smooths are read from their recorded files.
000251  :::
000253  ## Purpose
000255  H04 asks whether hourly self-reported activity categories are associated with
000256  hourly geometric mean melanopic equivalent daylight illuminance (melEDI).
000257  Near-eye melEDI is primary; chest melEDI is complementary. The five
000258  interpretable categories are At home, Office/home working, Outdoors,
000259  Vehicle/public transport, and Sleeping. Other/unspecified activity remains in
000260  the additive mean model but is heterogeneous and supports no substantive
000261  claim.
000263  Activity categories are not mutually exclusive behavioural states. When an
000264  hour contains several retained labels, that hour contributes $1/k$ to each of
000265  its $k$ labels. The central category estimand is therefore average melEDI
000266  associated with each reported collapsed activity category, with the hour's
000267  contribution shared across concurrent reports. It is not a causal effect.
000269  This page identifies the data and code that produced the result, explains why
000270  each operation was needed, and shows the recorded evidence used to check it.
000272  ## Analysis map
000298  The arrows describe data and fitted-output dependencies. They do not mean
000299  that this page repeats model estimation.
000301  ## What is calculated when this page opens
000324  ## 1. Verified inputs
000326  ### File identity
000328  Five files determine the scientific H04 frames and displays: the normalized
000329  activity diary, the primary near-eye and complementary chest one-hour
000330  outcomes, the gap-timing-unaware one-hour outcomes, and the submitted site
000331  registry. Their complete SHA-256 identities were recorded before fitting.
000332  The shared near-eye and chest objects were subsequently repinned when
000333  metric-setting provenance was extended. An R 4.6.1 rebuild established
000334  zero-tolerance equality of all values in all 14 downstream H04 analysis
000335  frames; the accepted model objects and results therefore remain controlling
000336  and no model was refit.
000419  ### Activity transformation
000421  The diary transformation is outcome-blind. It first combines the three
000422  outdoor flags—travel by bicycle/on foot, working outdoors, and free time
000423  outdoors—into **Outdoors**. It then deduplicates collapsed labels within an
000424  hour. If Other is selected with a named category, only the named category or
000425  categories are retained; Other is retained only when it is the sole selected
000426  label. Free text is neither inspected nor recoded.
000428  After these operations, the retained distinct labels are pivoted to long
000429  format. If hour $h$ contains $k_h$ labels, each long row receives
000431  $$
000432  w_{hc}=\frac{1}{k_h}, \qquad \sum_{c=1}^{k_h} w_{hc}=1.
000433  $$
000435  Thus an original hour contributes one total unit, whether it supplies one,
000436  two, or three activity memberships. Using unweighted long rows would instead
000437  estimate a selection-level association in which multi-select hours count more
000438  than single-select hours.
000463  ### Frozen-frame integrity
000465  The primary long frames contain one row for each distinct retained
000466  hour/activity membership. A repeated outcome is therefore intentional when an
000467  hour has several labels; it is not a repeated independent observation. The
000468  integrity checks group those rows back to their original hour and verify the
000469  response, participant, participant-day, $k$, and total weight.
000519  The source diary contained co-selected Other in 67 retained near-eye hours
000520  and 119 retained chest hours. In each case the named categories were retained
000521  and no Other row was added. Other was the sole retained category in 391
000522  near-eye and 631 chest hours.
000524  ## 2. Model frames and support
000526  ### Sample flow
000528  Hours with no retained category are not interpreted as “no activity.” They
000529  remain outside the category model and are counted separately below.
000557  The primary near-eye frame contains 16,526 unique participant-hours, 17,266
000558  long rows, 126 participants, 724 participant-days, and nine sites. The chest
000559  frame contains 20,128 unique participant-hours, 21,071 long rows, 150
000560  participants, 875 participant-days, and eight sites. The effective weighted
000561  hours equal the unique hours exactly.
000563  ### Number of retained labels per hour
000596  Most fitted hours have one retained label. The 716 near-eye and 918 chest
000597  multi-select hours retain their original outcome once but distribute their
000598  total analytical weight across two or three labels.
000600  ### Participant-day support
000648  The participant-day rows behind this summary are available as [source
000649  data](../../../artifacts/11_source_data/H04/H04_preparation_participant_day_support.csv).
000651  ### Outcome definition and exact zeros
000653  The response is the exact one-hour zero-aware geometric mean melEDI in lux,
000654  calculated from at least 30 valid minutes. No logarithmic offset is added to
000655  the primary response. Exact zero remains in every fitted mean model.
000742  The exact weighted bin values are available as [figure source
000743  data](../../../artifacts/11_source_data/H04/H04_preparation_positive_response_distribution.csv).
000745  ### Category support
000781  Other/unspecified is shown separately and quietly. Its support does not make
000782  it a coherent scientific category.
000851  The exact bar values are available as [figure source
000852  data](../../../artifacts/11_source_data/H04/H04_preparation_category_support.csv).
000854  ### Site-specific support
000856  A site/category result is reportable only when that cell contains at least 20
000857  unique participant-hours, five participants, and five participants who also
000858  reported At home at that site. The primary near-eye frame contains 51
000859  supported and three observed-but-sparse cells; chest contains 47 supported and
000860  one observed-but-sparse cell. No absent site/category cell is invented.
000970  The exact tile and marker values are available as [figure source
000971  data](../../../artifacts/11_source_data/H04/H04_preparation_site_category_support.csv).
000973  ### Local-time availability
000975  The exploratory temporal models use the same fractionally weighted long rows.
000976  A category/hour cell is locally sparse when it is observed but has fewer than
000977  20 unique participant-hours or fewer than five participants. Locally sparse
000978  times remain fitted but are marked as such in displays; no curve is drawn
000979  where there is no observation.
001050  The exact tile and marker values are available as [figure source
001051  data](../../../artifacts/11_source_data/H04/H04_preparation_clock_category_support.csv).
001053  ## 3. Primary model construction and inference
001055  ### Additive population-mean model
001057  The primary question is tested with the exact Wilkinson formula:
001063  `geo_medi_1h` is the one-hour zero-aware geometric melEDI in lux. The model
001064  uses a log link and the quasi-Tweedie working variance
001065  $\operatorname{Var}(Y\mid X)=\phi\mu^{1.539919}$. Site is a fixed factor with
001066  sum contrasts; activity is a fixed six-level factor with At home as the
001067  treatment reference. Each long row receives its exact `analysis_weight = 1/k`.
001069  Repeated memberships and longitudinal observations are addressed for
001070  inference with participant-clustered HC1 sandwich covariance. Denominator
001071  degrees of freedom equal the number of participant clusters minus one, and
001072  reported confidence intervals are two-sided 95% intervals. The formula
001073  contains no random effect.
001101  Cluster-robust covariance protects coefficient uncertainty against
001102  unspecified within-participant dependence under its large-cluster assumptions.
001103  It does not remove serial structure from residuals or make the working
001104  Tweedie distribution a calibrated model of the exact-zero process.
001106  ### Exact formulas and robust restrictions
001108  The comparison formulas below are recorded for construction and rank checks.
001109  The category decisions use robust Wald restrictions on the full fitted model;
001110  they are not likelihood-ratio tests against the comparison formula.
001137  The primary restriction tests equality of the five named category means with
001138  four independent rows while leaving the Other coefficient unrestricted. The
001139  secondary restriction adds Other and tests equality of all six categories
001140  with five rows.
001171  The near-eye primary test is $F(4,125)=83.59$, raw $p<0.001$. The separate
001172  chest result is $F(4,149)=93.31$, raw $p<0.001$.
001174  ### Estimands, references, and standardization
001176  For a category and each site observed in a placement, a design row is
001177  constructed on the fitted log-mean scale. Sites contribute equal weight, the
001178  design rows are averaged, and one exponential back-transformation is applied.
001179  This is a geometric equal-site expectation, not an arithmetic mean of
001180  response-scale site predictions.
001206  At home is the reader-facing and model reference. The four named-versus-home
001207  ratios form one complete BH family within each placement. Other is not a
001208  member of that family.
001210  ### Site heterogeneity
001212  Site heterogeneity is examined separately from the additive primary omnibus
001213  with the five interpretable categories:
001219  Other-only hours are omitted from this model. The near-eye and chest designs
001220  are full rank and do not force an Other interaction in a sparse site. Site
001221  specific outputs are emitted only for cells that pass the reporting support
001222  rule.
001290  The supported heterogeneity tests were $F(32,125)=8.03$ near eye and
001291  $F(28,149)=11.62$ at chest, both raw $p<0.001$. These results justify using
001292  the heterogeneity models for the descriptive named-category summaries. They
001293  do not support post-hoc site ranking.
001295  ### Multiplicity families
001322  ## 4. Diagnostics and influence
001324  ### Convergence, rank, covariance, and residual structure
001326  Weighted long-row residual contributions are combined back to one value per
001327  original hour before the residual-shape, calibration, zero-mass, and serial
001328  dependence summaries are formed. Concurrent memberships are therefore not
001329  treated as separate diagnostic observations.
001384  Both additive models converged without warnings, were full rank, and retained
001385  finite positive-definite participant-clustered covariance. Large dispersion,
001386  fitted-value-dependent spread, and residual lag-one correlations of 0.445 and
001387  0.498 remain visible. The mean models are therefore acceptable with
001388  limitation, not calibrated generative models.
001390  ### Exact-zero calibration
001421  The working Tweedie model substantially overpredicts exact zeros. Observed
001422  zeros remain in the outcome, while probability claims about the zero process
001423  are avoided.
001446  ### Diagnostic interpretation
001471  ### Deterministic influence checks
001473  Every site was removed once within each placement. The five participants with
001474  the largest cluster-score contribution were also removed once within each
001475  placement. The model family, formula, weight definition, contrast direction,
001476  and equal-site standardization rule were preserved.
001517  None of the 27 deletion refits changed the five-category omnibus decision.
001518  Category-ratio changes and intervals remain in the recorded influence table.
001520  ## 5. Predefined sensitivities
001522  The predefined branches change one aspect of the frame, weight, working
001523  variance, or placement while preserving the category estimand whenever that
001524  is possible. Exactly-one-category hours remove concurrent memberships.
001525  Retaining co-selected Other deliberately reverses the primary specificity
001526  rule. Excluding Other-only hours removes that heterogeneous category.
001527  Unweighted long rows change the estimand by allowing multi-select hours to
001528  contribute more than one total unit. The placement-matched branch fits each
001529  sensor separately on identical participant-hours.
001531  The **gap-timing-unaware dataset** still requires at least 50% valid minutes
001532  per hour and at least 80% valid hours per day, but the timing of the remaining
001533  missing observations is not used for an additional metric-specific
001534  adjustment. At this first explanation only, the primary dataset could be
001535  interpreted as a time-sensitive primary metric dataset; hereafter it is called
001536  the primary dataset.
001613  Every sensitivity omnibus was estimable and raw $p<0.001$. The recorded
001614  [category-level sensitivity
001615  comparison](../../../artifacts/09_tables/H04/H04_sensitivity_comparison.csv)
001616  contains the ratios, 95% intervals, relative changes, direction checks, and
001617  whether the primary ratio lies inside each sensitivity interval.
001619  ## 6. Exploratory time-of-day context
001621  ### Weighted activity GAMs
001623  The exploratory time-of-day analysis is separate from the primary GLM
001624  inference. It uses the same long rows and exact $1/k$ weights, with this exact
001625  formula:
001636  The global time smooth uses a cyclic cubic basis and joins across midnight.
001637  The activity and site sum-to-zero deviations use the approved thin-plate
001638  marginal basis and are not cyclic, so their fitted endpoints may separate at
001639  midnight. Participant-specific smooths and participant-day random intercepts
001640  remain in the fit. Activity-specific residual runs ensure that concurrent
001641  memberships at one timestamp never become lag neighbours.
001663  `mgcv::bam()` used a fixed-power Tweedie mean with log link, fREML, discrete
001664  computation, one R thread, and cyclic knots at 0 and 24 hours. A preliminary
001665  zero-correlation fit supplied the boundary-aware lag-one residual correlation;
001666  the final model used that value as its working AR(1) parameter.
001759  Activity deviations improved AIC by 3,215 near eye and 4,984 at chest, while
001760  deviance explained increased from 81.8% to 84.8% and from 79.5% to 83.2%.
001761  Both fits are retained as exploratory context.
001801  The models converged at full coefficient rank with positive smoothing
001802  Hessians. Residual dependence, 41 near-eye and 40 chest locally sparse
001803  clock/activity cells, the working zero-mass mismatch, and non-cyclic
001804  activity/site endpoints remain explicit limitations.
001806  ### Population curves and uncertainty
001808  Displayed time/activity curves set participant and participant-day
001809  contributions to zero, evaluate every site observed in that placement, average
001810  those design rows equally on the fitted log-mean scale, and back-transform
001811  once. Their ribbons are model-based pointwise 95% intervals from the fitted
001812  coefficient covariance.
001839  No bootstrap or simulation enters these curves. The intervals are not
001840  simultaneous bands, and no time-specific significant period or curve-wide
001841  inference is reported.
001885  The [near-eye](../../../artifacts/10_figures/H04/H04_temporal_near_eye.png)
001886  and [chest](../../../artifacts/10_figures/H04/H04_temporal_chest.png) results
001887  figures show the fitted activity curves, activity-to-global ratios, and
001888  effective weighted support. Their exact curve and support values are stored
001889  under `artifacts/11_source_data/H04/`.
001891  ## 7. Code and output provenance
001893  ### Executable code in analytical order
001925  Only the last row creates lightweight descriptive inputs for this page. All
001926  inferential and exploratory model outputs were produced before rendering and
001927  are read by identity.
001929  ### Stored output chain
001957  The preparation-report manifest records this authoring source, the
001958  byte-identical website source copy, the rendered HTML, H04 scripts and tests,
001959  stored scientific outputs, paired source data, and environment identities. It
001960  deliberately excludes itself from its checksum inventory.
001962  ## 8. Reproduction and environment
001964  ### Environment identity
001966  The scientific analysis and this companion use R 4.6.1 and the synchronized
001967  project library. The stored environment table records the primary GLM and
001968  reporting packages; the same library supplies `mgcv` and `gratia` for the
001969  exploratory temporal analysis.
002009  ### Examination order
002011  For a bounded verification that does not recompute a model:
002013  1. verify the five scientific input identities in
002014     `artifacts/06_model_data/H04/H04_input_audit.csv`;
002015  2. inspect `H04_model_frames.rds`, `H04_model_frame_index.csv`, and the seven

===== notebooks/hypotheses/H05.qmd =====
000800  ## Question
000802  The preregistered hypothesis was:
000804  > “H5: LEBA questionnaire factors correlate with selected personal light exposure metrics.”
000806  The Light Exposure Behaviour Assessment (LEBA) asks about habitual
000807  light-related behaviours. This analysis tests whether four LEBA factor scores
000808  are associated with 17 personal light-exposure metrics after accounting for
000809  study site. Higher scores mean that the behaviour described by a factor was
000810  reported more often.
000812  ::: {.callout-note title="Answer in brief"}
000813  None of the 68 primary near-eye associations remained statistically supported
000814  after the complete Benjamini--Hochberg correction. The two largest positive F2
000815  estimates were ratios of
000816  `r format_number(near_duration$estimate_practical_per_sd)` (95% CI
000817  `r format_number(near_duration$conf_low_practical_per_sd)`–`r format_number(near_duration$conf_high_practical_per_sd)`)
000818  for time above 1,000 lx melEDI and
000819  `r format_number(near_dose$estimate_practical_per_sd)` (95% CI
000820  `r format_number(near_dose$conf_low_practical_per_sd)`–`r format_number(near_dose$conf_high_practical_per_sd)`)
000821  for melEDI dose; both had BH-adjusted *p* =
000822  `r format_p(near_duration$p_adjusted)` and therefore remain descriptive rather
000823  than multiplicity-retained. Complementary chest results likewise retained
000824  zero of 68 associations but reproduced the positive direction of both leading
000825  F2 estimates, and the gap-timing-unaware dataset left the family-wide
000826  conclusion unchanged; its exact 478-day MDER placement comparison retained all
000827  four directions with overlapping component intervals. Uncertainty remains:
000828  several small estimates changed
000829  direction when a site was omitted, the small complementary chest MDER
000830  coefficients were sensitive to upper-tail observations but retained
000831  zero-crossing sensitivity intervals, and the four planned sleep-environment
000832  models are unfit for H05 inference under this response definition and model
000833  structure.
000834  :::
000836  The exact data preparation, model construction, diagnostic sequence, and
000837  output lineage are documented in the companion
000838  [H05 analysis preparation and provenance](../../audit/hypotheses/H05/H05_analysis_preparation.html).
000840  ## What was analysed
000842  Near-eye measurements are primary because they more closely represent light
000843  near the eyes. All available chest measurements are analysed separately as
000844  complementary evidence. A common-sample analysis then fits near-eye and chest
000845  models to the same participants and participant-days, so placement differences
000846  are not confused with differences in who contributed data. The placements are
000847  never pooled as independent observations.
000849  A predefined sensitivity uses the **gap-timing-unaware dataset**. It still
000850  passed the general 50%-per-hour and 80%-per-day coverage rules. The term means
000851  that the timing of the remaining missing observations is not used for an
000852  additional metric-specific adjustment; it does not mean that gaps,
000853  missingness, or coverage were ignored. For this contrast only, the approved
000854  primary preparation could be interpreted as a **time-sensitive primary metric
000855  dataset**, because it uses the timing of remaining missing observations where
000856  that timing is relevant to the metric. Below, it is called simply the primary
000857  dataset.
000879  F2 includes one reverse-coded item; F3--F5 are sums of their ordered items.
000880  Every factor score was complete. Within each exact model sample, the score was
000881  centred and expressed both per raw point and per participant-level standard
000882  deviation. The report uses the latter so estimates can be compared across the
000883  four differently ranged factor scores.
000885  ### Deviations and operational clarifications
000887  The preregistration gives the broad model `Metric ~ LEBA + (1|Site)` but does
000888  not fully define the repeated-measure structure, response families, metric
000889  set, multiplicity family, clock handling, or placement roles. The following
000890  choices make those elements explicit.
000983  For MDER, duplicated local minutes on a daylight-saving fall-back day are
000984  averaged channel by channel before their ratio is formed; absent
000985  spring-forward minutes remain missing. The daily value gives each viable
000986  minute ratio equal weight. It is not a ratio of daily integrals, and the MDER
000987  support rule changes only MDER availability rather than deleting the day from
000988  other metric analyses.
000990  For darkest-10-hour mean melEDI, this numerical-zero rule changed three
000991  primary near-eye and five primary chest participant-days from approximately
000992  4.16 × 10^-17^ lx to exactly 0 lx. It did not change a fitted sample. Only
000993  the corresponding primary L10 models, diagnostics, influence checks,
000994  paired/common primary comparison, and the mathematically dependent complete
000995  BH families were refreshed. Coefficients changed by at most 3.61 × 10^-12^
000996  per LEBA point; two adjusted p-values changed below the report's
000997  three-decimal display precision, no family rank changed, and all three
000998  families still retained zero of 68 associations. The gap-timing-unaware L10
000999  values were already exact zeros and were not refitted.
001001  ## Models
001003  For participant-day outcomes, the full model asks whether LEBA improves the
001004  site-adjusted description while accounting for repeated days from the same
001005  participant. The reduced model removes only LEBA. Interdaily stability (IS)
001006  and intradaily variability (IV) have one response per participant, so they do
001007  not use a participant random intercept. Fixed site effects use the registered
001008  site order and sum-to-zero contrasts; the LEBA coefficient is therefore
001009  adjusted for site without being tied to one reference site.
001011  The following evaluated cell reads and prints the exact formula objects used
001012  for the full, reduced, and registered random-site sensitivity models.
001066  Gaussian full and reduced models were compared using maximum likelihood.
001067  Tweedie models used a log link and maximum likelihood. Effects and 95% Wald
001068  confidence intervals are then presented on a practical response scale: ratios
001069  for log-scale and Tweedie outcomes, an odds ratio for IS, and differences for
001070  identity-scale outcomes. The response specifications are listed below.
001114  ## Primary near-eye result
001116  Depending on the metric, the primary models used 139--141 participants,
001117  655--816 participant-days for participant-day outcomes, and all nine sites.
001118  IS and IV used 141 participant rows and represented 816 observed days. Exact
001119  metric-specific samples are shown below.
001128  The exact sample table is available as
001129  [source data](../../artifacts/11_source_data/H05/H05_reader_near_eye_samples.csv).
001131  ### Complete association pattern
001145  The heatmap colours show the signed effect on each model's fitted scale, while
001146  the printed value is on the practical response scale. Colours therefore show
001147  direction and relative within-outcome magnitude, not a common physical unit
001148  across all 17 rows. The tables provide every estimate, 95% confidence
001149  interval, Raw p, and BH-adjusted p.
001165  Across the complete planned family, zero of 68 BH-adjusted p-values were at or
001166  below 0.050. The four sleep-environment models remain in that archived family
001167  so the correction is not changed after seeing the diagnostics, but they are
001168  unfit for inference and their estimates and p-values are suppressed from the
001169  reader-facing matrices.
001170  The two smallest raw values were both for F2, spending time outdoors:
001172  - time above 1,000 lx melEDI increased by a factor of
001173    `r format_number(near_duration$estimate_practical_per_sd)` per participant
001174    SD (95% CI `r format_number(near_duration$conf_low_practical_per_sd)`–`r format_number(near_duration$conf_high_practical_per_sd)`),
001175    Raw *p* = `r format_p(near_duration$p_raw)`, BH-adjusted
001176    *p* = `r format_p(near_duration$p_adjusted)`; and
001177  - melEDI dose increased by a factor of
001178    `r format_number(near_dose$estimate_practical_per_sd)` (95% CI
001179    `r format_number(near_dose$conf_low_practical_per_sd)`–`r format_number(near_dose$conf_high_practical_per_sd)`),
001180    Raw *p* = `r format_p(near_dose$p_raw)`, BH-adjusted
001181    *p* = `r format_p(near_dose$p_adjusted)`.
001183  These estimates remain useful for describing magnitude and uncertainty, but
001184  neither is a multiplicity-retained result.
001186  Complete near-eye numerical results and figure data are available as

001190  ### Near-eye model assessment
001218  All fixed-site fits completed, and all applicable Hessian checks were positive
001219  definite. That numerical completion does not make every response model
001220  scientifically defensible. Nineteen models were acceptable, 45 were acceptable
001221  with a specified limitation, and the four sleep-environment models were
001222  classified **unfit for inference**. Most retained limitations were ordinary
001223  Gaussian residual warnings. They qualify how closely the working residual
001224  distribution represents these data but do not indicate numerical fit failure.

001228  residual-versus-fitted plot helps reveal fitted-dependent residual structure; the quantile
001229  plot compares standardized residuals with a normal reference. For the
001230  Tweedie sleep model, the simulation-based tests in the subsequent table—not
001231  the normal-reference plot—govern the assessment.
001259  #### H05 sleep-environment models are unfit for inference
001261  The four models for time below 1 lx melEDI during sleep require a stronger
001262  qualification than the ordinary residual warnings. Their simulated residuals
001263  showed pronounced non-uniformity, zero-mass disagreement, and outlier
001264  evidence. They also produced 183--187 fitted values above the
001265  participant-specific response support. These models are retained because the
001266  prespecified response-family assessment did not identify an adequate common
001267  replacement. They are therefore shown for audit completeness but classified
001268  **unfit for inference**: their estimates, confidence intervals, and p-values
001269  are not used to support any scientific claim.
001271  This classification is specific to this H05 sleep-environment response and
001272  the model structure used here. It does not determine whether the underlying
001273  metric can be analysed in another hypothesis that uses a different response
001274  variable or model structure.
001283  The near-eye family supports the overall null conclusion through the 64
001284  reader-interpretable cells: 19 are acceptable and 45 have specified residual
001285  limitations. The four additional planned sleep-environment cells remain in
001286  the archived multiplicity family but are unfit for inference.

001427  correction. In the primary near-eye analysis, the four differences per LEBA SD

001647  ### Site adjustment and site influence
001649  Fixed site was selected as primary because the inherited 17-metric
001650  site-structure evaluation fitted all fixed-site models successfully, whereas
001651  the corresponding random-site descriptions included two unstable, two
001652  non-estimable, and two singular fits. In the H05 sensitivity itself, 15 of 136
001653  main all-available random-site fits were unstable—seven near eye and eight
001654  chest—while 121 passed. This instability is why random site is a sensitivity
001655  rather than the primary adjustment.
001667  All 612 required leave-one-site-out refits completed. Twenty associations
001668  were stable, 27 retained direction but were magnitude-sensitive, and 21
001669  changed direction after at least one site was omitted. The two leading F2
001670  associations had no site-omission sign reversal and were classified stable.
001671  The broader pattern confirms that some small, near-zero estimates depend on
001672  which of the nine sites is represented, reinforcing the restrained
001673  family-wide interpretation.
001685  ### Gap-timing-unaware dataset
001687  The same model implementation was applied to the gap-timing-unaware dataset,
001688  including the same mean-of-viable-momentary-ratios MDER definition. This
001689  sensitivity gave no

001691  `r format_p(min(preparation_results$p_adjusted, na.rm = TRUE))`. Sixty-four of
001692  68 estimates retained their sign and had overlapping component intervals;
001693  four changed sign, also with overlapping component intervals. The main null
001694  conclusion is unchanged. For MDER specifically, 687 near-eye days from 137
001695  participants entered H05-F3, and its smallest MDER value was BH-adjusted *p* =

001702  The all-available longest continuous period above 250 lx melEDI is an
001703  observed lower bound because a data gap can interrupt identifiability. The
001704  strict sensitivity retained only participant-days whose longest period was
001705  exactly identified: 500 participant-days from 132 participants at all nine
001706  sites. All four factor directions agreed with the all-available analysis.
001733  For F2, the ratio attenuated from 1.117 to 1.046 (95% CI 0.930--1.175),
001734  supporting the decision not to promote this lower-bound metric as a positive
001735  association.
001737  Complete sensitivity sources are available for the
001738  [common-sample placement comparison](../../artifacts/09_tables/H05/H05_paired_placement_comparison.csv),
001739  [random-site models](../../artifacts/09_tables/H05/H05_random_site_sensitivity.csv),
001740  [leave-one-site-out analysis](../../artifacts/09_tables/H05/H05_leave_one_site_out_summary.csv),
001741  [gap-timing-unaware dataset](../../artifacts/11_source_data/H05/H05_gap_timing_unaware_dataset.csv),
001742  [gap-timing-unaware matched MDER placement comparison](../../artifacts/09_tables/H05/H05_mder_metric010_gap_paired_placement_comparison.csv),
001743  and [exactly identified longest-period analysis](../../artifacts/09_tables/H05/H05_exactly_identified_longest_bout_sensitivity.csv).
001745  ## Interpretation
001747  The strongest defensible conclusion is that, after fixed-site adjustment and
001748  one complete 68-test Benjamini--Hochberg correction, these data provide no
001749  multiplicity-retained evidence that any of the four LEBA factors is associated
001750  with the 17 selected personal light-exposure metrics.
001752  The positive F2 estimates for time above 1,000 lx melEDI and melEDI dose are
001753  consistent across the principal stability checks. Their confidence intervals
001754  describe non-zero associations before multiplicity adjustment, but their
001755  adjusted values do not support confirmatory claims. They should be treated as
001756  descriptive patterns that may motivate future work, not as established
001757  effects of spending time outdoors.
001759  This is an observational association analysis. LEBA scores are self-reported,
001760  site adjustment cannot remove all person-level confounding, only nine sites
001761  contribute to the primary analysis, and several small estimates are sensitive
001762  to site omission. Near-eye and chest measurements also represent related but
001763  non-identical light fields. Finally, the sleep-environment Tweedie models are
001764  unfit for inference because of material response-support and
001765  simulated-residual failures; their estimates and p-values are suppressed from
001766  the reader-facing results and cannot support a claim in H05. This judgment is
001767  specific to the response variable and model structure used here and does not
001768  preclude analysing the metric in another hypothesis with a different response
001769  or model.

===== audit/hypotheses/H05/H05_analysis_preparation.qmd =====
000238  ::: {.callout-note title="About this analysis record"}
000239  This page explains how verified light-exposure metrics and questionnaire scores
000240  were turned into the H05 results. The companion
000241  [H05 results report](../../../notebooks/hypotheses/H05.html) presents the
000242  scientific findings.
000244  Rendering this page recalculates only file-identity checks, schema and key
000245  checks, and lightweight descriptive summaries from the frozen H05 model
000246  frames. It reads the fitted-model archive, estimates, intervals, diagnostics,
000247  influence screens, and sensitivity outputs from their recorded files. It does
000248  not fit or refit a model, calculate predictions, re-estimate residual
000249  diagnostics, rerun leave-one-site-out analyses, bootstrap, or simulate data.
000250  :::
000252  ## Purpose
000254  H05 asks whether four Light Exposure Behaviour Assessment (LEBA) questionnaire
000255  factors are associated with 17 personal light-exposure metrics. The primary
000256  analysis uses near-eye measurements; all-available chest measurements provide
000257  complementary evidence. Participant-level metrics and participant-day metrics
000258  use different model structures, but they share the same fixed-site adjustment,
000259  questionnaire-score scaling, and multiplicity family.
000261  This page identifies the data and code that produced those results, explains
000262  why each analytical operation was necessary, and shows the stored evidence
000263  used to check the analysis.
000265  ## Analysis map
000287  The arrows describe dependencies. They do not mean that this page repeats the
000288  model computation.
000290  ## What is calculated when this page opens
000313  ## 1. Verified inputs
000315  ### File identity
000317  The analysis begins from named files whose SHA-256 identities were pinned
000318  before model construction. The table below is limited to inputs that determine
000319  the current H05 results or the site-structure decision.
000398  ### Schema, key, content, and value checks
000400  The LEBA table is keyed by site and participant identifier. Each metric frame
000401  is keyed either by participant or by participant plus local date. The model
000402  index adds run, metric, and factor identifiers, so every one of its 544 rows
000403  refers to one unique analysis cell.
000466  ## 2. Questionnaire predictors
000468  ### Factor construction
000470  The four LEBA factors are complete-score sums. Ordered item responses run from
000471  1 (“Never”) to 5 (“Always”). F2 contains one reverse-coded item; the other
000472  factors use the recorded item direction. A factor score is non-estimable if
000473  any contributing item is missing.
000499  The recorded reconstruction found no missing score and no mismatch for any of
000500  the 184 participants. Scores were not standardised within site. For each exact
000501  model frame, the participant-level mean was subtracted from the raw factor
000502  score, and the reported effect was expressed both per raw point and per one
000503  participant-level standard deviation.
000557  The exact aggregated counts used by the bars are available as
000558  [figure source data](../../../artifacts/11_source_data/H05/H05_preparation_leba_score_distribution.csv).
000560  ## 3. Metric construction and analytical samples
000562  ### Coverage, gaps, and metric support
000564  The primary dataset applied the general requirement of at least 50% valid
000565  minutes per hour and at least 80% valid hours per day, together with
000566  metric-specific support rules where the timing of remaining gaps affects what
000567  the metric can represent. Unsupported values remained missing rather than
000568  being interpreted as darkness. Light-level metrics use melEDI in reader-facing
000569  labels, with a 0.1 lx offset where a logarithmic transform requires it.
000571  One predefined sensitivity uses the **gap-timing-unaware dataset**. It still
000572  passed the general 50%-per-hour and 80%-per-day coverage rules, but the timing
000573  of the remaining missing observations was not used for an additional
000574  metric-specific adjustment. For contrast at this first explanation, the
000575  primary dataset could be interpreted as a **time-sensitive primary metric
000576  dataset**. Hereafter it is called simply the primary dataset.
000578  MDER is the arithmetic mean of viable one-minute melEDI/illuminance ratios.
000579  Both channels must be finite and strictly positive, and at least 720 of the
000580  complete 1,440 local wall-clock minutes must provide a viable ratio. Repeated
000581  local minutes at daylight-saving fall-back are averaged channel-wise before
000582  forming the ratio; absent spring-forward minutes remain missing. Each viable
000583  minute ratio has equal weight: there is no time-profile weighting, gating, or
000584  scaling, and the daily value is not a ratio of daily integrals. Failure of this
000585  support rule makes only MDER missing; it does not delete the day from other
000586  metric analyses.
000588  The longest continuous period above 250 lx melEDI is an observed lower bound
000589  when a gap can interrupt the period. Its exact-identifiability sensitivity
000590  therefore retains only participant-days for which the longest **period** is
000591  fully observed. The L10 midpoint retains its continuous range and its
000592  strict-after-16:00 representation; no noon sensitivity was needed.
000638  ### Model-frame construction
000640  `h05_prepare_metric_rows()` selects one placement, preparation, sample rule,
000641  and metric at a time. It applies metric eligibility, checks the participant or
000642  participant-day key, joins LEBA by site and participant, excludes rows without
000643  a complete factor score, fixes the registered site order, and sorts rows by
000644  site, participant, and local date. Interdaily stability and intradaily
000645  variability contribute one row per participant. The other 15 metrics
000646  contribute one row per eligible participant-day.
000648  The archive contains 136 run-by-metric frames (8 × 17). Joining the four LEBA
000649  factors creates the 544 model-index rows without changing a metric frame's
000650  participants or observations.
000700  The corresponding [exact table source](../../../artifacts/11_source_data/H05/H05_preparation_sample_support.csv)
000701  retains internal metric and placement identifiers as well as every displayed
000702  count.
000758  The figure uses the same
000759  [paired source data](../../../artifacts/11_source_data/H05/H05_preparation_sample_support.csv)
000760  as the exact sample table.
000762  ### Site contributions
000764  The primary near-eye analysis represents all nine submitted-manuscript sites.
000765  The complementary chest data represent eight. Site-specific counts vary with
000766  metric eligibility; the displayed range is therefore taken across the 17
000767  exact model frames, not from a pooled approximation.
000853  The plotted range has
000854  [exact source data](../../../artifacts/11_source_data/H05/H05_preparation_site_support_summary.csv).
000855  The complete
000856  [metric-by-site source](../../../artifacts/11_source_data/H05/H05_preparation_site_support.csv)
000857  contains 1,122 rows across every predefined run.
000859  ## 4. Model construction and inference
000861  ### Exact formulas supplied to the models
000863  The analysis uses fixed site with sum-to-zero contrasts as the primary site
000864  adjustment. Participant-day models add a participant random intercept because
000865  several days can come from the same participant. Participant-level IS and IV
000866  models contain one row per person and therefore do not add that intercept.
000868  The formula registry below replaces the raw printed R list with a compact
000869  reader-facing table while retaining the exact Wilkinson formulas evaluated by
000870  the model functions.
000901  ### Engines and estimation settings
000926  ### Site specification
000928  The fixed-site primary specification differs from the registered random-site
000929  structure. The decision was based on the inherited 17-metric site-structure
000930  evidence: every fixed-site model converged with a positive-definite Hessian and
000931  none was singular, whereas the random-site descriptions contained unstable,
000932  non-estimable, and singular fits. Keeping site fixed also estimates each
000933  site's adjustment directly rather than relying on a variance component from
000934  only nine sites. The random-site structure remains a sensitivity.
000957  Across the H05 random-site sensitivity itself, 121 of 136 fits passed the
000958  descriptive stability checks and 15 were unstable. This is consistent with
000959  using fixed site for primary inference and random site only as a stability
000960  assessment.
000962  ### Estimand, uncertainty, and multiplicity
000964  For each metric and factor, the full model adds the centered LEBA score to the
000965  reduced site-adjusted model. The raw inferential p-value comes from that
000966  full-versus-reduced likelihood-ratio comparison. Each declared family contains
000967  all 17 metrics × 4 factors = 68 tests, and Benjamini–Hochberg adjustment is
000968  performed on the complete 68-value vector. Significance is decided at adjusted
000969  *p* ≤ 0.05 before any display formatting.
000971  Coefficients are converted to the metric's practical scale and reported per
000972  one participant-level LEBA standard deviation. Gaussian identity outcomes are
000973  differences, log-transformed light outcomes are ratios, and IS is an odds
000974  ratio. Every reported interval is a two-sided 95% Wald interval from the final
000975  model. No secondary significance screen is attached to descriptive
000976  sensitivities.
001005  ::: {.callout-note title="Why a less conservative adjustment can accompany fewer retained associations"}
001006  For one unchanged vector of raw p-values, Benjamini–Hochberg cannot retain
001007  fewer associations than Bonferroni at the same 0.05 threshold. The H05 family
001008  above is based on likelihood-ratio p-values from the fixed-site models. It is
001009  therefore not interchangeable with a family built from unadjusted rank
001010  correlations or another raw test. Multiplicity adjustment and the construction
001011  of the raw p-values are separate analytical decisions; the latter can change
001012  which associations enter the smallest ranks before either correction is
001013  applied.
001014  :::
001016  ## 5. Diagnostics and influence
001018  ### Diagnostic sequence
001020  Every stored final model was checked for convergence, Hessian status,
001021  singularity, residual behaviour, response bounds, metric-specific timing
001022  support, and within-participant serial dependence where applicable. Gaussian
001023  models use transformed-residual and influence checks. Tweedie models add
001024  simulation-based uniformity, dispersion, zero-support, and outlier checks.
001025  Candidate influential participants were ranked by Cook's distance for ordinary
001026  linear models or by maximum absolute Pearson residual for mixed models; this
001027  screen did not silently remove observations.
001029  The compact table below excludes the sleep-environment metric because that
001030  outcome has a separate, stronger disposition immediately afterwards.
001065  ### Numerical-zero normalization for darkest-10-hour mean melEDI
001067  The shared [numerical-zero decision](../../../audit/decisions/l10_numerical_zero_normalization.md)
001068  sets a geometric-mean back-transformation residual to exact zero only when it
001069  lies within the unit-aware floating-point tolerance and every finite source
001070  minute entering the selected window is exactly zero. For these L10 windows,
001071  the tolerance is 2.220446049250313 × 10^-14^ lx. Missing source minutes remain
001072  missing and are never imputed as zero.
001074  The verified primary rebuild changed three near-eye and five chest
001075  participant-days from 4.163336342344337 × 10^-17^ lx to exact zero. It did
001076  not alter any fitted sample, non-L10 value, hourly or 30-minute value,
001077  participant-level value, or site/daylight-context value. The
001078  gap-timing-unaware L10 frame already contained exact zeros and was repinned
001079  but not refitted.
001146  The bounded updater replaced eight primary inferential L10 bundles, rebuilt
001147  eight registered random-site sensitivities and 36 primary L10
001148  leave-one-site-out refits, and refreshed the matched primary placement
001149  comparison. It ran zero gap L10 and zero non-L10 refits. All 22 frozen-scope
001150  reconciliation checks pass. The exact result-change, complete-family, input-
001151  cell, and preservation records are linked through the
001152  [L10 artifact-update manifest](../../../artifacts/12_manifests/H05/H05_metric011_artifact_update_manifest.csv).
001154  ### Current MDER slice and influence boundary
001156  The MDER-dependent portion was refreshed from the approved momentary-ratio
001157  inputs without repeating any non-MDER model. Its exact all-available samples,
001158  current upper-tail screen, and bounded influence refits are summarized below.
001159  The outer-fence rule identifies observations for sensitivity checks; it is not
001160  an exclusion rule.
001360  The current sample contains 702 near-eye participant-days from 137
001361  participants at nine sites and 732 chest participant-days from 152
001362  participants at eight sites. One near-eye and three chest days exceeded the
001363  outer fence. All 44 day- and participant-deletion refits passed, and every
001364  chest sensitivity interval contained zero. The four near-eye F5 sensitivity
001365  intervals remained below zero, consistent with its unadjusted primary
001366  interval, but that association is not retained after the complete-family BH
001367  correction. The small chest coefficients were more upper-tail-sensitive than
001368  the near-eye coefficients, including one sign reversal for a coefficient
001369  already close to zero. No observation was removed from the primary analysis.
001371  The repaired gap-timing-unaware samples contain 687 near-eye participant-days
001372  from 137 participants and 723 chest participant-days from 152 participants.
001373  The paired/common comparison contains 478 days from 107 participants at eight
001374  sites, with all four directions concordant and all component intervals
001375  overlapping. One near-eye and three chest days exceeded the outer fence. All
001376  44 gap deletion refits passed and 40 intervals contained zero. The near-eye
001377  coefficients changed by at most 0.233 full-model standard errors without a
001378  sign reversal; the small chest F2 coefficient reversed direction in two
001379  deletions, and every chest interval contained zero. The repaired H05-F3 family
001380  still retained zero of 68 associations after BH correction.
001382  The [upper-tail summary](../../../artifacts/08_diagnostics/H05/H05_mder_metric010_upper_tail_summary.csv),
001383  [influence-refit record](../../../artifacts/08_diagnostics/H05/H05_mder_metric010_influence_refits.csv),
001384  and [bounded reconciliation](../../../artifacts/12_manifests/H05/H05_metric010_reconciliation.csv)
001385  retain the exact inputs, samples, estimates, and preservation evidence. All 20
001386  reconciliation checks pass; the other 16 metrics remain frozen, except for
001387  permitted complete-family BH ranks and adjusted p-values derived after the
001388  four MDER raw p-values were replaced.
001390  The repaired sensitivity has a separate [gap influence record](../../../artifacts/08_diagnostics/H05/H05_mder_metric010_gap_influence_refits.csv), [paired/common comparison](../../../artifacts/09_tables/H05/H05_mder_metric010_gap_paired_placement_comparison.csv), and [16-check reseal reconciliation](../../../artifacts/12_manifests/H05/H05_metric010_gap_reseal_reconciliation.csv). The reconciliation records 27 non-MDER adjusted-p changes and 26 rank changes caused solely by replacing the four MDER slots in H05-F3; every non-MDER raw test and fitted object is unchanged.
001392  ::: {.callout-note title="Sleep-environment outcome: unfit for H05 inference"}
001393  The four primary near-eye models for **time below 1 lx melEDI during sleep**
001394  are unfit for H05 inference. The observed response contains exact zeros, while
001395  the fitted Tweedie simulations generated none; simulation-based uniformity and
001396  outlier checks also showed material failure, and fitted values crossed the
001397  admissible response support. Their coefficients, intervals, and p-values are
001398  therefore suppressed from the H05 results. The complementary chest models show
001399  the same material response-support and simulated-residual limitations and are
001400  also not used as inferential evidence.
001402  This disposition is specific to this outcome **as the H05 response under this
001403  model structure**. It does not mean that the sleep-environment metric cannot be
001404  analysed in another hypothesis that uses a different response variable,
001405  estimand, or model structure.
001406  :::
001471  ## 6. Sensitivity analyses
001473  Sensitivity branches preserve the accepted primary estimand unless their
001474  purpose explicitly changes the sample or site representation. They are used to
001475  describe stability, not to promote a non-retained primary association.
001576  The paired/common placement figure in the results report uses near eye on the
001577  x-axis and chest on the y-axis, matched factor–metric estimands only, identity
001578  and null lines, and equal axis geometry. Its exact source is
001579  [H05_paired_effect_comparison_data.csv](../../../artifacts/11_source_data/H05/H05_paired_effect_comparison_data.csv).
001580  It describes agreement and differences without claiming equivalence. The
001581  stored component estimates come from the exact common sample; no model was
001582  fitted solely for that plot.
001584  Complete stored sensitivity records are available for the
001585  [paired/common placement comparison](../../../artifacts/09_tables/H05/H05_paired_placement_comparison.csv),
001586  [random-site analysis](../../../artifacts/09_tables/H05/H05_random_site_sensitivity.csv),
001587  [leave-one-site-out analysis](../../../artifacts/09_tables/H05/H05_leave_one_site_out_summary.csv),
001588  [gap-timing-unaware analysis](../../../artifacts/09_tables/H05/H05_manuscript_prepared_comparison.csv),
001589  [gap-timing-unaware matched MDER placement comparison](../../../artifacts/09_tables/H05/H05_mder_metric010_gap_paired_placement_comparison.csv),
001590  and [exactly identified longest-period analysis](../../../artifacts/09_tables/H05/H05_exactly_identified_longest_bout_sensitivity.csv).
001592  ## 7. Code map and execution order
001594  ### Function modules
001624  ### Executable entry points
001654  The scientific model run was executed from the repository root in a clean R
001655  4.6.1 session using the project library. The command is shown for
001656  reproducibility; this page does not execute it.
001671  ## 8. Output and claim provenance
001673  The model-frame index records the exact sample and SHA-256 frame identity for
001674  each run, metric, and factor. The model manifest connects each of those cells
001675  to its full, reduced, and final model object. Result, diagnostic, and
001676  sensitivity CSV files then connect the stored fits to the reader tables and
001677  figures. Figure source-data files contain the exact values used by their plot
001678  layers, so a reader does not need to digitise an image.
001732  The H05 preparation-report manifest is created after the final website render.
001733  It records complete identities for the authoring source, the byte-identical
001734  website QMD copy, rendered HTML, page assets, H05 scripts and tests, scientific
001735  outputs, source data, shared configuration snapshot, and R environment. The
001736  manifest excludes itself and the handoff that quotes its identity, preventing
001737  a circular checksum chain.
001739  ## 9. How to examine or reproduce H05
001741  A reader can follow the analysis in five passes:
001743  1. inspect `H05_input_audit.csv`, `h05_contract.R`, and the factor, metric,
001744     formula, and run registries to verify the frozen inputs and specifications;
001745  2. compare the exact samples above with `H05_model_frame_index.csv` and the
001746     136 frames in `H05_model_frames.rds`;
001747  3. follow `run_h05_stage2.R` through `h05_modeling.R`, then the bounded MDER
001748     and L10 reseal scripts, to connect each frame to its current model object,
001749     coefficient, interval, likelihood-ratio test, diagnostic, and sensitivity
001750     record;
001751  4. inspect the complete 68-value family audit before interpreting any
001752     individual association; and
001753  5. read the [H05 results report](../../../notebooks/hypotheses/H05.html)
001754     alongside its tables, figures, and paired source data.
001756  The most important boundary is that the preparation record documents a frozen
001757  analysis; it does not create an alternate result. If an input, metric rule,
001758  response model, or estimand changes, the affected scientific pipeline must be
001759  rerun before this page is refreshed.

===== notebooks/hypotheses/H06.qmd =====
000410  # Analytical question
000412  The preregistered hypothesis was:
000414  > **H6: Day type, daily exercise, and sleep variables predict daily light exposure metrics.**
000416  The approved analysis deliberately narrows that broad question to supported
000417  one-hour, zero-aware geometric mean melEDI. It asks whether expected hourly
000418  near-eye melEDI differs between work and free days, between sedentary and
000419  active days, and per additional hour of previous sleep duration. It estimates
000420  an average over the retained supported hours; it does not estimate daily
000421  light-exposure metrics. Time of day is examined separately and exploratorily.
000423  The companion [H06 analysis preparation and
000424  provenance](../../audit/hypotheses/H06/H06_analysis_preparation.html) traces
000425  the verified inputs, fitted samples, formulas, diagnostics, sensitivities, and
000426  stored outputs underlying this report.
000428  ::: {.callout-note title="Answer in brief."}
000429  - **Primary associations.** Activity status (**BH-adjusted p < 0.001**) and
000430    day type (**BH-adjusted p = 0.004**) met the adjusted 0.050 threshold.
000431    Previous sleep did not (BH-adjusted p = 0.745).
000433  - **Site and model dependence.** In the site-varying model, the equal-site
000434    activity contrast remained below the adjusted threshold (**three-predictor
000435    BH-adjusted p < 0.001**), whereas day type did not (three-predictor
000436    BH-adjusted p = 0.298). Day-type
000437    heterogeneity across sites met the adjusted threshold (**BH-adjusted p =
000438    0.017**), but day type did not under the alternative working variance
000439    (BH-adjusted p = 0.260). The day-type result is therefore site- and
000440    model-dependent.
000442  - **Exploratory site screens.** Borås and Dortmund were pointwise above the
000443    equal-site day-type average; no site was pointwise below it. Those nine
000444    localization intervals were not multiplicity adjusted. Separate nine-site
000445    adjustments retained the free/work associations at Borås and Dortmund and
000446    the active/sedentary association at Tübingen. These tests compare each
000447    site's ratio with 1, not with another site or the equal-site average.
000449  - **Exploratory clock-time patterns.** Active status was higher from about
000450    07:15 to 19:00 and lower from about 23:30 to 05:15 across midnight, when
000451    expected melEDI was low. The free-day curve was lower from about 06:45 to
000452    09:15 and 18:45 to 19:15. These windows exclude 1 on the ratio scale,
000453    equivalent to excluding 0 on a difference scale; they are local
000454    15-minute-grid descriptions, not simultaneous or multiplicity-controlled
000455    whole-curve tests.
000457  All associations are observational. Chest, paired-day, data-sensitivity, and
000458  diagnostic results remain qualifications.
000459  :::
000461  # Data and analysis
000463  ## Outcome, predictors, and fitted samples
000465  The primary outcome is the zero-aware geometric mean melEDI for each retained
000466  supported hour at the near-eye placement. Activity is binary: sedentary means
000467  no reported exercise, while active includes light, moderate, or vigorous
000468  reported exercise. Day type and activity are day-level diary variables;
000469  previous sleep duration is linked from the preceding sleep period. Because
000470  days contribute 19–24 supported hours, the estimand is supported-hour
000471  weighted rather than participant-day weighted.
000509  The primary fit contains 16,596 supported hours from 715 participant-days,
000510  137 participants, and nine study sites. The smallest primary site ×
000511  category cells contain 185 hours, 8 participant-days, and 4 participants;
000512  these minima do not all occur in the same cell. [Download the exact category
000513  cell registry](../../artifacts/06_model_data/H06/H06_category_cells.csv).
000515  The TUM exercise-diary identity check uses the approved immutable
000516  `melidosData` 1.0.6 object and finds 7 S001 rows and 0 S101 rows, with no local
000517  identifier rewrite. The `KNUST_S005` numeral 3,600 is interpreted as 3,600
000518  seconds, or one sedentary hour, only for eligible exploratory analyses; it is
000519  not added to the primary analysis.
000521  ## Model and inference
000523  The primary model is a zero-inclusive quasi-Poisson GLM with a log link and
000524  sum-coded fixed site. Participant-cluster HC3 covariance (`fix = FALSE`) makes
000525  participants, rather than hours, the inferential replicates. One-coefficient
