000526  tests use a *t* reference with 136 degrees of freedom. Site-interaction block
000527  tests use Wald *F* tests with restriction-rank and 136 denominator degrees of
000528  freedom. Overall ratios give each of the nine sites equal weight on the linear
000529  predictor scale before back-transformation.
000567  The three primary association tests, three site-heterogeneity tests, and
000568  three practical contrasts are separate, declared Benjamini–Hochberg families.
000569  Only multiplicity-adjusted p-values are displayed. The exploratory
000570  site-specific screen uses three additional, separate nine-site adjustments,
000571  one within each predictor. Chest, paired-placement, weekday/weekend,
000572  influence, and other exploratory outputs do not create post hoc p-value
000573  families.
000591  # Primary near-eye results
000612  Expected supported-hour near-eye melEDI was 1.45 times as high on free days
000613  as on work days (95% CI 1.14–1.85; BH-adjusted p = 0.004) and 2.06 times as
000614  high on active as on sedentary days (1.54–2.76; BH-adjusted p < 0.001). The
000615  ratio per additional hour of previous sleep duration was 0.98 (0.85–1.12;
000616  BH-adjusted p = 0.745). That interval allows a modest decrease or increase; it
000617  does not establish the absence of an association.
000639  The work/free association varied across sites (BH-adjusted p = 0.017).
000640  Activity and sleep showed no multiplicity-retained site heterogeneity. The
000641  overall work/free estimate therefore needs the site-specific context below.
000642  The primary 1.45 ratio comes from a model that forces one common work/free
000643  association across sites; it is not the average of the ratios from the
000644  interaction model. In the interaction model, the equal-site average is the
000645  geometric mean on the ratio scale: 1.15 (95% CI 0.93–1.44). Three sites are
000646  above that average and six are below it, because three larger positive
000647  log-deviations balance six smaller negative log-deviations. This is not an
000648  algebraic inconsistency, but the attenuation from 1.45 to 1.15 is important
000649  evidence that the work/free conclusion depends on model structure. The
000650  corresponding interaction-model averages remain close to the primary results
000651  for activity (1.93 versus 2.06) and previous sleep (0.97 versus 0.98).
000664  [Source CSV](../../artifacts/11_source_data/H06/H06_reader_primary_effects_figure.csv)
000678  [Source CSV](../../artifacts/11_source_data/H06/H06_stage3_site_specific_significance_screen_figure.csv)
000991  [Site-specific results CSV](../../artifacts/09_tables/H06/H06_stage3_site_specific_significance_screen.csv)
000993  [Site-to-average deviation source CSV](../../artifacts/11_source_data/H06/H06_primary_work_free_site_deviations.csv)
000995  # Sensitivity and placement analyses
000997  ## Required data sensitivity
000999  At first use, this report calls the comparison input the
001000  **gap-timing-unaware dataset**. It passed the 50%-per-hour and 80%-per-day
001001  coverage rules, but the timing of remaining within-hour and within-day gaps
001002  was not used for an additional metric-specific adjustment. The primary
001003  analysis could therefore be interpreted as time-sensitive. Hereafter, the
001004  current corrected input is called the **primary dataset**.
001006  The all-available near-eye comparison contains 16,329 hours from 702
001007  participant-days, 137 participants, and nine sites. On the exact common-hour
001008  frame, 16,329 hour keys remain and only 12 response values differ. The three
001009  near-eye ratios are stable: 1.44 for free/work, 2.08 for active/sedentary, and
001010  0.96 per additional hour of sleep. Work/free site heterogeneity also remains
001011  multiplicity-retained (BH-adjusted p = 0.015).
001052  ## Chest and paired-day results
001054  The all-available chest ratios are 1.51 (95% CI 1.21–1.89), 1.81
001055  (1.28–2.54), and 0.95 (0.84–1.07) for free/work, active/sedentary, and sleep,
001056  respectively. The paired-day sample contains 553 participant-days, 109
001057  participants, and eight sites at each placement. Near-eye ratios are 1.59,
001058  1.96, and 0.96; chest ratios are 1.53, 1.82, and 0.95. The two frames each
001059  contain 12,842 hours, but three hourly keys differ at each placement, so the
001060  figure compares estimates rather than paired observations. Similar directions
001061  do not establish placement equivalence.
001074  [Source CSV](../../artifacts/11_source_data/H06/H06_paired_placement_effects_figure.csv)
001076  ## Working variance and targeted influence
001078  The fixed-power quasi-Tweedie sensitivity (`p = 1.8`) changes the work/free
001079  estimate to 1.15 (95% CI 0.91–1.45; BH-adjusted p = 0.260). Activity remains
001080  1.91 (1.49–2.45; BH-adjusted p < 0.001), and sleep is 0.95 (0.88–1.04;
001081  BH-adjusted p = 0.260). HC1 and HC2 change uncertainty only slightly, while
001082  equal-site and observed-sample standardization coincide for the additive
001083  model. The weekday/weekend substitution gives 1.23 (0.92–1.66) for weekend
001084  versus weekday and has no new p-value family.
001128  Seven score-ranked participants and all nine sites were deleted one at a time;
001129  all 16 refit sets passed the numerical gate. Activity intervals exclude one
001130  in all 16 checks, and sleep intervals do so in none. Work/free intervals
001131  exclude one after all seven participant deletions but only seven of nine site
001132  deletions: omitting Dortmund gives 1.26 (0.98–1.62), and omitting Borås gives
001133  1.34 (0.99–1.80).
001163  # Diagnostics
001165  All approved additive and full fits converge, have full-rank fixed-effect
001166  designs, and have finite, positive-definite unmodified HC3 covariance
001167  matrices. These checks establish numerical estimability, not adequate fit.
001220  The primary response contains 28.3% exact-zero hours and has working
001221  dispersion 3,618. Fitted-decile observed/fitted ratios range from 0.78 to
001222  1.61. Residual–fitted Spearman correlation is -0.564, and absolute
001223  residual–fitted correlation is 0.553. Residual correlation is 0.464 at lag 1,
001224  0.233 at lag 2, and 0.013 at lag 6. Participant-cluster HC3 accommodates
001225  within-participant dependence for uncertainty but does not whiten residuals or
001226  repair the mean structure. The residual-clock display shows remaining daytime
001227  structure.
001240  [Source CSV](../../artifacts/11_source_data/H06/H06_primary_residual_clock_figure.csv)
001242  # Exploratory analyses
001244  ## Additional diary measures
001246  Exercise location, active travel, sedentary time, sleep onset, and final wake
001247  time were fitted one at a time on their own exact samples. They are descriptive
001248  extensions, have 95% CIs but no confirmatory p-values, and do not select or
001249  rescue the primary model.
001282  ## Time of day
001284  The exploratory time-of-day model separates occurrence (melEDI greater than
001285  zero, binomial-logit) from positive magnitude (Gamma-log). Multiplying their
001286  response-scale expectations gives expected hourly melEDI. Both components
001287  use four cyclic factor-by clock smooths for work/free day × activity status,
001288  with one smoothing parameter shared across the four curves. Site is a
001289  parametric adjustment only; there is no site-specific clock smooth and no
001290  `sz` component. Participant and participant-day random-effect smooths,
001291  true-sequence `AR.start`, and residual working correlations remain. The chosen
001292  basis is `k = 16`, fitted with `bam(method = "fREML", discrete = TRUE)`.
001317  The occurrence and positive-magnitude models converge with full-rank
001318  fixed-smooth designs. Their residual working correlations are 0.418 and
001319  0.440. Occurrence calibration overpredicts the lowest fitted-probability
001320  decile (observed/fitted ratio 0.829); positive-magnitude calibration ranges
001321  from about 0.89 to 1.21. The four occurrence smooths have `k`-check p-values
001322  0.013–0.023, although their edf remain below the basis ceiling; this is a
001323  residual model-structure warning. Positive-magnitude `k`-check p-values are
001324  0.18–0.21. Moving from `k = 12` to `k = 16` changes the combined curves by
001325  15.1% at the 95th percentile, so the curves provide timing context rather
001326  than confirmatory whole-curve tests.
001328  ### Reader displays and temporal contrasts
001330  Each figure has three aligned parts: (A) expected curves, (B) the
001331  corresponding complete response-scale
001332  ratio with a null reference of one, and (C) participant-hour support. The
001333  contrast panels are derived from the accepted frozen two-part fits without
001334  refitting; they compare complete expected melEDI rather than one smooth term.
001335  Free day is divided by work day after equal weighting over activity status and
001336  the nine sites. Active is divided by sedentary after equal weighting over
001337  work/free day and the nine sites.
001350  [Curve source CSV](../../artifacts/11_source_data/H06/H06_reader_temporal_day_type_curves.csv) ·
001351  [Ratio source CSV](../../artifacts/11_source_data/H06/H06_reader_temporal_day_type_ratios.csv) ·
001352  [Support source CSV](../../artifacts/11_source_data/H06/H06_reader_temporal_day_type_support.csv)
001365  [Curve source CSV](../../artifacts/11_source_data/H06/H06_reader_temporal_activity_curves.csv) ·
001366  [Ratio source CSV](../../artifacts/11_source_data/H06/H06_reader_temporal_activity_ratios.csv) ·
001367  [Support source CSV](../../artifacts/11_source_data/H06/H06_reader_temporal_activity_support.csv)
001369  The paired prediction gradients retain covariance between numerator and
001370  denominator within each component. The interval calculation treats smoothing
001371  parameters as fixed and sets cross-component occurrence–magnitude covariance
001372  to zero. It also inherits the residual working-correlation, occurrence
001373  calibration, and basis-sensitivity limitations above. Overnight ratios and
001374  intervals widen where numerator and denominator expectations approach zero.
001375  No time segment is labelled significant, and no global or simultaneous
001376  whole-curve inference is made.
001378  # Figure reproducibility
001380  All six reader-facing figures were assessed at 170 mm on an A4 portrait page
001381  with 20-mm side margins. Native and intended widths are equal, and the
001382  smallest essential text remains 7.5 pt. Physical-size inspection found no
001383  clipping, overlap, harmful wrapping, distortion, or materially imbalanced
001384  data region. Every figure has accessible alt text and linked source data.
001404  The analysis used R 4.6.1 and the project library. Scientific transformations,
001405  model fits, diagnostics, contrasts, tables, and figures are implemented in
001406  H06-scoped R scripts. The temporal contrasts use the accepted frozen model
001407  objects and did not refit a GAMM. No production bootstrap was required, no
001408  package was installed, no shared input was edited, and no full-project render
001409  was run.
001411  The companion [analysis preparation and
001412  provenance](../../audit/hypotheses/H06/H06_analysis_preparation.html) provides
001413  the bounded data, model, diagnostic, sensitivity, and file-identity record for
001414  these results.

===== audit/hypotheses/H06/H06_analysis_preparation.qmd =====
000275  This page records how the accepted H06 analysis was prepared. The companion
000276  [H06 results](../../../notebooks/hypotheses/H06.html) report the associations,
000277  uncertainty, diagnostics, and interpretation.
000279  ::: {.callout-note title="Execution boundary"}
000280  This render reads frozen frames, tables, diagnostics, figures, and file
000281  identities. It performs only schema, identity, key, and lightweight descriptive
000282  summaries needed to explain the preparation. It does not fit or refit a model,
000283  generate model predictions, estimate residual correlation, rerun a sensitivity,
000284  resample, or simulate.
000285  :::
000287  ## Analysis path
000323  ## Verified inputs and provenance
000341  The shared preparation was later resealed after a correction to a daily metric
000342  that H06 does not use. The current base manifest and input-bundle identities
000343  are pinned above. An independent R comparison rebuilt all six H06 hourly
000344  frames and found exact equality with the frozen frames, including attributes.
000345  The transition therefore changes provenance identities only; it does not
000346  change an H06 row, value, model, estimate, interval, diagnostic, sensitivity,
000347  or claim.
000364  At first use, this report calls the comparison input the
000365  **gap-timing-unaware dataset**. It passed the 50%-per-hour and 80%-per-day
000366  coverage rules, but the timing of the remaining within-hour and within-day
000367  gaps was not used for an additional metric-specific adjustment. The primary
000368  dataset could therefore be interpreted as time-sensitive. Below, the current
000369  corrected input is called simply the **primary dataset**.
000371  ### Exercise-diary identity
000373  The TUM exercise diary is the immutable `melidosData` 1.0.6 object rather than
000374  a remote moving target. Its upstream correction contains seven `TUM_S001`
000375  rows and no `S101` row; no local identifier rewrite is applied.
000395  One KNUST record reported 3,600 minutes of sedentary time. For the exploratory
000396  sedentary-time analysis only, that entry is interpreted as 3,600 seconds, or
000397  one hour. It is not added to the three-predictor primary analysis.
000399  ## Constructing the fitted samples
000401  ### Outcome and analytical unit
000403  The outcome is the zero-aware geometric mean melEDI for each retained
000404  supported hour. The quantity remains in lx, exact zeros remain valid, and an
000405  hour is retained only after the upstream one-hour coverage rule is met. The
000406  model therefore estimates expected melEDI for a supported hour; it does not
000407  estimate a daily light-exposure metric. Because participant-days contribute
000408  19–24 supported hours, days with more retained hours receive more weight.
000474  The primary near-eye source contains 19,584 one-hour records. After outcome
000475  admissibility, work/free linkage, binary activity linkage, and preceding-sleep
000476  linkage, 16,596 hours remain. Activity is the largest source of exclusion.
000477  Missingness is handled by constructing an exact complete frame before fitting;
000478  model-specific row dropping is not left to the fitting function.
000480  ### Day type, activity, and previous sleep
000482  - `work_free_day` is a participant-day diary label: Work day or Free day.
000483  - `activity_status` is Sedentary when no exercise was reported and Active when
000484    light, moderate, or vigorous exercise was reported. The four original
000485    intensity labels are not used as separate primary levels.
000486  - `previous_sleep_duration_h` is taken from the preceding eligible sleep
000487    period and centred at 8 h. The fitted coefficient is therefore the ratio for
000488    one additional hour of previous sleep.
000511  The sleep onset may lie on the preceding calendar date or after midnight on
000512  the wake date. Both relations are valid when they belong to the same eligible
000513  sleep interval; the one unavailable interval is quarantined. This prevents a
000514  calendar-date join from assigning sleep to the wrong participant-day.
000516  ### Exact samples
000553  The primary near-eye fit contains **16,596 supported hours from 715
000554  participant-days, 137 participants, and nine sites**. The complementary chest
000555  fit contains 18,352 hours from 789 participant-days, 149 participants, and
000556  eight sites. Paired placement analyses use identical participant-day and hour
000557  keys at both placements.
000584  All six frames have unique participant-hour keys, complete core fields,
000585  non-negative finite responses, and current hashes matching the exact
000586  invariance evidence. The historical and current file hashes are both retained
000587  so that the provenance-only reseal is visible rather than mistaken for a
000588  scientific change.
000590  ## Observed response and predictor support
000592  ### Original-unit melEDI
000692  [Response-distribution source CSV](../../../artifacts/11_source_data/H06/H06_preparation_response_distribution.csv)
000720  The near-eye frame contains 4,697 exact-zero hours (28.3%) and the chest frame
000721  5,337 (29.1%). The symlog transformation is display-only: it preserves zero,
000722  is linear from 0 to 1 lx, and is base-10 logarithmic above 1 lx. Source values
000723  and fitted responses are unchanged.
000725  ### Site, day type, and activity support
000776  [Site-by-category source CSV](../../../artifacts/11_source_data/H06/H06_preparation_site_day_activity_support.csv)
000831  The smallest cells are reported explicitly because the full interaction model
000832  requires site-specific information for every predictor. These cells remain
000833  estimable, but their wide site-specific intervals and sensitivity to site
000834  deletion limit localized interpretation.
000836  ### Local-clock support
000879  [Clock-support source CSV](../../../artifacts/11_source_data/H06/H06_preparation_clock_day_activity_support.csv)
000910  [Participant-day support CSV](../../../artifacts/11_source_data/H06/H06_preparation_participant_day_support.csv)
000912  ## Temporal ordering and repeated hours
000914  Repeated supported hours are ordered by their true UTC interval, not by a
000915  naive local-clock sort. A new sequence begins at a participant-day boundary,
000916  after an elapsed gap, or around a non-one-to-one wall-clock interval. Local
000917  clock time describes when an observation occurred; it is not the correlation
000918  coordinate.
000939  The primary near-eye frame has 1,052 true-time sequences and three irregular
000940  elapsed bins. The clustered primary covariance addresses repeated
000941  participant-level observations; the sequence information is retained for
000942  temporal residual diagnostics and the separate clock-time analysis.
000944  ## Primary model and estimands
000946  ### Exact formulas
000967  The additive formula answers whether expected supported-hour near-eye melEDI
000968  differs, on average, between Free and Work days, between Active and Sedentary
000969  days, and per additional hour of previous sleep. Local clock time is not a
000970  primary predictor. The interaction formula permits each of those three ratios
000971  to vary by site; it is not a different reference outcome or a test of whether
000972  a site lies above an average site effect.
000988  The quasi-Poisson specification is used as a log-mean estimating model, not as
000989  a claim that the observed melEDI distribution is Poisson. Participant-cluster
000990  HC3 uncertainty is the primary inferential device. The equal-site estimand
000991  standardizes predictions with the same weight for each included site and then
000992  back-transforms once; participant-hours do not determine site weights.
000994  ### Multiplicity
001012  The primary common-effect tests, primary site-heterogeneity tests, and
001013  practical standardized contrasts each form a separate three-test family. The
001014  near-eye gap-timing-unaware counterparts use separate three-test families.
001015  Only adjusted p-values are interpreted in the reader report. The exploratory
001016  site-specific display applies a separate nine-site Benjamini–Hochberg
001017  adjustment within each predictor, rather than pooling all 27 site estimates.
001018  Those site-wise tests compare each site's ratio with the null ratio 1; they do
001019  not test whether sites differ from the equal-site average or from one another.
001021  ## Diagnostics, influence, and sensitivity
001023  ### Numerical and distributional checks
001064  All stored fits converged with full-rank fixed designs and finite,
001065  positive-definite HC3 covariance matrices. Large dispersion and the positive
001066  association between absolute residuals and fitted values show that the
001067  working distribution is an approximation. These checks support finite
001068  log-mean estimation; they do not establish a fully specified outcome
001069  distribution.
001089  Residual correlation remains positive at one hour, especially near eye. The
001090  primary inference clusters by participant, while the temporal model below
001091  adds a working within-sequence correlation. Neither device turns local clock
001092  time into a primary effect.
001094  ### Influence
001130  Targeted participant deletions remained numerically stable. Leave-one-site-out
001131  results are more consequential for the day-type association, which is why the
001132  reader report describes that result as site- and model-dependent. These are
001133  diagnostic stability checks, not additional independent samples.
001135  ### Sensitivity map
001171  The gap-timing-unaware, common-hour, paired-day, chest, covariance, and
001172  working-variance outputs are preserved separately. Reusing the exact model
001173  frames across comparisons prevents a changed denominator from being hidden
001174  inside a model label.
001176  ## Exploratory local-clock analysis
001178  Time of day is described separately from the three primary average
001179  associations. The accepted exploratory analysis has two parts: a binomial
001180  model for whether hourly melEDI is positive and a Gamma log-link model for the
001181  positive magnitude. Their response-scale product gives expected melEDI.
001212  The four day-type-by-activity curves use cyclic cubic factor-by smooths with a
001213  shared smoothing parameter. They are not sum-to-zero site-deviation smooths,
001214  and no site-specific clock smooth is fitted. Site enters parametrically, and
001215  the displayed curves average equally across the nine near-eye sites. Both
001216  components use fREML and `discrete = TRUE`; no `discrete = FALSE` computation
001217  is required.
001250  The selected occurrence component uses all 16,596 primary hours; the positive
001251  magnitude component uses 11,899 positive hours. Removing zeros splits some
001252  positive-magnitude sequences, so residual correlation never bridges an
001253  excluded zero. The working residual correlations are approximately 0.418 and
001254  0.440. They are GEE-like working adjustments for non-Gaussian `bam` fits, not
001255  fully generative joint autoregressive likelihoods.
001283  The occurrence basis check remains a warning: its k-check p-values are small,
001284  although effective degrees of freedom remain below the basis ceiling. The
001285  lower-basis comparison changes some response-scale curves. Together with
001286  occurrence calibration, residual working correlation, and omitted
001287  cross-component covariance, this prevents confirmatory whole-curve inference.
001288  Active/Sedentary and Free/Work contrasts are response-scale pointwise displays
001289  with null ratio 1; filled points only indicate that a pointwise 95% interval
001290  excludes 1. They are neither simultaneous bands nor multiplicity-controlled
001291  whole-curve tests.
001293  ### Additional diary measures
001320  Exercise location, active travel, sedentary time, previous sleep onset, and
001321  final wake time are retained as exploratory context. They are not inserted
001322  into the primary model, and no temperature or unsupported predictor is added.
001324  ## Code and stored outputs
001352  The full H06 manifests record every model frame, fitted object, diagnostic,
001353  table, figure, and paired source CSV. Hashes identify exact files; they do not
001354  replace the scientific checks of samples, formulas, estimands, multiplicity,
001355  and diagnostics documented above.
001367  The analysis and this provenance display use R 4.6.1 and the project library.
001368  No package is installed or updated by this render.
001370  ## How to examine the chain
001372  A reproducibility check can proceed in this order:
001374  1. verify the pinned scientific inputs and the current base manifest;
001375  2. inspect candidate flow, missingness, exact frames, and category support;
001376  3. confirm the two Wilkinson formulas, site contrasts, cluster covariance, and
001377     multiplicity families;
001378  4. inspect convergence, residual, temporal, and influence diagnostics;
001379  5. compare the required dataset, placement, paired-day, working-variance, and
001380     covariance sensitivities; and
001381  6. trace each reader table and figure to its paired stored CSV and manifest
001382     identity.
001404  This record preserves the analysis boundary: the primary estimand is an
001405  average supported-hour association, the site-varying model provides localized
001406  context, and the clock-time analysis remains exploratory and separate. The
001407  companion [H06 results](../../../notebooks/hypotheses/H06.html) provide the
001408  reader-facing estimates and qualifications.

===== notebooks/hypotheses/H07.qmd =====
000613  ## Question
000615  The preregistered hypothesis was:
000617  > “H7: There is a ceiling (nonlinear) effect of absolute latitude and photoperiod on level-, duration-, and exposure-history-based light metrics.”
000619  This report asks the part of that question that these data can address: is a
000620  pooled association between civil photoperiod and each planned personal
000621  light-exposure metric visible, and does the fitted association change from a
000622  pointwise detected increase to a derivative that remains statistically
000623  compatible with zero through the longest recorded photoperiod?
000625  ::: {.callout-note title="Answer in brief"}
000626  A derivative-defined plateau pattern was visible for **six of nine primary
000627  near-eye metrics**: mean melEDI, brightest 10 h mean, darkest 10 h mean, time
000628  above 1,000 lx melEDI, time above 250 lx melEDI during wake, and melEDI dose.
000629  Their transition brackets ranged from
000630  `r fmt_range(min(near_patterns$plateau_transition_lower), max(near_patterns$plateau_start))`
000631  h of civil photoperiod. The complementary chest analysis showed the pattern
000632  for **seven of nine metrics**: mean melEDI, darkest 10 h mean, both
000633  above-threshold durations, time below 1 lx melEDI during sleep, the longest
000634  continuous period above 250 lx melEDI, and melEDI dose. Their transition
000635  brackets ranged from
000636  `r fmt_range(min(chest_patterns$plateau_transition_lower), max(chest_patterns$plateau_start))`
000637  h. These are visible pooled-data patterns under a pointwise derivative rule,
000638  not evidence that a physiological or environmental ceiling exists. Absolute
000639  latitude cannot be separated from site in this design, and site, collection
000640  period, incomplete photoperiod overlap, model form, and influential sites
000641  materially limit interpretation.
000642  :::
000644  ## What was analysed
000646  All nine planned metrics were analysed independently of fitted results from
000647  other hypotheses. Near-eye measurements are primary because they more closely
000648  represent light near the eyes; chest measurements provide complementary
000649  evidence. The placements were modelled separately. A common-sample sensitivity
000650  uses the same participants and participant-days for both placements, but it
000651  does not test or establish placement equivalence.
000653  The primary dataset retains the approved metric-specific treatment of gaps.
000654  A predefined sensitivity uses the **gap-timing-unaware dataset**. It still
000655  passed the general 50%-per-hour and 80%-per-day coverage rules. The term means
000656  that the timing of the remaining missing observations is not used for an
000657  additional metric-specific adjustment; it does not mean that gaps,
000658  missingness, or coverage were ignored. For this contrast only, the approved
000659  primary preparation could be interpreted as a **time-sensitive primary metric
000660  dataset**, because it uses the timing of remaining missing observations where
000661  that timing is relevant to the metric. Below, it is called simply the primary
000662  dataset.
000664  The analysis unit was a participant-day, with one observation for each
000665  available metric, participant, day, and placement. Repeated days were handled
000666  with a participant random-effect smooth nested within site. The main near-eye
000667  samples span 15 August 2023 to 19 October 2025; chest samples span 14 May 2024
000668  to 19 October 2025. Exact metric-specific counts are reported with each
000669  placement below.
000707  ## Model and plateau rule
000709  Each metric and placement used a separate generalized additive model fitted by
000710  restricted maximum likelihood. The reported model was:
000723  Here, `response_value` is the response shown in the specification table,
000724  `photoperiod_hours` is civil photoperiod, `site` is the study site, and
000725  `site_participant` identifies a participant within site. The smooth used a
000726  thin-plate regression spline with basis dimension *k* = 6. A broader *k* = 10
000727  smooth and a model treating site as a fixed effect were fitted as model-form
000728  sensitivities.
000730  Absolute latitude is fixed within site. It is therefore structurally
000731  confounded with site, and a latitude-by-photoperiod surface cannot separately
000732  identify a latitude association while accounting for site. The planned joint
000733  surface was inspected as an identifiability check but is not used for
000734  inference or for the plateau classification. No temperature or other
000735  unplanned environmental predictor was added.
000737  For each reported smooth, the first derivative was evaluated at 100 equally
000738  spaced values over that metric's exact recorded photoperiod range. Central
000739  finite differences used a step of 0.01 h, with one-sided differences at the
000740  two boundaries. Unconditional model-coefficient covariance produced
000741  pointwise 95% confidence intervals on the model's linear-predictor scale.
000743  A **derivative-defined plateau pattern** required all three conditions:
000745  1. the lower pointwise 95% confidence limit for the derivative was above zero
000746     at one or more photoperiod values;
000747  2. a later grid point had a 95% confidence interval containing zero; and
000748  3. every subsequent interval through the longest recorded photoperiod also
000749     contained zero.
000751  The reported transition is the bracket from the last pointwise detected
000752  increase to the first point of the sustained zero-compatible tail. This is the
000753  requested change from a statistically supported positive derivative to a
000754  non-significant derivative that remains non-significant to the end of the
000755  recorded range. The rule does not define a scientifically negligible slope,
000756  prove equivalence to zero, locate a true asymptote, or demonstrate a mechanism.
000757  The 100 pointwise checks and the nine cross-metric classifications were not
000758  multiplicity-adjusted; the classification is therefore descriptive.
000760  ## Primary near-eye result
000762  Depending on the metric, the primary near-eye models used 139–141
000763  participants, 655–816 participant-days and observations, and all nine sites.
000764  The modelled civil-photoperiod range was 10.33–20.52 h for every metric.
000773  The exact sample counts are available as
000774  [source data](../../artifacts/09_tables/H07/H07_main_samples.csv).
000791  The fitted smooths answer whether an association is visible in the pooled
000792  data; the derivative panels apply the stated pattern rule. The figures do not
000793  require every site to cover every photoperiod, but the rugs make the uneven
000794  empirical support visible.
000803  The endpoint derivative for the longest continuous period above 250 lx melEDI
000804  remained positive, so this metric did not satisfy the pattern rule. Neither
000805  low-light duration had a preceding interval with a pointwise detected positive
000806  derivative. For time below 10 lx melEDI before sleep, the endpoint derivative
000807  was negative rather than zero-compatible; this is not a positive-to-flat
000808  pattern.
000810  The plotted response-scale smooths and derivative calculations are available
000811  as [smooth source data](../../artifacts/09_tables/H07/H07_main_curve_points.csv),
000812  [derivative source data](../../artifacts/09_tables/H07/H07_revised_derivative_points.csv),
000813  and [figure settings](../../artifacts/09_tables/H07/H07_revised_paired_figure_settings.csv).
000815  ## Complementary chest result
000817  Depending on the metric, the chest models used 153–154 participants, 743–902
000818  participant-days and observations, and eight sites. The modelled
000819  civil-photoperiod range was again 10.33–20.52 h. Tübingen contributed near-eye
000820  but not chest data, which is one reason the chest result is complementary
000821  rather than a direct replication on an identical sample.
000852  Brightest 10 h mean remained pointwise increasing at the recorded maximum.
000853  Time below 10 lx melEDI before sleep again lacked a preceding pointwise
000854  detected increase. The late transition for time below 1 lx melEDI during sleep
000855  is especially uncertain because its response-distribution check failed, as
000856  described below.
000858  ## Model checks
000860  All 18 reported models converged. Gradients were small and all reported
000861  Hessian minima were positive. Consecutive-day residual dependence was modest
000862  in the pooled descriptive check, with maximum absolute lag-1 correlations of
000863  `r fmt_number(max(abs(adapted_diagnostics$pooled_consecutive_day_lag1[adapted_diagnostics$placement == "near_eye"])), 3)`
000864  near eye and
000865  `r fmt_number(max(abs(adapted_diagnostics$pooled_consecutive_day_lag1[adapted_diagnostics$placement == "chest"])), 3)`
000866  at the chest. This does not prove temporal independence, but it did not
000867  indicate a strong residual day-to-day process after participant clustering.
000918  The basis-dimension check flagged three near-eye smooths and one chest smooth.
000919  The expanded-basis sensitivity therefore carries more weight than a single
000920  diagnostic threshold: it reproduced 16 of 17 evaluable classifications, with
000921  one near-eye fit failing its Hessian check and the chest sleep low-light
000922  classification changing.
000924  Residual normal-reference correlations were generally high, but residuals
000925  still showed fitted-value dependence and tail departures for some outcomes.
000926  The Tweedie response checks were adequate for the two above-threshold duration
000927  metrics. They were not adequate for time below 1 lx melEDI during sleep: each
000928  placement had four observed zeros, the fitted model expected fewer than 0.0001
000929  zeros, and none of 100 pilot simulations contained a zero. This distributional
000930  mismatch makes the chest sleep low-light pattern particularly weak evidence;
000931  no larger simulation can repair the response model's failure to reproduce the
000932  observed zero mass.
000934  The photoperiod smooth also had estimated concurvity of approximately 0.9985–
000935  0.9988 with the participant random-effect smooth. This reflects the clustered,
000936  repeated-day design and limits separation of between-person photoperiod
000937  coverage from the pooled smooth. Smooth-term p-values are therefore not used
000938  or displayed. The derivative intervals remain conditional on the fitted model
000939  and should be read with this identifiability limitation.
000941  ## Sensitivity analyses
000943  The gap-timing-unaware dataset reproduced all nine near-eye classifications
000944  and eight of nine chest classifications; the chest melEDI-dose pattern was the
000945  exception. The paired common sample used 110–112 participants, 505–643
000946  participant-days and observations, and eight sites. It reproduced four of nine
000947  near-eye and six of nine chest classifications, showing that several pooled
000948  patterns depend on which participant-days and sites contribute rather than on
000949  placement alone.
001012  Restricting the longest continuous period above 250 lx melEDI to days whose
001013  period duration was exactly identified changed the near-eye classification
001014  from no pattern to a pattern at 15.38–15.48 h; the chest classification was
001015  unchanged. Comparing the observed and corrected melEDI-dose definitions on
001016  identical rows left both placement classifications unchanged. Restricting the
001017  two preparation variants to exact common rows preserved every near-eye
001018  classification but removed the chest sleep low-light pattern in both variants.
001043  The broader smooth reproduced eight of eight evaluable near-eye and eight of
001044  nine chest classifications; the near-eye longest-period fit failed its Hessian
001045  check, and the chest sleep low-light pattern disappeared. Treating site as a
001046  fixed effect reproduced eight of nine classifications at each placement: the
001047  near-eye longest-period pattern appeared, while the chest sleep low-light
001048  pattern disappeared. These changes reinforce that both metrics sit near a
001049  classification boundary.
001051  ## Site influence and support
001053  Omitting one site at a time did not act as a new eligibility rule; it tested
001054  whether a pooled pattern depended strongly on one site's observations. Near-eye
001055  mean melEDI and both low-light metrics reproduced their main classification in
001056  all nine omissions. Five positive near-eye patterns changed only when Tübingen
001057  was omitted. The near-eye longest-period classification changed in four of
001058  nine omissions. Chest results were less stable for several outcomes, most
001059  notably time below 1 lx melEDI during sleep, which matched the main
001060  classification in only three of eight omissions.
001076  The observed civil-photoperiod range was broad overall but uneven across
001077  sites. For example, near-eye observations ranged from 10.33 to 12.35 h in
001078  Madrid, 16.41 to 17.45 h in Munich, and 12.49 to 12.71 h in Kumasi; San José
001079  contributed only a narrow range around 13.47 h, while Borås covered values up
001080  to 20.52 h. Sites were also measured in different collection periods and, in some
001081  cases, different years. Thus the pooled smooth can answer whether an
001082  association is visible in this dataset, but it cannot separate photoperiod
001083  from every site-specific or collection-period difference. Leave-one-site-out
001084  changes show where this limitation affects the descriptive classification.
001086  ## Interpretation
001088  The pooled analysis shows several fitted photoperiod associations that rise
001089  and then meet the prespecified pointwise rule for a sustained
001090  zero-compatible derivative. The recurring patterns for mean melEDI, darkest
001091  10 h mean, both above-threshold duration metrics, and melEDI dose across the
001092  two placements are the most consistent descriptive findings. Brightest 10 h
001093  mean differs by placement, the longest-period result is sensitive to sample
001094  definition and model form, and the chest sleep low-light result is undermined
001095  by distributional failure, common-row analyses, model-form changes, and site
001096  influence.
001098  The strongest defensible conclusion is therefore that **derivative-defined
001099  plateau patterns are visible for several personal light-exposure metrics in
001100  the pooled data**, subject to substantial design and model limitations. The
001101  results do not establish a ceiling, an equivalence region, a causal effect of
001102  photoperiod, a distinct latitude effect, a health or clinical consequence, or
001103  equivalence between near-eye and chest placement.
001105  Exact input identities, metric-specific model frames, evaluated formulas,
001106  diagnostic provenance, code order, and output identities are documented in
001107  the [H07 analysis preparation and provenance companion](../../audit/hypotheses/H07/H07_analysis_preparation.html).
001109  Models were fitted with R `r session_record$r_version[[1]]`, mgcv
001110  `r session_record$mgcv_version[[1]]`, and gratia
001111  `r session_record$gratia_version[[1]]`.

===== audit/hypotheses/H07/H07_analysis_preparation.qmd =====
000413  ::: {.callout-note title="Execution boundary"}
000414  This companion reads frozen H07 inputs, participant-day model frames, fitted
000415  output summaries, diagnostics, figures, and manifests. Its executable cells
000416  perform identity, schema, key, value, and lightweight descriptive checks and
000417  format tables. They do not fit or refit a model, predict a curve, calculate a
000418  derivative, rerun a diagnostic simulation, bootstrap, or otherwise alter a
000419  scientific result. The interpreted findings are in the
000420  [H07 results report](../../../notebooks/hypotheses/H07.html).
000421  :::
000423  The **gap-timing-unaware dataset** is a predefined sensitivity in which the
000424  timing of remaining missing observations is not used for an additional
000425  metric-specific correction. The general 50%-per-hour and 80%-per-day coverage
000426  rules still apply, so gaps and missingness are not ignored. For this contrast,
000427  the primary preparation could be interpreted as a **time-sensitive primary
000428  metric dataset** because it uses gap timing where that timing is relevant to a
000429  metric. Below, the main input is called simply the primary dataset.
000431  ## Scientific target
000433  The preregistered hypothesis was:
000435  > “H7: There is a ceiling (nonlinear) effect of absolute latitude and photoperiod on level-, duration-, and exposure-history-based light metrics.”
000437  The design cannot separate absolute latitude from site, because each site has
000438  one latitude. The estimable target is therefore narrower: for each of nine
000439  planned metrics and each placement, describe the pooled association with civil
000440  photoperiod and ask whether the fitted smooth changes from a pointwise detected
000441  increase to a first derivative whose pointwise interval contains zero at every
000442  remaining grid point through the longest recorded photoperiod. This is a
000443  derivative-defined plateau pattern. It is not an equivalence test and does not
000444  establish a physiological or environmental ceiling.
000486  ## Verified inputs and exact frames
000488  ### Consequential inputs
000490  The primary near-eye and chest inputs are
000491  `artifacts/06_model_data/base/metrics_glasses_participant_day_enriched.rds`
000492  and
000493  `artifacts/06_model_data/base/metrics_chest_participant_day_enriched.rds`.
000494  Site-by-date civil photoperiod comes from
000495  `artifacts/06_model_data/context/site_solar_context.rds`. The sensitivity input
000496  is the recorded `participant_day_metrics.rds` object identified below. The
000497  downloadable identity table retains every exact repository-relative path.
000499  The inputs include the sealed METRIC-011 numerical-zero normalization. It
000500  changed three near-eye and five chest darkest-10-h-mean cells from a residual
000501  `4.163336342344337e-17` lx to exact zero. A bounded reconciliation reopened
000502  only L10 mean model branches, their declared diagnostics and sensitivities,
000503  and BH fields in families containing the L10 slot. It preserved every
000504  non-L10 fit and raw test and changed no H07 scientific classification,
000505  transition grid point, REPORT-008 display value, or fitted sample.
000539  [METRIC-011 reconciliation summary](../../../artifacts/12_manifests/H07/H07_METRIC-011_reconciliation_summary.csv)
000568  [Input-identity source data](../../../artifacts/11_source_data/H07/preparation/H07_preparation_input_identities.csv)
000570  ### Identity, schema, keys, and values
000572  Each primary frame has one row per site, participant, local date, metric, and
000573  placement. The unique key is `site + Id + local_date` within a metric and
000574  placement. Required fields are site, participant, date, absolute latitude,
000575  civil photoperiod, original metric value, transformed response, nested
000576  participant identifier, metric identity, and run identity. All 18 frozen
000577  frames match their recorded serialized-object hashes; their keys are unique,
000578  their response transformations reconstruct exactly, and their stored counts
000579  match the frames.
000592  [Frame-integrity source data](../../../artifacts/11_source_data/H07/preparation/H07_preparation_frame_integrity.csv)
000594  No frame is selected using a fitted result from another hypothesis. Missing
000595  metric values are removed within the relevant metric and placement, so the
000596  sample is metric-specific. Civil photoperiod and latitude must be finite.
000597  Temperature is not part of the contract and is not introduced.
000599  ## Candidate samples, exclusions, and support
000601  ### Exact fitted samples
000603  The primary near-eye analysis contains 139–141 participants, 655–816
000604  participant-days and observations, and nine sites, depending on the metric.
000605  The complementary chest analysis contains 153–154 participants, 743–902
000606  participant-days and observations, and eight sites. One participant-day is one
000607  observation; therefore the participant-day and observation counts are equal.
000659  [Metric-sample source data](../../../artifacts/11_source_data/H07/preparation/H07_preparation_sample_support.csv)
000661  ### Site, photoperiod, latitude, and collection period
000663  Absolute latitude is constant within every site in every primary frame. It is
000664  therefore structurally inseparable from site. Civil photoperiod varies within
000665  site over collection dates, but the site ranges overlap only partially and
000666  collection periods differ by site. The pooled smooth can describe a visible
000667  association in the combined data; it cannot isolate photoperiod from every
000668  site-specific, seasonal, or collection-period difference.
000682  [Site-range source data](../../../artifacts/11_source_data/H07/preparation/H07_preparation_site_photoperiod_ranges.csv)
000713  The downloadable all-metric table contains 153 metric-by-placement-by-site
000714  rows, preserving metric-specific participants, participant-days, dates,
000715  latitude, and photoperiod ranges.
000717  [All metric-specific site-support source data](../../../artifacts/11_source_data/H07/preparation/H07_preparation_site_support.csv)
000719  The earlier pooled joint-support grid is retained as an identifiability
000720  diagnostic, not as an eligibility rule. The derivative classification uses
000721  the complete recorded metric-specific photoperiod range. Partial site overlap
000722  therefore does not erase the association question, but it remains a central
000723  limitation on interpretation and motivates the site-omission checks.
000725  ## Metrics, units, and response construction
000727  All nine planned metrics enter independently. Reader-facing names use melEDI
000728  and “period.” Internal identifiers are retained only for reproducibility.
000765  The three illuminance-like metrics, the longest continuous period above 250
000766  lx melEDI, and melEDI dose use `log10(metric + 0.1)` with a Gaussian identity
000767  model. Time above 1,000 lx melEDI, time above 250 lx melEDI during wake, and
000768  time below 1 lx melEDI during sleep use a Tweedie distribution with log link.
000769  Time below 10 lx melEDI before sleep is untransformed Gaussian. The response
000770  reconstruction check confirms these rules exactly in every frozen frame.
000772  ## Temporal order and repeated participant-days
000774  Dates are local calendar dates. Civil photoperiod is joined by site and local
000775  date. Frames are ordered by site, participant, and date before fitting.
000776  Participants contribute repeated days, so each model contains a random-effect
000777  smooth for participant nested within site. Site also has its own random-effect
000778  smooth. One participant-day contributes only one row per metric and placement;
000779  there is no within-day residual time series in H07.
000781  No independent temperature, season, or collection-period predictor is added.
000782  Season and collection period remain embedded in the observed date and
000783  photoperiod pattern. Consecutive-day residual correlations are descriptive
000784  checks of remaining temporal dependence rather than a fitted autocorrelation
000785  structure.
000787  ## Models and estimands
000789  ### Exact evaluated formulas
000791  Every formula below is the exact Wilkinson formula supplied to the recorded
000792  model fit. The reported pooled association uses:
000823  [Formula-registry source data](../../../artifacts/11_source_data/H07/preparation/H07_preparation_formula_registry.csv)
000825  The fitting call was `mgcv::gam()` with REML, `na.action = stats::na.fail`,
000826  unused factor levels dropped, and the default non-tracing `gam.control`. It was
000827  not a `bam()` fit. Consequently, `discrete` has no role in H07 and no
000828  `discrete = FALSE` alternative is required. The latitude–photoperiod tensor
000829  models are identifiability diagnostics rather than the source of the reported
000830  plateau classifications.
000853  ### Association and derivative estimands
000855  The association estimand is the fitted pooled civil-photoperiod smooth for one
000856  metric and placement, conditional on the model structure. Response-scale
000857  curves are used for scientific display. The plateau estimand is the first
000858  derivative of that smooth on the model's linear-predictor scale per hour of
000859  civil photoperiod.
000861  For each metric and placement, derivatives are evaluated at 100 equally spaced
000862  points from the exact minimum to maximum recorded photoperiod. Central finite
000863  differences use an increment of 0.01 h; the lower boundary uses a forward
000864  difference and the upper boundary a backward difference. The pointwise 95%
000865  interval uses the unconditional covariance matrix.
000867  A pattern is present only when:
000869  1. the immediately preceding grid point has a derivative interval wholly
000870     above zero;
000871  2. the next point has a derivative interval containing zero; and
000872  3. every later point through the recorded maximum also has an interval
000873     containing zero.
000875  The transition is bracketed by the preceding and current grid points. A
000876  non-significant derivative at one point, a visual flattening, or an AIC
000877  threshold is not sufficient. The rule has no scientifically defensible
000878  equivalence margin, no simultaneous curve band, and no curve-wide or
000879  cross-metric multiplicity adjustment. It therefore supports only a
000880  pointwise, descriptive derivative-defined pattern, not a ceiling claim.
000894  Smooth-term p-values and latitude–photoperiod tensor tests are not released as
000895  evidence because the design shows structural latitude/site aliasing and very
000896  high participant-smooth concurvity. No p-value enters the reported derivative
000897  classification.
000927  ## Diagnostic evidence
000929  All 18 reported photoperiod-smooth fits converged. Four smooths—three near-eye
000930  and one chest—met the recorded basis-dimension flag criterion, motivating the
000931  broader-basis sensitivity. Estimated concurvity supplied by the nested
000932  participant smooth to the photoperiod smooth was approximately 0.9985–0.9988.
000933  This is the principal identifiability limitation: the pooled photoperiod shape
000934  is difficult to separate from which participants contribute at which
000935  photoperiods.
000964  Residual summaries retained fitted-value dependence and tail checks for every
000965  metric. Consecutive-day lag-1 correlations did not cross the prespecified 0.30
000966  descriptive warning threshold, but that does not prove temporal independence.
000968  Three metrics use a Tweedie response. A frozen 100-draw pilot checked zero
000969  mass and positive-tail behaviour. The two above-threshold duration metrics
000970  were broadly compatible with the fitted distributions. For time below 1 lx
000971  melEDI during sleep, each placement had four observed zeros, the analytic
000972  expected count was below 0.0001, and none of the 100 pilot draws contained a
000973  zero. This distributional failure weakens any pattern for that metric; more
000974  simulation cannot repair the model's inability to reproduce the observed zero
000975  mass.
001002  The pilot is displayed rather than expanded to production computation. No
001003  bootstrap, simulation, or derivative calculation runs when this page renders.
001005  ## Sensitivity and influence provenance
001007  ### Candidate samples
001009  The sensitivity battery preserves exact samples for the gap-timing-unaware
001010  dataset, paired/common placement rows, cross-dataset common rows, exactly
001011  identified longest-period rows, and observed/corrected melEDI dose on common
001012  rows.
001028  ### Classification stability
001030  The gap-timing-unaware dataset reproduced all nine near-eye classifications
001031  and eight of nine chest classifications. The paired/common sample reproduced
001032  four of nine near-eye and six of nine chest classifications. Cross-dataset
001033  common rows preserved all near-eye classifications and eight of nine chest
001034  classifications. The exactly identified longest-period sensitivity changed
001035  the near-eye classification. These comparisons show that several patterns are
001036  sample- or definition-sensitive.
001068  The broader `k = 10` smooth had one near-eye Hessian failure and reproduced 16
001069  of 17 evaluable classifications. Fixed site effects reproduced 16 of 18.
001070  Neither sensitivity resolves the underlying latitude/site identity problem.
001072  ### Leave-one-site-out influence
001074  Every site represented in a metric frame was omitted once and the same
001075  photoperiod model and derivative rule were applied to the reduced frame. This
001076  is an influence diagnostic, not a support gate. Only three of nine metrics per
001077  placement retained the same classification after every site omission.
001122  The combination of incomplete site overlap, collection-period differences,
001123  high concurvity, and site-omission changes means that a visible pooled pattern
001124  must not be interpreted as an isolated effect of photoperiod or latitude.
001126  ## Code order and output provenance
001128  The following scripts constitute the current result-producing chain. The
001129  analysis core constructs frames and fits models; later scripts read stored
001130  fits to prepare diagnostics, sensitivities, derivative classifications, and
001131  figures. The final two rows build descriptive provenance artifacts and render
001132  this companion without refitting.
001168  ### Durable outputs
001198  Every preparation figure has a paired source CSV. The two results figures use
001199  `H07_main_curve_points.csv`, `H07_revised_derivative_points.csv`,
001200  `H07_revised_plateau_summary.csv`, and
001201  `H07_revised_derivative_photoperiod_rows.csv`; their 9 × 18 inch, 270-dpi
001202  export geometry is recorded in `H07_revised_paired_figure_settings.csv`.
001203  Physical-size readability evidence is retained separately with the final
001204  artifact identities.
001206  ## Computational environment and bounded reproduction
001224  The project library and R 4.6.1 are authoritative. A bounded reproduction of
001225  the display/provenance layer runs the preparation-artifact builder, renders
001226  the H07 results and this companion, rebuilds the H07 preparation manifest,
001227  and runs the H07-focused tests. It does not rerun the model fits or the frozen
001228  100-draw distributional pilot.
001230  ::: {.callout-tip title="Interpretation boundary"}
001231  The complete provenance supports one defensible statement: several pooled
001232  civil-photoperiod smooths show the declared derivative-defined plateau pattern
001233  within the recorded data. It does not support a distinct latitude effect, a
001234  causal effect of photoperiod, a physiological or environmental ceiling,
001235  near-eye/chest equivalence, or a clinical or health-outcome claim. See the
001236  [H07 results report](../../../notebooks/hypotheses/H07.html) for metric-level
001237  estimates, 95% intervals, figures, diagnostics, and sensitivity
001238  interpretation.
001239  :::

===== notebooks/hypotheses/H08.qmd =====
000271  ## Hypothesis and analytical question
000273  The preregistered hypothesis was:
000275  > **H8: Duration-, exposure-history-, and level-based metrics are associated
000276  > with VLSQ-8 light sensitivity scores.**
000278  The analytical question is whether participant-level self-reported visual
000279  light sensitivity is associated with repeated participant-day personal
000280  light-exposure metrics after adjustment for study site. Near-eye measurements
000281  are primary because they more closely represent light near the eyes during
000282  wear. Chest measurements provide complementary evidence and are not pooled
000283  with the near-eye measurements.
000285  ::: {.callout-note title="Results in brief"}
000286  Across nine light-exposure metrics, neither the primary near-eye average
000287  associations nor their site-specific heterogeneity met the multiplicity-adjusted
000288  criterion. The strongest near-eye directional pattern was lower corrected
000289  melEDI dose per VLSQ-8 SD (ratio 0.846, 95% CI 0.716–0.999; raw *p* = 0.051,
000290  BH-adjusted *p* = 0.160), so it is not a multiplicity-retained finding.
000291  Complementary chest results and the central sensitivity analyses did not
000292  change that conclusion, although site influence and specified model
000293  limitations require caution.
000294  :::
000296  ::: {.callout-note title="Exact-zero normalization audit"}
000297  Shared decision [METRIC-011](../../audit/decisions/l10_numerical_zero_normalization.md)
000298  normalised eight primary L10 participant-day round-off remnants to exact zero
000299  (three near eye and five chest). A bounded H08 reseal updated only inherited
000300  L10 branches and the four affected BH families. It changed no displayed
000301  effect, p-value, retained decision, diagnostic disposition, sensitivity
000302  classification, or interpretation; all untargeted results and model objects
000303  passed the recorded [reconciliation](../../artifacts/12_manifests/H08/H08_metric011_reconciliation.csv).
000304  :::
000306  ## What was analysed
000308  VLSQ-8 was available for all 184 participants across nine sites. The stored
000309  score equalled the sum of eight ordered 1–5 item codes plus 5 for every
000310  participant. Scores ranged from 13 to 39, with a mean of 21.598 and a
000311  participant SD of 5.540. The raw score was centred at the observed mean; no
000312  within-site standardisation was used.
000314  Nine participant-day outcomes covered light level, duration, continuous
000315  periods, and exposure history. All use manuscript metric names and melEDI
000316  units. The longest continuous period is an observed lower bound unless its
000317  start and end are both exactly identified; that stricter definition is
000318  examined as a sensitivity.
000320  The predefined preparation sensitivity is called the
000321  **gap-timing-unaware dataset**. It still passed the general 50%-per-hour and
000322  80%-per-day coverage rules. The name means that the timing of the remaining
000323  missing observations is not used for an additional metric-specific adjustment;
000324  it does not mean that gaps, missingness, or coverage were ignored. For this
000325  one-time first explanation, the primary could be interpreted as a
000326  time-sensitive primary metric dataset because its approved preparation uses
000327  the timing of remaining missing observations where that timing is relevant.
000328  From here onward, it is called simply the **primary dataset**.
000359  ## Statistical models
000361  Participant-day models include a participant random intercept nested within
000362  site. Fixed site effects use the submitted site order and explicit sum
000363  contrasts, so the additive VLSQ-8 coefficient is the site-adjusted average
000364  slope rather than the slope at one reference site. The primary likelihood-ratio
000365  test compares the site-only and additive models. A separate test compares the
000366  additive and interaction models to ask whether the slope differs by site.
000368  The following evaluated cell constructs and prints every exact Wilkinson
000369  formula used for the principal comparisons and the two structured
000370  sensitivities.
000417  Average-association and site-heterogeneity tests were adjusted separately.
000418  Near-eye and chest placements and the primary and gap-timing-unaware datasets
000419  also had separate families, giving eight complete nine-test Benjamini–Hochberg
000420  families. Paired-placement and common-sample sensitivities report estimates
000421  and 95% confidence intervals without creating additional *p*-value screens.
000448  ## Exact fitted samples
000450  The primary near-eye models used 139–141 participants, 655–816
000451  participant-days, and all nine sites, depending on the metric. Complementary
000452  chest models used 153–154 participants, 743–902 participant-days, and eight
000453  sites. Every model used its metric-specific complete frame.
000478  The complete sample index is available as
000479  [source data](../../artifacts/06_model_data/H08/H08_model_frame_index.csv),
000480  with [site-specific cells](../../artifacts/06_model_data/H08/H08_model_frame_by_site.csv).
000482  ## Primary near-eye results
000484  None of the nine average near-eye associations remained statistically
000485  supported after the complete BH correction. Several point estimates suggested
000486  lower bright-light exposure at higher VLSQ-8 scores, but their uncertainty and
000487  family-adjusted evidence do not support a confirmatory claim.
000489  The corrected melEDI-dose ratio was 0.846 per VLSQ-8 SD (95% CI
000490  0.716–0.999). Its likelihood-ratio raw *p* was 0.051 and BH-adjusted *p* was
000491  0.160. The Wald interval and likelihood-ratio test use different inferential
000492  constructions; the complete-family conclusion is therefore inconclusive, not
000493  statistically significant.
000518  Figure and result sources are available as
000519  [figure source data](../../artifacts/11_source_data/H08/H08_near_eye_effects_data.csv)
000520  and the [complete numerical result table](../../artifacts/09_tables/H08/H08_model_results_master.csv).
000522  ### Centred prediction contrast
000524  Predictions at half an SD below and above the observed VLSQ-8 mean provide a
000525  natural-scale description of the fitted contrast. They are not additional
000526  hypothesis tests.
000563  ## Complementary chest results
000565  The complementary chest analysis repeated the same score, metric definitions,
000566  response models, fixed-site adjustment, and nine-test family. No average chest
000567  association remained after its separate BH correction. The directional
000568  pattern broadly resembled the near-eye result but does not create a second
000569  primary finding.
000594  [Chest figure source data](../../artifacts/11_source_data/H08/H08_chest_effects_data.csv)
000595  contain the plotted values and intervals.
000597  ## Site-specific heterogeneity
000599  The separate interaction tests ask whether the VLSQ-8 slope differs across
000600  submitted sites. None of the nine near-eye or nine chest interaction tests
000601  remained after their placement-specific BH corrections. Site-specific slopes
000602  are therefore descriptive components of globally inconclusive heterogeneity
000603  tests, not independently supported site findings.
000639  All site-specific slopes and 95% intervals, using submitted site names and
000640  order, are available in the
000641  [site-slope source table](../../artifacts/09_tables/H08/H08_site_specific_slopes.csv).
000643  ## Exact paired-placement comparison
000645  Near-eye and chest models were fitted separately on identical participant-day
000646  keys. The comparison does not pool placements, estimate a within-day placement
000647  difference, or establish equivalence. Ratio outcomes are shown on a common
000648  natural-log scale; the additive pre-sleep hour difference remains in the
000649  adjacent table because the two units must not share an axis.
000701  [Paired-placement figure and table source data](../../artifacts/11_source_data/H08/H08_paired_placement_effects_data.csv)
000702  include the exact sample hashes.
000704  ## Model adequacy and influence
000706  All 36 primary-dataset additive and interaction fits converged, had
000707  positive-definite Hessians, were nonsingular, and retained full-rank fixed
000708  effects. No primary or complementary effect was non-estimable. The common
000709  response-family gate retained all nine prespecified specifications; review
000710  flags require qualification, not a data-dependent change of model family.
000749  Three limitations are material. Darkest 10 h mean showed increasing residual
000750  spread with fitted values (absolute-residual versus fitted Spearman
000751  correlations 0.341 near eye and 0.349 chest). Time below 1 lx melEDI during
000752  sleep contained four exact zeros at each placement, whereas the fitted
000753  Tweedie models assigned near-zero expected counts. Corrected melEDI dose
000754  passed the numerical checks, but its value depends on the timing of remaining
000755  missing observations and is interpreted with the dataset and observed-dose
000756  sensitivities below.
000812  The [complete diagnostic table](../../artifacts/08_diagnostics/H08/H08_model_diagnostics.csv)
000813  and [adequacy-figure source data](../../artifacts/11_source_data/H08/H08_near_eye_model_adequacy_data.csv)
000814  contain the full checks.
000816  All 153 planned leave-one-site-out refits succeeded. Some estimates changed
000817  direction or magnitude when one site was omitted; this reinforces the
000818  inconclusive interpretation but does not identify a site for exclusion. No
000819  site or participant was removed after influence review.
000850  The ranked
000851  [participant-influence screen](../../artifacts/08_diagnostics/H08/H08_participant_influence_screen.csv)
000852  is an audit list rather than a deletion rule.
000854  ## Dataset sensitivity
000856  The strongest comparison fits the primary and gap-timing-unaware values on
000857  identical participant-day keys for every metric and placement. All 18 of
000858  these exact-common-sample comparisons were stable within model uncertainty.
000859  The scenario-specific all-available families also retained the same
000860  BH-adjusted conclusion. Near-eye wake time above 250 lx melEDI and corrected
000861  melEDI dose were precision-sensitive in the all-available comparison because
000862  zero-exclusion by a component 95% interval changed; all chest comparisons
000863  were stable. Neither classification creates a new hypothesis test.
000969  [Dataset-sensitivity figure source data](../../artifacts/11_source_data/H08/H08_gap_common_sample_effects_data.csv)
000970  and the [complete comparison table](../../artifacts/09_tables/H08/H08_gap_timing_unaware_sensitivity.csv)
000971  retain the exact row hashes and all-available classifications.
000973  ## Other structured sensitivities
000975  Linear centred photoperiod was added only as a sensitivity covariate, with
000976  site retained and no photoperiod interaction. Participant-summary models give
000977  each participant one outcome value and use the corresponding fixed-effects
000978  model. All 36 additive sensitivity fits converged and retained full-rank
000979  fixed effects. Their estimates were broadly compatible with the
000980  participant-day results and do not form new multiplicity families.
001068  The
001069  [photoperiod-adjusted](../../artifacts/09_tables/H08/H08_photoperiod_sensitivity.csv)
001070  and
001071  [participant-summary](../../artifacts/09_tables/H08/H08_participant_summary_sensitivity.csv)
001072  tables contain exact samples and fitted-frame hashes.
001074  ### Metric-definition sensitivities
001076  The primary longest-period metric is an observed lower bound. Requiring exact
001077  identification reduced the sample and produced imprecise estimates: near-eye
001078  ratio 0.958 (95% CI 0.856–1.072) and chest ratio 0.939 (95% CI
001079  0.855–1.032). On the exactly paired placement sample, the ratios were 0.943
001080  near eye and 0.952 chest, with both intervals crossing one.
001082  The observed-dose comparison holds the corrected-dose fitted sample fixed and
001083  replaces the value with uncorrected observed dose. It is descriptive and has
001084  no additional *p*-value family. The all-available near-eye ratio was 0.842
001085  (95% CI 0.713–0.995); its paired-sample interval crossed one, and the chest
001086  all-available interval included one. These component intervals do not
001087  override the primary BH family.
001126  Exact samples and hashes are available for the
001127  [longest-period](../../artifacts/09_tables/H08/H08_exactly_identified_longest_period_sensitivity.csv)
001128  and [observed-dose](../../artifacts/09_tables/H08/H08_observed_dose_sensitivity.csv)
001129  sensitivities.
001131  ## Interpretation
001133  On the accepted score, metric, sample, and model definitions, the observed
001134  data provide no multiplicity-adjusted evidence that VLSQ-8 score is associated
001135  with any of the nine primary near-eye personal light-exposure metrics. The
001136  complementary chest analyses and the central sensitivity analyses do not
001137  materially strengthen that evidence. Several estimates point toward lower
001138  bright-light exposure at higher scores, but their uncertainty, complete-family
001139  adjustment, and site influence make that directional pattern inconclusive.
001141  This conclusion is not evidence that every true association is exactly zero,
001142  and the paired-placement comparison is not an equivalence test. The analysis
001143  is observational: it does not establish that visual light sensitivity changes
001144  personal light exposure, nor does it support physiological or health-effect
001145  claims.
001147  ## Reproducibility
001149  The [H08 analysis preparation and provenance companion](../../audit/hypotheses/H08/H08_analysis_preparation.html)
001150  documents the verified path from analytical inputs to these reported outputs.
001152  All displayed estimates are read from the accepted, hash-audited H08 outputs;
001153  this report does not refit an inferential model. Consequential
001154  calculations were produced in R 4.6.1. The
001155  [master result table](../../artifacts/09_tables/H08/H08_model_results_master.csv),
001156  the [METRIC-011 result comparison](../../artifacts/09_tables/H08/H08_metric011_result_comparison.csv),
001157  the [complete-family recalculation record](../../artifacts/09_tables/H08/H08_metric011_bh_recalculation.csv),
001158  and the [figure manifest and alt text](../../artifacts/12_manifests/H08/H08_figure_manifest.csv)
001159  provide the numerical audit trail. The H08 reader-report manifest retains the
001160  file-level identities.

===== audit/hypotheses/H08/H08_analysis_preparation.qmd =====
000233  ::: {.callout-note title="About this analysis record"}
000234  This page explains how verified VLSQ-8 questionnaire scores and personal
000235  light-exposure metrics were turned into the H08 results. The companion
000236  [H08 results report](../../../notebooks/hypotheses/H08.html) presents the
000237  scientific findings.
000239  Rendering this page recalculates only file-identity checks, schema and key
000240  checks, and lightweight descriptive summaries from frozen H08 model frames.
000241  It reads stored fits, estimates, 95% confidence intervals, diagnostics,
000242  influence screens, and sensitivity outputs from their recorded files. It does
000243  not fit or refit a model, calculate predictions, re-estimate residual
000244  diagnostics, rerun site-omission analyses, bootstrap, resample, or simulate
000245  data.
000246  :::
000248  ## Purpose
000250  H08 asks whether nine duration-, exposure-history-, and level-based personal
000251  light-exposure metrics are associated with VLSQ-8 visual-light-sensitivity
000252  scores. Near-eye measurement is primary because it more closely represents
000253  light near the eyes during wear. All-available chest measurement is
000254  complementary and is not pooled with near-eye measurement. Every inferential
000255  outcome has one row per participant-day.
000257  The predefined preparation sensitivity uses the **gap-timing-unaware
000258  dataset**. It still passed the general requirement of at least 50% valid
000259  minutes per hour and at least 80% valid hours per day, but the timing of the
000260  remaining missing observations was not used for an additional metric-specific
000261  adjustment. For contrast at this first explanation, the primary could be
000262  interpreted as a **time-sensitive primary metric dataset**. Hereafter it is
000263  called simply the primary dataset.
000265  This page identifies the exact data, score rule, samples, formulae, code, and
000266  stored checks behind the accepted report. It documents provenance; it does
000267  not create an alternate analysis.
000269  ## Analysis map
000291  The arrows show dependencies. They do not mean that this page repeats model
000292  estimation.
000294  ## What is calculated when this page opens
000317  ## 1. Verified inputs
000319  ### File identity
000321  The accepted result chain is pinned to 16 current scientific, contextual, and
000322  provenance inputs. Four historical reconstruction inputs are retained in the
000323  complete audit record but are not used to produce the current results and are
000324  therefore omitted below.
000401  ### Schema, key, content, and value checks
000403  VLSQ-8 is keyed by site and participant. Every model frame is keyed by site,
000404  participant, local date, and metric. The 108-row model-frame index identifies
000405  one exact frame for each of 12 runs and nine metrics.
000461  ### METRIC-011 exact-zero maintenance
000463  The shared [METRIC-011 decision](../../decisions/l10_numerical_zero_normalization.md)
000464  normalised eight primary-dataset Darkest 10 h mean participant-day values from
000465  the floating-point remnant `4.163336342344337e-17` lx to exact zero: three
000466  near-eye and five chest cells. The H08 maintenance script rebuilt only the six
000467  primary-dataset L10 model branches that inherited those cells, four specified
000468  sensitivity bundles, 17 leave-one-site-out fits, and the four affected
000469  complete BH families. No resampling or full-analysis rerun was performed.
000492  The exact [result comparison](../../../artifacts/09_tables/H08/H08_metric011_result_comparison.csv),
000493  [BH recalculation](../../../artifacts/09_tables/H08/H08_metric011_bh_recalculation.csv),
000494  and [protected-scope reconciliation](../../../artifacts/12_manifests/H08/H08_metric011_reconciliation.csv)
000495  show that the accepted result displays, diagnostic dispositions, sensitivity
000496  classifications, and scientific claims did not change.
000498  ## 2. VLSQ-8 predictor
000500  ### Scale and score reconstruction
000502  VLSQ-8 contains eight ordered item codes from 1 to 5. For every participant,
000503  the stored score equalled the item sum plus 5. The constant offset does not
000504  affect a centred slope, but retaining it is necessary to reproduce the
000505  approved 13–39 score scale. All 184 participants had complete item and total
000506  scores. The score was centred at its observed participant mean, 21.598, and
000507  reported effects compare scores separated by the participant SD of 5.540
000508  points. Scores were not standardised within site.
000561  The bars use the exact aggregated
000562  [figure source data](../../../artifacts/11_source_data/H08/H08_preparation_vlsq_score_distribution.csv).
000563  The corresponding
000564  [site-specific score support](../../../artifacts/11_source_data/H08/H08_preparation_vlsq_site_support.csv)
000565  retains the submitted site order, participant counts, ranges, means, and SDs.
000567  ## 3. Metrics and analytical samples
000569  ### Metric definitions and missing values
000571  The nine outcomes retain the manuscript names and melEDI units. Unsupported
000572  values remain missing; they are never converted to darkness or zero. Four
000573  metrics use Gaussian models after `log10(value + 0.1)` transformation, three
000574  non-negative duration metrics use Tweedie models with a log link, and two use
000575  Gaussian identity models. Practical effects are ratios except for time below
000576  10 lx melEDI before sleep, which is an hour difference.
000578  The longest continuous period above 250 lx melEDI can be a lower bound when a
000579  remaining gap interrupts the observed period. A separate exact-identification
000580  sensitivity retains only participant-days for which both ends of the longest
000581  period are observed. The corrected melEDI dose uses the registered
000582  time-sensitive value; observed uncorrected dose is retained as a descriptive
000583  sensitivity on the same fitted sample.
000626  The preparation source records each available metric value and each explicit
000627  reason for an unavailable participant-day. The compact table below reports
000628  the stored counts without treating unavailable values as observations.
000660  The [availability source](../../../artifacts/11_source_data/H08/H08_preparation_metric_availability.csv)
000661  retains each metric, dataset, placement, availability reason, and count.
000663  ### Exact primary and complementary samples
000665  Each model used its metric-specific complete frame. Primary near-eye samples
000666  contained 139–141 participants, 655–816 participant-days, and nine sites.
000667  Complementary chest samples contained 153–154 participants, 743–902
000668  participant-days, and eight sites.
000710  The [exact table source](../../../artifacts/11_source_data/H08/H08_preparation_sample_support.csv)
000711  also retains run, metric, placement, row-key, and frame identities.
000766  The plot uses the same
000767  [exact source data](../../../artifacts/11_source_data/H08/H08_preparation_sample_support.csv)
000768  as the sample table.
000770  ### Site contributions
000772  The primary analysis represents all nine submitted-manuscript sites. The
000773  complementary chest analysis represents eight because chest data are absent
000774  for Tübingen. Site-specific participant and day counts vary by metric
