000775  eligibility, so the ranges below come from the nine exact model frames and
000776  not from a pooled approximation.
000864  The plotted ranges have
000865  [exact source data](../../../artifacts/11_source_data/H08/H08_preparation_site_support_summary.csv).
000866  The complete
000867  [metric-by-site source](../../../artifacts/11_source_data/H08/H08_preparation_site_support.csv)
000868  contains all 153 cells across the 18 main all-available metric frames.
000870  ## 4. Model construction and inference
000872  ### Exact formulae supplied to the models
000874  The primary specification uses fixed site with sum-to-zero contrasts and a
000875  participant random intercept nested within site. The additive coefficient is
000876  therefore a site-adjusted average VLSQ-8 slope rather than a slope at one
000877  reference site. A likelihood-ratio comparison of the site-only and additive
000878  models tests the average association. Comparing the additive and interaction
000879  models tests site heterogeneity. Photoperiod adjustment and participant-level
000880  summary models are structured sensitivities.
000882  The table retains the exact evaluated Wilkinson formulae used for the current
000883  analysis and its two structured sensitivity branches.
000917  ### Engines and estimation settings
000944  ### Multiplicity families
000946  Average-association and site-heterogeneity tests were adjusted separately.
000947  Near-eye and chest placements and the primary and preparation-sensitivity
000948  datasets were also separate. This gives eight complete nine-test
000949  Benjamini–Hochberg families. The family adjustment was calculated from the
000950  unrounded p-values; display rounding did not affect decisions.
000987  ## 5. Diagnostics and influence
000989  Every current additive and interaction fit was checked for convergence,
000990  Hessian status, singularity, fixed-effect rank, residual behaviour, response
000991  support, prediction bounds, and serial dependence. Exact response zeros and
000992  the expected zero mass were compared for Tweedie outcomes. The checks retain
000993  three explicit limitations: increasing residual spread for darkest 10 h mean,
000994  a small observed zero mass not reproduced for sleep time below 1 lx melEDI,
000995  and corrected-dose sensitivity to remaining-gap timing. No limitation was used
000996  as a significance screen.
001036  All 153 planned leave-one-site-out refits succeeded. Some estimates changed
001037  direction or magnitude after one site was omitted, which supports cautious,
001038  inconclusive interpretation. It does not identify a site for removal. The
001039  participant-influence screen likewise ranks observations for review and is
001040  not a deletion rule; no site or participant was removed after inspection.
001068  The [complete diagnostic table](../../../artifacts/08_diagnostics/H08/H08_model_diagnostics.csv)
001069  and [participant-influence screen](../../../artifacts/08_diagnostics/H08/H08_participant_influence_screen.csv)
001070  retain the model-level evidence.
001072  ## 6. Sensitivity analyses
001074  Sensitivity branches preserve the accepted estimand unless their purpose
001075  explicitly changes sample construction, adjustment, or metric definition.
001076  They describe stability and do not promote an association that was not
001077  retained in the primary multiplicity family.
001123  The paired-placement display in the results report puts near eye on the
001124  x-axis and chest on the y-axis, includes only exact common participant-day
001125  samples, and uses equal axis geometry, an identity line, and null lines. It
001126  describes agreement and differences without claiming equivalence. The stored
001127  component estimates and 95% confidence intervals were not recomputed for the
001128  display.
001130  Complete sensitivity records are available for the
001131  [preparation comparison](../../../artifacts/09_tables/H08/H08_gap_timing_unaware_sensitivity.csv),
001132  [photoperiod adjustment](../../../artifacts/09_tables/H08/H08_photoperiod_sensitivity.csv),
001133  [participant summaries](../../../artifacts/09_tables/H08/H08_participant_summary_sensitivity.csv),
001134  [exactly identified longest period](../../../artifacts/09_tables/H08/H08_exactly_identified_longest_period_sensitivity.csv),
001135  and [observed melEDI dose](../../../artifacts/09_tables/H08/H08_observed_dose_sensitivity.csv).
001137  ## 7. Code map and execution order
001139  ### Function modules
001164  ### Executable entry points
001191  The scientific model run used R 4.6.1 and the project library. These commands
001192  are shown for reproducibility; this page does not execute them.
001205  ## 8. Output and claim provenance
001207  The frame index records exact participant, participant-day, site, row-key,
001208  and frame identities for every run and metric. The model manifest connects
001209  each frame to its site-only, additive, and interaction objects. Stored result,
001210  diagnostic, and sensitivity tables then connect those fits to report tables
001211  and figures. Every reader figure links to an exact source CSV so it need not be
001212  digitised.
001259  The preparation-report manifest is created only after the final profile render
001260  and physical-size figure inspection. It records complete identities for the
001261  authoring source, byte-identical website source copy, rendered HTML, page
001262  assets, H08 scripts and tests, scientific outputs, source data, shared
001263  configuration snapshot, decision records, and R environment. It excludes
001264  itself and the handoff that quotes its identity, avoiding a circular checksum
001265  chain.
001267  ## 9. How to examine or reproduce H08
001269  A reader can follow the analysis in six passes:
001271  1. inspect the METRIC-011 decision, shared evidence manifest, H08 result
001272     comparison, and protected-scope reconciliation for the exact-zero
001273     maintenance boundary;
001274  2. inspect `H08_input_audit.csv`, `h08_contract.R`, and the score, metric,
001275     formula, and run registries to verify frozen inputs and specifications;
001276  3. compare the exact samples above with `H08_model_frame_index.csv` and the
001277     108 frames in `H08_model_frames.rds`;
001278  4. follow `run_h08_stage2.R` through `h08_modeling.R` to connect each frame to
001279     its stored object, practical effect, 95% interval, likelihood-ratio test,
001280     diagnostic, and sensitivity record;
001281  5. inspect all eight complete nine-value multiplicity families before
001282     interpreting an individual association; and
001283  6. read the [H08 results report](../../../notebooks/hypotheses/H08.html)
001284     alongside its numerical tables, accessible figures, and exact source CSV
001285     files.
001287  This preparation record documents a frozen analysis. If an input, score rule,
001288  metric definition, sample rule, response model, or estimand changes, the
001289  affected scientific pipeline must be rerun before this page is refreshed.

===== notebooks/hypotheses/H09.qmd =====
000263  ## Hypothesis and analytical question
000265  The preregistered hypothesis was:
000267  > **H9: Timing-based metrics are associated with chronotype (MCTQ, MEQ).**
000269  The analytical question is whether participant-level chronotype is associated
000270  with the local clock timing of personal light exposure across repeated
000271  participant-days, after accounting for study site. Near-eye measurements are
000272  primary because they more closely represent light near the eyes during wear.
000273  Chest measurements provide complementary evidence; placements are not pooled.
000275  ::: {.callout-note title="Answer in brief"}
000276  Later MCTQ corrected midsleep and lower MEQ morning preference were associated
000277  with later primary near-eye M10 midpoint, L10 midpoint, and first timing above
000278  250 lx melEDI after five-metric BH adjustment. The clearest near-eye estimate
000279  was for first timing above 250 lx melEDI: +0.381 h per one-hour later MCTQ MSFsc
000280  (95% CI +0.146 to +0.616; BH-adjusted *p* = 0.003) and −0.444 h per 10 points
000281  greater MEQ morning preference (95% CI −0.699 to −0.189; BH-adjusted *p* =
000282  0.001). Last timing and the midpoint of the longest continuous period were not
000283  supported. Complementary chest results broadly reinforced the first-timing
000284  pattern, and no site interaction survived multiplicity adjustment. Structured
000285  sensitivities supported the main directional interpretation, with explicit
000286  limitations for the unavailable longest-period dataset sensitivity and a few
000287  near-zero or precision-sensitive estimates.
000288  :::
000290  ## Chronotype constructs and timing outcomes
000292  MCTQ MSFsc and MEQ represent related but distinct constructs. MCTQ MSFsc
000293  describes corrected sleep timing: a larger value denotes later corrected
000294  midsleep. MEQ describes morningness-eveningness preference: a larger score
000295  denotes greater morning preference. The available analytical inputs contained
000296  the pinned aggregate calculated fields for 186 participants. MCTQ MSFsc was
000297  complete for 185 participants and missing for one; MEQ was complete for all
000298  186. The aggregate values were preserved exactly from their pinned source,
000299  but item-level questionnaire responses were unavailable for independent score
000300  reconstruction.
000302  The five registered participant-day outcomes were the midpoint of the
000303  brightest 10 hours (M10), midpoint of the darkest 10 hours (L10), first and
000304  last timing above 250 lx melEDI, and midpoint of the longest continuous period
000305  above 250 lx melEDI. M10 and L10 onset and offset were not analysed. Clock
000306  values were linearized because their observed support was adequately linear;
000307  night-time values used the specified negative-hour conversion where needed.
000308  No circular distribution was imposed solely because an outcome was
000309  clock-valued.
000327  The **gap-timing-unaware dataset** is the dataset applying the 50%-per-hour
000328  and 80%-per-day coverage rules but not using the remaining gaps' time of day
000329  in metric-specific support decisions. It can be contrasted once with the
000330  time-sensitive primary dataset; below, the latter is called simply the
000331  primary dataset.
000333  ## Statistical approach
000335  Each chronotype instrument was analysed separately. Participant-day Gaussian
000336  mixed models included fixed study-site effects and a participant random
000337  intercept nested within site. The additive model estimated a site-adjusted
000338  between-participant association; it was compared with a site-only model. A
000339  separate interaction model tested whether the chronotype slope differed by
000340  site. Site-specific slopes were treated as descriptive unless the global
000341  interaction survived adjustment.
000385  The centered predictor names encode simple changes of origin and scale.
000386  `mctq_hour_centered` is each participant's MCTQ MSFsc expressed in clock hours
000387  minus the participant mean (`r sprintf("%.3f", score_audit$mctq_center_hour)`
000388  h); an increase of one unit therefore means one hour later corrected midsleep.
000389  `meq_10_centered` is the MEQ score minus the participant mean
000390  (`r sprintf("%.3f", score_audit$meq_center_score)`) and divided by 10; an
000391  increase of one unit means 10 MEQ points toward greater morning preference.
000392  Centering places the model intercept at the observed mean chronotype. It does
000393  not change the chronotype slopes, their confidence intervals, or their tests.
000395  Four inferential families were kept distinct for each placement: MCTQ main
000396  effects, MEQ main effects, MCTQ-by-site interactions, and MEQ-by-site
000397  interactions. Each family used Benjamini–Hochberg adjustment across the five
000398  registered timing outcomes. Statistical significance was decided at
000399  BH-adjusted *p* < 0.050 before formatting. All displayed intervals are 95%
000400  Wald confidence intervals. The mean-timing sensitivity reported later was not
000401  added to these families.
000403  ## Exact fitted samples
000405  Primary near-eye fits used 131–141 participants, 478–816 participant-days and
000406  observations, 11,325.5–18,851.0 derivation hours, and nine sites, depending on
000407  the outcome and instrument. Complementary chest fits used 149–154
000408  participants, 547–902 participant-days and observations, 12,980.0–20,891.8
000409  derivation hours, and eight sites. Every estimate below reports its exact
000410  fitted sample.
000438  ## Primary near-eye results
000440  Both chronotype instruments showed the same substantive pattern in opposite
000441  score directions. Later MCTQ MSFsc was associated with later M10, L10, and
000442  first-above-250 timing. Greater MEQ morning preference was associated with
000443  earlier timing for those same three outcomes. The adjusted evidence did not
000444  support associations with last-above-250 timing or the midpoint of the longest
000445  continuous period.
000470  [Primary effect figure source data](../../artifacts/11_source_data/H09/H09_primary_effects_data.csv)
000471  and the [complete numerical results](../../artifacts/09_tables/H09/H09_model_results_master.csv)
000472  retain the plotted estimates, intervals, tests, and exact samples.
000474  ## Complementary chest results
000476  Chest measurements were analysed with the same outcome definitions and
000477  instrument-specific five-metric adjustment, without pooling with near-eye
000478  measurements. The first-above-250 association was supported for both
000479  instruments. MEQ associations were also supported for M10 and L10 midpoint;
000480  the chest MCTQ L10 result was close to but did not meet the adjusted rule
000481  (BH-adjusted *p* = 0.055). Last timing and the longest-period midpoint were not
000482  supported.
000491  ## Site-specific heterogeneity
000493  No MCTQ-by-site or MEQ-by-site interaction survived its separate five-metric
000494  BH family at either placement. The smallest interaction-adjusted *p*-value was
000495  0.070 for the near-eye longest-period midpoint with MEQ. Consequently,
000496  site-specific trends remain descriptive rather than separate inferential
000497  findings.
000536  The [descriptive site-specific slopes and 95% intervals](../../artifacts/09_tables/H09/H09_site_specific_slopes.csv)
000537  use the submitted site names and ordering.
000539  ## Placement-matched evidence
000541  Near-eye and chest models were fitted separately on identical participant-day
000542  keys for every registered metric and instrument. The display compares their
000543  component estimates and component 95% confidence intervals. It does not
000544  estimate a between-placement difference interval, pool placements, or test
000545  equivalence; no equivalence margin was prespecified.
000599  [Paired-placement source data](../../artifacts/11_source_data/H09/H09_paired_placement_effects_data.csv)
000600  retain exact sample hashes and component intervals.
000602  ## Model adequacy, dependence, and influence
000604  The diagnostic registry assessed 16 domains for each of 24 primary targets.
000605  Eighteen targets were acceptable across every domain; six retained at least
000606  one explicit limitation. Visual review of the full near-eye and chest panels
000607  supported acceptable final assessments for response/residual distribution and
000608  residual heteroscedasticity. Conservative numerical screen flags remain in the
000609  diagnostic source data and are not erased by that visual assessment.
000650  The two near-eye last-timing slopes were close to zero and changed direction
000651  under some participant-deletion and leave-one-site-out checks, although no
000652  deletion changed a slope by the material 0.25-hour threshold. Chest
000653  longest-period/MEQ had a singular maximum-likelihood interaction fit; its
000654  additive main-effect fit remained estimable, but the interaction diagnostic is
000655  qualified. The registered longest-period outcome was unavailable in the
000656  gap-timing-unaware prepared artifact at both placements and for both
000657  instruments. Fixed-effect matrices were full rank and Hessians positive
000658  definite for all stored fits. Continuous-time AR(1) corrections changed slopes
000659  by at most 0.016 h.
000693  The [complete assessment registry](../../artifacts/08_diagnostics/H09/H09_diagnostic_assessment_registry.csv),
000694  [visual-adjudication mapping](../../artifacts/08_diagnostics/H09/H09_diagnostic_author_adjudication.csv),
000695  [participant-influence summary](../../artifacts/08_diagnostics/H09/H09_participant_influence_summary.csv),
000696  and [leave-one-site-out summary](../../artifacts/08_diagnostics/H09/H09_leave_one_site_out_summary.csv)
000697  retain the thresholds, evidence, and final assessment basis.
000699  ## Dataset sensitivity
000701  For the four registered outcomes available in both datasets, primary and
000702  gap-timing-unaware models were compared on identical participant-day keys.
000703  Fifteen of 16 instrument-by-placement comparisons were stable within model
000704  uncertainty. Chest MCTQ L10 was precision-sensitive, with overlapping 95%
000705  intervals but a change in whether the interval excluded zero. The registered
000706  longest-period midpoint could not be evaluated because the gap-timing-unaware
000707  artifact contains neither that metric nor the endpoints needed to derive it.
000753  The [complete dataset-sensitivity table](../../artifacts/09_tables/H09/H09_gap_timing_unaware_sensitivity.csv)
000754  contains all exact samples, intervals, family-adjusted decisions, and row-key
000755  identities.
000757  ## Other structured sensitivities
000759  Within-site photoperiod adjustment was stable for 21 of 24 targets; its two
000760  direction-sensitive results were the near-zero near-eye last-timing slopes,
000761  and one chest mean-timing estimate was precision-sensitive. Equal-weight
000762  participant summaries were stable for 20 of 24 targets; direction changes
000763  were confined to near-zero last-timing or chest longest-period slopes. All 24
000764  continuous-time AR(1) checks were stable. The stricter afternoon cut used to
000765  linearize L10 changed slopes by at most 0.037 h, and all four L10 checks were
000766  acceptable.
000815  The registered fifth outcome remained the midpoint of the longest continuous
000816  period. Mean timing across all supported exposure above 250 lx melEDI is a
000817  distinct sensitivity estimand and was not substituted for that outcome. Its
000818  near-eye MEQ estimate was −0.169 h per 10 MEQ points (95% CI −0.334 to −0.003;
000819  raw *p* = 0.041), but this raw result is outside the registered multiplicity
000820  families and does not alter the confirmatory conclusion.
000858  [Photoperiod](../../artifacts/09_tables/H09/H09_photoperiod_sensitivity.csv),
000859  [participant-summary](../../artifacts/09_tables/H09/H09_participant_summary_sensitivity.csv),
000860  [continuous-time AR(1)](../../artifacts/09_tables/H09/H09_ar1_sensitivity.csv),
000861  [L10 clock-cut](../../artifacts/09_tables/H09/H09_l10_cut_sensitivity.csv),
000862  and [mean-timing](../../artifacts/09_tables/H09/H09_fifth_outcome_sensitivity.csv)
000863  source tables retain exact samples and 95% confidence intervals.
000865  ## Interpretation
000867  The primary near-eye evidence supports an association between chronotype and
000868  the timing of several, but not all, features of personal light exposure.
000869  Participants with later corrected midsleep tended to have later M10, L10, and
000870  first-above-250 timing; participants with greater morning preference tended to
000871  show the corresponding earlier timing. The agreement of these distinct
000872  chronotype constructs strengthens the directional interpretation without
000873  making them interchangeable.
000875  The data did not support an association with last-above-250 timing or the
000876  registered longest-period midpoint, nor did they support site-specific
000877  heterogeneity after multiplicity adjustment. Chest evidence was complementary
000878  and broadly consistent, but it neither replaces the primary near-eye results
000879  nor establishes placement equivalence. The analysis is observational and
000880  between participants; it does not establish that chronotype causes a change
000881  in personal light exposure timing.
000883  The conclusion should be read alongside the direction instability of the
000884  near-zero near-eye last-timing estimates, the qualified chest
000885  longest-period/MEQ interaction fit, and the unavailable longest-period
000886  gap-timing-unaware comparison. These limitations do not change the supported
000887  M10, L10, and first-timing pattern, but they constrain claims about the
000888  unsupported outcomes and cross-dataset robustness.
000890  ## Reproducibility
000892  All displayed scientific results are read from the accepted, hash-audited H09
000893  outputs; this report does not refit an inferential model. The
000894  [analysis-preparation and provenance companion](../../audit/hypotheses/H09/H09_analysis_preparation.html)
000895  documents the path from verified inputs to these outputs. Consequential
000896  calculations were produced in R 4.6.1. The [master result table](../../artifacts/09_tables/H09/H09_model_results_master.csv),
000897  [complete diagnostic registry](../../artifacts/08_diagnostics/H09/H09_diagnostic_assessment_registry.csv),
000898  [figure manifest and alt text](../../artifacts/12_manifests/H09/H09_figure_manifest.csv),
000899  and reader-report manifest provide the numerical and file-level audit trail.

===== audit/hypotheses/H09/H09_analysis_preparation.qmd =====
000217  ::: {.callout-note title="About this analysis record"}
000218  This page explains how verified MCTQ and MEQ fields and personal
000219  light-exposure timing metrics were turned into the H09 results. The companion
000220  [H09 results report](../../../notebooks/hypotheses/H09.html) presents the
000221  scientific findings.
000223  Rendering this page recalculates only bounded file-identity, schema, count,
000224  key, and checksum checks and lightweight descriptions from frozen H09
000225  indexes. It reads stored estimates, 95% confidence intervals, diagnostics,
000226  influence summaries, sensitivities, and figures from their recorded files. It
000227  does not fit or refit a model, calculate predictions, re-estimate diagnostics,
000228  repeat sensitivity models, bootstrap, resample, or simulate data.
000229  :::
000231  ## Purpose
000233  H09 asks whether participant-level chronotype is associated with five
000234  participant-day timing metrics of personal light exposure. MCTQ corrected
000235  midsleep and MEQ morningness-eveningness preference remain distinct
000236  predictors. Near-eye measurement is primary; chest measurement is
000237  complementary and is never pooled with near-eye measurement.
000239  The **gap-timing-unaware dataset** is the dataset applying the 50%-per-hour
000240  and 80%-per-day coverage rules but not using the remaining gaps' time of day
000241  in metric-specific support decisions. At this first explanation it can be
000242  contrasted with the time-sensitive primary dataset; hereafter the latter is
000243  called simply the primary dataset.
000245  This page identifies the exact inputs, derived fields, analytical frames,
000246  formulae, execution order, diagnostics, sensitivities, outputs, and file
000247  identities behind the results. It documents provenance and does not create an
000248  additional analysis.
000250  ## Analysis map
000272  The arrows show dependencies. They do not mean that this page repeats model
000273  estimation.
000275  ## What is calculated when this page opens
000298  ## 1. Verified inputs
000300  ### File identity
000302  Eleven pinned inputs define the current result-producing chain: preparation
000303  manifests, near-eye and chest participant-day data, the normalized chronotype
000304  table, the preparation-sensitivity data, and the site and metric display
000305  registries. Their complete 64-character SHA-256 values were recorded before
000306  analysis.
000374  ### Bounded integrity checks
000376  The stored indexes establish the joins and analytical denominators without
000377  reopening model objects. Chronotype is keyed by site and participant; model
000378  frames are keyed by dataset, placement, site, participant, local date, metric,
000379  and instrument.
000417  ## 2. Chronotype predictors
000419  ### Aggregate-field provenance and missingness
000421  The analysis uses the pinned aggregate calculated fields `msf_sc` and `meq`.
000422  The available source does not contain item-level questionnaire responses, so
000423  item scoring cannot be independently reconstructed. Value-preservation,
000424  ranges, missingness, and joins were verified for the exact aggregate fields.
000425  MCTQ and MEQ were not combined: MCTQ describes corrected sleep timing, whereas
000426  MEQ describes morningness-eveningness preference.
000444  ### Centering and scaling
000446  The two model-column names, `mctq_hour_centered` and `meq_10_centered`, encode
000447  only a change of origin and, for MEQ, a reporting scale:
000449  - **MCTQ:** `mctq_hour_centered = MCTQ MSFsc in hours - 4.1135843`
000450  - **MEQ:** `meq_10_centered = (MEQ score - 52.8602151) / 10`
000452  Thus one MCTQ unit means one hour later corrected midsleep, while one MEQ unit
000453  means 10 score points toward greater morning preference. A centered value of
000454  zero is the observed participant mean. Centering changes the intercept's
000455  reference point; it does not change the chronotype slope, its 95% confidence
000456  interval, or its test.
000479  ## 3. Timing metrics and analytical samples
000481  ### Metric definitions
000483  Five registered participant-day outcomes enter each confirmatory family. The
000484  midpoint of the longest continuous period is calculated only when the selected
000485  period is exactly identifiable from its recorded endpoints. Mean timing above
000486  250 lx melEDI is a distinct sensitivity estimand and is not substituted for
000487  the registered midpoint. M10 and L10 onset and offset are excluded.
000489  Clock values were linearized because their observed support was adequately
000490  linear. Night-time values use the specified negative-hour conversion where
000491  applicable; no circular response distribution was imposed merely because an
000492  outcome is clock-valued.
000516  ### Exact primary and complementary samples
000518  Each metric and instrument uses its exact complete participant-day frame.
000519  Primary near-eye frames contain 131–141 participants and 478–816
000520  participant-days. Complementary chest frames contain 149–154 participants and
000521  547–902 participant-days.
000616  The figure uses the exact
000617  [model-frame index](../../../artifacts/06_model_data/H09/H09_model_frame_index.csv)
000618  as its paired source data. Metric order follows the preceding table.
000620  ### Placement and dataset common samples
000622  Near-eye and chest effects were fitted separately on exactly matched
000623  participant-day keys for the placement comparison. Primary and
000624  gap-timing-unaware effects likewise use exact common keys where the same
000625  estimand exists in both datasets. The registered longest-period midpoint is
000626  unavailable in the preparation-sensitivity artifact and is not imputed.
000646  ## 4. Model construction and inference
000648  ### Exact formulae supplied to the models
000650  For each metric, placement, dataset, sample, and chronotype instrument, the
000651  site-only model is compared with an additive chronotype model. A second
000652  comparison adds the site-by-chronotype interaction. Restricted maximum
000653  likelihood additive fits provide coefficients and 95% Wald confidence
000654  intervals; maximum-likelihood fits provide the nested likelihood-ratio tests.
000655  A natural-spline fit is used only to assess whether the observed predictor
000656  support is adequately represented as linear.
000682  The sensitivity formulae retain the same chronotype definitions. Photoperiod
000683  is centered within site and added without an unsupported interaction;
000684  participant-summary models use one outcome mean per participant; continuous
000685  time AR(1) fits use elapsed day only for the within-participant correlation
000686  structure.
000716  ### Model records and intermediate quantities
000718  The analysis driver wrote one immutable frame identity per estimable target
000719  and five stored model records per frame. Effects and tests are kept in
000720  separate tables before being assembled into the master result table.
000742  ### Multiplicity families
000744  MCTQ main effects, MEQ main effects, MCTQ-by-site interactions, and MEQ-by-site
000745  interactions form distinct five-outcome Benjamini–Hochberg families. Near-eye,
000746  chest, paired, and preparation-sensitivity runs retain separate family
000747  instances. Significance is decided from unrounded adjusted values before
000748  three-decimal display. Site-specific slopes are descriptive unless the global
000749  interaction family supports heterogeneity.
000770  ## 5. Diagnostics and influence
000772  Sixteen domains were interpreted for each of 24 primary metric-by-instrument-
000773  by-placement targets. Stored checks cover questionnaire provenance, sample and
000774  join identity, site support, clock representation, linearity, residual
000775  distribution and spread, convergence, singularity, rank, temporal dependence,
000776  participant and site influence, placement identity, preparation sensitivity,
000777  and multiplicity.
000779  Visual inspection of the complete residual panels supported acceptable final
000780  response/residual distribution and heteroscedasticity assessments. The
000781  quantitative screen values remain stored. Eighteen targets are acceptable
000782  across all domains; six retain an influence, interaction-fit, or unavailable
000783  preparation-sensitivity limitation.
000807  The complete [diagnostic registry](../../../artifacts/08_diagnostics/H09/H09_diagnostic_assessment_registry.csv),
000808  [screen-to-final mapping](../../../artifacts/08_diagnostics/H09/H09_diagnostic_author_adjudication.csv),
000809  [participant-influence summary](../../../artifacts/08_diagnostics/H09/H09_participant_influence_summary.csv),
000810  and [leave-one-site-out summary](../../../artifacts/08_diagnostics/H09/H09_leave_one_site_out_summary.csv)
000811  preserve the values and assessment basis. No diagnostic flag automatically
000812  removes an observation.
000814  ## 6. Sensitivity analyses
000816  The stored sensitivity battery separates dataset preparation, sample
000817  composition, seasonal timing, repeated-measure dependence, clock
000818  linearization, placement, and the distinct mean-timing estimand. These are
000819  comparisons of prespecified or approved analysis components, not new outcome
000820  searches.

===== notebooks/hypotheses/H10.qmd =====
000287  # Hypothesis and question
000289  The preregistered hypothesis was: **“Personal light exposure metrics depend on age and gender.”** The available normalized demographic variable records **biological sex** as Female or Male. Gender identity was neither substituted for this variable nor inferred from it, so the confirmatory question evaluated here is whether age or measured biological sex is associated with personal light-exposure metrics.
000291  ::: {.callout-note title="Answer in brief"}
000292  At the primary near-eye placement, each additional decade of age was associated with a 1.31-fold brightest-10-hour mean (95% CI 1.09–1.58; BH-adjusted *p* = **0.018**), 1.27-fold time above 1,000 lx melEDI (1.11–1.45; **0.009**), and 1.31-fold melEDI dose (1.09–1.56; **0.018**). No near-eye Female–Male contrast or site-heterogeneity comparison met its separately labelled 17-metric BH rule. Complementary chest models retained six age associations and two Female–Male contrasts, but the placements were not pooled and their similarity was not treated as equivalence. The directions of all 11 retained main findings persisted in the common-sample, remaining-gap, and preregistration-exclusion checks for which they were estimable.
000293  :::
000295  # Methods and rationale
000297  ## Data, outcomes, and estimands
000299  Near-eye measurements were primary and chest measurements complementary. The 17 registered outcomes cover daily stability and fragmentation, light level, duration, timing, exposure history, and spectrum. Depending on the outcome, the primary near-eye models used 137–141 participants and 655–816 participant-days; chest models used 152–154 participants and 732–902 participant-days. The observed age range was 18–68 years. Exact fitted samples and valid support hours are reported with every main-effect estimate below.
000301  The primary estimand was the **site-adjusted common association** across the observed sites. Site interactions were evaluated separately as heterogeneity, not folded into the common association. Age was represented as `age_decade = age / 10`, so its coefficient is directly the change per ten years. This rescaling changes only the coefficient's unit: using age in years and dividing the resulting coefficient and confidence limits by the corresponding scale would give the same fitted values and inferential *p*-values.
000303  Measured biological sex was treatment-coded with Male as the reference, making each contrast Female minus Male. Age and measured biological sex were fitted in separate model sets. Near-eye and chest observations were never pooled, and no equivalence margin was specified.
000305  Daily MDER was defined as the arithmetic mean of viable one-minute
000306  melEDI/illuminance ratios on a complete 1,440-minute local wall-clock grid. A
000307  minute was viable only when both channels were finite and strictly positive,
000308  and a daily value required at least 720 viable minutes. Duplicate fall-back
000309  minutes were averaged channel-wise before the ratio was formed; absent
000310  spring-forward minutes remained missing. No time-profile weighting was used.
000312  ## Models and multiplicity
000314  Participant-day outcomes used a participant random intercept nested in site. IS and IV have one row per participant and therefore used the same fixed effects without a random intercept.
000337  Gaussian mixed models used maximum likelihood for nested comparisons and REML for final coefficients and 95% CIs. Participant-level Gaussian models used partial *F* tests and *t*-based CIs. Tweedie/log mixed models used likelihood-ratio tests and normal-approximation CIs. Time below 10 lx melEDI before sleep used a **Gaussian identity model on hours**. No bootstrap, simulation, or other resampling was required.
000361  Multiplicity was controlled with four separate Benjamini–Hochberg (BH) families for each placement and dataset: 17 age main effects, 17 biological-sex main effects, 17 age-by-site comparisons, and 17 biological-sex-by-site comparisons. Significance was decided from the numerical adjusted value before formatting. Bold adjusted *p*-values in the tables meet the relevant family rule at 0.05; raw and adjusted values are labelled separately.
000393  # Overview of statistically supported associations
000395  The overview below places the participant age distributions beside every main association that met its separately labelled 17-metric BH rule and the site-specific components of the two retained age-by-site comparisons. Age distributions use one point per participant rather than repeated participant-day rows. Site names, order, and colours follow the submitted-manuscript display registry. The main-effect estimates are standardized only to share a visual axis; practical estimates, 95% CIs, exact samples, and raw and adjusted *p*-values remain in the tables below.
000410  The overview has a single paired [source-data file](../../artifacts/11_source_data/H10/H10_age_site_significant_associations_data.csv). It contains 295 de-identified participant display rows, the 11 retained main estimates, and all 16 site-specific estimates for the two retained heterogeneity comparisons.
000412  # Primary near-eye evidence
000414  ## Age
000416  Three age associations met the near-eye age-family rule. Per decade, the brightest-10-hour mean was 1.31-fold (95% CI 1.09–1.58; raw *p* = 0.003; BH-adjusted *p* = **0.018**), time above 1,000 lx melEDI was 1.27-fold (1.11–1.45; raw *p* <0.001; adjusted *p* = **0.009**), and melEDI dose was 1.31-fold (1.09–1.56; raw *p* = 0.003; adjusted *p* = **0.018**).
000438  The figure has paired [source data](../../artifacts/11_source_data/H10/H10_primary_age_associations_data.csv); the complete numerical results are available [here](../../artifacts/09_tables/H10/H10_primary_main_results.csv).
000440  ## Measured biological sex
000442  No near-eye Female–Male contrast met its 17-metric BH rule. The estimates and 95% CIs are retained below to show their precision; failure to retain a contrast is not evidence of equivalence.
000464  The figure has paired [source data](../../artifacts/11_source_data/H10/H10_primary_biological_sex_associations_data.csv).
000466  # Complementary chest evidence
000468  ## Age
000470  Six age associations met the complementary chest age-family rule. Per decade, the ratios were 1.16 (95% CI 1.04–1.30; adjusted *p* = **0.017**) for mean melEDI, 1.35 (1.15–1.57; **<0.001**) for brightest-10-hour mean, 1.31 (1.18–1.46; **<0.001**) for time above 1,000 lx melEDI, 1.16 (1.06–1.26; **0.004**) for waking time above 250 lx melEDI, 1.18 (1.09–1.27; **<0.001**) for the longest continuous period above 250 lx melEDI, and 1.40 (1.21–1.61; **<0.001**) for melEDI dose.
000479  ## Measured biological sex
000481  Two complementary chest contrasts met the biological-sex family rule. Female-to-Male ratios were 0.74 (95% CI 0.60–0.92; raw *p* = 0.006; adjusted *p* = **0.048**) for mean melEDI and 0.76 (0.65–0.90; raw *p* <0.001; adjusted *p* = **0.016**) for darkest-10-hour mean. These findings complement rather than replace the primary near-eye evidence.
000490  # Site heterogeneity
000492  Two complementary chest age-by-site comparisons met their separate 17-metric BH rule: midpoint of the brightest 10 hours (raw *p* = 0.004; adjusted *p* = **0.045**) and last light timing above 250 lx melEDI (raw *p* = 0.005; adjusted *p* = **0.045**). No near-eye age-by-site comparison and no biological-sex-by-site comparison at either placement met its adjusted rule.
000539  All non-selected site-specific age estimates for these two outcomes are shown next. They describe heterogeneity and are not separate site-level significance tests.
000581  Complete interaction comparisons are available as [source data](../../artifacts/09_tables/H10/H10_primary_interaction_results.csv), with the full site-specific estimates [here](../../artifacts/09_tables/H10/H10_site_specific_effects.csv).
000583  # Model assessment
000585  All 68 primary additive models converged, had positive-definite Hessians where applicable, retained full-rank fixed-effect designs, were non-singular, and produced no fit warning. Twenty-five models were assessed **acceptable**, 43 **acceptable with specified limitations**, and zero **not acceptable**. Thus every reported main estimate was considered usable for inference, with the declared qualifications applied.
000620  The diagnostic findings were interpreted as follows:
000622  - **Convergence and numerical checks:** 68/68 models passed convergence, Hessian, rank, singularity, and warning checks.
000623  - **Residual and distributional checks:** selected darkest-10-hour mean, near-eye darkest-10-hour midpoint, and MDER models crossed descriptive Q–Q or spread thresholds. For time below 1 lx melEDI during sleep, four observed zeros at each placement exceeded the fitted Tweedie zero mass; standardized differences were 467–546. These models were acceptable only with that distributional limitation.
000624  - **Temporal checks:** all 60 participant-day models passed the consecutive-day residual screen; the largest absolute pooled correlation was 0.239, below the declared 0.30 review threshold. The eight participant-level models were correctly marked not applicable.
000625  - **Participant influence:** deleting the participant selected by the residual screen changed no estimate by one full-model standard error; the maximum change was 0.439 standard errors. All 68 checks passed.
000626  - **Site influence:** all leave-one-site-out fits remained estimable and converged. Thirty-five models carried a limitation because at least one omission changed adjusted support, reversed a small estimate, or changed it by at least one full-model standard error. The largest shift was 1.72 standard errors.
000628  The assessment heatmap has paired [source data](../../artifacts/11_source_data/H10/H10_diagnostic_assessment_data.csv); complete interpreted assessments are available [here](../../artifacts/08_diagnostics/H10/H10_primary_diagnostic_assessment.csv).
000630  ## Core residual diagnostics
000632  The assessment above is complemented by the standard residual-versus-fitted
000633  and normal Q-Q displays below. They show every main-association model that met
000634  its separately labelled 17-metric BH rule: nine age models and two measured-
000635  biological-sex models. Each panel has its own limits because fitted scales and
000636  residual ranges differ across metrics. Points use submitted site colours so
000637  site-specific clustering or tail behaviour remains visible.
000652  The age display has paired [source data](../../artifacts/11_source_data/H10/H10_retained_age_core_diagnostic_data.csv).
000667  The biological-sex display has paired [source data](../../artifacts/11_source_data/H10/H10_retained_biological_sex_core_diagnostic_data.csv). A [68-page diagnostic appendix](../../artifacts/10_figures/H10/H10_all_primary_model_core_diagnostics.pdf) provides the same two plots for every primary metric-placement-association model, with an exact [page and assessment index](../../artifacts/11_source_data/H10/H10_all_primary_core_diagnostic_index.csv) and complete [plot data](../../artifacts/11_source_data/H10/H10_all_primary_core_diagnostic_data.csv).
000669  The Q-Q panels are descriptive checks of stored Pearson residuals, including
000670  for Tweedie responses; they are not separate normality tests and do not replace
000671  the family-specific distribution, zero-mass, temporal, convergence, or
000672  influence assessments. The plots make the recorded tail and spread
000673  qualifications visible but do not change any model's acceptability assessment.
000675  # Sensitivity findings
000677  ## Placement-matched evidence
000679  For each of the 15 participant-day metrics, near-eye and chest models were fitted separately on identical participant-day keys. All key hashes matched. Participant-level IS and IV were unavailable for this comparison because no approved artifact recomputes them on identical paired participant-day sets; they were not approximated.
000694  On matched participant-days, near-eye time above 1,000 lx melEDI retained adjusted support for age; brightest-10-hour mean and melEDI dose retained positive directions but not adjusted support. All eight complementary chest main findings retained their directions and adjusted support. The paired analysis additionally supported chest pre-sleep time below 10 lx melEDI for age and chest brightest-10-hour mean for measured biological sex; these remain sensitivity-only findings.
000696  The display has paired [source data](../../artifacts/11_source_data/H10/H10_paired_placement_effects_data.csv), and the complete paired estimates, 95% CIs, exact samples, and *p*-values are available [here](../../artifacts/08_diagnostics/H10/sensitivity/H10_paired_placement_main_effects.csv).
000698  ## Remaining-gap timing and exact common samples
000700  The **gap-timing-unaware dataset** is the dataset applying the 50%-per-hour and 80%-per-day coverage rules but not using the remaining gaps’ time of day in metric-specific support decisions. It is contrasted here with the time-sensitive primary dataset; subsequently, that dataset is called simply the primary dataset.
000702  All 34 placement-by-metric key sets matched exactly before the common-sample models were fitted. Every retained main finding kept its direction and adjusted support in the all-available gap-timing-unaware dataset. On the exact common samples, every retained finding kept its direction; the chest mean-melEDI Female–Male contrast lost adjusted support in the primary common subset but regained it in the corresponding gap-timing-unaware subset.
000717  The display has paired [source data](../../artifacts/11_source_data/H10/H10_gap_common_sample_effects_data.csv), with complete common-sample estimates, 95% CIs, samples, and *p*-values [here](../../artifacts/08_diagnostics/H10/sensitivity/H10_primary_gap_common_sample_effects.csv).
000719  ## Exclusions and metric-specific checks
000721  The documented preregistration-exclusion check removed nine unique participants: one older than 65 years, two recorded as not employed, and seven recorded as marginally employed; one person met both the age and not-employed criteria. Students and trainees were not removed solely for that status. Every retained main finding kept its direction and adjusted support. Complete estimates, 95% CIs, samples, and *p*-values are available as [source data](../../artifacts/08_diagnostics/H10/sensitivity/H10_preregistered_exclusion_effects.csv).
000723  Further metric-specific checks found:
000725  - Restricting the longest continuous period above 250 lx melEDI to exactly identified periods retained positive age estimates at both placements: near eye, 132 participants and 500 participant-days/observations, raw *p* = 0.003; chest, 150 participants and 564 participant-days/observations, raw *p* <0.001.
000726  - Moving the darkest-10-hour midpoint unwrap threshold from 16:00 to noon did not reveal an age or measured biological-sex association; every raw *p* was at least 0.597.
000727  - Current MDER distributions had upper tails, especially at chest. All four primary MDER models passed convergence, temporal, fitted-bound, and participant-deletion checks. They were acceptable with specified residual Q–Q limitations; two also carried a leave-one-site-out limitation. No MDER age or Female–Male association met its 17-metric BH rule.
000728  - A waking-only MDER check was unavailable because no approved current artifact derives that outcome.
000763  The primary MDER sample contained 702 participant-days from 137 participants
000764  near eye and 732 participant-days from 152 participants at chest. In the
000765  same-estimand gap-timing-unaware analysis, the near-eye sample contained 687
000766  participant-days from 137 participants and the chest sample 723 participant-days
000767  from 152 participants. The near-eye age association was +0.011 MDER units per
000768  decade (95% CI -0.005 to +0.027; raw *p* = 0.155; BH-adjusted *p* = 0.264).
000769  None of the eight primary or gap-timing-unaware MDER age and Female–Male
000770  results met its labelled family rule.
000772  The MDER placement-matched samples contained 489 participant-days from 107
000773  participants in the primary dataset and 478 participant-days from 107
000774  participants in the gap-timing-unaware dataset at each placement. The exact
000775  primary-versus-gap common samples contained 687 near-eye participant-days from
000776  137 participants and 723 chest participant-days from 152 participants. After
000777  the documented preregistration exclusions, the MDER samples contained 675
000778  near-eye participant-days from 131 participants and 693 chest participant-days
000779  from 143 participants. No MDER result in these comparison families met its
000780  adjusted rule; their estimates and 95% CIs are retained in the linked source
000781  tables.
000854  ## Stability of retained findings
000856  All 11 retained main findings were directionally stable across every available approved sample and dataset check. Eight also retained adjusted support in every available comparison. Adjusted support changed, without a sign change, for near-eye brightest-10-hour mean, near-eye melEDI dose, and the chest mean-melEDI Female–Male contrast.
000889  # Interpretation and limitations
000891  The primary evidence supports positive age associations with selected near-eye measures of brighter exposure and accumulated melEDI. It does not support a near-eye Female–Male contrast after correction, nor does it support adjusted near-eye site heterogeneity. Complementary chest results broaden the age pattern and identify two Female–Male contrasts, but placement-specific measurement context prevents treating these results as pooled evidence or as proof that placements are equivalent.
000893  The coefficients describe cross-sectional associations within the observed age range and sites; they are not causal effects of ageing or biological sex. Biological sex was the construct actually recorded, and the analysis provides no inference about gender identity. Site adjustment addresses measured site differences in the specified model but cannot remove all demographic, behavioural, seasonal, occupational, or environmental confounding.
000895  Residual and leave-one-site-out findings qualify several estimates even though no model failed the explicit acceptability rule. The strongest retained main findings were directionally robust across the available approved checks, but three changed adjusted-support status under at least one stricter common-sample comparison. The model-ready input layer and current light-metric values, including the gap-timing-unaware dataset, were independently verified; complete independent reconstruction of the exact current state-support classification remains open under PREP-003/FIND-044. That provenance qualification is not evidence that the stored values are incorrect.
000897  The analysis ran under R 4.6.1 with the project library. Rendering this report reads frozen numerical tables and figure assets; it does not fit, refit, predict, simulate, bootstrap, or otherwise regenerate scientific results.
000899  # Reproducibility and provenance
000901  The [H10 analysis preparation and provenance companion](../../audit/hypotheses/H10/H10_analysis_preparation.html)
000902  documents the verified path from analytical inputs through metric-specific
000903  samples, model construction, diagnostics, sensitivities, figures, source data,
000904  and checksums to the results reported here.

===== audit/hypotheses/H10/H10_analysis_preparation.qmd =====
000549  ::: {.callout-note title="About this analysis record"}
000550  This page explains how verified demographic data and personal light-exposure
000551  metrics were turned into the H10 results. The companion
000552  [H10 results report](../../../notebooks/hypotheses/H10.html) presents the
000553  scientific findings.
000555  Rendering this page recalculates only bounded SHA-256 identities, required
000556  columns, unique row keys, recorded row counts, the exact `age / 10` unit
000557  conversion, and lightweight summaries from frozen H10 frames and tables. It
000558  reads stored model specifications, fits, estimates, 95% confidence intervals,
000559  diagnostics, influence screens, sensitivities, figures, and source data from
000560  their recorded files. It does not fit or refit a model, calculate predictions,
000561  re-estimate residual or temporal diagnostics, rerun site or participant
000562  deletions, bootstrap, resample, or simulate data.
000563  :::
000565  ## Purpose
000567  The preregistered hypothesis was:
000569  > Personal light exposure metrics depend on age and sex.
000571  The inferential construct was the measured biological-sex variable. Biological
000572  sex and gender were kept conceptually distinct; gender was neither substituted
000573  nor inferred. Age was analysed per 10-year increase. The primary estimands were
000574  the site-adjusted average age association and Female-minus-Male association,
000575  while site interactions were treated as heterogeneity. Near-eye evidence was
000576  primary and chest evidence complementary; the placements were not pooled.
000578  The predefined preparation sensitivity used the **gap-timing-unaware
000579  dataset**. It applied the 50%-per-hour and 80%-per-day coverage rules but did
000580  not use the remaining gaps' time of day in metric-specific support decisions.
000581  For contrast at this first explanation, the primary dataset could be viewed as
000582  a time-sensitive primary metric dataset. Hereafter it is called simply the
000583  primary dataset.
000585  ## Analysis map
000607  The arrows show dependencies. They do not mean that this page repeats model
000608  estimation.
000610  ## What is calculated when this page opens
000628  The four render-time check groups all pass:
000645  ## Verified analytical inputs
000647  ### Current preparation identities
000649  The result-producing package used the current Preparation 06 layer. Its base
000650  objects contained 816 near-eye and 902 chest participant-days before
000651  metric-specific availability was applied.
000681  The complete [input audit](../../../artifacts/06_model_data/H10/H10_input_audit.csv)
000682  contains 36 exact file identities. The current Preparation 06 model-ready
000683  layer, METRIC-010 MDER values, and METRIC-011 L10 numerical-zero
000684  normalization were independently verified, including the corrected
000685  gap-preparation release. METRIC-011 changed exactly eight primary L10 values
000686  from numerical noise to exact zero without changing a sample, a non-L10
000687  scientific value, or an inferential decision. Complete
000688  independent reconstruction of the exact current state-support classification
000689  remains open under PREP-003/FIND-044. This is a provenance qualification, not
000690  evidence that the data are incorrect. FIND-049/CHG-101 is resolved: the former
000691  gap-timing-unaware chest zero is now reason-coded missing because it had no
000692  viable momentary ratio, and every finite gap MDER value is strictly positive.
000694  ### Input roles and checks
000696  The current analysis used result-producing data, demographic, support,
000697  display-registry, response-contract, and deviation-record inputs. Historical
000698  comparison inputs are retained in the full audit but are not part of the
000699  reader-facing analytical chain below.
000755  ## Demographic provenance and site support
000757  Age and biological sex were joined as participant-level variables and were
000758  complete in every fitted frame. Biological sex had exactly the levels Male and
000759  Female. Male was the reference, so the reported coefficient is Female minus
000760  Male. Site was explicitly sum-coded. No gender field or unsupported predictor
000761  entered a model.
000763  The smallest fitted site-by-sex cell was the near-eye San José Male cell with
000764  two participants. This was estimable but motivated the interaction and
000765  leave-one-site-out qualifications.
000829  The figure has an exact [paired source-data CSV](../../../artifacts/11_source_data/H10/H10_preparation_age_distribution_data.csv)
000830  with 295 de-identified participant display rows. Submitted site order and
000831  colours come from the shared site-display registry.
000833  ## Metrics, transformations, and model frames
000835  ### Seventeen registered metrics
000837  IS and IV contributed one outcome row per participant. The other 15 metrics
000838  contributed participant-day rows. The final response package used 14 Gaussian
000839  responses and three Tweedie responses with a log link. Pre-sleep time below
000840  10 lx melEDI was an untransformed Gaussian response, so its effects are
000841  differences in hours and minutes rather than ratios.
000877  For METRIC-010, daily MDER is the arithmetic mean of viable one-minute
000878  melEDI/illuminance ratios on a complete 1,440-minute local wall-clock grid. A
000879  minute is viable only when both channels are finite and strictly positive, and
000880  the daily value is retained with at least 720 viable minutes. Fall-back
000881  duplicate local minutes are averaged channel-wise before forming the ratio;
000882  spring-forward absent minutes remain missing. The analysis does not use
000883  time-profile weighting or a ratio of daily integrals.
000885  ### Exact frame construction
000887  Four run definitions crossed the primary and gap-timing-unaware datasets with
000888  near-eye and chest placement. Each run contained 17 metric-specific frames,
000889  giving 68 frames. Rows with missing outcome, age, biological sex, site or
000890  participant identifier were excluded before the frame was indexed. Every
000891  stored row had a unique key that included dataset, placement, metric, site,
000892  participant, and participant or date unit.
000917  The primary metric-specific samples ranged from 137–141 participants and
000918  655–816 participant-days at near eye, and from 152–154 participants and
000919  732–902 participant-days at chest. Participant-level IS and IV used 141
000920  near-eye and 153 chest participant rows; their stored provenance records 816
000921  and 900 contributing participant-days, respectively.
000936  The denominator is 141 near-eye or 154 chest participants for participant
000937  metrics and 816 near-eye or 902 chest participant-days for participant-day
000938  metrics. Exact numerators, participants, participant-days, support hours,
000939  site counts, keys, and frame identities are in the [paired source-data CSV](../../../artifacts/11_source_data/H10/H10_preparation_sample_support_data.csv).
000981  ## Model construction and inference
000983  ### Exact Wilkinson formulae
000985  For participant-day outcomes, the random intercept `(1 | site:Id)` represents
000986  repeated days within participant and site. IS and IV have one row per
000987  participant, so an ordinary site-adjusted linear model avoids a non-identifiable
000988  one-observation random intercept. The exact evaluated formulae stored with the
000989  model records are:
001019  Age was represented as `age_decade = age / 10`; this changes the coefficient's
001020  unit but not fitted values or test statistics. Site used sum contrasts.
001021  Biological sex used Male then Female treatment coding. A site-adjusted additive
001022  coefficient was the average association estimand; an interaction comparison
001023  asked separately whether that association varied among sites.
001025  ### Engines, estimation, and confidence intervals
001027  Gaussian participant-day comparisons were fitted by maximum likelihood and
001028  their final coefficients by REML. Gaussian participant-level models used
001029  ordinary least squares. Tweedie/log models used maximum likelihood. The model
001030  record contains 612 fits: five comparison fits and four final fits for each of
001031  68 frames.
001065  Every reported coefficient has a model-based 95% confidence interval. Ordinary
001066  linear-model coefficients use a residual-degree-of-freedom *t* interval;
001067  mixed-model and Tweedie coefficients use the fixed-effect covariance matrix
001068  with a normal critical value. Logit effects are odds ratios, base-10
001069  transformed Gaussian effects are ratios, Tweedie/log effects are ratios, and
001070  identity-scale effects are differences. Clock-time differences are reported in
001071  minutes.
001073  ### Comparisons and multiplicity
001075  Four separate 17-metric Benjamini–Hochberg families were evaluated for each
001076  dataset and placement. Age main effects, biological-sex main effects,
001077  age-by-site interactions, and biological-sex-by-site interactions were never
001078  combined into one family.
001123  The stored primary package contains 136 main-effect estimates and 272 model
001124  comparisons. Eleven main associations and two chest age-by-site comparisons
001125  met their separately labelled adjusted rules. Site-specific interaction
001126  components were stored with 95% intervals as heterogeneity descriptions, not
001127  as a new set of site-level significance tests.
001129  ## Diagnostics and influence
001131  All 612 fits converged, had positive-definite Hessians and full-rank fixed
001132  effects, were non-singular, and emitted no fit error. Diagnostics were
001133  interpreted for 68 primary metric-placement-association targets: 25 were
001134  acceptable and 43 acceptable with specified limitations; none was classified
001135  not acceptable.
001155  The most recurrent qualifications were residual-spread or Q–Q flags for
001156  selected right-skewed or timing responses, plus a zero-mass mismatch for sleep-
001157  environment time below 1 lx melEDI. Four observed zeros at each placement
001158  remained in those fitted Tweedie models. These issues were retained in the
001159  interpretation rather than used to select an unregistered replacement model.
001161  Complete numerical diagnostics are available in the [diagnostic assessment](../../../artifacts/08_diagnostics/H10/H10_primary_diagnostic_assessment.csv),
001162  [participant-deletion](../../../artifacts/08_diagnostics/H10/H10_participant_deletion_influence.csv),
001163  and [leave-one-site-out](../../../artifacts/08_diagnostics/H10/H10_leave_one_site_out_summary.csv)
001164  files.
001166  The results report also displays residual-versus-fitted and descriptive normal
001167  Q-Q plots for all 11 multiplicity-retained main-association models. A
001168  [68-page appendix](../../../artifacts/10_figures/H10/H10_all_primary_model_core_diagnostics.pdf)
001169  provides the same core plots for all 68 primary models, with an exact
001170  [page and assessment index](../../../artifacts/11_source_data/H10/H10_all_primary_core_diagnostic_index.csv)
001171  and complete [plot data](../../../artifacts/11_source_data/H10/H10_all_primary_core_diagnostic_data.csv).
001172  These displays are constructed from the stored fitted values, Pearson
001173  residuals, and Q-Q points already produced by the analysis; no model is
001174  refitted and no diagnostic classification is recalculated.
001176  ## Sensitivity branches
001178  The central sensitivity branches changed one declared feature at a time while
001179  retaining the H10 estimand and model implementation. They were not used to
001180  replace the primary conclusion.
001199  Exact placement-matched keys passed for all 15 participant-day metrics in both
001200  datasets. Paired participant-level IS and IV were unavailable because those
001201  metrics would need to be recomputed on identical paired day sets; all-available
001202  participant values were not substituted. All 34 primary versus gap-timing-
001203  unaware common-key sets matched exactly. The exclusion sensitivity removed
001204  nine unique participants: one older than 65 years, two recorded as not
001205  employed, and seven recorded as marginally employed, with one person meeting
001206  both age and employment criteria. Students and trainees were not excluded
001207  solely for that status.
001222  No bootstrap, simulation, or other resampling was required. Waking-only MDER
001223  remained unavailable because no approved current artifact derives that metric.
001224  The primary MDER frames contained 702 near-eye participant-days from 137
001225  participants and 732 chest participant-days from 152 participants. The
001226  gap-timing-unaware MDER frames contained 687 near-eye participant-days from 137
001227  participants and 723 chest participant-days from 152 participants. The paired
001228  gap MDER branch used 478 participant-days from 107 participants at each
001229  placement, and the exact primary-versus-gap common samples used 687 near-eye
001230  and 723 chest participant-days. Every finite MDER value was strictly positive;
001231  the chest day with no viable momentary ratio was reason-coded missing.
001233  ## Code in execution order
001235  The analysis is separated into a contract, reusable modelling operations, one
001236  result-producing driver, and display/provenance builders. This separation makes
001237  the model contract auditable without causing this page to execute it.
001261  The result-producing drivers are deliberately not called by this document. The
001262  display builders read stored tables or de-identified descriptive rows; neither
001263  can change an estimate, interval, *p*-value, diagnostic or sensitivity result.
001265  ## Stored outputs and checksums
001267  ### Result and claim map
001292  ### Current key identities

001295  page renders. They are structural checksums, not scientific calculations.
001345  The [complete result inventory](../../../artifacts/12_manifests/H10/H10_stage3_artifacts.csv)
001346  records the accepted reader package.
001347  The preparation-report manifest builder excludes itself and evolving handoff

001350  ## Computational environment
001352  R 4.6.1 and the project library are authoritative. Packages were neither

001364  The environment record includes `resampling = None`: no production resampling
001365  or pilot computation was needed. The project lockfile, H10 execution record,

001369  ## Reproducibility boundary
