===== notebooks/hypotheses/H11.qmd =====
000529  ## Question
000531  The preregistered hypothesis was:
000533  > “H11: Diurnal exposure patterns differ by sex.”
000535  The analytical question is whether the fitted 24-hour personal melEDI profile
000536  differs between participants recorded as Female and Male after accounting for
000537  site-specific temporal patterns, repeated observations within participant and
000538  day, and short-lag residual dependence.
000540  ::: {.callout-tip title="Answer in brief"}
000541  The primary near-eye Female–Male curve differed globally (BH-adjusted *p* =
000542  `r format_p_inline(near_global$p_adjusted, near_global$p_adjusted < 0.05)`),
000543  and the complementary chest curve also differed globally (BH-adjusted *p* =
000544  `r format_p_inline(chest_global$p_adjusted,
000545  chest_global$p_adjusted < 0.05)`). The secondary level/shape analysis could not
000546  cleanly assign either globally supported curve to an independently resolved
000547  level or shape component; this does not overturn the global results. In the
000548  exploratory activity-complete sample, near eye was already unsupported before
000549  activity adjustment (*p* = `r format_p_inline(near_activity_unadjusted$p_raw,
000550  near_activity_unadjusted$p_raw < 0.05)`) and remained unsupported after it
000551  (*p* = `r format_p_inline(near_activity_adjusted$p_raw,
000552  near_activity_adjusted$p_raw < 0.05)`). At chest, the corresponding *p*-value
000553  changed from `r format_p_inline(chest_activity_unadjusted$p_raw,
000554  chest_activity_unadjusted$p_raw < 0.05)` to
000555  `r format_p_inline(chest_activity_adjusted$p_raw,
000556  chest_activity_adjusted$p_raw < 0.05)`. This sensitivity is compatible with
000557  attenuation but cannot identify activity as a mechanism. All fitted models
000558  were acceptable with specified limitations.
000559  :::
000561  ## What was analysed
000563  The recorded predictor is **biological sex**, coded Female or Male. Gender is
000564  a distinct construct and was not measured by this variable, so this analysis
000565  does not make a claim about gender differences.
000567  Near-eye measurements are primary because they more closely represent the
000568  light field near the eyes. Chest measurements are analysed separately as
000569  complementary evidence; the placements are neither pooled nor treated as
000570  independent replicates. The response is the 30-minute arithmetic mean melEDI,
000571  modelled as `log10(melEDI + 0.1)`. The estimand is the complete fitted Female-
000572  minus-Male 24-hour curve at equal clock-time weighting and equal-site
000573  conditioning.
000584  The primary near-eye model contains 141 participants (79 Female, 62 Male), 816
000585  participant-days, 37,756 30-minute observations, and all nine sites. The chest
000586  model contains 154 participants (86 Female, 68 Male), 902 participant-days,
000587  41,842 observations, and eight sites. These are the accepted temporal-model
000588  samples; no extra H11-specific row deletion was applied.
000590  ### Model and inference
000592  The model inherits the finalized temporal structure: a cyclic common daily
000593  curve, a cyclic Female-specific deviation, sum-to-zero site-specific temporal
000594  deviations, participant-specific temporal curves, and participant-day random
000595  intercepts. One run-specific AR(1) parameter is estimated from a zero-rho pass,
000596  and sequence starts prevent correlation from crossing participant-day or gap
000597  boundaries. Models use Gaussian identity-link GAMs fitted by fREML with
000598  `discrete = TRUE`.
000600  The following evaluated R cell reads and prints the exact Wilkinson formula
000601  supplied to every accepted H11 full model. Here, `response` is the transformed
000602  melEDI outcome and Male is the reference level.
000613  The confirmatory test jointly evaluates the 48-bin Female-minus-Male curve.
000614  Scores and residuals are whitened using the fitted AR boundary contract,
000615  summed by participant, and entered into a participant-cluster CR1 sandwich
000616  covariance with the smoothing-bias component retained. A finite-cluster F
000617  reference uses the effective fractional rank of the tested covariance. The
000618  global curve is one decision per placement and dataset. Planned level and
000619  shape decompositions form a separate two-test BH family within each placement.
000620  Those secondary tests assess component attribution; they do not repeat the
000621  global test, and failure to resolve either component does not invalidate a
000622  supported global curve.
000624  All curve and contrast confidence intervals are participant-cluster-robust
000625  **pointwise 95% intervals**. They describe uncertainty at one displayed clock
000626  bin at a time and do not support a simultaneous significant-period claim.
000628  ### Deviations and operational clarifications
000630  The analysis differs from, or makes operationally explicit, the preregistered
000631  plan in the following ways.
000701  ## Primary near-eye result
000710  The complete near-eye Female-minus-Male curve was supported: raw *p* =
000711  `r format_p_inline(near_global$p_raw, near_global$p_raw < 0.05)` and
000712  BH-adjusted *p* = `r format_p_inline(near_global$p_adjusted,
000713  near_global$p_adjusted < 0.05)`. This is a joint test of the time-constant and
000714  time-varying sex contributions.
000729  The minimum displayed ratio was
000730  `r format_ratio_ci(near_context$minimum_ratio,
000731  near_context$minimum_ratio_lower_95,
000732  near_context$minimum_ratio_upper_95)` at
000733  `r format_clock(near_context$minimum_clock)`. The maximum was
000734  `r format_ratio_ci(near_context$maximum_ratio,
000735  near_context$maximum_ratio_lower_95,
000736  near_context$maximum_ratio_upper_95)` at
000737  `r format_clock(near_context$maximum_clock)`. Pointwise intervals excluded one
000738  in the displayed 30-minute bins from 07:00–10:00 and 16:00–18:30, with Female
000739  lower. These clock ranges are descriptive collections of pointwise intervals;
000740  they are not a familywise significant period.
000742  ### Can the global result be separated into level and shape?
000756  The secondary decomposition did not cleanly resolve the globally supported
000757  curve into an independently supported level or shape component. The shape
000758  component had raw *p* = **0.043** but BH-adjusted *p* = 0.075; the level
000759  component had raw and adjusted *p* = 0.075. The global result is not formed by
000760  adding or combining these two *p*-values: it is a separate joint test of the
000761  complete curve. Evidence can be distributed across correlated level and shape
000762  directions, so this component result expresses uncertainty about attribution;
000763  it does not make the global result invalid.
000765  ### Effect-size context
000767  Because the global near-eye test was supported, the prespecified conditional
000768  effect-size reporting gate opened.
000777  The conditional level ratio is compatible with both a moderate lower Female
000778  level and a negligible difference. The fitted-curve variation is small on the
000779  model scale despite a statistically supported structured curve difference.
000781  ## Complementary chest result
000790  The complete chest curve was also supported: raw *p* =
000791  `r format_p_inline(chest_global$p_raw, chest_global$p_raw < 0.05)` and adjusted
000792  *p* = `r format_p_inline(chest_global$p_adjusted,
000793  chest_global$p_adjusted < 0.05)`.
000808  The chest ratio was effectively time-constant at
000809  `r format_ratio_ci(chest_context$minimum_ratio,
000810  chest_context$minimum_ratio_lower_95,
000811  chest_context$minimum_ratio_upper_95)`. All 48 displayed pointwise intervals
000812  excluded one. Because the intervals are pointwise, this observation does not
000813  establish a simultaneous all-day difference.
000827  The chest global result is visually level-like, but the secondary decomposition
000828  did not resolve that joint result independently to the level term: its raw
000829  *p* = **0.029** and BH-adjusted *p* = 0.057. The shape component had raw and
000830  adjusted *p* = 0.986. This limits attribution to a specific component; it does
000831  not overturn the separately supported global curve result.
000840  ### Why there is no direct placement identity plot
000842  The all-available near-eye and chest models use different samples, so plotting
000843  their estimates against one another would confound placement with sample
000844  composition. A stored common frame contains 112 participants, 643 participant-
000845  days, 29,786 matched observations, and eight sites, but no accepted H11 model
000846  was fitted to that frame and no scalar temporal estimand was predeclared.
000847  Therefore a matched identity scatterplot is not applicable. The separate
000848  near-eye and chest curves above are the closest valid comparison; they do not
000849  test placement differences or establish equivalence.
000851  ## Exploratory activity-context sensitivity
000853  The accepted global test remains unadjusted for activity. An exploratory
000854  sensitivity asked whether the estimated Female-minus-Male curve changed after
000855  adding the recorded hourly activity context. To separate adjustment from sample
000856  selection, the accepted H11 model was first refitted without activity on the
000857  exact activity-complete sample and then refitted with activity on those same
000858  observations.
000860  Diary hours were eligible only when exactly one activity was selected. Hours
000861  with multiple selections, no selection, all missing responses, or only an
000862  unspecified “other” activity were excluded rather than duplicated across
000863  categories. The retained contexts were home (reference), sleep, road or
000864  vehicle, indoor work, and outdoor activity. A 30-minute exposure observation
000865  was attached only when it lay wholly within one unique diary hour. This
000866  restriction produced the following exact samples.
000875  The near-eye activity-complete sample contains 126 participants (71 Female, 55
000876  Male), 724 participant-days, 30,499 30-minute observations, and nine sites. The
000877  chest sample contains 150 participants (83 Female, 67 Male), 875 participant-
000878  days, 36,711 observations, and eight sites.
000880  The following evaluated R cell prints the exact Wilkinson formulas used for
000881  the same-sample comparison. The adjusted model adds both the activity level and
000882  activity-specific cyclic temporal deviations. A single placement-specific
000883  rho estimated from the preliminary adjusted model was held fixed in both final
000884  fits so that the comparison did not change its autocorrelation assumption.
000905  For near eye, restricting the data to observations with eligible activity
000906  information was already sufficient to change the global result from the
000907  accepted all-available raw *p* =
000908  `r format_p_inline(near_global$p_raw, near_global$p_raw < 0.05)` to raw *p* =
000909  `r format_p_inline(near_activity_unadjusted$p_raw,
000910  near_activity_unadjusted$p_raw < 0.05)` before activity was added. The
000911  activity-adjusted result was raw *p* =
000912  `r format_p_inline(near_activity_adjusted$p_raw,
000913  near_activity_adjusted$p_raw < 0.05)`. Therefore the near-eye comparison cannot
000914  attribute the loss of threshold support to activity adjustment; sample
000915  restriction precedes it.
000917  At chest, the exact same-sample raw *p*-value changed from
000918  `r format_p_inline(chest_activity_unadjusted$p_raw,
000919  chest_activity_unadjusted$p_raw < 0.05)` without activity to
000920  `r format_p_inline(chest_activity_adjusted$p_raw,
000921  chest_activity_adjusted$p_raw < 0.05)` with activity. The exact adjusted value
000922  is 0.050214. This small threshold crossing is compatible with attenuation, but
000923  it is not evidence that activity mediates or explains the observed sex
000924  association.
000939  The activity-adjusted near-eye minimum ratio was
000940  `r format_ratio_ci(near_activity_pointwise$minimum_ratio,
000941  near_activity_pointwise$minimum_ratio_lower_95,
000942  near_activity_pointwise$minimum_ratio_upper_95)` at
000943  `r format_clock(near_activity_pointwise$minimum_clock)`. The activity-adjusted
000944  chest ratio was approximately
000945  `r format_ratio_ci(chest_activity_pointwise$minimum_ratio,
000946  chest_activity_pointwise$minimum_ratio_lower_95,
000947  chest_activity_pointwise$minimum_ratio_upper_95)`; its exact upper confidence
000948  limit was 1.000034. No displayed activity-adjusted pointwise interval excluded
000949  one at either placement. These intervals remain pointwise and do not define a
000950  simultaneously significant period.
000952  Under the approved gate, an additional activity-adjusted sex effect-size
000953  estimator was required only if the adjusted global sex curve had raw *p* <
000954  0.050. That gate remained closed at both placements, so no effect-size
000955  bootstrap was run for this sensitivity.
000964  All four same-sample fits were **acceptable with specified limitations**. They
000965  converged without serious warnings, retained cyclic closure and the inherited
000966  site constraint, and had no severe basis-dimension flag. Final boundary-aware
000967  lag-1 residual correlations were 0.144 and 0.084 near eye and 0.131 and 0.075
000968  at chest for the unadjusted and adjusted fits, respectively. The sensitivity
000969  is limited to uniquely mapped single-activity diary hours and remains
000970  observational; neither adjustment nor a change around a significance threshold
000971  identifies a behavioural mechanism.
000973  ## Data-preparation sensitivity
000975  A predefined sensitivity uses the **gap-timing-unaware dataset**. It still
000976  passed the general 50%-per-hour and 80%-per-day coverage rules. “Gap-timing-
000977  unaware” means that the timing of the remaining missing observations is not
000978  used for metric-specific adjustment; gaps, missingness, and coverage were not
000979  ignored. For this contrast only, the primary dataset could be interpreted as a
000980  **time-sensitive primary metric dataset**. Below, it is called simply the
000981  primary dataset.
000992  The sensitivity retains 141 near-eye participants and 154 chest participants,
000993  but contains 809 and 894 participant-days and 37,603 and 41,664 observations,
000994  respectively.
001005  Near eye remained supported (raw and adjusted *p* = **0.026**) and chest
001006  remained supported (raw and adjusted *p* = **0.037**).
001017  The secondary sensitivity decompositions likewise did not resolve the globally
001018  supported curves into independently supported level or shape components after
001019  BH adjustment. This does not alter either sensitivity global result.
001021  ::: {.panel-tabset}
001023  ### Near eye
001038  The minimum ratio was
001039  `r format_ratio_ci(sensitivity_near_context$minimum_ratio,
001040  sensitivity_near_context$minimum_ratio_lower_95,
001041  sensitivity_near_context$minimum_ratio_upper_95)` at
001042  `r format_clock(sensitivity_near_context$minimum_clock)`. The same displayed
001043  pointwise clock ranges excluded one as in the primary dataset.
001045  ### Chest
001060  The chest ratio was approximately
001061  `r format_ratio_ci(sensitivity_chest_context$minimum_ratio,
001062  sensitivity_chest_context$minimum_ratio_lower_95,
001063  sensitivity_chest_context$minimum_ratio_upper_95)`, with all 48 pointwise
001064  intervals excluding one. Again, this is not a simultaneous all-day claim.
001066  :::
001068  The central conclusion is stable to this preparation change: the complete
001069  near-eye and chest curves remain supported. The secondary level/shape analyses
001070  do not cleanly attribute either global result to one independently resolved
001071  component.
001073  ## Diagnostics
001082  All four models are **acceptable with specified limitations**. They converged
001083  without serious fitting warnings; the cyclic sex curves close at midnight;
001084  site deviations satisfy their sum-to-zero constraint; and no severe basis-
001085  dimension flag was found. The accepted AR structure reduced boundary-aware
001086  lag-1 residual correlation from approximately 0.60–0.62 before adjustment to
001087  0.070–0.083 in the final residuals.
001089  ::: {.panel-tabset}
001091  ### Near eye
001106  ### Chest
001121  :::
001136  [Residual-distribution diagnostic source data](../../artifacts/08_diagnostics/H11/stage3/H11_reader_primary_residual_summary.csv)
001138  Together, the residual figures show the main continuous diagnostic outcomes:
001139  the accepted AR(1) structure removes most short-lag dependence, while the
001140  final residuals remain centered near zero but retain heavy tails and
001141  fitted-value-dependent spread. Convergence, cyclic closure, the site
001142  constraint, and basis-dimension flags remain in the diagnostic table because
001143  they are pass/fail or count outcomes rather than quantities that benefit from
001144  a separate plot.
001146  No single participant dominates the robust covariance: the largest
001147  participant contribution is 2.8–3.0% near eye and 7.5–7.6% at chest. The
001148  coefficient-held-fixed delete-one-participant covariance check retains the
001149  global decision for every participant. This check probes covariance influence;
001150  it is not a leave-one-participant-out model-refit sensitivity.
001152  The remaining limitations are material but do not invalidate the specified
001153  test. Exact zeros, heavy tails, heteroscedasticity, and concurvity remain under
001154  the Gaussian log-scale mean model. Results are conditional on the observed
001155  sites; the site sample is too small for broad site-population generalisation,
001156  and leave-one-site-out refits remain unresolved. The registered hourly
001157  geometric-mean outcome, a fitted common-placement sample, an all-zero-inclusive
001158  preparation, and alternative temporal model forms remain unresolved. The
001159  activity-context sensitivity is now reported, but it is limited to uniquely
001160  mapped single-activity diary hours and cannot distinguish covariate adjustment
001161  from selection into the activity-complete sample without the explicit
001162  same-sample comparison shown above.
001164  ## Interpretation
001166  These data support a biological-sex difference in the complete fitted daily
001167  melEDI profile at near-eye level, with complementary support at chest level.
001168  Near-eye pointwise estimates indicate the largest relative separation around
001169  the morning, whereas the chest contrast is nearly time-constant. However, the
001170  planned component analysis cannot attribute the supported joint result
001171  specifically to a level or shape component after multiplicity adjustment. That
001172  attribution limitation does not weaken the definition or validity of the
001173  separate global curve test.
001175  Activity adjustment did not yield a supported adjusted global curve on the
001176  activity-complete sample at either placement. For near eye, however, the
001177  same-sample unadjusted curve was already unsupported, so the change cannot be
001178  assigned to activity. For chest, the curve magnitude changed modestly and the
001179  raw *p*-value moved just above 0.050. Neither pattern establishes mediation or
001180  a causal activity pathway.
001182  The effect-size summaries are modest on the fitted model scale, and all clock-
001183  specific confidence intervals are pointwise. The result is therefore evidence
001184  for an overall structured association, not proof of a significant clock period,
001185  not an equivalence or placement-difference analysis, and not evidence that
001186  biological sex causally determines light exposure. Differences may reflect
001187  behavioural, occupational, environmental, or other correlated factors that
001188  this observational model does not identify.
