===== audit/hypotheses/H11/H11_analysis_preparation.qmd =====
000227  ::: {.callout-note title="About this analysis record"}
000228  This page explains how the verified H02 temporal frames and recorded biological
000229  sex were turned into the H11 inference. The companion
000230  [H11 results report](../../../notebooks/hypotheses/H11.html) presents the
000231  scientific findings.
000233  Rendering this page recalculates only file-identity checks, schema and key
000234  checks, and lightweight descriptive summaries from frozen analysis frames. It
000235  reads model specifications, fitted summaries, estimates, intervals,
000236  diagnostics, influence screens, and sensitivity outputs from recorded files.
000237  It does not fit or refit a model, estimate autocorrelation, calculate model
000238  predictions, rerun a bootstrap or simulation, or repeat robust inference.
000239  :::
000241  ## Purpose
000243  H11 asks whether the 24-hour pattern of personal melanopic equivalent daylight
000244  illuminance (melEDI) differs by the biological-sex variable recorded in the
000245  study. Biological sex and gender are distinct constructs; no gender variable
000246  was used in this analysis. Near-eye measurements are primary, and chest-level
000247  measurements provide complementary evidence.
000249  The central sensitivity uses the exact term **gap-timing-unaware dataset**. It
000250  still passed the general requirement for at least 50% valid data per hour and
000251  at least 80% valid data per participant-day. “Gap-timing-unaware” means that
000252  the timing of the remaining missing observations is not used for
000253  metric-specific adjustment. For contrast only in this first explanation, the
000254  primary dataset could be interpreted as a **time-sensitive primary metric
000255  dataset**; hereafter it is called simply the primary dataset.
000257  The reported estimand is the complete Female-minus-Male fitted curve across 48
000258  half-hour clock bins, averaged equally across included sites and conditional on
000259  the fitted temporal, participant, participant-day, site, and autocorrelation
000260  structure. The participant is the independent unit for robust inference; the
000261  rows supplied to the model are 30-minute observations nested within
000262  participant-days and participants.
000264  ## Analysis map
000279  ## What is calculated when this page opens
000281  The executable cells read frozen CSV summaries, verify their recorded
000282  identities and dimensions, format tables and p-values, and embed previously
000283  exported figures. Scientific estimates are never regenerated here. The
000284  preparation script that created the source-data CSVs performed only integrity
000285  and descriptive checks in R; it did not call a model-fitting, prediction, or
000286  resampling function.
000288  ## 1. Verified inputs
000290  ### File identity
000292  Ten consequential inputs were checked against the identities recorded when
000293  the analysis frames were accepted. They include the signed preregistration,
000294  the extracted H11 contract, the finalized H02 temporal specification,
000295  biological-sex metadata, the site display registry, four placement/scenario
000296  frames, and the normalized activity diary.
000320  [Full-precision input identity source data](../../../artifacts/11_source_data/H11/preparation/H11_preparation_input_identities.csv)
000322  ### Schema, keys, and values
000324  All six frozen frames have the required fields, unique participant-day and
000325  clock-bin keys, the complete set of 48 clock positions, non-negative melEDI,
000326  exact response reconstruction as $\log_{10}(\mathrm{melEDI}+0.1)$, matching
000327  biological-sex encodings, and boundary flags consistent with their recorded
000328  reasons. Their file identities and stored sample counts also match.
000375  ## 2. Temporal and analytical-frame construction
000377  ### Inherited time structure
000379  H11 inherits the finalized H02 temporal structure rather than selecting a new
000380  one. Local wall-clock time is represented by 48 half-hour midpoints from 00:15
000381  to 23:45. The smooth is cyclic at midnight. Site-specific deviation smooths
000382  sum to zero, participant curves use factor-smooth interactions, and
000383  participant-days enter as random intercepts.
000385  Autocorrelation sequences are based on true elapsed instants. A sequence
000386  restarts at participant-day boundaries, elapsed-time discontinuities, and
000387  non-one-to-one wall-clock outcomes, including daylight-saving-time ambiguity.
000388  The accepted frames therefore do not infer continuity merely because two rows
000389  have adjacent clock labels.
000427  ### Coverage, missingness, and transformation
000429  The general data-admission rules required at least 50% valid data per hour and
000430  at least 80% valid data per participant-day. The primary model frames then
000431  required 15–30 valid wall-clock minutes in each retained 30-minute melEDI bin.
000433  The **gap-timing-unaware dataset** still passed those general coverage rules,
000434  but the timing of the remaining missing observations was not used for
000435  metric-specific adjustment. The unavailable metric-specific timing fields in
000436  the sensitivity frames are therefore intentional, not missing evidence for
000437  the general coverage checks.
000439  All non-negative melEDI values, including exact zeros, are retained. The model
000440  response is $\log_{10}(\mathrm{melEDI}+0.1)$, so zero maps exactly to -1 on the
000441  model scale. The descriptive figure uses a true symlog display only for the
000442  positive original-unit distribution and shows the retained zeros separately.
000472  The primary near-eye model uses **141 participants, 816 participant-days,
000473  37,756 observations (18,878.0 nominal hours), and 9 sites**. The complementary
000474  chest model uses **154 participants, 902 participant-days, 41,842 observations
000475  (20,921.0 nominal hours), and 8 sites**. These counts reconcile exactly with
000476  the accepted H02 fitted samples.
000478  ### Site and biological-sex support
000492  [Paired site-support source data](../../../artifacts/11_source_data/H11/preparation/H11_preparation_site_sex_support.csv)
000520  ### Original-unit melEDI support
000534  [Paired untransformed distribution source data](../../../artifacts/11_source_data/H11/preparation/H11_preparation_melEDI_distribution.csv)
000566  ## 3. Activity-context construction
000568  The exploratory activity analysis uses the accepted primary placement frames
000569  and a normalized hourly diary. A 30-minute observation is retained only when
000570  there is exactly one containing diary hour and exactly one eligible mapped
000571  activity: Home, Sleep, Public transport/car, Indoor work/home office, or
000572  Outdoors/active travel. Missing flags, no selection, multiple selections, and
000573  an unspecified “other” selection are not imputed. Autocorrelation sequences
000574  restart across gaps created by this filter.
000588  [Paired activity-attrition source data](../../../artifacts/11_source_data/H11/preparation/H11_preparation_activity_attrition.csv)
000608  This creates same-sample near-eye models with 126 participants, 724
000609  participant-days, and 30,499 observations, and chest models with 150
000610  participants, 875 participant-days, and 36,711 observations. The comparison
000611  between models with and without activity is descriptive contextualization; it
000612  cannot show that activity mediates or causes a biological-sex association.
000614  ## 4. Model construction
000616  ### Exact Wilkinson formulas
000618  The following are the exact formulas supplied to the accepted model fits. The
000619  cells are displayed for auditability and are not executed on this page.
000669  The sex factor uses Male as the treatment-reference level. The ordered
000670  `sex_smooth` factor identifies the cyclic Female-minus-Male deviation. The
000671  main cyclic smooth is the Male reference curve; the sex-deviation smooth adds
000672  the Female shape difference. Site deviations use the sum-to-zero basis,
000673  participant curves use factor smooths, and participant-day random intercepts
000674  account for repeated observations within days. In the activity model, Home is
000675  the treatment reference and the ordered activity deviation smooths share one
000676  smoothing parameter (`id = 2`).
000712  ### Engine and estimation settings
000714  All models are Gaussian identity-link additive models fitted by fast REML with
000715  `discrete = TRUE` and cyclic knots `time_hour = c(0, 24)`. For each run, a
000716  preliminary full model with $\rho=0$ estimates the lag-1 residual correlation
000717  within the inherited `AR_start` sequences. The final full model uses that one
000718  run-specific $\rho$. Any conditional effect-size baseline uses the same frame,
000719  $\rho$, knots, and non-sex structure.
000767  ## 5. Estimand, uncertainty, and multiplicity
000769  ### Complete-curve inference
000771  The preregistered question is represented by one global test of the complete
000772  48-bin Female-minus-Male curve within each placement and dataset. Inference
000773  uses participant-summed, AR-whitened scores; a participant-cluster CR1
000774  covariance; the smoothing-bias term $V_p-V_e$; and a finite-cluster,
000775  fractional-rank $F$ reference. Confidence intervals for fitted curves,
000776  pointwise contrasts, and displayed scalar estimates are **pointwise 95%
000777  intervals**, not simultaneous bands.
000779  The primary near-eye global raw p-value is
000780  `r format_p(near_global$p_raw)` and the complementary chest raw p-value is
000781  `r format_p(chest_global$p_raw)`. Each is the sole member of its explicitly
000782  labelled global family, so its stored adjusted value is numerically identical.
000828  ### What the two-component decomposition can and cannot say
000830  The complete-curve result is the overall test. A separate, secondary
000831  decomposition asks whether that curve difference can be attributed cleanly to
000832  (1) a constant parametric level component or (2) a cyclic shape component.
000833  Those two attribution tests form a two-test Benjamini–Hochberg family within
000834  each placement and dataset.
000836  No component remains supported after that two-test adjustment. This does
000837  **not** invalidate or reverse the significant global curve result. It means
000838  only that the evidence cannot cleanly separate the overall curve association
000839  into an independent “level” contribution and an independent “shape”
000840  contribution after accounting for the two attribution attempts. The two
000841  components are parameterization-dependent parts of one fitted curve and are
000842  not a second omnibus test of whether the full curve differs.
000897  ### Conditional effect-size branch
000899  The predeclared effect-size branch opens only when the placement-specific raw
000900  global test has $p<0.050$. It opened for both primary placements. The reported
000901  curve-variation estimator is the mean, across 48 clock bins, of the sample
000902  variance across the two equally weighted fitted biological-sex curves. It is a
000903  conditional descriptive magnitude on the squared model-prediction scale, not
000904  variance explained. Its 95% interval uses the accepted participant-cluster
000905  robust covariance and first-order delta method.
000928  The fitted parametric level coefficient is also reported with its
000929  participant-cluster-robust pointwise 95% interval, but its interpretation is
000930  conditional on the cyclic sex-deviation smooth and must not be used as a
000931  stand-alone replacement for the complete-curve test.
000952  ## 6. Diagnostics and influence
000954  The diagnostic sequence checked convergence and warnings, cyclic closure at
000955  midnight, site sum-to-zero constraints, basis dimensions, residual
000956  distributions, boundary-aware residual autocorrelation, formal concurvity,
000957  robust covariance behaviour, participant influence, and agreement between the
000958  curve-space and coefficient-block formulations of the global test.
000982  All four models were assessed as **acceptable with specified limitations**.
000983  They converged without serious warnings, closed cyclically at midnight,
000984  preserved the inherited site constraint, had no severe basis-dimension flags,
000985  and reduced boundary-aware lag-1 residual correlation to 0.070–0.083. The
000986  remaining limitations are the Gaussian log-scale mean assumption, exact-zero
000987  mass, heavy residual tails, heteroscedasticity, and concurvity inherited from
000988  the H02 temporal analysis. These caution against interpreting the curves as a
000989  complete generative account of melEDI, but they did not trigger the prespecified
000990  unacceptable-model criteria.
001022  Every delete-one-participant primary and sensitivity result remained below
001023  0.050. Maximum participant score contribution ranged from 2.7% to 7.6%, while
001024  the effective number of contributing clusters was smaller than the nominal
001025  participant count, especially for chest. The robust covariance therefore
001026  materially protects inference from within-participant dependence, but the
001027  chest result should still be read as complementary.
001029  ## 7. Sensitivity findings
001031  ### Gap timing
001033  Applying the same model and global test to the gap-timing-unaware dataset gave
001034  raw global p-values of
001035  `r format_p(global_tests$p_raw[global_tests$dataset_label == "Gap-timing-unaware dataset" & global_tests$placement_label == "Near eye"])`
001036  near eye and
001037  `r format_p(global_tests$p_raw[global_tests$dataset_label == "Gap-timing-unaware dataset" & global_tests$placement_label == "Chest"])`
001038  at chest. This supports stability to whether the timing of remaining missing
001039  observations is used for metric-specific adjustment. It does not test every
001040  possible missing-data mechanism.
001042  ### Activity context
001044  The activity comparison separates sample restriction from covariate
001045  adjustment. First, the unadjusted sex-pattern model is refitted to the exact
001046  activity-complete sample. Second, activity level and activity-specific cyclic
001047  deviations are added on that same sample. Because both models use identical
001048  rows, the difference is not confounded by the activity-related attrition
001049  between those two fits.
001095  On the activity-complete near-eye sample, the unadjusted raw p-value was
001096  `r format_p(activity_comparison$p_raw[activity_comparison$placement_label == "Near eye" & activity_comparison$analysis_step == "Activity-complete sample, unadjusted"])`
001097  and the activity-adjusted raw p-value was
001098  `r format_p(activity_comparison$p_raw[activity_comparison$placement_label == "Near eye" & activity_comparison$analysis_step == "Same sample, activity-adjusted"])`.
001099  At chest these were
001100  `r format_p(activity_comparison$p_raw[activity_comparison$placement_label == "Chest" & activity_comparison$analysis_step == "Activity-complete sample, unadjusted"])`
001101  and
001102  `r format_p(activity_comparison$p_raw[activity_comparison$placement_label == "Chest" & activity_comparison$analysis_step == "Same sample, activity-adjusted"])`.
001103  Thus the activity-adjusted exploratory global tests do not meet the 0.050
001104  rule. This is compatible with attenuation or reduced precision, but it does
001105  not establish that activity explains, mediates, or makes the accepted primary
001106  association disappear.
001140  The activity-adjusted effect-size branch was deliberately not run: both
001141  activity-adjusted raw global p-values were at or above 0.050, so the
001142  predeclared gate for the no-sex baseline and its bootstrap pilot remained
001143  closed. No post hoc effect-size estimate was manufactured for an unsupported
001144  adjusted global effect.
001177  ### Placement comparability and analyses not run
001179  A scalar near-eye-versus-chest identity plot is not valid for H11. The stored
001180  primary fits use different all-available samples, and H11 has no fitted
001181  common-sample placement outputs or prespecified scalar reduction of its
001182  temporal estimand. The reader report therefore presents separate near-eye and
001183  chest curves without implying equivalence or a paired placement difference.
001217  Other analyses remain outside the accepted result set: the registered hourly
001218  geometric-mean outcome, a paired common-sample placement analysis,
001219  leave-one-site-out refits, an all-zero-inclusive frame that has not been
001220  provided, and alternative temporal bases or response families. They could
001221  address different robustness questions, but none is necessary to document
001222  the provenance of the accepted result and none was silently substituted for
001223  the prespecified analysis.
001252  ## 8. Code map and execution order
001254  The scientific computation was separated into bounded, checkpointed steps so
001255  that each fitted object and downstream calculation has a recorded input
001256  identity. This page itself executes only the final display step.
001278  ## 9. Output and claim provenance
001280  Every claim in the result report can be traced to a full-precision stored
001281  table or figure source. The preparation figures also have paired CSVs and
001282  physical-size QA records. Internal filenames preserve computational
001283  traceability even where reader-facing terminology has been updated.
001336  ## 10. How to examine or reproduce H11
001338  Use the project R 4.6.1 library and run only the H11 entry points in their
001339  recorded order. The frozen fitted outputs and source-data files allow the
001340  reader report and this preparation record to be rendered without repeating
001341  model fits or resampling. Rebuilding scientific artifacts should occur only
001342  when an analytical input or approved method changes, and should then produce
001343  new hashes and manifests rather than silently replacing accepted evidence.
001355  The preparation-report manifest records every H11-scoped script, test,
001356  scientific artifact, source-data CSV, figure, report source, rendered report,
001357  and consequential shared policy file with SHA-256, byte size, producer, and R
001358  version. Its own integrity is checked after rendering so that the manifest
001359  does not contain a circular self-hash.

===== notebooks/sensitivity_battery.qmd =====
000006  ## Purpose
000008  Run the predefined comparison and alternative analyses after the main metric
000009  definitions and models have been fixed.
000011  ## Manuscript-prepared-data sensitivity
000013  Every hypothesis receives a **manuscript-prepared-data sensitivity**. The same
000014  new H01--H11 code and models are fitted twice: once to the newly prepared data
000015  for the main analysis and once to the dataset prepared for the manuscript,
000016  with only objectively demonstrable data errors corrected. This comparison
000017  therefore isolates the effect of data preparation and metric calculation.
000019  It does not rerun the manuscript's modelling implementation. Differences
000020  between the full manuscript-generating analysis chain and the main analysis
000021  are discussed in the audit with the author, but they are not treated as this
000022  sensitivity analysis.
000024  The permitted data corrections and required comparison fields are fixed in
000025  [`manuscript_prepared_data_sensitivity.md`](../audit/decisions/manuscript_prepared_data_sensitivity.md).
000026  Both scenarios report the same exact fitted-model sample sizes and 95%
000027  confidence intervals.
000029  ## What this notebook uses and produces
000031  - Uses: the main H01--H11 results, the H01--H11 results obtained from the
000032    manuscript-prepared dataset, the placement comparison, the decisions about
000033    which metrics remain interpretable, and a table listing every planned
000034    alternative analysis.
000035  - Alternatives considered: 70%, 80%, and 90% coverage requirements; all
000036    near-eye records versus participant-days (one participant's record for one
000037    calendar day) measured at both positions; complementary chest results;
000038    omission of sparse sites or one site at a time; justified alternative
000039    outcome distributions or transformations; alternative ways to represent
000040    repeated measurements and time dependence; and approved alternatives for
000041    gaps or state definitions.
000042  - Produces: the main-versus-manuscript-prepared-data comparison; sample counts
000043    for each additional alternative; and direct comparisons restricted to
000044    records shared by the main and alternative analyses. All comparisons report
000045    estimates with 95% confidence intervals; the exact participants,
000046    participant-days, time-resolved observations, and sites used by each model;
000047    model-fitting and distribution checks; influential-observation checks;
000048    warnings; and a conclusion about stability. Every comparison also reports
000049    the participants, participant-days or participant-level rows, one-hour or
000050    30-minute observations where applicable, and sites shared by both models.
000051  - Fixed rules: change only one choice at a time; do not test every possible
000052    combination of alternatives; do not add temperature.
000069  Each conclusion will be classified as:
000071  - **stable** when the conclusion and practical magnitude remain similar;
000072  - **quantitatively sensitive** when the conclusion remains but the magnitude
000073    crosses a practical threshold defined before the comparison;
000074  - **qualitatively sensitive** when the direction or statistical conclusion
000075    changes;
000076  - **inconclusive** when the available information does not support a clear
000077    conclusion;
000078  - **invalid** when the alternative analysis violates a required assumption; or
000079  - **non-estimable** when the model cannot estimate the requested quantity.

===== notebooks/assemble_artifacts.qmd =====
000005  ## Purpose
000007  Collect the checked analysis results into the final manuscript and
000008  supplementary tables and figures without copying files by hand.
000010  ## What this notebook uses and produces
000012  - Uses: approved descriptive, placement, H01--H11, and sensitivity-analysis
000013    results.
000014  - Produces: the final main-text and supplementary tables and figures; CSV files
000015    containing the exact displayed data; a map linking each manuscript element
000016    to the notebook or script that generated it; a map linking claims to results;
000017    and file checksums, which act as digital fingerprints. These files are
000018    stored under `artifacts/09_tables/` through `artifacts/12_manifests/`.
000019  - Fixed rules: every manuscript display has one identified notebook or script
000020    that generates it. Manually copied files in `assets/` are not used as the
000021    source of a result. Preparing the journal-upload folder remains a separate
000022    final step.
000039  The notebook will stop with a clear error if a required generating file, input
000040  checksum, source-data file, caption, unit, or output-map entry is missing.

===== audit/hypotheses/implementation_result_comparison_contract.qmd =====
000012  ## Purpose
000014  This document retains the substantive comparison requirements shared across
000015  the hypothesis audits. H01 and H02 keep the review structure already in use.
000016  H03 through H11 use the staged workflow in
000017  [`H03-H11_gated_workflow.qmd`](H03-H11_gated_workflow.qmd), which distributes
000018  these requirements across separate audit, implementation-comparison,
000019  standalone reader-facing, and pipeline-provenance documents, with an optional
000020  production-computation gate between implementation and reader-facing results.
000021  The descriptive analysis is outside that workflow.
000023  In H03--H11 audit documents, **V0 analysis** is the short name for the
000024  analysis that produced the submitted manuscript. The final reader-facing
000025  hypothesis documents do not use that term.
000027  The main question for each hypothesis is:
000029  > How do the analysis method, numerical results, and defensible claim after
000030  > audit differ from those in the submitted analysis?
000032  Comparison with the preregistration remains mandatory, but is reported in a
000033  separate section. The submitted analysis, rather than the preregistration, is
000034  the reference for the direct audit discussion.
000036  A separate **gap-timing-unaware dataset sensitivity** applies the same new
000037  H01--H11 implementation to the primary dataset and to the predefined
000038  comparison dataset, with only objectively demonstrable data errors corrected.
000039  It isolates data-preparation changes and does not rerun the submitted modelling
000040  implementation. Its rules are fixed in
000041  [`manuscript_prepared_data_sensitivity.md`](../decisions/manuscript_prepared_data_sensitivity.md).
000043  At first reader-facing use, explain that the gap-timing-unaware dataset still
000044  passed the general 50%-per-hour and 80%-per-day coverage rules, but the timing
000045  of the remaining missing observations is not used for an additional
000046  metric-specific adjustment. For contrast in that first explanation only, the
000047  primary dataset may be described as something that could be interpreted as a
000048  time-sensitive primary metric dataset. It is called simply **the primary
000049  dataset** thereafter. Internal identifiers may retain historical names for
000050  provenance; visible Stage 3 and Stage 4 labels follow `REPORT-010`.
000052  ## Site names, order and colours
000054  Every hypothesis uses the submitted manuscript's site names, ordering and
000055  colour mapping in visible prose, tables, legends, facets and figures. The
000056  authoritative mapping is `config/site_display_registry.csv` and its full rules
000057  are recorded in
000058  [`site_display_conventions.md`](../decisions/site_display_conventions.md).
000060  Missing sites are omitted without changing the order or colours of the sites
000061  that remain. Internal data codes and model reference coding are unaffected.
000062  When the shared German colour would make a figure ambiguous, use direct
000063  labels, shapes or line types in addition to—not instead of—the registered
000064  colour.
000066  ## Required comparison content
000068  H01 and H02 retain the comparison order already in use. For H03--H11, the
000069  following content is distributed across the gated documents rather than
000070  forced into one page:
000072  1. the question and exact comparison being estimated;
000073  2. what the manuscript reported;
000074  3. the main analysis;
000075  4. a direct submitted-versus-main table covering magnitude, direction,
000076     uncertainty, statistical conclusion, analytical sample, and claim;
000077  5. the gap-timing-unaware dataset sensitivity using the same new implementation
000078     for both datasets;
000079  6. the reason for every method or data-preparation change;
000080  7. diagnostics and other registered sensitivities;
000081  8. relation to the preregistration; and
000082  9. verification status, decisions still needed, and conditions that require a
000083     new review.
000085  A preregistered method is never substituted for the method represented in the
000086  manuscript merely because the two differ.
000088  ## Files used to record the comparisons
000090  [`hypothesis_implementation_audit.csv`](../ledgers/hypothesis_implementation_audit.csv)
000091  records one row for each hypothesis, comparison being estimated, analytical
000092  role, sensor placement, and analysis scenario. It identifies the submitted and
000093  main-analysis code and output files and records why the method stayed the same
000094  or changed. The gap-timing-unaware scenario points to the same new model
000095  implementation as the main scenario and to a different prepared-data artifact.
000097  [`hypothesis_result_comparisons.csv`](../ledgers/hypothesis_result_comparisons.csv)
000098  records one row per exact result, contrast, model comparison, or conclusion
000099  about a fitted curve. It retains the numerical values rather than relying only
000100  on qualitative summaries.
000102  [`hypothesis_data_sensitivity_comparisons.csv`](../ledgers/hypothesis_data_sensitivity_comparisons.csv)
000103  records the same new implementation fitted to the primary and
000104  gap-timing-unaware datasets, including both exact model samples, their common
000105  sample, intervals, multiplicity fields, diagnostics, and stability
000106  classification.
000108  [`hypothesis_comparison_status.csv`](../ledgers/hypothesis_comparison_status.csv)
000109  tracks whether each H01--H11 HTML page has completed its method, result, claim,
000110  preregistration, and verification sections.
000112  The existing
000113  [`result_differences.csv`](../ledgers/result_differences.csv) remains the
000114  detailed record for preparation and metric changes. In that file, the field
000115  `baseline_value` stores the value from the submitted analysis, never a
000116  preregistered value. Hypothesis-level rows link to these detailed differences
000117  where relevant rather than duplicating them.
000119  ## Method comparison
000121  The method comparison explicitly covers:
000123  - which data release and file were used, how rows were matched, and any
000124    correction to source records;
000125  - sensor placement and analysis scenario;
000126  - inclusion rules and the denominator used to calculate a proportion;
000127  - outcome, predictor, contrast, transformation, and unit;
000128  - whether the analysis concerns participants, participant-days, hours, or
000129    individual observations, and how repeated observations are handled;
000130  - the assumed outcome distribution and scale, the fixed and random model
000131    terms, and how correlation between observations---including observations
000132    close together in time---is handled;
000133  - the set of related tests adjusted together for multiple testing and the
000134    adjustment method;
000135  - checks of model fit, influential observations, and whether model fitting
000136    completed stably; and
000137  - code, fitted-model file, result table, figure, and the exact data used to
000138    produce the figure.
000140  The gap-timing-unaware dataset sensitivity must use the same new analysis
000141  implementation as the main analysis. Only its prepared-data input differs.
000142  Each correction made to the gap-timing-unaware dataset has an identifier and
000143  evidence locator. A model cannot be simplified only for this sensitivity.
000145  For every multiple-testing correction, the hypothesis page must state which
000146  single test is selected from each outcome model before the results are
000147  examined. Individual coefficients and follow-up contrasts are not automatically
000148  added to the same set of tests.
000150  ### Exact formula display
000152  Every H01--H11 audit page must contain a visible, evaluated R code cell that
000153  prints the exact Wilkinson formula objects supplied to the fitting functions.
000154  The display must include the selected model and every fitted model used in a
000155  likelihood, Wald, score, AIC, or other model-structure comparison, including
000156  full, reduced, null, and sensitivity models.
000158  The displayed formulas must be generated from the same formula-building
000159  function or recovered directly with `stats::formula()` from the fitted model;
000160  a separately typed mathematical equation is explanatory but does not satisfy
000161  this requirement. A generic `response ~ ...` formula may represent several
000162  outcomes only when their right-hand side, random effects, smooths, offsets, and
000163  correlation structure are identical. If any of these differ, each distinct
000164  formula must be shown and linked to its model ID.
000166  Wilkinson notation does not encode the response distribution, link,
000167  estimation method, contrasts, or residual-correlation settings. Those must be
000168  shown immediately beside the formula output and remain in the model manifest.
000169  The formula cell and manifest must agree exactly before a hypothesis audit can
000170  be marked complete.
000172  ### Pilot before a computation-heavy bootstrap
000174  Every computation-heavy bootstrap or simulation must first run a separate
000175  50- or 100-replicate pilot with the proposed production code. The pilot is
000176  used to verify failure handling, checkpointing, output tables and figures, and
000177  to estimate the duration and resource needs of the full run. It is labelled
000178  as non-inferential and cannot supply manuscript values or claims.
000180  Before the full run starts, the hypothesis task must show the pilot output,
000181  report successful and failed replicates, provide a reasoned full-runtime range
000182  and expected completion time, and obtain explicit author confirmation. Any
000183  code, diagnostic or display repair requires another pilot. The complete rule
000184  and the exception for the H01 run already underway are recorded in
000185  [`bootstrap_execution_policy.md`](../decisions/bootstrap_execution_policy.md).
000187  Each difference is assigned a `change_class`. `none` is an affirmative result
000188  and means that the submitted method was reproduced and retained. A change is
000189  not called a repair unless the evidence and verification justify that
000190  classification.
000192  ## Result comparison
000194  The direct submitted-versus-main table records, where applicable:
000196  - the exact fitted-model counts—not counts from the prepared dataset—for
000197    participants, participant-days, hours, 30-minute observations, other
000198    observations, and sites;
000199  - valid measurement hours used to derive a participant-day or
000200    participant-level outcome, clearly separated from observations that entered
000201    the fitted model;
000202  - category counts, including category × site counts when an interaction or
000203    sparse-cell decision is involved;
000204  - exact common-sample counts, where a common sample means that both analyses
000205    use the same participants, participant-days, or time-resolved observations;
000206  - estimate, standard error, confidence interval, raw and adjusted
000207    \(p\)-values, the set of related tests adjusted together, and the result's
000208    position within that set;
000209  - changes in direction, uncertainty, statistical significance, or conclusion;
000210  - the submitted manuscript claim and the main-analysis claim; and
000211  - links to the exact submitted and main-analysis code and output files.
000213  The accompanying data-sensitivity table repeats the result using the same new
000214  H01--H11 implementation on the gap-timing-unaware dataset. It reports both
000215  model samples, their exact common sample, estimates and 95% confidence
000216  intervals, raw and adjusted \(p\)-values, diagnostics, and a stability
000217  classification.
000219  ### P-value display
000221  Every displayed raw or adjusted p-value follows `REPORT-008`: use a leading
000222  zero and exactly three decimal places, show values strictly below 0.001 as
000223  `<0.001`, and use bold type only when that displayed value meets its explicitly
000224  labelled significance rule. Raw and adjusted values are labelled separately.
000225  Full numeric precision remains in CSV and model artifacts; display rounding is
000226  never used to decide significance. The shared helper is
000227  `scripts/pipeline/p_value_display.R`.
000229  The numerical values take precedence over a qualitative label. A difference is described as
000230  `negligible` or `material` only when a practical margin was declared before
000231  the comparison. Otherwise it is `not_classified_no_margin`.
000233  ### Paired near-eye and chest comparison
000235  Each Stage 3 report applies `REPORT-009`. When separately fitted near-eye and
000236  chest estimates exist for the identical paired/common sample, outcome,
000237  predictor, contrast, model structure, transformation, and scale, show their
000238  relationship with near-eye on the horizontal axis, chest on the vertical axis,
000239  an identity line, null lines, and equal axis geometry. Component intervals may
000240  appear in an adjacent table when including them in the plot would be illegible.
000242  Do not compare unmatched all-available samples in this display or treat
000243  closeness to the identity line as equivalence. When a scalar comparison would
000244  be invalid, state why and use a matched curve, contrast plot, coefficient plot,
000245  or concise table instead. Retain paired source data, an informative caption,
000246  and alt text.
000248  ### Figure readability and layout
000250  Every figure follows `REPORT-011`. Judge text at the final rendered size, not
000251  in an enlarged plotting window. Unless a narrower placement is specified,
000252  assess the figure at a 170-mm display width. An A4 page may be used only as a
000253  QA simulation of that width; final assets are tightly bounded to the figure
000254  and are not saved on an A4 canvas.
000256  Main axis, tick, legend, facet, direct-label, and annotation text is normally
000257  approximately 5--7 pt; only minor non-essential text may be approximately
000258  3.5--5 pt. Match canvas dimensions and panel layout to the amount of content
000259  rather than shrinking all labels.
000261  Record the base design width and height, literal export-scale multiplier,
000262  exported dimensions, intended display width, display reduction factor,
000263  smallest essential nominal text, and effective final text. The `ggsave()`
000264  scale argument changes the device dimensions and is not implemented by
000265  enlarging theme fonts or graphical marks. Browser pixels or an enlarged native
000266  canvas alone do not establish a pass.
000268  After changing text size, wording, wrapping, dimensions, or layout, inspect
000269  the final output for clipping, overlap, distorted or unusually wide/narrow
000270  text, undesirable line breaks, overly long unwrapped labels, compressed data
000271  regions, and marks that become illegible after scaling. A successful render is
000272  not a visual-QA pass. Display-only repairs consume stored outputs and do not
000273  rerun scientific computation.
000275  For non-negative, strongly right-skewed values containing meaningful exact
000276  zeros—melEDI in particular—reader-facing plots follow `REPORT-013` and use
000277  `LightLogR::symlog_trans(base = 10, thr = 1, scale = 1)`. Labels and breaks
000278  remain in the original unit and paired source data retain untransformed values.
000279  This is a display rule only and does not alter a fitted response or estimand.
000281  ## Preregistration section
000283  The preregistration section reports whether the audited analysis is aligned,
000284  clarified, an approved deviation, an unapproved deviation, not specified,
000285  ambiguous, could not be estimated as registered, or not applicable. It links
000286  to the preregistration summary and deviation register, while remaining
000287  separate from the submitted-versus-audited comparison.
000289  ## When author approval is needed
000291  Author approval is required before adopting a change to the comparison being
000292  estimated, inclusion rule, metric, statistical model, set of tests adjusted
000293  together, substantive result, preregistration interpretation, or manuscript
000294  claim. A hypothesis review cannot be completed until:
000296  - its submitted method and result are recoverable or explicitly
000297    marked otherwise;
000298  - its primary and gap-timing-unaware sensitivity code and output
000299    pass a run in a fresh R session;
000300  - every correction to the gap-timing-unaware dataset passes the data-fidelity
000301    rule or is removed;
000302  - method and result rows are complete and linked;
000303  - relevant diagnostics and sensitivities are classified;
000304  - consequential manuscript claims are linked to verified results; and
000305  - mandatory checks are `PASS`.
000307  A change in an earlier preparation or analysis step requires every affected
000308  hypothesis to be reviewed again.

===== audit/hypotheses/H03-H11_gated_workflow.qmd =====
000012  ## Scope
000014  This workflow applies to H03 through H11. It does not retroactively change
000015  the work already underway for H01, H02, or the descriptive tables and
000016  figures.
000018  Each hypothesis is completed in four stages:
000020  1. audit and proposed analysis;
000021  2. implementation and comparison with V0;
000022  3. a standalone reader-facing results report; and
000023  4. a scientific description and provenance record for the result-producing
000024     pipeline.
000026  When final inference requires a computation-heavy bootstrap or simulation,
000027  the production run is an additional approval gate between Stages 2 and 3. It
000028  is not a numbered reporting stage. Work stops after Stages 1 and 2, after any
000029  production-computation gate, and after Stage 3 until the author has reviewed
000030  the relevant HTML output. Stage 4 begins only after the reader-facing result
000031  has been accepted.
000033  One hypothesis task retains responsibility from Stage 1 through Stage 4. It
000034  pauses at each gate and resumes in the same task only after explicit author
000035  approval; a new task is not created for every stage. This continuity preserves
000036  the evidence and reasoning developed during the audit without weakening any
000037  approval gate.
000039  ## Terms used in the audit reports
000041  **V0 analysis** means the analysis that produced the submitted manuscript.
000042  It includes the methods, code, prepared data, numerical results, figures,
000043  tables, and claims used for that submission.
000045  **Main analysis** means the analysis developed and approved through this
000046  audit for the Nature Health submission.
000048  **V0-prepared data** means the data as prepared for V0, with only objectively
000049  demonstrable data errors corrected. Applying the new model implementation to
000050  these data is an audit-only data-preparation sensitivity. It is not a second
000051  version of the reader-facing analysis.
000053  The final reader-facing document does not use the term V0 and does not
000054  describe discarded candidate models or variants considered while developing
000055  the analysis.
000057  In Stage 3 and Stage 4, this sensitivity is called the
000058  **gap-timing-unaware dataset**. Its first mention explains that it still passed
000059  the general 50%-per-hour and 80%-per-day coverage rules; the label means that
000060  the timing of the remaining missing observations is not used for an additional
000061  metric-specific adjustment. At that first explanation only, the primary
000062  dataset may be described as something that could be interpreted as a
000063  time-sensitive primary metric dataset. It is called simply **the primary
000064  dataset** thereafter. Internal scenario identifiers and filenames may retain
000065  their established names for provenance.
000067  ## File layout and gate ownership
000069  For a hypothesis such as H03, the staged files are:
000071  - `audit/hypotheses/H03/01_audit_and_plan.qmd`;
000072  - `audit/hypotheses/H03/02_implementation_and_v0_comparison.qmd`;
000073  - `audit/hypotheses/H03/production_computation.qmd`, only when a production
000074    bootstrap or simulation requires a separate review gate;
000075  - `notebooks/hypotheses/H03.qmd`, the Stage 3 standalone reader-facing report;
000076    and
000077  - `audit/hypotheses/H03/H03_analysis_preparation.qmd`, the Stage 4 scientific
000078    description and provenance record for the result-producing pipeline.
000080  Every source document is rendered to Quarto HTML for review. Exact model
000081  outputs, fitted-model samples, figure data, and table data remain stored as
000082  separate RDS or CSV artifacts and are linked from the relevant report.
000084  The coordinating task owns shared preparation code, central ledgers, Quarto
000085  configuration, manuscript files, and the central gate-status table. A
000086  hypothesis task may prepare a gate report and a shared-change request, but it
000087  must stop rather than changing shared inputs or marking its own gate approved.
000088  After coordinator approval, the same hypothesis task may proceed to the next
000089  stage and edit only its stage-authorized Hxx scripts, tests, artifacts, audit
000090  files, and final hypothesis notebook.
000092  ## Stage 1: Audit and proposed analysis
000094  The first document audits the V0 analysis against:
000096  1. the exact preregistered hypothesis and analysis specification;
000097  2. all preregistration deviations stated before or in the submitted
000098     manuscript;
000099  3. the implementation and results that actually produced V0; and
000100  4. the analysis plan already developed during the Nature Health audit.
000102  The report must explain, in plain language:
000104  - what question and quantity the analysis is intended to address;
000105  - what V0 actually estimated and how it handled repeated observations;
000106  - where V0 agrees with or differs from the preregistration and stated
000107    deviations;
000108  - any coding, data, model, multiplicity, diagnostic, figure, table, or claim
000109    problem found;
000110  - the proposed main analysis and why each retained or changed choice serves
000111    the analysis goal;
000112  - the exact primary and complementary roles of near-eye and chest-level
000113    measurements;
000114  - which registered sensitivities will later be needed; and
000115  - every decision that needs author approval before implementation.
000117  The report contains visible evaluated R cells that print the exact Wilkinson
000118  formula objects used by V0 and proposed for the main analysis, including every
000119  full, reduced, null, or otherwise fitted comparison model. Distribution,
000120  link, contrasts, estimation method, correlation structure, and multiplicity
000121  families are stated beside those formulas because they are not encoded by
000122  Wilkinson notation.
000124  Stage 1 does not select a method by looking for a preferred new result. It may
000125  reproduce V0 and run checks needed to establish whether its implementation is
000126  valid, but the proposed estimand and model are fixed before the new
000127  inferential results are examined.
000129  ### Gate after Stage 1
000131  Implementation starts only after the author approves the proposed estimand,
000132  sample, outcome construction, model formulas, response distribution,
000133  comparisons, multiplicity families, diagnostics, and placement roles. Any
000134  unresolved major choice keeps the hypothesis at Stage 1.
000136  ## Stage 2: Implementation and comparison with V0
000138  The approved analysis is implemented and run in a clean R session. If a
000139  computation-heavy bootstrap is required, this stage uses only the approved
000140  50- or 100-replicate pilot and labels its intervals as preliminary and not for
000141  inference.
000143  The second document must:
000145  - show the exact formulas supplied to every fitted model;
000146  - report the exact participants, participant-days, hours, observations,
000147    sites, and relevant category cells that entered each model;
000148  - report estimates in practical units with 95% confidence intervals and raw
000149    and adjusted p-values where applicable;
000150  - format every displayed raw or adjusted p-value under `REPORT-008`: a leading
000151    zero and three decimal places, `<0.001` for values strictly below 0.001, and
000152    bold type only when the value meets its explicitly labelled significance
000153    rule;
000154  - show convergence, singularity, residual, distributional, temporal, and
000155    influence checks relevant to the model;
000156  - display those diagnostics with a plain-language interpretation and state
000157    whether each fitted model is acceptable, acceptable with a stated
000158    limitation, or not acceptable for inference;
000159  - compare the main-analysis implementation, results, and defensible claims
000160    directly with V0;
000161  - apply the identical new implementation to the V0-prepared data and report
000162    that audit-only data sensitivity separately;
000163  - recreate the V0 figures and tables faithfully, then make only sensible,
000164    explicitly documented adjustments for correctness and readability;
000165  - produce the corresponding near-eye and chest-level outputs; and
000166  - export the exact data behind every durable figure and table.
000168  Faithful recreation does not require preserving an error. When a V0 display
000169  cannot be retained, the report shows what changed and why. Site names, order,
000170  and colours follow `config/site_display_registry.csv`; metric names follow
000171  `config/metric_display_registry.csv`.
000173  Expensive secondary work, including the full sensitivity battery, may remain
000174  out of Stage 2. The report must nevertheless list every deferred analysis and
000175  state whether it could alter the interpretation.
000177  ### Gate after Stage 2
000179  The author reviews the method, preliminary or final estimates, V0 comparison,
000180  recreated displays, and proposed reader-facing presentation. A material
000181  change reopens Stage 1. Otherwise the author approves either the
000182  production-computation gate or, when no production computation is needed,
000183  Stage 3.
000185  ## Optional production-computation gate between Stages 2 and 3
000187  This stage is required whenever final inference depends on a
000188  computation-heavy bootstrap or simulation. It follows the separate
000189  pilot-first policy in `audit/decisions/bootstrap_execution_policy.md`.
000191  After explicit approval of the pilot display and estimated runtime, the full
000192  run uses the same inputs, targets, formulas, resampling unit, failure rules,
000193  and output schema as the approved pilot. Its Quarto report records:
000195  - requested, successful, failed, and warning-producing replicates;
000196  - seeds, checkpoint behavior, elapsed time, and compute resources;
000197  - final intervals and other bootstrap-derived quantities;
000198  - any difference between the pilot preview and the production presentation;
000199  - updated main-versus-V0 and V0-prepared-data comparisons; and
000200  - all diagnostics required before the result can be treated as final,
000201    together with their interpretation and an explicit acceptable,
000202    acceptable-with-limitations, or not-acceptable assessment.
000204  Pilot estimates are never substituted for production estimates. A code,
000205  model, target, or display change after the pilot requires a new pilot and a
000206  new runtime approval.
000208  ### Gate after the production run
000210  The final reader-facing report is blocked until the production outputs pass
000211  their checks and the author approves them. Material instability or a changed
000212  scientific conclusion reopens the relevant earlier stage.
000214  ## Stage 3: Standalone reader-facing hypothesis report
000216  The final `notebooks/hypotheses/Hxx.qmd` stands on its own. It contains no
000217  mention of V0, submitted-versus-new comparisons, discarded candidates,
000218  working variants, or the construction history of the analysis.
000220  In concise, accessible language, it presents:
000222  1. the question, including the exact preregistered hypothesis as a quotation;
000223  2. a compact **Answer in brief** note directly after that section, under
000224     `REPORT-012`;
000225  3. what was analysed and why the chosen method addresses the question;
000226  4. every deviation from the preregistration, listed explicitly;
000227  5. the result in prose, figures, and digestible tables;
000228  6. the exact model sample and uncertainty needed to interpret the result;
000229  7. a restrained interpretation and the important limitations; and
000230  8. how the conclusion changes under the registered scientific
000231     sensitivities.
000233  The **Answer in brief** element is a Quarto note callout of normally two to
000234  four sentences. It states the verified primary near-eye conclusion, a compact
000235  consequential magnitude and 95% confidence interval when scientifically
000236  valid, whether complementary chest evidence agrees materially, and whether a
000237  central sensitivity changes the conclusion. It does not introduce new
000238  calculations, omit multiplicity qualifications, convert inconclusive evidence
000239  into evidence of no effect, or mention workflow and artifact history.
000241  The data-preparation sensitivity is presented as the gap-timing-unaware
000242  dataset under the definition above. Historical artifact names do not appear
000243  in reader-facing prose, headings, tables, figures, captions, legends, alt text,
000244  or visible link text.
000246  The report also shows the model diagnostics needed to judge the analysis. It
000247  explains what the diagnostic displays or summaries indicate and states
000248  plainly whether the near-eye and chest-level models are acceptable,
000249  acceptable with specified limitations, or not acceptable. Diagnostic output
000250  without interpretation is not sufficient.
000252  Near-eye measurements remain primary. Every hypothesis also has a clearly
000253  labelled chest-level section that recreates the primary figures and tables
000254  with chest-level data, rather than merely referring to an audit table or
000255  listing chest coefficients in prose.
000257  Each report also assesses the `REPORT-009` paired-placement display. When the
000258  same estimand was fitted separately at near eye and chest on the identical
000259  paired/common sample and comparison scale, include a near-eye-versus-chest
000260  estimate plot with an identity line, null lines, and equal axis geometry. If a
000261  scalar plot would be scientifically invalid or the approved analysis did not
000262  produce common-sample estimates, explain why and use the closest valid matched
000263  curve, contrast, coefficient, or table comparison without fitting a new model
000264  solely for display.
000266  Reader-facing tables use `gt`. They are divided into conceptually coherent,
000267  legible pieces rather than continued across several pages or filled with
000268  audit-only columns. Complete numerical results remain available as linked CSV
000269  files. Figures and tables use familiar manuscript terminology, `melEDI` in
000270  reader-facing text, the registered site display rules, and 95% confidence
000271  intervals wherever they are estimable.
000273  Displayed p-values follow `REPORT-008` in prose, tables, figures, captions, and
000274  annotations. Raw and adjusted values are labelled separately; full precision
000275  is retained in source data and significance is decided before display
000276  rounding.
000278  Necessary technical terms are explained on first use. Code may be folded,
000279  but each analytical section states what was done, why it was done, what the
000280  display shows, and what the result means.
000282  ## Stage 4: Scientific description and provenance of the result-producing pipeline
000284  After the reader-facing result and its accepted artifacts are fixed, the same
000285  hypothesis task creates
000286  `audit/hypotheses/Hxx/Hxx_analysis_preparation.qmd`. This companion is the
000287  fourth documentation stage, but it does not refit or reinterpret the
000288  scientific result and does not introduce a new inferential gate. It explains
000289  how the accepted result was prepared and how its provenance can be checked
000290  without reintroducing V0 comparisons into the result report.
000292  “Stage 4” is internal workflow shorthand. Neither “Stage 4” nor “Step 4”
000293  appears in the companion's reader-facing title, prose, diagrams, captions,
000294  tables, callouts, or website navigation. The companion is written for an
000295  ordinary scientific reader, not for someone familiar with coordinator/worker
000296  roles, approvals, gates, task ownership, or migration history. Those internal
000297  mechanics and irrelevant construction variants are omitted.
000299  The page follows `REPORT-007` and the revised H02 exemplar. It explains what
000300  data and code produced the result, why each analytical operation was needed,
000301  what it contributed, and what evidence shows that it behaved as intended. In
000302  analytical order, it covers:
000304  - exact verified inputs and their identity, schema, key, content, and value
000305    checks;
000306  - model-frame and sample construction, exclusions, support, missingness,
000307    coverage, transformations, units, and temporal ordering;
000308  - exact participants, participant-days, observations or hours, sites, and
000309    relevant per-site or category samples;
000310  - model construction, central settings, estimands, contrasts, multiplicity,
000311    uncertainty, and inference;
000312  - convergence, distributional, residual, temporal, influence, and other
000313    applicable diagnostics;
000314  - registered sensitivity branches; and
000315  - the provenance of stored models, resampling results, predictions, figures,
000316    tables, diagnostics, source data, manifests, and claims.
000318  The page names the hypothesis-specific scripts or modules in execution order
000319  and explains what each reads, why it is separate, and what it produces. Useful
000320  compact `gt` tables and plots show input distributions, support, missingness or
000321  coverage, and intermediate quantities. A Mermaid data-to-result diagram is
000322  used when it materially clarifies the chain. Technical concepts are explained
000323  on first use in specific but plain publication language.
000325  ### Bounded rendering
000327  An informational note states which bounded identity checks and lightweight
000328  descriptive calculations run during rendering and which quantities are read
000329  from stored outputs. It does not use warning, important, caution, or danger
000330  styling.
000332  Rendering must not refit models, re-estimate autocorrelation, regenerate model
000333  predictions, rerun bootstraps or simulations, recompute Shapley analyses, or
000334  repeat other expensive scientific computation. Externally generated results
000335  need not be recomputed, but their producing scripts and exact output locations
000336  are identified.
000338  ### Displays, source data, and website identity
000340  Tables are readable and split into digestible pieces. Every figure has an
000341  informative caption, non-empty alt text, and exact paired source data. Axes use
000342  scientifically appropriate scales. Discrete masses such as exact zeros are
000343  shown separately when they would compress a continuous distribution, while
000344  the page states explicitly whether those observations remained in the fitted
000345  model.
000347  Every figure also follows `REPORT-011`. Text is assessed at final displayed
000348  size. Unless a narrower placement is specified, the required reference is a
000349  170-mm figure width. An A4 page may be used only as a QA simulation; final
000350  figure files are tightly bounded to the figure and are not saved on an A4
000351  canvas. Text is normally approximately 5--7 pt for axes, ticks, legends,
000352  facets, direct labels, and important annotations; approximately 3.5--5 pt is
000353  reserved for minor non-essential text. After any typography or layout change, inspect the
000354  rendered figure for clipping, overlap, distorted text, poor line wrapping,
000355  labels that compress the data region, and marks that become illegible after
000356  scaling. Figure-only repairs use stored outputs and do not trigger a model or
000357  data rerun.
000359  The QA evidence records the base design width and height, literal export-scale
000360  multiplier, exported dimensions, intended display width, display reduction
000361  factor, smallest essential nominal text, and its effective final size. The
000362  `ggsave()` scale argument enlarges the device; it is not reproduced by
000363  enlarging plot fonts, lines, points, or keys. An enlarged image or browser-width
000364  inspection without a final-display calculation is `NOT TESTED`.
000366  Reader-facing plots of non-negative, strongly right-skewed values containing
000367  meaningful exact zeros follow `REPORT-013`. For melEDI this means
000368  `LightLogR::symlog_trans(base = 10, thr = 1, scale = 1)`, with axis labels and
000369  breaks in lx and untransformed values retained in the paired source-data CSV.
000370  This display rule does not change model fitting or the scientific estimand.
000372  The companion and `notebooks/hypotheses/Hxx.qmd` link to each other. The
000373  coordinator adds the companion immediately after the result in the Nature
000374  Health render list and navigation only after the source exists. The authoring
000375  QMD remains available in the website output and its copied bytes must match
000376  the authoring source exactly.
000378  The hypothesis-specific preparation manifest records the source, website QMD
000379  copy, HTML, figures, paired source data, scripts, scientific outputs,
000380  environment identity, byte sizes, and SHA-256 checksums. It excludes the final
000381  worker inventory and any other self-reference that would make the checksum
000382  chain circular.
000384  ### Structural verification and closure
000386  Every hypothesis adds a focused structural test for reader-facing wording,
000387  informational callout semantics, reciprocal links and navigation, figure
000388  captions and alt text, `gt` tables, paired source data, bounded executable R
000389  chunks, source-copy identity, and manifest coverage. The shared verifier is
000390  `scripts/pipeline/hypothesis_preparation_provenance_contract.R`; each
000391  hypothesis extends it with its own required content and expensive function
000392  names.
000394  The hypothesis task refreshes its preparation manifest, non-circular worker
000395  inventory, tests, and handoff after coordinator-owned website integration.
000396  This documentation closure does not reopen an accepted result unless it
000397  exposes a substantive discrepancy. The complete contract is recorded in
000398  `audit/decisions/hypothesis_preparation_provenance_companions.md`.
000400  ## Reopening rules
000402  An approved stage reopens when an upstream input changes or when a finding
000403  changes an estimand, inclusion rule, metric, model, multiplicity family,
000404  substantive result, preregistration interpretation, or claim. A low-risk
000405  formatting repair may proceed without reopening only when tests establish that
000406  the data, fitted models, estimates, uncertainty, and interpretation are
000407  unchanged.
